{"id": "fd2ad3a7beb8fc7e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:45:47.227974+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Uniform small-frequency common-metric obstruction, independent of conditioning: for ANY legal quadratic-minimal-polynomial prefix of total mass S with |S alpha|<=delta, 0<=S² sigma<=delta² and delta<=1/100, every free completion and final b in[0,2] obeys 2F<= (1/2+10delta)/S. Hence on the assigned near-critical ruler the ENTIRE elliptic disk |z|S_pre<=1/100 has limsup benchmark ratio <=12/(5e)<0.9; if |z|S_pre tends to0 the ratio is <=2/e. Arbitrarily singular common metrics cannot rescue this low-frequency degeneration.", "evidence": "# Small spectral frequencies cannot hide a useful nonnormal transient\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No new verifier or numerical allocation\nis used. All new arguments, including the benchmark comparison, are proved\nbelow. Accepted facts1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d supply only the\ninterpolation convention and the exact benchmark recursions. Shared candidate\nbf591e52671f6d33 suggested the sharper exponent comparison; its argument is\nincluded in full rather than adopted as a verified lemma.\n\n## 1. General finite-prefix statement\n\nLet q>=1, let h_0,...,h_(q-1)>=0 have total mass S>0, and let queried\nreal vectors and values satisfy\n\n    ||x_0||=1, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, 0<=i<q,\n    g_i=A x_i, 0<=i<=q.\n\nHere A is a fixed real linear map on the invariant span of these vectors.\nAssume it satisfies\n\n    A^2-2 alpha A+sigma I=0,\n    |S alpha|<=delta, 0<=S^2 sigma<=delta^2,\n    0<=delta<=1/100.                                      (1)\n\nSuppose the SAME jets (x_i,g_i,f_i), including all query indices0,...,q\nand the optimum(0,0,0), are interpolable by a convex differentiable\n1-smooth function. For EVERY such interpolant F and EVERY b in[0,2],\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q) <= (1/2+10 delta)/S <=3/(5S).            (2)\n\nThere is no restriction on the gradient at the unqueried point x_q-b g_q.\nNo bound on ||A|| or a similarity-map condition number is assumed. Zero\nprefix steps cause no problem. Only the positive total mass S is required.\n\nIn particular (1) holds for ANY elliptic common-metric prefix\n\n    x_i=T(Re P_i(z),Im P_i(z)),\n    g_i=T(Re[zP_i(z)],Im[zP_i(z)]),\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), ||T e_1||=1,\n\nwith arbitrary invertible real T, whenever S|z|<=delta: set\nalpha=Re z, sigma=|z|^2 and\nA=T [[Re z,-Im z],[Im z,Re z]] T^(-1).\nThe result also includes any quadratic-minimal-polynomial degeneracies\nsatisfying (1). The proof does not use positivity of the symmetric part\nof A on unqueried vectors.\n\n## 2. Variation controlled by physical vectors\n\nWrite\n\n    a_i=h_i/S, r_i=S g_i, Abar=S A,\n    alpha_bar=S alpha, sigma_bar=S^2 sigma,\n    R=||r_0||, G=max_(0<=i<=q)||r_i||,\n    D=max_(0<=i<=q)||r_i-r_0||,\n    X=max_(0<=i<=q)||x_i||.\n\nThe a_i are nonnegative and sum to1. Since\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)a_t r_t, one has X<=1+G. Cayley--Hamilton in (1)\ngives the exact identity\n\n    Abar r_i=2 alpha_bar r_i-sigma_bar x_i.\n\nConsequently\n\n    r_i-r_0=-sum_(t<i)a_t[2 alpha_bar r_t-sigma_bar x_t],\n    D<=(2delta+delta^2)G+delta^2,\n    G<=R+D.\n\nPut kappa=2delta+delta^2<=201/10000. Solving for D yields\n\n    D<=(kappa R+delta^2)/(1-kappa)\n      <=(201R+1)/9799.                                 (3)\n\nThis is a statement about the actual physical gradients, not about an\narbitrarily chosen coordinate norm. It remains true when T diverges or\nbecomes arbitrarily close to singular as the horizon varies.\n\n## 3. Ordered interpolation bounds the initial scaled gradient\n\nUse Q_ij<=0 in the convention of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nThe optimum inequalities give\n\n    0<=f_q, S f_0<=<x_0,r_0>-||r_0||^2/(2S)<=R.\n\nThe ordered consecutive inequality with gradient g_(i+1) gives\n\n    f_i-f_(i+1)\n       >=h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>+||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2.\n\nMultiply by S and sum, dropping the nonnegative square terms. From\nr_i=r_0+(r_i-r_0) and Cauchy--Schwarz,\n\n    R>=S(f_0-f_q)\n      >=sum_i a_i<r_(i+1),r_i>\n      >=R^2-2RD-D^2.                                  (4)\n\nIf R>=1, (3) implies\n\n    D/R<=(201+1)/9799<1/48,\n\nbecause202*48=9696<9799. Substitution in (4) gives\n\n    R<=2304/2207<21/20.\n\nIf R<1 the same final upper bound holds directly. Thus in all cases\n\n    R<=21/20,\n    D<=(201*(21/20)+1)/9799=4241/195980<1/45,\n    G<=R+D<43/40.                                     (5)\n\nRetaining delta rather than its maximum gives a useful vanishing bound.\nSince kappa<=201delta/100 and delta^2<=delta/100,\n\n    D<= [(201/100)*(21/20)+1/100]delta/(9799/10000)\n      =(21205/9799)delta <(13/6)delta.                  (6)\n\nAll constants here are rational inequalities. For the last inequality,\n6*21205=127230<127387=13*9799.\n\n## 4. Free completion and its entire terminal-correlation credit\n\nLet r=r_q and set\n\n    C_tail=sum_(i<q)a_i<r,r-r_i>.\n\nThis is the exact terminal-correlation correction to a constant gradient.\nIt obeys, without any sign assumption,\n\n    C_tail<=2 G D.\n\nThe optimum inequality at the last query and the trajectory identity give\n\n    2S f_q<=2<x_q,r>-||r||^2/S\n      =2<x_0,r>-2||r||^2+2 C_tail-||r||^2/S\n      <=1/2+4 G D.                                    (7)\n\nThe last line uses 2||r||-2||r||^2<=1/2. Equations (5)--(6) now yield\n\n    2S f_q<=1/2+4*(43/40)*(13/6)delta\n            =1/2+(559/60)delta <=1/2+10delta.           (8)\n\nFor any 1-smooth completion of the queried jets and b in[0,2], the\nsmoothness inequality gives\n\n    F(x_q-b g_q)<=f_q-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=f_q.\n\nTogether with (8), this proves (2) for every completion, including the\npointwise maximal one furnished by accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nNo endpoint orbit has been imposed and no terminal gradient has been\nchosen to make the estimate convenient.\n\n## 5. Consequence on the assigned near-critical ruler\n\nFor each m>=128 put\n\n    s=1+sqrt(2), c=s(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1.\n\nHold c,a fixed throughout each horizon. Let S_m be the sum of H_m and\nlet S_pre=S_m-d_0 be the sum of the retained prefix with its final d_0\ndeleted. The final queried index is q=n-1 and its initial distance is1.\n\nFor every legal common-metric elliptic prefix with\n\n    |z| S_pre <=1/100,                                 (9)\n\nequation (2) gives, uniformly over all its metrics, values and completions,\n\n    sup_(0<=b<=2) 2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1)) <=3/(5 S_pre).  (10)\n\nThe original final step d_0 lies in[0,2]. We next compare (10) with the\nACTUAL exact recursion B_n, not with a proposed dyadic asymptotic value.\n\nLet rho=log_2 s. Accepted factb98bb807d514e80d defines the primitive mass\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 through the increasing join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand defines B_n through the increasing join\n\n    Gjoin(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nDirectly from those recursions we prove\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (11)\n\nTo see this, put V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 for t>0. Differentiating with\nt=exp(u) gives\n\n    d^2/du^2 log V(exp u)\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.\n\nThe numerator is positive because rho>1 and the derivative of\n(1+t)^rho-1-rho t is rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0. Convexity at u and -u\nimplies V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=(s-1)^2=2. Given positive A,B, write\nt=(A/B)^(1/rho) and D=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho. Then\n\n    (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB,\n    D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0,\n    Omega(A,B)<=D.\n\nZero arguments follow by continuity. In the primitive recursion a split\ninto lengths k,l with k+l=n-1 therefore has\n\n    Omega(C_k,C_l)<=[(k+1)+(l+1)]^rho=(n+1)^rho.\n\nInduction proves the first bound in (11). Moreover\nsqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence Gjoin(A,D)<=4A+D. If the dominant split\nhas k+l=n-1, induction gives\n\n    B_n<=4(k+1)^rho+1+4l^rho<=1+4n^rho.\n\nThe last inequality follows from (u+v)^rho>=u^rho+v^rho for nonnegative\nu,v, since rho>1. This proves (11) directly.\n\nThe ruler sum is obtained by summing its geometric step multiplicities:\n\n    S_m=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),\n    1+S_m=A_mass c^m+(1-A_mass)2^m,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).\n\nAs m tends to infinity, A_mass tends to1 because\ns(s-2)=1, and c^m/s^m=(1+1/m)^m tends to e. Since 2/s<1 and d_0 is\nbounded, S_pre/s^m tends to e. Therefore (10)--(11) give\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity)\n      B_(2^m-1) sup_(all legal data satisfying(9),0<=b<=2)\n                       2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))\n      <=12/(5e)<9/10.                                 (12)\n\nFor the last strict comparison, the series for e gives\ne>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3. The supremum includes horizon-dependent metrics\nwith arbitrary conditioning and all legal queried function values.\n\nMore sharply, if |z_m|S_pre tends to zero, apply the first bound in(2)\nwith delta=|z_m|S_pre. The right side in(12) improves to2/e<1.\nThus a singular similarity-map limit at a vanishing scaled eigenvalue\ncannot create a constant-level improvement over the Huber scale.\n\n## 6. Exact scope and the next unresolved interval\n\nThis result excludes the entire disk (9), not only circular metrics or\nfixed eigenvalue arguments. It is uniform over all chronologies with\nthe stated mass in the general result; chronology becomes important\nagain outside this perturbative disk. The prior candidate unit-box\nexclusion is separate and is not a premise here.\n\nThe order-one scaled region |z|S_pre>1/100 remains open, as do frequencies\noutside both the small disk and the previously studied unit neighborhood.\nThis is not an upper bound on unrestricted W, an exclusion of several\ndifferent elliptic modes, or a solution to the arbitrary-schedule target.\nThe useful residual question is whether an actual legal common-metric\norbit at |z|S_pre of order1 can pay the missing terminal-correlation\nconstant while meeting every chronological interpolation cycle.\n\n## Retrieval and verification record\n\nFact search returned the accepted interpolation, circular-orbit and\nbenchmark statements above. Matlas searches on nonsymmetric linear\nconvex-gradient transcripts and Cayley--Hamilton variation returned\nunrelated results, including arXiv2102.07922,0802.4382,2306.07098 and\n1511.01635. No theorem from those papers is used. A web background search\non convex gradient flow did not supply an applicable finite-prefix\nresult and is not a mathematical dependency. This proof is symbolic.\nEvery new displayed bound remains unverified pending a future explicitly\nallocated fact_submit; no unverified candidate is treated as a fact.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["19d9b5250dd8a4fc", "bf591e52671f6d33", "723bb8436582cf0b"], "artifact": "research/small_frequency_common_metric_obstruction.md", "status": "complete_unverified_candidate", "new_fact_submit_calls": 0, "new_numerical_evaluations": 0}, "glossary": {"S_pre": "Total mass of H_m after deleting its final d_0, for the assigned near-critical family.", "Abar": "Scaled physical orbit map S A, where S is the retained prefix mass.", "r_i": "For this candidate, the physical scaled queried gradient S g_i.", "C_tail": "Exact scaled terminal-correlation correction sum_i(h_i/S)<r_q,r_q-r_i>.", "Gjoin": "Benchmark dominant mass join2A+D/2+sqrt(D²+8AD)/2.", "A_mass": "Coefficient a/(c-2) in the near-critical ruler sum formula."}}
{"id": "666b5b9b14038a0f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:49:06.114244+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Resumed general VARIABLE-MATRIX credit lane after C33 audit: explicit affine completion now parameterizes ALL real interpolation representations of any full PSD remainder. M must satisfy M e=0 and r^T M r=1-(1+s)^2 r_n^2; a displayed skew-completion formula reconstructs every actual multiplier. On a four-positive-step quadratic-balanced face this leaves a full3x3 PSD matrix and3 skew parameters, with every actual sign constraint retained. This is a concrete next interface, NOT a repair or cone solution.", "evidence": "# A concrete affine completion for the variable-matrix repair lane\n\nStatus: new self-contained candidate algebra, NOT verifier-accepted. This is\nan explicit parameterization for the next proof attempt, not a re-selection\ntheorem or a strict cone separation. No numerical solver or fact_submit.\n\n## Definitions and full matrix identity\n\nFix h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0 with sum s>0. All n+1 formal gradients are independent.\nLet x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and define the actual Q_ij and intrinsic V_h by\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                               -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nDefine the column e of n+1 ones, the lower triangular matrix A with A_ii=1\nand A_it=h_t for t<i, and r=A^(-1)e. Thus r_0=1 and\nr_(i+1)=(1-h_i)r_i. The vectors e,r are independent because s>0.\nWrite omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T and\n\n D_quad=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),\n c_quad=1-(1+s)^2 r_n^2.\n\nFor a real off-diagonal multiplier array lambda, put\nL=diag(lambda e)-lambda. Value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q is exactly\nL^T e=omega. By definition L e=0. The COMPLETE formal gradient matrix is\n\n                 M=D_quad+sym(L A),                 (1)\n\nwhere sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. To check this, the position contribution is\nsym(L(A-I)). The squared-difference terms are sym(L)-diag(omega)/2.\nAdding the diagonal terms in V_h gives exactly (1).\n\n## Which PSD matrices admit some real multiplier representation?\n\nClaim. A symmetric PSD matrix M has a real multiplier representation (1)\nwith the actual value cancellation if and only if\n\n                    M e=0, r^T M r=c_quad.           (2)\n\nThis permits arbitrary real multiplier signs; the actual sign restrictions\nfor a primitive or E_theta certificate remain separate and are not deleted.\n\nNecessity: evaluating the value-canceling identity on the affine Huber run\nwith every gradient equal to1 gives e^T M e=0, hence M e=0 by PSD.\nThe full-curvature quadratic trajectory has gradients r_i and every Q_ij=0.\nTelescoping h_i(2-h_i)r_i^2=r_i^2-r_(i+1)^2 gives\nr^T D_quad r=1-(1+s)^2 r_n^2=c_quad, and evaluating the same identity on\nthat legal quadratic gives the second assertion in (2).\n\nHere is an explicit sufficiency construction. Put C=M-D_quad and set\n\n U=[e,r], Z=[C e-A^T omega,-C r], H=U^T U, W=U^T Z.\n\nThe two-by-two matrix H is positive definite. The matrix W is skew-symmetric:\n\n e^T C e=omega^T A e=s^2+sum h_i^2,\n r^T C r=0,\n e^T(-C r)+r^T(C e-A^T omega)=-omega^T A r=-omega^T e=0.\n\nThe first equality uses M e=0 and the displayed diagonal D_quad, and the\nsecond uses r^T M r=r^T D_quad r. Define\n\n K_0=Z H^(-1)U^T-U H^(-1)Z^T\n                          -U H^(-1)W H^(-1)U^T.     (3)\n\nIt is skew-symmetric, and multiplication by U gives K_0 U=Z: the last\ntwo correction terms cancel because Z^T U=-W. Now put\n\n B=C+K_0, L=B A^(-1), lambda_ij=-L_ij for i!=j, lambda_ii=0.\n\nThe relations K_0 r=-C r and K_0 e=C e-A^T omega give\nL e=B r=0 and L^T e=A^(-T)B^T e=omega. Thus L is precisely the row\nLaplacian of its extracted off-diagonal array, and its flows cancel every\nactual value coefficient. Also sym(L A)=C, proving (1).\n\nAll representations of this same M arise by replacing K_0 with K_0+S,\nwhere S is any skew-symmetric matrix satisfying S e=S r=0. Indeed their\ndifference must be skew after multiplying L by A, and must kill both e,r;\nthe converse follows from the same equations. This also recovers directly\nthe fixed-remainder obstruction already independently proved earlier.\n\nConsequently a PSD real-multiplier identity with no sign restrictions exists\nif and only if c_quad>=0. For sufficiency, let\nz=r-e(e^T r)/(e^T e), so z!=0 and r^T z=||z||^2. The rank-one choice\n\n                 M=c_quad z z^T/(r^T z)^2\n\nsatisfies (2); use (3). The case c_quad=0 allows M=0. This consequence is\nNOT a smooth-convex validity theorem: unrestricted negative multipliers can\ninvalidate such an implication. It merely separates the full PSD condition\nfrom the indispensable actual interpolation sign inequalities.\n\n## Concrete next interface for four positive steps\n\nIf n=4 and the schedule is quadratic-balanced, c_quad=0, every permitted PSD\nmatrix must annihilate both e and r. The space orthogonal to span{e,r} has\ndimension3. Thus M is an arbitrary PSD three-by-three matrix on that space,\nand the free S in (3) has three independent skew entries on that same space.\nAll twenty actual multipliers are then EXPLICIT affine functions of these\nsix symmetric and three skew coordinates, through (3).\n\nThe actual E_theta requirements remain\n\n lambda_ij>=0 for j!=4,\n lambda_i4>=-theta h_i for i<4, 0<=theta<1.\n\nOrdinary primitivity requires lambda_i4>=0 as well. A successful repair must\nmove the symmetric M coordinates, not just S: the earlier unit-step family\nalready refutes fixed-M repair in arbitrarily many positive steps. No change\nto M is declared PSD merely because its scalar quadratic calibration is\ncorrect; all three eigen-directions in the displayed quotient must remain\nPSD and every multiplier sign must be checked.\n\nThe next concrete attack is to start on a four-step quadratic-balanced face\nand either construct such a simultaneous M/S repair for one negative terminal\nedge, or give an exact E_theta identity and a legal negative-V witness for\nthe same actual schedule. Physical capacity and objective amplification are\nadditional obligations. This note supplies neither outcome and does not\nclaim the general cone problem solved. All new algebra is shared research\nevidence only; no fact was added to the graph.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_credit_general_reselection", "branch_id": "max4_variable_matrix_credit_completion", "status": "partial; explicit_completion_but_sign_repair_unresolved", "finding_ids": ["4aaee6131a92da0e", "05f35c4b34104f68"], "artifact": "local_memory/variable_matrix_credit_completion.md", "attempt_type": "direct", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted_stronger_fixed_matrix_repair", "summary": "4aaee6131a92da0e gives an arbitrary-length strict quotient-PSD counterexample to retaining the entire remainder; a different matrix remains possible."}, "key_stuck_points": ["Choose simultaneous symmetric/skew changes preserving every actual nonterminal multiplier sign and full PSD while eliminating a negative terminal coefficient.", "Alternatively provide one actual nonprimitive E_theta-certified physical tail of length>=4 and a useful capacity/amplification comparison."], "fact_submit_calls": 0, "new_pep_evaluations": 0}, "glossary": {"A": "Lower triangular chronological matrix with diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i.", "e": "All-one vector of n+1 entries.", "r": "A^(-1)e, the full-curvature quadratic gradient vector.", "omega": "Required finite value-flow vector(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s).", "D_quad": "Diagonal matrix with entries h_i(2-h_i) and final-s(s+2).", "c_quad": "Full-curvature energy1-(1+s)^2r_n^2.", "U,Z,H,W,K_0": "Explicit two-column skew-completion objects defined in the evidence.", "S": "Free skew matrix annihilating both e and r, preserving a supplied full remainder."}}
{"id": "a0bb50ebcb4cb599", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:50:33.418550+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C35 closes EVERY ordered fresh-child TREE incidence relay on near-critical H_m, not just paths: explicit real simplex vectors, an exact backward optimal-energy recursion retaining every normal, and true-distance objective/primitive formulas. All dyadic depths and all tree shapes have best union payoff exactly Huber K; for >=3 checkpoints score/K is uniformly <0.964 and decays doubly exponentially in depth. Split-and-merge positive correlations remain outside the obstruction.", "evidence": "# C35. Every ordered fresh-child tree relay is too weak on the near-critical ruler\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no verifier or solver. All ingredients used\nbelow are proved internally. The result covers the exact incidence-flow class\ndefined here, not arbitrary tree-indexed vectors or arbitrary simplex walks.\n\n## 1. A genuine real tree family with all normal inequalities\n\nPartition a schedule into N ordinary blocks of masses S_i and checkpoints\nb_i>1 after each block except the last. Set M_i=S_i+1, L_i=b_i-1 and\nT_i=M_i+L_i for i<N-1. Choose T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1).\n\nChoose ANY rooted tree on vertices 0,...,N-1 with root0 and with each parent\nstrictly earlier than its children. Write Ch_i for the children of i, and\nW_i={j>i:parent(j)<i} for the waiting vertices at phase i. Define positive\nenergies E_i and actual vectors in the standard basis of R^N:\n\n    alpha_i=sqrt(E_i)/T_i,\n    beta_ij=sqrt(E_j)/T_i  (j in Ch_i),\n    p_i=alpha_i e_i-sum_(j in Ch_i) beta_ij e_j.                (1)\n\nThe vector matrix is triangular with positive diagonal, hence these are\nlinearly independent vectors with an explicit positive definite real Gram\nmatrix. Distinct normals have negative inner product exactly along a tree\nedge, and zero inner product otherwise. Let C=conv(0,p_i) and\n\n    F(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nIts maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_C(x). The projection variational\ninequalities give firm nonexpansiveness, hence Lipschitz continuity, of P_C.\nBounding maximization differences with the two maximizing linear forms gives\ngrad F=P_C. Thus F is one fixed everywhere-defined convex 1-smooth function.\n\nStart at x_init=sqrt(E_0)e_0. At the beginning of phase i the nonzero\ncoordinates are sqrt(E_j) for the current vertex i and for j in W_i.\nDuring ordinary partial mass u the current coordinate is\n(T_i-u)alpha_i, each waiting coordinate stays sqrt(E_j), and each child\ncoordinate is u beta_ij. After the checkpoint, the current coordinate\nvanishes and every child coordinate becomes sqrt(E_j). This is the\nprescribed GD recursion whenever its projection normals hold.\n\nFor y=x-p_i, all its coordinates are nonnegative. Its own normal value is\n\n    delta_i(u)=(T_i-u-1)alpha_i^2\n                         -(u+1)sum_(j in Ch_i)beta_ij^2.      (2)\n\nA past vertex has nonpositive dot product with y: its positive coordinate\nhas been exhausted, and all its remaining coordinates are nonpositive.\nA future vertex j has no mass yet in its children, since the tree has a\nunique parent. Thus its dot product with y is exactly\n\n    E_j/T_j                      if j in W_i,\n    (u+1)sqrt(E_j)alpha_j/T_i     if j in Ch_i,\n    0                            otherwise.\n\nThe comparison with zero is also required. The own value (2) decreases in u;\nthe child comparisons increase; the waiting comparisons are constant.\nTherefore ALL comparisons against ALL past/future vertices and zero are\nequivalent to the following endpoint conditions for i<N-1:\n\n    L_i E_i-M_i sum_(j in Ch_i)E_j >=\n       T_i^2 max(0,\n           max_(j in W_i) E_j/T_j,\n           (M_i/T_i) max_(j in Ch_i) E_j/T_j).                 (3)\n\nEmpty maxima are zero. The terminal phase has no children or waiting\nvertices, and its condition is just T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1).\nThese checks include every actual ordinary query and checkpoint query.\nThey construct a fixed legal smooth-convex function, without oracle resets.\n\nThe minimizer set is C^polar: a violated dot-product inequality yields a\npositive maximand at a small multiple of the corresponding vertex.\nIts inequalities force every coordinate of a minimizer nonpositive, by\nbackward induction through the tree. Thus 0 is a closest minimizer to\nsqrt(E_0)e_0, with true squared initial distance E_0.\n\nWriting T=T_(N-1) and D=2S_(N-1)+1, the exact normalized objective is\n\n    score=(2T-D) E_(N-1)/(T^2 E_0).                            (4)\n\nFor fixed tree, phase masses, T and terminal energy E_(N-1)=1, setting (3)\nto equality in decreasing order of i constructs the componentwise least\npositive feasible E_i. Indeed every right hand side is a nondecreasing\nfunction of already-set future energies. This proves, without numerical\noptimization, that the recursion attains the best score in this exact class.\nThere is always a positive future waiting or child energy until the last\nphase, so the constructed E_i are positive.\n\nThe primitive defect is also exact on the SAME normalized trajectory.\nAt each projection query F_t=<x_t,g_t>-||g_t||^2/2. Consequently\n\n    2h_t F_t+h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2\n                         =||x_t||^2-||x_(t+1)||^2.\n\nIf s is the entire schedule mass and\nV=2sum_t h_t(F_t-F_end)+sum_t h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2\n  -s(s+1)||g_end||^2, then\n\n    V/E_0=1-[(T-S_(N-1)+s)/T]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.                 (5)\n\nNo raw negative sign is substituted for objective or distance control.\n\n## 2. A chronology bound valid for EVERY tree shape\n\nPut H_i=E_i/T_i. The next chronological vertex i+1 is either a child of i\nor already waiting. If it is waiting, (3), even after dropping all child\ncosts, implies\n\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i/T_i.\n\nIf it is a child, retaining its own term in the child sum and its own\nnormal comparison gives\n\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i T_i/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))].\n\nHence every legal tree relay satisfies\n\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <=\n        max(L_i/T_i, L_i T_i/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))]).             (6)\n\nThis is only a necessary bound; the constructive recursion (3) still keeps\nevery other comparison. The upper bound (6) is used only to exclude payoff.\n\n## 3. Uniform quantitative exclusion on H_m\n\nFix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m),\na=(1-1/m)/c, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).\nLet S_j be the mass of H_j and M_j=1+S_j. Geometric summation gives\n\n    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2) in (0,1).\n\nChoose ANY dyadic blocking depth r<m. Put k=m-r, N=2^k, M=M_r and\nq=a c^r/M. The N ordinary blocks are all copies of H_r and\n\n    L_i=M q c^(nu_2(i+1)),\n    T_i=M(1+q c^(nu_2(i+1)))  (i<N-1).                       (7)\n\nThe following simple uniform bounds will suffice:\n\n    12/5<c<39/16,      2/5<q<=c-2<7/16.                    (8)\n\nFor the upper bound, sqrt(2)<99/70 and\n(169/70)(129/128)<39/16. Also a<c-2 because ac<1<c(c-2).\nThis gives 0<A<1 and q<=c-2. Since M_r<=c^r, q>=a.\nFinally a=(m-1)/[lambda(m+1)] >=127/(129lambda)\n>2540/6321>2/5, using lambda<49/20. This proves (8).\n\nEvery even index i<N-1 has the low checkpoint q_i=q. In (6), its\nwaiting option is at least its child option. For a nonterminal next phase\nthis is q+q^2<=1+q_(i+1), which follows from q<1 and q_(i+1)>=q.\nFor the terminal next phase it follows from q+q^2<1<=T/M.\nThus every such low transition obeys\n\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <= q/(1+q).                                  (9)\n\nEvery odd index has q_i=q c^ell for some ell>=1, and is followed by\na low checkpoint. Its child option dominates the waiting option:\nq_i^2+q_i>1+q, because q_i>24/25 while q<7/16.\nTherefore\n\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <= q c^ell / D_ell,\n    D_ell=(2+q+q c^ell)/(1+q c^ell)>1.                       (10)\n\nThere are N/2 low transitions and 2^(k-ell-1) transitions of level ell>=1.\nCounting binary valuations gives\n\n    product_(i<N-1)q c^(nu_2(i+1))=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k).\n\nSince (2T-D)/T<2, (4), (9), and (10) yield\n\n    score < [2/(M(1+q)c^k)]\n             (qc)^(N-1) /\n             [(1+q)^(N/2) product_(ell=1)^(k-1)\n                           D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].             (11)\n\nLet K=1/(2M_m-1), the global Huber score. The exact mass relation is\n\n    M_m=M[(1-q/(c-2))2^k+(q/(c-2))c^k] <=M c^k.\n\nIt follows that score/K<B_k, where\n\n    B_k=4(qc)^(2^k-1) /\n       [(1+q)^(2^(k-1)+1)\n          product_(ell=1)^(k-1)D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].          (12)\n\nFor k=2,\n\n    B_2=4(qc)^3(1+qc)/[(1+q)^3(2+q+qc)].\n\nThis increases in both q and c. Its logarithmic q derivative is\n3/[q(1+q)]+(c-1)/[(1+cq)(2+(1+c)q)]>0. Its logarithmic c\nderivative is 3/c+q/(1+cq)-q/(2+(1+c)q)>0. Consequently (8) gives\n\n    B_2 <\n      273^3*529/[1024*23^3*897]\n      =10763254593/11175730176 =: eta <1.                    (13)\n\nThe recurrence is exact:\n\n    B_(k+1)/B_k^2 = (qc)(1+q)/(4D_k)\n                  <6279/16384 =: zeta <1.                  (14)\n\nThus, for every k>=2,\n\n    score/K < B_k\n             <=zeta^(2^(k-2)-1) eta^(2^(k-2)) <1.            (15)\n\nThis excludes EVERY ordered rooted tree and EVERY legal positive energy\nchoice in the specified family. If k grows, the ratio tends to zero\nexponentially in 2^k. In particular no choice of tree shape avoids the loss.\n\nWhen k=1 the sole tree is the two-vertex path. The exact endpoint condition\ngives, with T_0=M(1+q) and D=2M-1,\n\n    score=q(2T-D)/[T(T+T_0)].\n\nThe maximum is attained at T=(D+sqrt(D^2+2D T_0))/2. Writing epsilon=1/M\nits ratio to K is\n\n    2q[2(2+q)-epsilon] /\n       [3+q-epsilon+sqrt((2-epsilon)(4+2q-epsilon))]\n      <=4q(2+q)/[2+q+sqrt(3+2q)].\n\nThe last expression increases in q>=0 because\nsqrt(3+2q)/(2+q) decreases. As q<9/20 it is less than\n441/[245+10sqrt(390)]<1; the latter inequality is 196^2<39000.\n\nAllowing r=m leaves just one vertex and one ordinary block. The choice\nT=2S_m+1 attains K. Therefore the union over ALL blocking depths, ALL\nordered rooted trees and ALL legal parameters has exact best score K.\n\n## 4. The obstruction and the next genuinely different mechanism\n\nTree branching reduces the number of edges from root to terminal, but it\ncreates waiting coordinate masses. Their supporting planes must be below\nthe active plane at every intervening query. Inequality (9) is the\nquantitative price at each low checkpoint, independent of the chosen tree.\n\nThe unique-parent condition is load-bearing. With two parents contributing\nto one child coordinate, two earlier gradient vectors can have positive\ncorrelation. A future plane can then have a negative term from a child\nalready preloaded by another parent. The waiting comparison E_j/T_j used\nabove is no longer correct, so (6) and (15) do not cover directed acyclic\nsplit-and-merge constructions. Nor do they cover signed coordinate mass,\nrevisiting exhausted coordinates, other simplex geometries, or general\nsmooth-convex functions. The original all-schedule minimax target is open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["f8b4d9f258ab98c0", "e865afe9e0f7093c", "bf591e52671f6d33"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "research/c35_tree_flow_nearcritical_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"E_tree_i": "Squared coordinate mass loaded into tree vertex i before its phase.", "H_tree_i": "Waiting support height E_tree_i/T_i; distinct from the ruler word H_m.", "Ch_tree_i": "Future children of i in an ordered rooted tree.", "W_tree_i": "Future vertices whose unique parent was processed strictly before i.", "eta_tree": "Exact rational 10763254593/11175730176<1 bounding the four-phase tree score/K.", "zeta_tree": "Rational 6279/16384<1 controlling the recursive tree score bound."}}
{"id": "1d9af6b7bf48b5e3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T16:55:58.704243+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C36 positively correlated split-and-merge diamond is also closed: every legal four-phase near-critical diamond has an explicit no-merge replacement preserving EXACT normalized objective, primitive defect and closest-minimizer distance, while satisfying every normal. Hence its payoff/K is <10763254593/11175730176. A separate complete all-endpoint interface covers arbitrary forward nonnegative transfer graphs; the diamond result does not cover those general graphs.", "evidence": "# C36. All-normal forward-flow interface and a complete four-phase merge deletion\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier/solver used. This gives an actual\nfamily of positively correlated real gradients and closes its smallest merge\non the four-block near-critical word. It does not exclude all forward graphs,\nall correlations or unrestricted W.\n\n## 1. General acyclic nonnegative coordinate flow\n\nThere are N chronological phases, ordinary masses S_i and checkpoints b_i>1\nfor i<N-1. Set M_i=S_i+1, L_i=b_i-1 and T_i=M_i+L_i. The artificial\nterminal T_(N-1) is at least M_(N-1). Choose nonnegative transfer amounts\na_ij for 0<=i<j<N. Put R_0>0 and R_j=sum_(i<j)a_ij>0 for j>=1, and define\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i.\n\nThese are explicit independent real vectors, so C=conv(0,p_i) is a simplex.\nThe function F(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2) is convex and 1-smooth\nwith grad F=P_C: the maximization is strictly concave in p, the projection\nvariational inequality gives firm nonexpansiveness, and maximization\ndifferences give differentiability.\n\nStart at R_0 e_0. At the start of phase i, define\nr_j^(i)=sum_(ell<i)a_(ell,j) for j>=i; the current coordinate is R_i,\nall processed coordinates vanish, and each future coordinate is r_j^(i).\nAfter ordinary partial mass u, the current coordinate is\n(1-u/T_i)R_i and future coordinate j is r_j^(i)+(u/T_i)a_ij.\nThe checkpoint exhausts coordinate i and adds a_ij to each future coordinate.\n\nFor y=x-p_i the current coordinate is (1-(u+1)/T_i)R_i and the future\ncoordinates are r_j^(i)+((u+1)/T_i)a_ij. All are nonnegative. Its own\nnormal value is\n\n    delta_i(u)=[(T_i-u-1)R_i^2\n        -T_i sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i)\n        -(u+1)sum_(j>i)a_ij^2]/T_i^2.                        (1)\n\nEvery past vertex has nonpositive dot product with y. For future j>i,\n\n    <p_j,y>=\n      [R_j(r_j^(i)+(u+1)a_ij/T_i)\n       -sum_(k>j)a_jk(r_k^(i)+(u+1)a_ik/T_i)]/T_j.           (2)\n\nALL normal inequalities are therefore exactly delta_i(u)>=0 and\ndelta_i(u)>=(2), for every future j, at BOTH u=0 and u=S_i.\nEach difference is affine in u, so these two actual endpoint queries suffice.\nThe terminal condition is T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1). This supplies a complete\nsame-function realization test, not a local-correlation surrogate.\n\nThe negative second term in (2) is the inherited-coordinate cancellation\nabsent from unique-parent trees. Explicit positive correlations occur when\ntwo vertices share a child:\n\n    <p_i,p_j>=[-a_ij R_j+sum_(k>j)a_ik a_jk]/(T_i T_j), i<j.\n\nThus this construction genuinely goes beyond pairwise orthogonality and\nthe negative-edge tree Gram class.\n\nBackward induction in <p_i,z><=0 shows every coordinate of a minimizer is\nnonpositive. Hence 0 is closest to R_0e_0, with distance R_0.\nAt the terminal sample, writing S=S_(N-1), T=T_(N-1), s=sum of ALL steps,\n\n    score=2F_end/R_0^2=(2T-2S-1)R_(N-1)^2/(T^2 R_0^2),     (3)\n    V/R_0^2=1-[(T-S+s)/T]^2 R_(N-1)^2/R_0^2.               (4)\n\nTo prove (4) directly, use F_t=<x_t,g_t>-||g_t||^2/2 in\n2h_t F_t+h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2=||x_t||^2-||x_(t+1)||^2,\nthen telescope the definition of V. Both the cost and defect are retained.\n\n## 2. The four-phase diamond\n\nTake four equal ordinary masses S=M-1 with checkpoint excesses\nMq, M(b), Mq, where\n\n    2/5<q<7/16,  12/5<c<39/16,\n    b=cq,  A=1+q,  B=1+b,\n    T_0=T_2=MA, T_1=MB, T_3=Mt,  t>=1.\n\nThese include the ACTUAL four copies of H_(m-2) inside H_m for every\nm>=128, with c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c and\nq=a c^(m-2)/(1+sum H_(m-2)). Indeed\nM_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j, 0<a<c-2, so a<=q<=c-2.\nThe elementary bounds\nlambda<169/70, c<39/16 and\na>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5 give the displayed ranges.\n\nNormalize R_0=1. Use exactly the four edges\n\n    a_01=x, a_02=y, a_13=v, a_23=w,\n\nwhere x,y>0, v,w>=0 and R=v+w>0. All other transfers vanish. The gradients are\n\n    p_0=(e_0-x e_1-y e_2)/(MA),\n    p_1=(x e_1-v e_3)/(MB),\n    p_2=(y e_2-w e_3)/(MA),\n    p_3=R e_3/(Mt).\n\nWhen vw>0, the two middle gradients have the strictly positive correlation\n\n    <p_1,p_2>=vw/(M^2 AB).\n\nThe necessary endpoint normals from (1)-(2) give\n\n    q >= (1+A/B)x^2+y^2,              q >=x^2+2y^2,          (5)\n    b x^2 >=v^2+(B^2/A)y^2-(B/A)vw,                         (6)\n    q y^2 >= A wv+w^2+(A^2/t)R(v+w/A).                     (7)\n\nThese are exact for every finite M, not large-M limits: at the final\nordinary query u=S=M-1, one has u+1=M.\n\nThe remaining necessary normals are NOT being discarded as sufficient.\nThey will all be checked on the replacement construction below.\n\n## 3. Removing the merge preserves every payoff and defect\n\nFor fixed R,t write z=v/R in [0,1]. Inequalities (6)-(7) imply\n\n    y^2/R^2 >= Y(z)=(1+qz)(1-z+A/t)/q,\n    x^2/R^2 >= X(z)=[z^2-(B/A)z(1-z)+(B^2/A)Y(z)]/b.       (8)\n\nThe function Y is concave and\n\n    Y(0)-Y(1)=1/q-A/t >=1/q-A>0.\n\nTherefore Y(z)>=Y(1)=A^2/(qt) throughout [0,1].\n\nNext,\n\n    b X'(z)=2[1+B/A-B^2/A]z\n                  +(B/A)[-1+B(1-1/q+A/t)].                 (9)\n\nBoth coefficients on the right are strictly negative in the stated ranges.\nFor the coefficient of z, B>49/25 and b>24/25 imply\nB b>1176/625>23/16>A. For the constant, t>=1 gives\n\n    1-1/q+A/t <1-16/7+23/16=17/112,\n    B(17/112)<(529/256)(17/112)=8993/28672<1.\n\nThus X decreases and X(z)>=X(1), where\n\n    Y_1=A^2/(qt),\n    X_1=[1+B^2 A/(qt)]/b.                                   (10)\n\nGiven ANY fully legal diamond, replace it by\n\n    v'=R, w'=0, x'=R sqrt(X_1), y'=R sqrt(Y_1).\n\nEquation (8) gives x'<=x and y'<=y. The two root conditions (5) therefore\nremain true. The replacement is a unique-parent tree:\n0 has children1 and2, vertex1 has child3, and vertex2 is a leaf.\n\nHere is the full legality check, independently of any unverified tree lemma.\nAt the root, the only positive future comparisons are with p_1 and p_2;\n(5) is precisely their endpoint condition. The comparison with p_3 is zero.\nDuring phase1 the endpoint comparison with p_2 is equality because\n\n    b x'^2-R^2=(B^2/A)y'^2.\n\nIts comparison with p_3 is also valid, since\n\n    b x'^2-R^2=(B^2 A/(qt))R^2 >= (B/t)R^2,\n\nusing BA>q. During phase2 the endpoint comparison with p_3 is equality:\n\n    q y'^2/A^2=R^2/t.\n\nIts other future comparisons are absent. Every comparison with a past\nvertex is nonpositive, and every own endpoint value is nonnegative.\nIn this replacement, distinct gradient inner products are nonpositive.\nHence the difference between an own normal and any future normal decreases\nwith u; checking the last query checks every query in each phase.\nThe terminal phase has positive own normal, nonpositive comparisons with\nall past vertices, and t>=1. Thus ALL comparisons against every vertex\nand zero hold on the SAME whole trajectory.\n\nThe replacement has identical R_0=1, R_3=R and t. Formulas (3)-(4) show\nthat it has exactly the same normalized terminal objective AND primitive\ndefect, with the same actual closest-minimizer distance. The diamond's\npositive middle-gradient correlation is therefore unnecessary for either\nperformance criterion in this regime.\n\n## 4. Standalone quantitative bound after deletion\n\nFor the replacement write E_i=R_i^2, H_i=E_i/T_i.\nIts low transitions i=0 and i=2 satisfy H_(i+1)/H_i<=q/(1+q):\na next waiting coordinate obeys that bound directly from its normal;\na next child obeys q T_i/(T_i+T_(i+1)), which is no larger, since\nq+q^2<1<=T_3/M and q^2<1 for the other low transition.\nThe high transition i=1 to the waiting vertex2 satisfies H_2/H_1<=b/B.\nIn particular it obeys the larger bound bB/(A+B), because B b>A.\n\nThus, retaining an upper bound uniform over all four-node trees,\n\n    score <2/[MA] * [q/(1+q)]^2 * bB/(A+B).\n\nThe actual full word mass satisfies M_m=M[(2+q)^2+(c-2)q]\n=M[4+q(c+2)]<=M c^2, since q<=c-2. With K=1/(2M_m-1),\n\n    score/K <\n      4(qc)^3(1+qc)/[(1+q)^3(2+q+qc)]\n      <=10763254593/11175730176 <1.                         (11)\n\nFor the first inequality, multiplying the previous score bound by\n2M_m-1<2M c^2 gives exactly its displayed expression.\nThe last rational bound follows because the expression increases in both\nq and c: its logarithmic q derivative is\n3/[q(1+q)]+(c-1)/[(1+cq)(2+(1+c)q)]>0, and its logarithmic c\nderivative is 3/c+q/(1+cq)-q/(2+(1+c)q)>0. Substitute\nq=7/16,c=39/16 to obtain the rational number.\n\nConsequently EVERY legal nonnegative four-phase diamond on this actual\nnear-critical blocking is strictly below K. The merge deletion is exact,\nnot an exponent-only or asymptotic-loss argument. This excludes only the\nspecified four-edge graph. More complicated shared descendants, inherited\nsigned coordinates, and repeated visits to old vertices remain open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["a0bb50ebcb4cb599", "f8b4d9f258ab98c0", "bf591e52671f6d33"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "research/c36_forward_flow_and_diamond_merge.md"}, "glossary": {"a_flow_ij": "Nonnegative coordinate mass transferred from earlier phase i to later coordinate j at the checkpoint.", "R_flow_i": "Total mass loaded into coordinate i before its phase.", "r_flow_j_i": "Mass already preloaded in future coordinate j before phase i.", "delta_flow_i": "Own projection normal value, defined explicitly in equation(1).", "X_diamond": "Required squared first-branch mass relative to terminal mass, equation(8).", "Y_diamond": "Required squared second-branch mass relative to terminal mass, equation(8)."}}
{"id": "2d75b4696641736c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:02:59.206999+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete VARIABLE-FULL-MATRIX re-selection candidate for an unbounded-length class: any signed intrinsic certificate with arbitrary negative incoming-terminal coefficients becomes an ordinary certificate whenever the schedule has a finite chain of saturated right-tail cuts ending in <=2 positive steps. The earlier prefix blocks are arbitrary; their ordinary certificates are forced by the input. At each cut the full matrix splits and theta transports as theta_b=1-(1-theta)(D/T)^2; replacing the leaf matrix and rebuilding gives every actual nonnegative coefficient. Includes original zero-step lifting and a fully explicit four-step E_(1/2) example h=(1,2,1,1). This is not general re-selection or a capacity/profile gain.", "evidence": "# Variable-matrix terminal-credit repair along saturated right tails\n\nStatus: complete self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical\nsolver and no fact_submit call. This is a certificate theorem in a specified\nunbounded-length class, not the unrestricted primitive-cone theorem and not an\nobjective-profile bound. The only accepted background used is the actual\ninterpolation inequality from fact 1252b8823bb5a290. All additional algebra and\nthe short-leaf repair are proved here rather than imported from findings.\n\n## 1. The exact certificate class and result\n\nFor a positive list h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), write s=sum h_i, C=1+s,\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                                   -sC||g_n||^2,\n J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n),   E_theta(h)=V_h-theta J_h.\n\nAll n+1 formal gradients are independent. A signed terminal certificate is\nan array lambda_ij on distinct finite pairs such that:\n\n (a) V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij cancels EVERY formal f_i coefficient;\n (b) the resulting FULL quadratic form M_h is positive semidefinite;\n (c) lambda_ij>=0 whenever j!=n, while lambda_(i,n) may be any real number.\n\nAn ordinary certificate additionally has lambda_(i,n)>=0. A nonnegative\ncertificate for E_theta is exactly a signed certificate with\nlambda_(i,n)>=-theta h_i: to pass from the signed to the actual E_theta\narray, add theta h_i on i->n. No interpolation inequality is replaced.\n\nDefine a class R of positive schedules recursively. Every list of length at\nmost two belongs to R. A longer list belongs to R if it has a decomposition\n\n h=(a,1+r,b),   A=1+sum a,   D=1+sum b,\n T=1+sum h=A+D+r,   r>0,   rT=AD,                 (1)\n\nwhere a is ANY positive list (possibly empty) and b belongs to R. No\nprimitivity assumption is imposed on a. Fix one such finite decomposition\nchain when a schedule has several choices. The word \"right\" only indicates\nthat the recursion continues inside b. The earlier lists a can be arbitrarily\nlong and need not themselves have a decomposition.\n\nCandidate theorem. Every signed terminal certificate for any h in R can be\nconverted explicitly into an ordinary certificate for that SAME h. The\nconstruction is allowed to change the full PSD remainder. More precisely,\nat each cut (1), a supplied certificate forces an ordinary certificate for a\nand a signed certificate for b. If the input has the E_theta lower bounds,\n0<=theta<1, then the inherited tail bounds are exactly those of E_theta_b,\nwhere\n\n kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,\n theta_b=1-(1-theta)/kappa.                        (2)\n\nThe construction uses every actual multiplier, value flow, and full matrix.\nIt remains valid for nonnegative schedules whose positive compression is in\nR, with a final certificate on all original independent gradients.\n\nThe assumptions include existence of the stated finite certificate. No dual\nattainment theorem identifying arbitrary universal validity with this\ncertificate class is asserted.\n\n## 2. A saturated cut has two explicit equality trajectories\n\nFix (1). Let L be the bridge query, so a occupies steps 0,...,L-1.\nCall queries 0,...,L the prefix group P. The tail group B comprises the query\nimmediately after the bridge, all subsequent tail queries, and final query z.\nUse local tail indices j=0,...,m, with z=m, and always embed them into the\noriginal list when they occur in Q. Put p=g_L, q=g_z,\nH_b=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j, and eta=r/D=A/T.\n\nFirst use a scalar unit-threshold Huber function, starting sufficiently far\nout on its positive affine ray that every query has gradient 1. It is convex,\ndifferentiable, and 1-smooth. Every finite Q is zero and\n\n V_h=2sum_i h_i sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t\n          +sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0.\n\nHere 2sum_i h_i sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t=s^2+sum_i h_i^2.\n\nThe second scalar function is obtained by integrating\n\n f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),   f(0)=0,\n\nand starts at x_0=A. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz, so it is a legal convex 1-smooth function with minimizer 0.\nIf R_i^a is the remaining prefix step sum at query i in P, its prefix data are\n\n x_i=1+R_i^a,   g_i=1,   f_i=R_i^a+1/2.\n\nAt L, R_L^a=0. The bridge lands at -r. At a tail query with remaining tail\nsum R_j^b, the data are\n\n x_j=-eta(1+R_j^b),   g_j=-eta,\n f_j=eta^2(R_j^b+1/2).\n\nIn particular x_z=g_z=-eta. All same-group Q are zero. Expanding the displayed\ndata gives every cross residual:\n\n Q_(i,j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j^b,   i in P, j in B,\n Q_(j,i)=-(1+eta)R_i^a,       i in P, j in B.        (3)\n\nFor example the first expression is\nR_i^a+1/2-eta^2(R_j^b+1/2)-eta(1+R_j^b)\n -(1+R_i^a)+(1+eta)^2/2. The second expression follows by swapping the\nroles in the definition of Q and equals -(1+eta)R_i^a.\n\nEvery incoming-terminal residual is zero: j=z has R_z^b=0 and same-group\ntail residuals are zero. At every query one also has\nf_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Telescoping squared positions therefore gives\n\n 2sum_(i<n)h_i f_i+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n     =||x_0||^2-||x_z||^2,\n\nand consequently V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0 by eta=A/T.\n\n## 3. Complementarity forces the full mixed block, even with signed credit\n\nLet lambda be any supplied signed certificate from Section 1. On either\nequality trajectory, V_h=0 and every term having a potentially negative\ncoefficient has Q_(i,z)=0. All other coefficients are nonnegative and their\nactual Q are nonpositive, by (3) or fact 1252b8823bb5a290. Hence\n\n 0<=M_h=V_h+sum lambda Q<=0.\n\nEvery term with a strictly negative Q must therefore have coefficient zero.\nBecause the steps inside each list are positive, (3) leaves only these cross\nedges:\n\n nu_i=lambda_(i,z), i in P;     mu_j=lambda_(j,L), j in B.     (4)\n\nThe nu_i are initially allowed either sign; all mu_j are nonnegative.\nThe two equality gradient vectors are e_P+e_B and e_P-eta e_B. A PSD matrix\nannihilates a vector on which its quadratic form is zero (diagonalize the\nreal symmetric matrix, whose eigenvalues are nonnegative). Since eta>0,\nboth block indicators e_P and e_B lie in the kernel of the FULL M_h.\n\nThe only mixed prefix-tail terms remaining after (4) are\n\n -sum_(i in P)nu_i <g_i,H_b+q>\n       +r sum_(j in B)mu_j <p,g_j>.                (5)\n\nTo check (5), the prefix-to-terminal position term has tail contribution\n-<g_i,H_b>, and its squared difference contributes -<g_i,q>.\nFor j->L, the position contribution involving p is (1+r)<g_j,p>, while the\nsquared difference contributes -<g_j,p>, leaving r<g_j,p>.\nAll within-group Q and V_h have no mixed term.\n\nFor i<L, row i of M_h e_B is -D nu_i/2. Thus\nnu_i=0 for every i<L. Rows j in B of M_h e_P then give\n\n mu_j=(nu_L/r)b_j for j<m,    mu_z=nu_L/r.          (6)\n\nValue cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q means that outgoing minus incoming\nflow at every nonterminal index i is -2h_i. Summing through P yields\nsum_j mu_j-nu_L=2(A+r). Using (6) gives\n\n nu_L=2r(A+r)/(D-r)=r(kappa-1),\n mu_j=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),     mu_z=kappa-1.         (7)\n\nHere D>r, kappa>1, and\n(kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r), obtained by substituting rT=AD and T=A+D+r.\nAll mixed coefficients in (5) now vanish individually. Thus the full\nprefix-tail matrix is zero, not just its value on the equality trajectories.\n\nThis proof permits arbitrary negative incoming-terminal coefficients. Their\nsigns never enter the complementarity step because the corresponding Q all\nvanish on both equality runs.\n\n## 4. Full energy identity and the exact inherited credit\n\nLet\n\n J_b=sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,z),\n C_cross=sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).\n\nThe identity needed for restriction and rebuilding is\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)(C_cross+J_b).          (8)\n\nHere V_a has terminal query L, and V_b uses the original tail queries.\nFor a full coefficient check put C_aux=C_cross+J_b. Its value and gradient\nparts respectively are\n\n C_aux,f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z,\n C_aux,g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n          +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.    (9)\n\nThe identity\n2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>\n =||H_b||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2\ngives (9) by expanding the four kinds of Q in C_aux; the mixed products\nin p,H_b,q cancel. Inserting (9) into (8) matches the prefix coefficients\nunchanged. It matches the bridge value and squared-gradient coefficients\n2(1+r) and -r(1+r), and terminal coefficients -2(T-1) and -T(T-1).\nThese matches use\n\n (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2.\n\nLet lambda^a, lambda^b be the supplied within-group arrays. Define\n\n lambda_hat^b_ij=lambda^b_ij/kappa\n                   -(1-1/kappa)b_i 1_(i<m,j=z).    (10)\n\nEquations (7)-(8) give the exact formal identity\n\n M_h=M_a+kappa M_b,\n M_a=V_a+sum lambda^a_ij Q_ij,\n M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat^b_ij Q_ij.                 (11)\n\nThe prefix and tail values are disjoint, so cancellation of all original\nvalues implies separate cancellation in (11). Their formal gradients are\ndisjoint, and the mixed block is zero, so PSD of the full M_h implies PSD of\nBOTH full blocks. The prefix coefficients are nonnegative because their\ntargets differ from z. The tail coefficients are nonnegative except possibly\nthose into z. Thus (11) is an ordinary certificate for a and a signed one\nfor b, as claimed.\n\nIf lambda_(i,z)>=-theta h_i, then (10) gives\n\n lambda_hat^b_(i,z)>=-[1-(1-theta)/kappa]b_i.\n\nEvery other tail sign remains nonnegative. This proves (2), with\n0<=theta_b<1 when 0<=theta<1. No lower bound on the signed terminal entries\nis needed for the unrestricted signed-certificate statement.\n\nFor reference, in the actual nonnegative E_theta multiplier array Lambda,\nthe forced cross entries are exactly\n\n Lambda_(i,z)=theta a_i                         (i<L),\n Lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1)+theta(1+r),\n Lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j                      (j<m),\n Lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,\n\nwith all other cross entries zero. This lists the ACTUAL interpolation\ncoefficients rather than a formal enlargement of their allowed signs.\n\n## 5. Repair of the terminal leaf, with all value-flow equations retained\n\nAn empty schedule has V=0 and needs no multiplier. For one positive step u,\nlet c=lambda_01. Value cancellation gives lambda_10=c+2u. The constant\ngradient equality puts (1,1) in the PSD kernel. Its second row equals\nu(c-2)/2, so c=2. The full form is\n\n (2-u^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nThus PSD forces u<=sqrt(2), and both coefficients are already nonnegative.\n\nFor two positive steps (u,v), put s=u+v and\nc=lambda_02, d=lambda_20, a=lambda_01, b=lambda_12.\nHere c,b can be signed, whereas a,d,lambda_10,lambda_21 are nonnegative.\nValue cancellation and the constant-gradient PSD kernel give respectively\n\n lambda_10=a+c-d+2u,       lambda_21=b+c-d+2s,\n u a+s c=2u,              v(b+c)+u d=2s(u+1).\n\nFor example these kernel equations follow by expanding the first and last\nrows in the following six residuals, where F_ij=f_i-f_j:\n\n Q_01=F_01+(1/2-u)||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2/2-<g_0,g_1>,\n Q_10=F_10+(||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2)/2+(u-1)<g_0,g_1>,\n Q_12=F_12+(1/2-v)||g_1||^2+||g_2||^2/2-<g_1,g_2>,\n Q_21=F_21+(||g_1||^2+||g_2||^2)/2+(v-1)<g_1,g_2>,\n Q_02=F_02+(1/2-u)||g_0||^2+||g_2||^2/2\n                                     -v<g_0,g_1>-<g_0,g_2>,\n Q_20=F_20+(||g_0||^2+||g_2||^2)/2\n                                   +(u-1)<g_0,g_2>+v<g_1,g_2>.\n\nSolving the four scalar equations gives\n\n a=2-(s/u)c,\n b=2s(u+1)/v-c-(u/v)d,\n lambda_10=2(u+1)-(v/u)c-d,\n b=2(u+1)+(u/v)lambda_10>0.                       (12)\n\nWrite U=g_0-g_1, Z=g_1-g_2. Since the constant gradient is in the kernel,\nthe entire full form is obtained by setting g_2=0 and expanding the six\ndisplayed residuals. The result is\n\n [2-u^2+v(u-1)c/u]||U||^2\n  +[c-(u-1)d]<U,Z>+[C_0-(u/v)d]||Z||^2,\n C_0=s[2(u+1)/v-s].                               (13)\n\nIf c>=0, all supplied coefficients are nonnegative, by (12), so retain them\nand their full PSD matrix. If c<0, PSD in (13) forces C_0>=0 because d>=0.\nIt also forces u<=sqrt(2): this holds directly when u<1, and when u>=1 the\nc contribution to the first diagonal is nonpositive, so 2-u^2>=0.\nReplace the supplied array by\n\n lambda'_01=2, lambda'_10=2+2u,\n lambda'_12=2s(u+1)/v, lambda'_21=2s(u+1)/v+2s,\n lambda'_02=lambda'_20=0.                         (14)\n\nEvery coefficient is nonnegative, all value-flow equations remain valid,\nand the full remainder, by (13) with c=d=0, is\n\n (2-u^2)||U||^2+C_0||Z||^2>=0.                    (15)\n\nThis deliberately changes the whole leaf matrix; no fixed-matrix repair is\nclaimed.\n\n## 6. Explicit bottom-up reconstruction on arbitrarily long schedules\n\nRepeatedly apply Sections 2-4 to the fixed decomposition chain. This extracts\nordinary certificates (lambda^a,M_a) for every earlier prefix block, and one\nsigned certificate for the final leaf. Apply Section 5 to that leaf.\n\nAt a cut, suppose sigma^b is the newly obtained ordinary tail array and\nN_b its full PSD matrix. Define the rebuilt parent array by:\n\n within P: use lambda^a;\n within B: use kappa sigma^b_ij+(kappa-1)b_i 1_(i<m,j=z);\n cross: use (kappa-1)b_j on j->L for j<m,\n        kappa-1 on z->L, and r(kappa-1) on L->z;\n every other cross entry: zero.                   (16)\n\nAll actual coefficients in (16) are nonnegative. Equation (8) gives, with\nevery value term retained,\n\n V_h+sum lambda_new,ij Q_ij=M_a+kappa N_b>=0.       (17)\n\nThe right side is a block-diagonal full PSD matrix on all original gradient\ncoordinates at this level. Repeat (16) until the root is rebuilt. The\nrecursion is finite because each tail is strictly shorter. This proves the\ncandidate theorem without a solver, a removed interpolation edge, or an\nassumed tail coefficient floor.\n\nAlong multiple cuts the retained credit also has the exact telescoping law\n\n 1-theta_leaf=(1-theta_root)(C_leaf/C_root)^2,       (18)\n\nsince each factor 1/kappa is the squared capacity ratio. This states credit\ntransport only; validity and repair come from the full coefficient proof.\n\n## 7. Original-index zero steps\n\nFor a nonnegative schedule, partition query indices into classes at identical\npositions caused by consecutive zero steps. Restrict the supplied full\ncertificate to the subspace where each such class has equal f and g. This is\na linear congruence of its PSD matrix. Between members of one class Q=0.\nFor different classes, aggregate all coefficients between the two classes.\nEvery nonterminal-target coefficient remains nonnegative. Only the terminal\nclass can have signed incoming coefficients. For an E_theta certificate its\naggregated lower bound is -theta times the sum of h_i in the source class,\nwhich is the corresponding positive compressed step.\n\nThe restricted energy is exactly the positive-compressed V. Apply the theorem\nto this compressed signed certificate. To lift the ordinary output, choose\nthe original positive-step query as representative of each nonterminal\nclass, and original n as terminal representative. Put each compressed\nmultiplier on the corresponding original pair, and all others zero. The\nselected positions agree as formal identities because omitted steps have\nzero coefficient. Thus the output energy and interpolation sum give the\ncompressed PSD quadratic on the selected independent gradients; every unused\noriginal gradient has a zero row and column. No equality between distinct\nformal gradients is imposed in the final certificate. The empty compression\nhas V=0 and the all-zero certificate.\n\n## 8. An explicit unbounded family where this move changes the matrix\n\nFor any integer k>=0 take a to be k unit steps and b=(1,1). Put A=k+1,\nD=3, and choose the positive root r of r(A+3+r)=3A. Let T=A+3+r and\nkappa=(T/3)^2. This gives an (k+3)-positive-step schedule in R.\nThe prefix has the explicit ordinary adjacent certificate\n\n lambda^a_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,\n lambda^a_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2), 0<=i<k,\n M_a=sum_(i<k)(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.\n\nSubstitution of the unit-step residuals verifies cancellation and this\nidentity; at query i the net flow is -2, and at query k it is 2k.\n\nSet c=-1+1/(2kappa), so -1<c<0. On the two-unit-step tail use\n\n lambda^b_01=2-2c, lambda^b_10=4-c,\n lambda^b_12=8-c,  lambda^b_21=12,\n lambda^b_02=c,   lambda^b_20=0.\n\nIts full remainder is ||U||^2+c<U,Z>+4||Z||^2, equal to\n||U+(c/2)Z||^2+(4-c^2/4)||Z||^2, so it is PSD. Build a signed parent by\nthe formulas (16), using this signed tail instead of an ordinary tail.\nThe parent's only negative coefficient is\n\n lambda_(tail 0,z)=kappa c+(kappa-1)=-1/2.\n\nAll other nonterminal-target coefficients are nonnegative, and all other\nterminal coefficients are nonnegative. It is therefore an E_(1/2)\ncertificate: adding h_i/2 to every incoming-terminal coefficient leaves\nevery actual multiplier nonnegative (the displayed negative entry becomes\nexactly zero). Its full matrix is\n\n M_a+kappa[||U||^2+c<U,Z>+4||Z||^2].\n\nThe repair (14)-(16) yields the ordinary full matrix\n\n M_a+kappa[||U||^2+4||Z||^2].\n\nThe difference is -kappa c<U,Z>, an indefinite quadratic when c!=0.\nThus this is a genuine change of the PSD matrix, not the addition of an\nautomatically PSD term or a claim that all old coefficients already met the\nordinary floor. No claim that every possible fixed-matrix repair fails on\nthis example is made. No capacity gain or asymptotic improvement is claimed.\n\nA rational calibration is k=1, giving h=(1,2,1,1), r=1, T=6, kappa=4,\nc=-7/8. The ACTUAL nonnegative E_(1/2) coefficients are\n\n Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=4,\n Lambda_21=Lambda_31=Lambda_41=3, Lambda_14=4,\n Lambda_23=15, Lambda_32=39/2,\n Lambda_34=39, Lambda_43=48, Lambda_04=1/2,\n\nwith every unlisted entry zero. Its full remainder is\n\n ||g_0-g_1||^2+4||g_2-g_3||^2\n -(7/2)<g_2-g_3,g_3-g_4>+16||g_3-g_4||^2.\n\nThe ordinary repaired coefficients are\n\n lambda'_01=2, lambda'_10=4,\n lambda'_21=lambda'_31=lambda'_41=lambda'_14=3,\n lambda'_23=8, lambda'_32=16,\n lambda'_34=35, lambda'_43=48, lambda'_24=3,\n\nwith every unlisted entry zero. Its full remainder is\n||g_0-g_1||^2+4||g_2-g_3||^2+16||g_3-g_4||^2.\nExpanding the ten possible scalar Gram monomials and five independent\nfunction values verifies both identities exactly. A trivial rational\ncoefficient check reproduced these expansions; it is not the proof, an SDP\nsolve, or a verification-service verdict.\n\n## 9. Exact scope and the unresolved interface\n\nThe local rigidity requires rT=AD. Without this equality, the second Huber\ntrajectory has energy A^2-T^2r^2/D^2 instead of zero, and it supplies neither\nthe two kernel vectors nor (7). The theorem therefore cannot be used to\nrepair a schedule with no such saturated decomposition.\n\nEven for a saturated decomposition ending in a longer irreducible tail, the\nrestriction (10) only yields another signed certificate, with precisely the\nincreased credit (2). It is not an ordinary certificate until that remaining\ntail is separately repaired. In particular, this proof does not settle a\ngeneral four-step leaf, general terminal-saturation of primal witnesses, or\nthe unrestricted exact B_N objective comparison. Its useful output is a\nconstructive, full-matrix repair on the specified class, and a narrower\nremaining location for any strict cone separation.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_recursive_saturated_credit_repair", "plan_id": "bc4ba33c9bec7227", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "local_memory/recursive_saturated_credit_repair.md", "status": "complete_self_contained_candidate_unverified", "numerical_solver_calls": 0, "fact_submit_calls": 0, "independent_checks": "Tiny exact coefficient expansion for both four-step full identities; no service verification."}, "glossary": {"R": "Local class of positive schedules with a recursively saturated chain of right tails ending in a list of at most two entries; not the universal real-number symbol R in mathematical typesetting.", "V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy 2sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2.", "J_h": "Actual terminal-interpolation sum sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n).", "E_theta": "V_h-theta J_h, with actual coefficient signs retained.", "A,D,T": "Capacities 1+sum a, 1+sum b, 1+sum h at the specified saturated cut.", "r": "Positive bridge excess satisfying rT=AD and bridge step1+r.", "kappa": "Squared cut capacity ratio (T/D)^2=(A/r)^2.", "P,B": "Disjoint formal-gradient query groups: prefix through bridge query, and tail after bridge through final query.", "lambda_hat^b": "Tail signed array obtained by dividing within-tail coefficients by kappa and subtracting (1-1/kappa)b_i on incoming-terminal entries.", "M_a,M_b": "Full PSD quadratic remainders of the extracted prefix and signed tail certificates.", "C_cross": "Weighted sum sum b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z) of actual cross inequalities."}}
{"id": "2727558341bbeb56", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:06:06.689987+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New CHRONOLOGICAL whole-sector obstruction on the assigned near-critical ruler: EVERY legal single elliptic common-metric prefix with0<Re(z)/|z|<=7/40, at ANY spectral magnitude and arbitrary conditioning, satisfies S_pre<(143/100)(2Re(z)/|z|²-1/2) and |z|S_pre<1001/2000. Every free completion and b in[0,2] obeys2F<=[1+|z|²(S_pre²+sum h_pre²)/2]²/(2S_pre+1); hence limsup benchmark ratio<=578/(225e)<0.96. All actual chronological queries and arbitrary legal values are retained. Moderate-angle frequencies remain open.", "evidence": "# A chronological obstruction for every wide-angle elliptic frequency\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No new fact_submit, solver evaluation,\nnumerical sweep, or external theorem is used. The proof below is self-contained\napart from the interpolation convention and benchmark recursions in accepted\nfacts1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d. The earlier small-frequency and\nunit-neighborhood candidates are NOT premises.\n\n## 1. Statement on the assigned family\n\nFor each m>=128 define, with parameters fixed over that whole horizon,\n\n    s=1+sqrt(2), c=s(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1.\n\nRetain the n-1 steps of H_m after deleting its last d_0, and all n queried\njets at indices0,...,n-1. Write S for this retained stepsize sum and S2 for\nits squared-step sum. Consider any complex z=alpha+i beta and any invertible\nreal2x2 map T, with ||T e_1||=1, giving\n\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),\n    x_i=T(Re P_i,Im P_i),\n    g_i=T(Re[zP_i],Im[zP_i]).\n\nAssume these SAME real data, some values f_i, and the optimum jet(0,0,0)\nare fully interpolable by a convex differentiable1-smooth function. There\nis no bound on T or its conditioning, and all parameters may depend on m.\nThe unqueried final point is x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1), with a completely free\ninterpolating function and endpoint gradient.\n\nIf z!=0, put r=|z| and omega=alpha/r. This candidate excludes the ENTIRE\nangular sector\n\n    0<omega<=7/40,                                     (1)\n\nat every spectral magnitude. Indeed full legality forces\n\n    S < (143/100)[2alpha/|z|^2-1/2],\n    r S <1001/2000.                                   (2)\n\nMoreover EVERY convex1-smooth interpolant F of the queried jets satisfies,\nfor EVERY b in[0,2], the explicit finite-horizon estimate\n\n    2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))\n       <= [1+|z|^2(S^2+S2)/2]^2/(2S+1).               (3)\n\nIn particular, uniformly over this entire sector, all metrics, legal values\nand free completions,\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))\n          <=578/(225e)<24/25<1.                       (4)\n\nThe original last step d_0 belongs to[0,2]. Nonpositive alpha is impossible\nfor a nonzero z on a legal prefix of this form; z=0 gives objective zero.\nNo conclusion is claimed here for omega>7/40, several distinct modes,\nchanging generators, or unrestricted W.\n\n## 2. A physical initial budget, with no matrix condition number\n\nLet A=T [[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]] T^(-1), so g_i=A x_i and\n\n    A^2-2alpha A+sigma I=0, sigma=|z|^2>0.\n\nSet t_0=<x_0,g_0> and R_0=||g_0||. Invertibility gives R_0>0.\nThe two optimum interpolation inequalities give\n\n    t_0>=R_0^2,\n    0<=f_0<=t_0-R_0^2/2.                              (5)\n\nThe first step h_0=d_0 is positive. Adding the two interpolation inequalities\nbetween queries0 and1 and dividing by h_0^2 gives\n\n    <A g_0,g_0>>=||A g_0||^2>=0.\n\nThe quadratic identity for A therefore implies\n\n    2alpha R_0^2-sigma t_0>=0.\n\nConsequently alpha>0 and, on putting\n\n    hcrit=2alpha/sigma,\n\none has hcrit>=1, t_0<=hcrit R_0^2, and the stronger initial-value budget\n\n    f_0<=(hcrit-1/2)R_0^2.                            (6)\n\nThis budget is what the chronological prefix will violate. It is derived\nfrom the actual Euclidean vectors and holds for every similarity metric.\n\n## 3. Exact two-vector coefficients on a positive prefix\n\nFor an arbitrary beginning of the retained schedule let s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t.\nThere are real coefficients C_i,V_i such that\n\n    x_i=(C_i+2alpha V_i)x_0-V_i g_0,\n    g_i=C_i g_0+sigma V_i x_0,\n    C_0=1, V_0=0,\n    V_(i+1)=V_i+h_i C_i,\n    C_(i+1)=C_i-2alpha h_i C_i-sigma h_i V_i.           (7)\n\nSubstitution verifies both recurrences using A^2=2alpha A-sigma I.\nIf a prefix has total mass T and\n\n    2alpha T+sigma T^2/2<1,                            (8)\n\nthen induction in (7) gives, at every one of its queries,\n\n    0<C_i<=1, 0<=V_i<=s_i,\n    C_i>=1-2alpha s_i-sigma s_i^2/2.                   (9)\n\nHere is the induction detail. While the preceding C's are positive, V is\nnondecreasing with increments at most h_i, and C is nonincreasing. Summing\nthe C increments, using C_t<=1 and V_t<=s_t, gives\n\n    C_i>=1-2alpha s_i-sigma sum_(t<i)h_t s_t\n        >=1-2alpha s_i-sigma s_i^2/2>0.\n\nThus positivity is maintained and the argument reaches the last query.\n\nBecause t_0>=0, the representation in (7) implies\n\n    <g_(i+1),g_i>>=C_(i+1)C_i R_0^2.\n\nThe ordered interpolation inequality gives\n\n    f_i-f_(i+1)\n      >=h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>+||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2.\n\nLet e_i=2alpha s_i+sigma s_i^2/2. Under (8), (9) and\n(1-e_i)(1-e_(i+1))>=1-e_i-e_(i+1) yield\n\n    f_0-f_end\n      >=[T-2alpha T^2-sigma T^3/2]R_0^2.              (10)\n\nIn detail, the telescoping identities needed for this sum are\n\n    sum_i h_i(s_i+s_(i+1))=T^2,\n    sum_i h_i(s_i^2+s_(i+1)^2)\n         =(2/3)T^3+(1/3)sum_i h_i^3<=T^3.\n\nThus (10) uses the WHOLE chronological value drop, not just a local\ncocoercivity condition or a radial polynomial bound.\n\n## 4. The first actual crossing contradicts the budget\n\nWe need an actual queried prefix with controlled mass, not an artificial\nsubdivision of a large step. On the assigned ruler,\n\n    2<c<49/20, 0<a<c-2.                               (11)\n\nFor the first bound, s<17/7 and m>=128 give\nc<2193/896<49/20. For the second, s(s-2)=1,\nc(c-2)>1, and ac=1-1/m<1.\n\nWrite S_j=sum H_j. Direct summation gives\n\n    1+S_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).\n\nEvery occurrence of a level-j step d_j has at least the mass S_j before it:\nits first occurrence follows H_j and all later occurrences have more\npreceding nonnegative steps. Hence for EVERY actual retained step, with\npreceding mass u,\n\n    h_t=1+a c^j<=(c-2)u+c-1.                          (12)\n\nPut k=143/100 and assume, for contradiction, that\n\n    S>=k(hcrit-1/2).\n\nChoose the FIRST actual queried index whose accumulated mass T reaches\nthis threshold. Such an index exists in the retained prefix. By (12), its\nmass obeys\n\n    k(hcrit-1/2)<=T<(c-1)[k(hcrit-1/2)+1].\n\nLet tau=1/hcrit in(0,1] and v=T/hcrit. Then\n\n    L(tau)<=v<U(tau),\n    L(tau)=k(1-tau/2),\n    U(tau)=(29/20)[k+(1-k/2)tau].                      (13)\n\nIn particular U(tau)<=U(1)=9947/4000<5/2. Since\nomega^2<=(7/40)^2=49/1600<1/32,\n\n    2alpha T+sigma T^2/2\n        =omega^2(4v+2v^2)<(1/32)(10+25/2)=45/64<1.\n\nThe coefficient and value-drop proof above applies to this actual prefix.\nIts endpoint value is nonnegative. Dividing (10) by hcrit R_0^2 and using\n(6) therefore imposes\n\n    1-tau/2 >= v-omega^2(4v^2+2v^3)\n              >=E(v),\n    E(v)=v-(49/1600)(4v^2+2v^3).                     (14)\n\nWe show that (13) gives the strictly opposite inequality. E is concave\nfor v>=0. Both E(L(tau))-1+tau/2 and E(U(tau))-1+tau/2 are concave\nfunctions of tau. It suffices to check their endpoints tau=0,1.\n\nAt tau=0, exact arithmetic gives\n\n    E(143/100)-1=313657/800000000>0,\n    E(4147/2000)-1=5062894373/6400000000000>0.          (15)\n\nAt tau=1, L(1)=143/200<3/4 and U(1)=9947/4000 lies in(12/5,5/2).\nUsing49/1600<1/32 gives the explicit lower estimates\n\n    E(L(1)) >143/200-99/1024>1/2,\n    E(U(1)) >12/5-225/128>1/2.                        (16)\n\nConcavity therefore makes E exceed1-tau/2 at BOTH endpoints in (13),\nand then at every v in that interval. This contradicts (14). We conclude\n\n    S<k(hcrit-1/2).\n\nMultiplying by r and using r hcrit=2omega gives\n\n    rS<2k omega(1-1/(2hcrit))<=2*(143/100)*(7/40)\n        =1001/2000,                                  (17)\n\nwhich proves both assertions in (2).\n\n## 5. The signed coefficient formula controls free completion\n\nFor the full retained prefix, (2) implies S/hcrit<k<5/2. The positivity\ncondition (8) consequently holds just as in Section4. Thus (7)--(9) apply\nto EVERY queried jet; C_i decreases and V_i increases.\n\nWrite q=n-1 and g=g_q. From (7) and (5),\n\n    <g,g_0>=C_q R_0^2+sigma V_q t_0>=0,\n    <g,x_0>=C_q t_0+sigma V_q>=0.\n\nThe exact signed difference of two gradients is\n\n    g-g_i=-(C_i-C_q)g_0+sigma(V_q-V_i)x_0.\n\nMoreover V_q-V_i=sum_(t=i)^(q-1)h_t C_t<=S-s_i. Therefore the complete\nterminal-correlation correction satisfies\n\n    sum_(i<q)h_i<g,g-g_i>\n       <=sigma<g,x_0>sum_(i<q)h_i(S-s_i)\n       =sigma(S^2+S2)<g,x_0>/2.                       (18)\n\nThe nonpositive term containing C_i-C_q was discarded with its correct\nsign. This estimate is sharper than simply bounding total gradient\nvariation in norm and retains the schedule's squared-step mass.\n\nThe outgoing last-query interpolation inequality to the optimum yields\n\n    2f_q<=2<x_q,g>-||g||^2\n         =2<x_0,g>-(2S+1)||g||^2\n                       +2sum_i h_i<g,g-g_i>.\n\nBy (18), ||x_0||=1 and completing the square in ||g||,\n\n    2f_q<=[1+sigma(S^2+S2)/2]^2/(2S+1).               (19)\n\nEvery convex1-smooth interpolant has, for0<=b<=2,\n\n    F(x_q-b g_q)<=f_q-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=f_q.\n\nThis proves (3) for every free completion, including the maximal one from\naccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290. The endpoint is never forced to follow A.\n\n## 6. Exact benchmark comparison and the squared-step limit\n\nThe sums of the FULL word H_m are\n\n    S_full=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),\n    S2_full=2^m-1+2a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n                         +a^2(c^(2m)-2^m)/(c^2-2).\n\nThe retained sums are S=S_full-d_0 and S2=S2_full-d_0^2. Since\na/(c-2) tends to1 and c^m/s^m=(1+1/m)^m tends to e,\n\n    S/s^m ->e,\n    S2/S^2 ->(s-2)^2/(s^2-2)<1/20.                   (20)\n\nFor the last strict inequality, substitution of s=1+sqrt(2) reduces it\nto59<42sqrt(2), whose square is3481<3528. Hence, for all sufficiently\nlarge m, (17) implies\n\n    1+sigma(S^2+S2)/2\n       <=1+(21/40)(1001/2000)^2<17/15.                (21)\n\nThe last comparison is the integer inequality\n15*21042021=315630315<320000000.\n\nFor completeness, derive the needed sharp exponent directly from the\naccepted benchmark recursions. Put rho=log_2 s and C_n=S_circle(n)+1.\nThe primitive and dominant mass joins are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gjoin(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nWe claim\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                    (22)\n\nLet V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 for t>0. Differentiating with t=exp(u) gives\n\n    d^2/du^2 log V(exp(u))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.\n\nThe numerator is positive since rho>1. Thus\nV(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=(s-1)^2=2. With t=(A/B)^(1/rho) and\nD=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho, this is\n(D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, so Omega(A,B)<=D. Continuity gives zero arguments\nas well. Induction in the primitive split k+l=n-1 now gives\nC_n<=[(k+1)+(l+1)]^rho=(n+1)^rho.\n\nAlso sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence Gjoin(A,D)<=4A+D. In the dominant\nsplit k+l=n-1, induction gives\nB_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho, because rho>1. This proves (22)\nfrom the actual recursions without assuming an asymptotic phase for B_n.\n\nCombining (19)--(22), and n=2^m-1, proves the uniform estimate\n\n    limsup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n      <= (2/e)(17/15)^2=578/(225e)<24/25.\n\nIndeed e>1+1+1/2+1/6+1/24=65/24>27/10, and\n578/(225*(27/10))=1156/1215<24/25 since28900<29160.\nThis completes the candidate proof of (4).\n\n## 7. Scope and retrieval record\n\nThis closes the entire sector0<Re(z)/|z|<=7/40 of the single common-metric\nelliptic ansatz on the assigned near-critical word. It includes arbitrary\neigenvalue magnitudes, all imaginary scales in that sector and arbitrarily\nill-conditioned horizon-dependent metrics. It does not assert that all\nlegal metrics isotropize. The initial value budget, positive two-vector\ncoefficients and first ACTUAL chronological crossing are the mechanism.\n\nThe remaining common-metric arena has Re(z)/|z|>7/40 and lies outside the\npreviously studied tiny mass-scaled disk and the fixed unit neighborhood.\nIn particular, order-one scaled frequencies at moderate angles remain\nunresolved. This is neither an unrestricted W upper certificate nor a\nsolution to the project's exact all-schedule profile problem.\n\nFact search found no accepted nonnormal sector theorem.
