{"id": "0e15279742ae0415", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:07:16.227148+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C38 closes the FREE UNQUERIED TERMINAL relaxation of C35's entire multiphase fresh-child-tree family and C36's four-edge split/merge diamond: retain all queried jets and optimum, then every smooth-convex completion remains below Huber K. Nonempty blocks keep exact closest distance; empty blocks pay an explicit distance factor. No prescribed terminal vertex survives in the completing function, and the two-cell task is excluded.", "evidence": "# C38. Free unqueried-terminal completion does not rescue the tree or diamond\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no solver or fact_submit. This addresses\nthe precise relaxation of the C35/C36 data: retain ALL actual queried jets and\nthe optimum jet (0,0,0), but allow an arbitrary smooth-convex terminal gradient\nand value as in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290. This is a MULTIPHASE construction\nscreen and does not repeat the separate two-cell calculation.\n\n## 1. Defined family and objective convention\n\nFix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Write M_j=1+sum H_j.\nFor a dyadic depth r with 0<=r<=m-2, put k=m-r, N=2^k, M=M_r,\nq=a c^r/M, A=1+q and\n\n    T_i=M(1+q c^(nu_2(i+1)))  (0<=i<N-1), T_(N-1)=T>=M.\n\nThere are N actual copies of H_r, with their original physical step order,\nseparated by the actual higher ruler checkpoints. Put S=M-1.\nChoose any ordered rooted tree on 0,...,N-1, parents earlier than children.\nFor positive masses R_i, define\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j child of i)R_j e_j)/T_i,\n    C=conv(0,p_i), F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2),\n    x_init=R_0 e_0.\n\nAssume this tree relay is legal with p_i the gradient throughout ordinary\nblock i and its checkpoint, including the original prescribed p_(N-1)\nat the terminal sample. This legality is equivalent to the following\nexplicit inequalities; hence the family is concrete, not arbitrary Gram data.\n\nLet E_i=R_i^2, M_i=M, L_i=T_i-M, Ch_i the children and\nW_i={j>i:parent(j)<i}. At the final ordinary query in phase i, the own\nnormal is\n\n    [L_i E_i-M sum_(j in Ch_i)E_j]/T_i^2.\n\nPast-vertex normals are nonpositive. Future normals are E_j/T_j for\nj in W_i, (M/T_i)E_j/T_j for j in Ch_i, and zero otherwise.\nThese comparisons with the own normal, together with comparison with zero,\nare necessary and sufficient at ALL queries: the own normal decreases,\nwaiting values stay fixed, and child values increase during the block.\nAt the terminal block T>=M suffices. These facts follow by following the\nnonnegative coordinate masses, which move from a parent to its children\nwhen its checkpoint exhausts the parent coordinate.\n\nAll p_i are actual independent real vectors; F_C is convex and 1-smooth\nwith grad F_C=P_C by the projection variational inequality. Every original\nqueried value is therefore\n\n    f_j=<x_j,g_j>-||g_j||^2/2.                              (1)\n\nNow retain the complete queried prefix and (0,0,0), and let f be ANY convex\n1-smooth extension. Its true normalization is dist(x_init,argmin f).\nEvery f allowed by the exact maximal completion of accepted1252b8823bb5a290\nis included. No terminal plane or terminal gradient is imposed on f.\n\n## 2. Necessary tree height bounds, derived internally\n\nPut H_i=E_i/T_i. Vertex i+1 is either waiting or a child of i. The complete\nnormal comparisons above imply respectively\n\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i/T_i,\n    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i T_i/[M(T_i+T_(i+1))].                (2)\n\nFor the child case keep its own E_(i+1) contribution in the child sum.\nDropping other nonnegative costs gives the bound. These are necessary\nbounds on the retained original family, not a new sufficiency claim.\n\nGeometric summation gives\n\n    M_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j,\n    2/5<q<=c-2<7/16,  12/5<c<39/16,  M_m<=M c^k.          (3)\n\nFor completeness a<c-2 follows from ac<1<c(c-2).\nAlso M_r<=c^r implies q>=a>2540/6321>2/5, using\na>=127/(129lambda), lambda<49/20. The c upper bound follows from\nlambda<169/70 and (169/70)(129/128)<39/16.\nThe exact mass relation giving the last inequality is\nM_m=M[(1-q/(c-2))2^k+(q/(c-2))c^k].\n\nAt each even i the low checkpoint has L_i/M=q. Its waiting bound in\n(2) dominates its child bound, since q^2<1 for a nonterminal next phase,\nand q+q^2<1<=T/M for a terminal next phase. Thus its height multiplier\nis at most q/A. At a high level ell>=1, followed by a low checkpoint,\nthe child bound dominates and is at most\n\n    q c^ell/D_ell,  D_ell=(2+q+q c^ell)/(1+q c^ell)>1.\n\nIndeed q c^ell>24/25 and its square plus itself exceeds1+q, which is\nexactly the comparison of the two alternatives in (2).\nThere are N/2 low checkpoints and 2^(k-ell-1) high level-ell checkpoints.\n\nDefine\n\n    Btree_k=4(qc)^(2^k-1) /\n       [A^(2^(k-1)+1) product_(ell=1)^(k-1)\n                        D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].              (4)\n\nThe following uniform estimates are algebraic:\n\n    Btree_2<=eta=10763254593/11175730176<1,\n    Btree_(k+1)<zeta Btree_k^2,\n    zeta=6279/16384<1.                                    (5)\n\nTo check the base case,\nBtree_2=4(qc)^3(1+qc)/[(1+q)^3(2+q+qc)] increases in q,c.\nIts logarithmic q derivative is\n3/[q(1+q)]+(c-1)/[(1+cq)(2+(1+c)q)]>0;\nthe c derivative is 3/c+q/(1+cq)-q/(2+(1+c)q)>0.\nSubstitution of q=7/16,c=39/16 gives eta.\nThe exact recurrence ratio is (qc)(1+q)/(4D_k), which is less than zeta.\nThus Btree_k<=zeta^(2^(k-2)-1)eta^(2^(k-2)) for all k>=2.\n\nLet K=1/(2M_m-1). This is the global Huber score, with no assumption about\nunrestricted W or the exact benchmark ratio.\n\n## 3. Nonempty ordinary blocks: unchanged bound for EVERY completion\n\nSuppose r>=1. Every p_i, including the old terminal p_(N-1), occurs at an\nACTUAL queried point, because each H_r has at least one step.\nFor any minimizer z of f, the accepted smooth-convex interpolation inequality\napplied to (1) and (z,0,0) gives <p_i,z><=0. Backward induction through\nthe tree forces all coordinates of z nonpositive. Since x_init=R_0e_0\nand0 is still a minimizer, the TRUE initial distance is exactly R_0.\n\nThe original final step is h_last=1+a. Its preceding gradient is\np_(N-1)=R_(N-1)e_(N-1)/T. Since the original polytope has this gradient\nboth at that query and at the endpoint, its values differ by\n-h_last||p_(N-1)||^2. Smoothness of ANY new extension at the retained last\nquery therefore gives\n\n    2f(x_n) <= 2F_C(x_n)+h_last^2||p_(N-1)||^2\n             =[2T-D+h_last^2] E_(N-1)/T^2,\n    D=2M-1.                                                (6)\n\nThis is an upper bound valid for every full completion, regardless of\nattainability of the smoothness bound. It does not discard any prefix\nconstraint as sufficient.\n\nCrucially D-h_last^2>0. Indeed M>=M_1=2+a, so\n\n    D-h_last^2>=2(2+a)-1-(1+a)^2=2-a^2>0.\n\nThus [2T-D+h_last^2]/T<2. Multiplying all the height bounds in\nSection2 and using M_m<=Mc^k gives, exactly as a finite product,\n\n    2f(x_n)/dist(x_init,argmin f)^2 < Btree_k K < K,        (7)\n\nfor every r>=1 and k>=2. No small-o term or untracked terminal parameter\nis needed. The same uniform margin eta and recursive decay hold even after\nthe final gradient and value are freed completely.\n\n## 4. Empty ordinary blocks: retain the changed distance explicitly\n\nNow r=0, so M=1, k=m, every original phase consists of just its checkpoint,\nand the old terminal p_(N-1) is NOT a queried gradient. One cannot use it\nto constrain the new minimizer set. We use a separate bound.\n\nLet i=N-2, the last queried phase. Its T_i=A=1+q and its checkpoint is A.\nIt has either the terminal vertex as its sole child or no children.\nIn the child case, its position is R_i e_i and\np_i=(R_i e_i-R_(N-1)e_(N-1))/A.\nIn the leaf case, its position is R_i e_i+R_(N-1)e_(N-1) and\np_i=R_i e_i/A. Equation(1) and one smoothness step imply in either case\n\n    2f(x_n) <= (1-A^(-2))E_i.                              (8)\n\nFor a child there is the additional term\n[1-2/A-1/A^2]E_(N-1), which is negative since A<23/16;\ndropping it gives (8). For a leaf, (8) is the exact smoothness bound.\n\nThe new closest-minimizer distance need not be R_0. Nevertheless the\nretained initial gradient has norm at least R_0/A. Its1-Lipschitz property\nand its vanishing at every minimizer give\n\n    dist(x_init,argmin f)^2 >= E_0/A^2.                    (9)\n\nThere are N/2-1 low transitions before phase i and the same high transitions\nas in Section2. Since T_i=T_0=A, their product bounds E_i/E_0.\nUsing (8)-(9), M_m<=c^k, and (4) gives\n\n    [2f(x_n)/dist(x_init,argmin f)^2]/K\n         < A^2(1+q/2) Btree_k\n         <= (20631/8192) Btree_k.                         (10)\n\nFor every k>=3 this is already below1. Indeed (5) gives\nBtree_k<zeta, and\n(20631/8192)(6279/16384)<1. Here k=m>=128.\nThis proves the free-terminal exclusion also for empty ordinary blocks,\nwithout silently retaining an unqueried terminal normal or the old distance.\n\n## 5. The split-and-merge diamond also stays excluded\n\nFor clarity, the exact deletion argument needed here is reproduced.\nUse four equal blocks M-1 and checkpoint excesses Mq,Mcq,Mq,\nwith A=1+q, B=1+cq, T_last=Mt. The real normals are\n\n    p_0=(e_0-x e_1-y e_2)/(MA),\n    p_1=(x e_1-v e_3)/(MB),\n    p_2=(y e_2-w e_3)/(MA),\n    p_3=(v+w)e_3/(Mt).\n\nFor any fully legal original diamond let R=v+w>0 and z=v/R.\nAll-normal endpoint necessities imply\n\n    q >=(1+A/B)x^2+y^2,   q>=x^2+2y^2,\n    y^2/R^2 >=Y(z)=(1+qz)(1-z+A/t)/q,\n    x^2/R^2 >=X(z)=[z^2-(B/A)z(1-z)+(B^2/A)Y(z)]/(B-1).\n\nThese follow by direct dot products with the zero and future planes; no\nremote comparisons are declared sufficient. The concave Y satisfies\nY(0)>Y(1), since Y(0)-Y(1)=1/q-A/t>0. Also\n\n    (B-1)X'(z)\n       =2[1+B/A-B^2/A]z+(B/A)[-1+B(1-1/q+A/t)]<0.\n\nFor the coefficient of z, B(B-1)>1176/625>A; for the constant,\nB(1-1/q+A/t)<(529/256)(17/112)<1. Hence set\n\n    v'=R, w'=0,\n    y'=R sqrt(A^2/(qt)),\n    x'=R sqrt([1+B^2 A/(qt)]/(B-1)).\n\nThen x'<=x,y'<=y, so both root comparisons remain valid. In phase1\nthe comparison with p_2 is equality, and its comparison with p_3 follows\nfrom BA>q. In phase2 the p_3 comparison is equality. All past comparisons\nare nonpositive, all own values nonnegative, and every nonterminal\ndifference decreases across its ordinary block because the replacement\nhas nonpositive distinct-normal correlations. Thus every normal at every\nquery and terminal point is legal. This is an actual tree replacement.\n\nThe replacement leaves R,T_last and the ENTIRE last-block positions,\ngradients and values unchanged. When r=m-2>=1, all four original normals\nare queried. In the original diamond, their triangular inequalities\n<p_i,z><=0 force all coordinates of any new minimizer nonpositive, so\nthe closest-minimizer distance remains1 even for a free completion.\n\nFor ANY free completion of the original diamond, the last-query bound (6)\nis therefore the SAME expression as for the tree replacement. Applying\nthe proof of (7) to that replacement's parameters gives\n\n    [2f(x_n)/dist(x_init,argmin f)^2]/K < eta<1.             (11)\n\nThe replacement does not purport to preserve the other queried jets.\nIt is used only to bound their shared terminal-position/last-query\nparameters. Every original completion is bounded by (6), and this same\nupper expression is bounded via the fully legal replacement.\n\n## 6. Scope\n\nEquations(7),(10) close free maximal-prefix completion for EVERY multiphase\nordered fresh-child tree in the stated dyadic family. Equation(11) closes\nthe exact four-edge diamond 01,02,13,23, including its positive middle\ncorrelation. No final gradient is prescribed to the newly completing\nfunction. The true initial distance is proved exactly for nonempty blocks\nand lower-bounded explicitly when empty blocks remove a queried normal.\n\nThis does not close arbitrary shared-coordinate DAGs, changed queried\nprefixes, or the original minimax problem. The k=1 two-cell family is\ndeliberately outside this result; it is another worker's separate task.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["a0bb50ebcb4cb599", "1d9af6b7bf48b5e3", "3b5baff6751d091e", "2f1477b9cbf7cce2"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "research/c38_free_terminal_tree_diamond.md"}, "glossary": {"Btree_k": "Explicit scalar obstruction bound(4), distinct from the project's exact benchmark B_n.", "eta_tree": "Rational10763254593/11175730176<1, the uniform four-phase score/K bound.", "zeta_tree": "Rational6279/16384<1, the recursive decay coefficient.", "E_i": "Squared coordinate mass R_i² in the explicit incidence family.", "H_i": "Support-height E_i/T_i in this proof, not the schedule word H_m."}}
{"id": "0d7490b3764f1303", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:13:11.885967+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Concrete primal terminal-saturation mechanism: append a scalar gradient Lambda+d_i/Lambda, impose every incoming-terminal equality by explicit values, and check EVERY other actual Q as A_ij+B_ij/Lambda². Strict linear rescaled-normal inequalities plus one energy inequality give an explicit finite legal negative-V witness with a genuine minimizer and fixed smooth interpolant. It exactly repairs the old h=(5,10) normal-shear failure, giving V=-8343/16. A four-step retained-geometry obstruction prevents promoting this move to a universal saturation theorem.", "evidence": "# An explicit terminal-saturation lift and its geometric obstruction\n\nStatus: self-contained candidate evidence, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical\nsolver or fact_submit. This is a definite primal construction with checkable\nhypotheses, together with an exact four-positive-step obstruction to making\nit universal. It does not prove general terminal saturation or strict\nenlargement of the E_theta cone.\n\nThe accepted interpolation background is fact 1252b8823bb5a290. No external\ntheorem is needed below. A Matlas cyclic-monotonicity search was background\nonly; the finite calculations and the interpolating function are explicit.\n\n## 1. Data, rescaled normals, and the two hypotheses of the lift\n\nLet h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be positive, n>=1, and let\n(x_i,g_i,f_i), 0<=i<=n, be an actual convex differentiable 1-smooth GD\ntrajectory, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. Define\n\n s=sum h_i, C=1+s, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, R_i=s-P_i,\n y_i=x_i-g_i, H_g=sum_(i<n)h_i g_i,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n),\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                                    -sC||g_n||^2,\n D_old=||H_g+g_n||^2-C^2||g_n||^2=V_h-2J_h.         (1)\n\nTo check the last equality, expand 2J_h and use\n2sum_i h_i<g_i,sum_(t>=i)h_t g_t>\n =||H_g||^2+sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2. All value and nonterminal diagonal\nterms then cancel in V_h-2J_h.\n\nChoose real numbers d_0,...,d_n, with d_n=0. Put\n\n Delta=sum_(i<n)h_i d_i,\n t_j=sum_(u=j)^(n-1)h_u d_u-d_j,\n A_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j-y_n>+R_j(d_i-d_j),\n B_ij=(d_i-d_j)t_j.                               (2)\n\nHere t_n=0. The two hypotheses are\n\n A_ij<0 for all j<n and i!=j, 0<=i<=n,             (3)\n epsilon=-(D_old+2C Delta)>0.                      (4)\n\nThese are explicit strict linear inequalities in the d_i, apart from the\nfixed supplied geometric coefficients. They are sufficient conditions only;\nnot every negative V_h trajectory satisfies them.\n\n## 2. Construction and every interpolation residual\n\nTake any positive Lambda with\n\n Lambda^2 >= max{1, 2Delta^2/epsilon,\n               max_(j<n,i!=j) 2(B_ij)_+/(-A_ij)},  (5)\n\nwhere u_+=max(u,0). Define one new scalar gradient coordinate and its exact\nGD positions by\n\n c_i=Lambda+d_i/Lambda,\n z_0=C Lambda,  z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i c_i,\n X_i=(x_i,z_i), G_i=(g_i,c_i).\n\nSet the new terminal value F_n=0 and all earlier values to\n\n F_i=<G_i,X_i-X_n>-||G_i-G_n||^2/2.               (6)\n\nEvery incoming-terminal residual Q^new_(i,n) is then exactly zero by\ndefinition. The new positions and gradients obey the original h exactly.\nNo old interpolation edge is deleted.\n\nThe following three-point identity follows by expanding the definition of Q\non any three finite jets:\n\n Q_ij-Q_(i,n)+Q_(j,n)\n       =<g_i-g_j,(x_j-g_j)-(x_n-g_n)>.             (7)\n\nUse (7) on the new data. The appended normal difference is\n\n (z_j-c_j)-(z_n-c_n)=R_j Lambda+t_j/Lambda.\n\nConsequently EVERY finite ordered residual is exactly\n\n Q^new_ij=A_ij+B_ij/Lambda^2.                     (8)\n\nFor j=n this is zero. For j<n, i!=j, inequalities (3)-(5) give\nQ^new_ij<=A_ij/2<0 if B_ij>0, and Q^new_ij<=A_ij<0 if B_ij<=0.\nThus all finite interpolation inequalities, including every outgoing\nterminal edge, are checked.\n\n## 3. A genuine minimizer and one fixed smooth convex function\n\nDefine adjusted new values Delta_i^val=F_i-||G_i||^2/2. Choose an index k\nminimizing them, and add the optimum jet\n\n X_star=X_k-G_k,  G_star=0,  F_star=Delta_k^val.\n\nFor every i direct expansion gives\n\n Q^new_(i,star)=Q^new_(i,k)<=0,\n Q^new_(star,i)=Delta_k^val-Delta_i^val<=0.\n\nThese relations check every missing pair with the optimum. Translate X_star\nand F_star to zero. Applying the finite construction in accepted fact\n1252b8823bb5a290 to these complete jets gives one everywhere finite convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth function with that actual minimizer. Explicitly, in\nthe translated coordinates, set\n\n H_new(v)=max_(j in {star,0,...,n})\n   {<G_j,X_j>-F_j+<X_j,v-G_j>+||v-G_j||^2/2},\n f_new(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H_new(v)}.\n\nThe accepted fact proves that this function interpolates all displayed jets.\nIt is one fixed function, so the complete prescribed GD run is realized.\n\nIts incoming-terminal Q are still zero after translation. Applying (1) to\nthe new data with J_new=0, and using d_n=0, gives the exact energy\n\n V_new=D_old+2C Delta+Delta^2/Lambda^2\n      =-epsilon+Delta^2/Lambda^2<=-epsilon/2<0.    (9)\n\nHere the extra-coordinate part follows directly from\nsum h_i c_i+c_n=C Lambda+Delta/Lambda and c_n=Lambda.\nThis is a legal negative-V witness with all incoming-terminal Q zero.\nNo initial-distance or normalized objective preservation is asserted.\n\n## 4. A necessary rescaled-normal condition for nearly affine padding\n\nFor each j<n put w_j=(y_j-y_n)/R_j; R_j>0. Conditions (3) imply, for any\ntwo different nonterminal indices i,j,\n\n <g_i-g_j,w_i-w_j>>0.                             (10)\n\nIndeed divide A_ij<0 by R_j, A_ji<0 by R_i, and add. The d_i-d_j terms\ncancel. More general directed cycles give analogous necessary cyclic\ninequalities, but the pair condition already gives the exact obstruction\nbelow.\n\nThere is a stronger quantitative statement that does not assume the specific\n1/Lambda ansatz. Keep ALL old x_i,g_i coordinates fixed. Append arbitrary\nvectors z_i,c_i in ANY additional finite-dimensional Euclidean space, with\nz_(i+1)=z_i-h_i c_i. Suppose some values F_i make the augmented jets legal\nand every incoming-terminal residual zero. Let\n\n rho=max_(0<=i<=n)||c_i-c_n||,\n e_i=c_i-c_n, U_j=sum_(t>=j)h_t e_t-e_j.\n\nThus e_n=0 and\n(z_j-c_j)-(z_n-c_n)=R_j c_n+U_j. Identity (7) and the two actual inequalities\nQ^new_ij<=0, Q^new_ji<=0 imply\n\n <g_i-g_j,w_i-w_j>\n   +<e_i-e_j,U_i/R_i-U_j/R_j> >=0.                (11)\n\nAll common appended gradients cancel in this expression, no matter how\nlarge their norm is. The bounds\n\n ||e_i-e_j||<=2rho,\n ||U_j/R_j||<=rho(1+1/R_j)\n\nshow that the absolute value of the second term in (11) is at most\n\n 2rho^2(2+1/R_i+1/R_j).                           (12)\n\nTherefore, if the retained geometry has\n<g_i-g_j,w_i-w_j>=-mu<0, every such terminal-saturated augmentation must\nhave\n\n rho^2 >= mu/[2(2+1/R_i+1/R_j)].                   (13)\n\nThis excludes ANY augmentation with appended gradient differences tending\nto zero, even if the common appended gradient tends to infinity and the\nadded dimension is allowed to vary. It does not exclude a substantially\nnonaffine augmentation, a change of the old coordinates, or a different\nnegative witness for the same schedule.\n\n## 5. Exact four-positive-step obstruction\n\nUse f(x)=x^2/4, x_0=1, and h=(1,8,8,8). The function is convex and its\ngradient has Lipschitz constant 1/2<=1. The exact trajectory is\n\n x=(1,1/2,-3/2,9/2,-27/2),\n g=(1/2,1/4,-3/4,9/4,-27/4),  f_i=g_i^2.\n\nEach GD equation holds and y_i=x_i-g_i=g_i. Its intrinsic energy is\n\n V_h=sum_(i<4)h_i(3-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+3)g_4^2\n    =-227-25*28*(729/16)=-128483/4<0.\n\nFor the first two query indices,\n\n R_0=25, R_1=24,\n w_0=(g_0-g_4)/25=29/100,\n w_1=(g_1-g_4)/24=7/24,\n (g_0-g_1)(w_0-w_1)=-1/2400.\n\nThus (13) becomes the concrete requirement\n\n rho^2 >= (1/2400)/[2(2+1/25+1/24)] =1/9992.      (14)\n\nIn particular, no choice of fixed d_i in Sections 1-3 can work for all large\nLambda on this retained trajectory: rho=max|d_i|/Lambda tends to zero and\neventually violates (14). In fact no choice can meet (3), by (10). This is\nan exact failure of an attempted universal affine-padding saturation move,\nnot a failure of the sufficient theorem under its actual hypotheses.\nIt is not a nonprimitive E_theta-valid schedule and not a general\nnonexistence result for terminal-saturated witnesses.\n\n## 6. The construction repairs the earlier failed normal-shear example\n\nTake the unit-threshold Huber function, h=(5,10), and its old data\n\n x=(1,-4,6), g=(1,-1,1), f=(1/2,7/2,11/2).\n\nHere y=(0,-3,5), C=16, J_h=-100, V_h=-440, and D_old=-240.\nChoose d=(1/10,-1,0). Then Delta=-19/2 and epsilon=544.\nThe complete A list with nonterminal target is\n\n A_01=-5, A_10=-13/2, A_20=-3/2, A_21=-6,\n\nand the corresponding B list is\n\n B_01=-99/10, B_10=264/25, B_20=24/25, B_21=-9.\n\nThe choice Lambda=2 satisfies (5). It gives\n\n X_0=(1,32),       G_0=(1,41/20), F_0=37409/800,\n X_1=(-4,87/4),    G_1=(-1,3/2),  F_1=243/8,\n X_2=(6,27/4),     G_2=(1,2),     F_2=0.\n\nTheir nontrivial finite Q are exactly\n\n Q_01=-299/40, Q_10=-193/50,\n Q_20=-63/50,  Q_21=-33/4,\n Q_02=Q_12=0.\n\nThe adjusted values F_i-||G_i||^2/2 are 1104/25, 115/4, -5/2.\nThus the explicit optimum is X_star=(5,19/4), F_star=-5/2, G_star=0.\nAll incoming optimum residuals are zero because the terminal index minimizes\nthe adjusted value and every Q_(i,2)=0; all outgoing optimum residuals are\nnonpositive by the displayed adjusted values.\n\nThe full legal negative energy is\n\n V_new=-544+361/16=-8343/16<0.\n\nIt can also be checked from the core norm identity:\n||X_0-X_star||^2-16^2||G_2||^2\n=16+11881/16-1280=-8343/16.\n\nUnlike the old energy-preserving normal shear, which created Q_10=5/8>0,\nthis different lift changes the geometry and energy while keeping every\nactual interpolation residual nonpositive. It establishes a successful\ninstance of the new construction; Section 5 shows why this success cannot\nbe promoted to a universal repair.\n\n## 7. Remaining obligation\n\nThe general negative-V-to-terminal-tight question remains open. The concrete\nmove above succeeds when both the strict rescaled-normal potential\ninequalities and the energy inequality hold. A universal proof using added\ncoordinates must overcome the exact retained-geometry obstruction (13),\nfor example by genuinely changing the old trajectory or allowing auxiliary\ngradient variation bounded away from zero. No generic SDP reparameterization,\nfixed-matrix repair, or profile comparison is being claimed.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_affine_padding_terminal_saturation", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "local_memory/affine_padding_terminal_saturation.md", "status": "complete_self_contained_candidate_unverified", "repairs_specific_failed_move": "347ec0d726695de2", "scope": "conditional scalar augmentation, not universal saturation or cone enlargement", "numerical_solver_calls": 0, "fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"D_old": "Value-independent norm difference ||sum h_i g_i+g_n||^2-(1+sum h_i)^2||g_n||^2=V_h-2J_h.", "R_i": "Remaining step sum sum_(t>=i)h_t, positive at every nonterminal index of a positive schedule.", "w_i": "Remaining-time rescaled old normal (x_i-g_i-x_n+g_n)/R_i.", "d_i": "Chosen scalar perturbation potentials, with d_n=0.", "Delta": "Weighted perturbation sum sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.", "A_ij": "Leading new residual <g_i-g_j,y_j-y_n>+R_j(d_i-d_j).", "B_ij": "Exact correction (d_i-d_j)(sum_(t>=j)h_t d_t-d_j).", "rho": "Largest difference between an appended gradient vector and its appended terminal gradient."}}
{"id": "209666ace998cb98", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:13:53.693013+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C39 COMPLETE six-edge four-phase shared-coordinate DAG is closed, including all positive correlations, direct terminal preload and cross-feed. Two exact split cancellations give an attained global optimum, realized by the three-edge tree01,03,12. Every original flow has a replacement preserving objective, primitive defect and true distance; even ALL free terminal completions on actual H_m have score/K<53235/91136<3/5. Larger inherited-coordinate windows remain open.", "evidence": "# C39. Exact optimum of the COMPLETE four-phase forward-flow class\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No solver or fact_submit. This extends\nthe four-edge diamond screen to ALL SIX possible forward transfers on four\nphases. All identities needed below are reproduced internally. It remains\na specified correlated construction class, not an unrestricted W bound.\n\n## 1. Actual schedule, vectors, normalization and full legality test\n\nLet M>=1, 2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16, and put\n\n    A=1+q, b=cq, B=1+b, s=A/B, alpha=1+s.\n\nTake four arbitrary ordered ordinary blocks of common mass M-1, separated\nby checkpoint steps 1+Mq,1+Mb,1+Mq. The actual near-critical H_m is included\nby taking the four copies of H_(m-2), M=1+sum H_(m-2),\nq=a c^(m-2)/M, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, m>=128.\n\nThe inclusion follows from\nM_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j, 0<a<c-2, M_j<=c^j.\nThus a<=q<=c-2; lambda<169/70 gives c<39/16, and\na>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5, using lambda<49/20.\n\nLet all six transfer amounts be nonnegative:\n\n    a_01=x, a_02=y, a_03=z, a_12=u, a_13=v, a_23=w.\n\nAssume x>0, Y=y+u>0, R=z+v+w>0. Let R_0>0 be the initial root mass\nand let t>=1 be the terminal parameter. The four independent real vectors are\n\n    p_0=(R_0 e_0-x e_1-y e_2-z e_3)/(MA),\n    p_1=(x e_1-u e_2-v e_3)/(MB),\n    p_2=(Y e_2-w e_3)/(MA),\n    p_3=R e_3/(Mt).                                      (1)\n\nTheir triangular coordinate matrix has positive diagonal, so this is an\nexplicit real positive definite Gram realization. For example,\n\n    <p_0,p_1>=(-x^2+yu+zv)/(M^2 AB),\n    <p_1,p_2>=(-uY+vw)/(M^2 AB).\n\nThus signs and nonlocal correlations are unrestricted within these actual\nnonnegative flow parameters; shared descendants are explicitly retained.\n\nLet C=conv(0,p_0,p_1,p_2,p_3) and\nF_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2). Its gradient is P_C, hence it is\nconvex and 1-smooth by the projection variational inequality and the two\nmaximization difference bounds.\n\nStart at R_0e_0. Each checkpoint exhausts its current coordinate and adds\nits outgoing transfers to future coordinates. Before phase1 the state is\nx e_1+y e_2+z e_3; before phase2 it is Y e_2+(z+v)e_3; before phase3\nit is R e_3. During an ordinary block, subtract the corresponding\nphysical partial mass times p_i. Its final proximal normal point is the\nstate after subtracting M p_i, since the block mass is M-1.\n\nALL normal inequalities are tested against every p_j and zero at both\nordinary endpoints. This is necessary and sufficient because each\ndifference is affine in the physical partial mass. Past normals are\nnonpositive: their positive coordinate is exhausted and their other\ncoordinates are nonpositive, while the proximal point's coordinates\nremain nonnegative. The following proof assumes every normal holds on\nthe original flow. It uses only certain NECESSARY inequalities to obtain\nan upper bound, and later verifies every inequality on an attaining flow.\n\nThe minimizer set is C^polar. Its triangular inequalities force every\ncoordinate of every minimizer nonpositive, by backward induction.\nTherefore the TRUE initial distance is R_0, attained at0.\n\nAt the terminal point x_end=[1-(M-1)/(Mt)]R e_3, the exact score is\n\n    score=2F_C(x_end)/R_0^2\n          =(2t-2+1/M)R^2/(M t^2 R_0^2).                  (2)\n\nIf htot is the total physical schedule mass, its primitive defect is\n\n    V/R_0^2\n       =1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 R^2/R_0^2.             (3)\n\nThis follows by telescoping\n2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2, since F_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\n## 2. Three retained normal comparisons suffice for the upper bound\n\nThe phase2 endpoint compared with the terminal vertex gives\n\n    qY^2 >= A w(z+v)+w^2+(A^2/t)R(z+v+w/A).             (4)\n\nThe phase1 endpoint compared with vertex2 gives\n\n    b x^2 >= (1+b theta)(1-theta+B/A)Y^2\n                              +[v-(B/A)w](v+Bz),          (5)\n    theta=y/Y in [0,1].\n\nTo verify (5), its own endpoint, multiplied by M B^2, is\nb x^2-B(uy+vz)-u^2-v^2.\nVertex2's value with the same multiplier is\n(B^2/A)[Y(y+u/B)-w(z+v/B)].\nMove terms to the right and substitute u=(1-theta)Y,y=theta Y.\nThe Y^2 coefficient is exactly (1+b theta)(1-theta+B/A).\n\nThe root endpoint compared with vertex1 gives\n\n    qR_0^2 >= alpha x^2\n        +[theta^2-s theta(1-theta)]Y^2+z^2-s vz.           (6)\n\nIndeed the own endpoint times MA^2 is\nqR_0^2-x^2-y^2-z^2, while vertex1's value with that multiplier is\n(A/B)(x^2-uy-vz). This proves (6).\n\nThese are actual queried endpoints, even when an ordinary block is empty.\nNo later function completion can invalidate a necessary constraint on them.\n\n## 3. Two exact cancellations remove both splits\n\nNormalize by R and write\n\n    sigma=(z+v)/R in [0,1],  zeta=z/R in [0,sigma],\n    w/R=1-sigma, v/R=sigma-zeta.\n\nEquation(4) is\n\n    Y^2/R^2 >= F(sigma)=(1+q sigma)(1-sigma+A/t)/q.         (7)\n\nInsert (5) into (6). The theta^2 terms cancel EXACTLY. Define\n\n    C0=alpha^2/(s b)=(A+B)^2/(A B b),\n    C1=2+1/s-alpha/b,\n    K0=alpha B/(b s)=(A+B)B/(A b).\n\nThen\n\n qR_0^2/R^2 >=\n   (C0+C1 theta)Y^2/R^2\n   +(alpha/b)[sigma-zeta-(1-sigma)/s](sigma+b zeta)\n   +zeta^2-s(sigma-zeta)zeta.                              (8)\n\nHere C1>0: s<1 gives 2+1/s>3, whereas alpha<2 and b>24/25\ngive alpha/b<25/12. Thus use (7) and drop C1 theta>=0.\n\nThe zeta^2 terms in the remaining expression ALSO cancel exactly.\nIts coefficient of zeta is\n\n    sigma(1-alpha/b)-alpha(1-sigma)/s < = 0.\n\nThis is nonpositive because alpha>3/2>b:\ns>1/2 follows from B<2A, and b<273/256<3/2.\nTherefore its minimum on 0<=zeta<=sigma is at zeta=sigma, giving\n\n    qR_0^2/R^2 >= P(sigma),\n    P(sigma)=C0 F(sigma)+sigma^2-K0 sigma(1-sigma).         (9)\n\nThere is no minimax exchange: each simplification only lowers a\nNECESSARY lower bound on R_0^2/R^2.\n\n## 4. The last merge is also disadvantageous\n\nThe coefficient of sigma^2 in P is d=1-C0+K0>0, because\nK0/C0=B^2/(A+B)>1. The last inequality follows from\nB(B-1)>1176/625>23/16>A. Thus P' increases on[0,1].\nAt its right endpoint,\n\n    P'(1)=2+K0-C0(1+1/q-A/t)\n            <=2+K0-C0(1/q-q).                             (10)\n\nThis is uniformly negative in the entire parameter box. Here is a fully\nrational certificate, with no parameter search. Since\ns<=575/784<1 and b<273/256,\n\n    C0=(s+2+1/s)/b >=9850032/2563925.\n\nThis uses that s+1/s decreases on(0,1). Also\nB/(B-1)<49/24 and B/A<2645/1792, so\n\n    K0=(B/(B-1))(1+B/A) <=10353/2048.\n\nFinally 1/q-q>207/112. The exact rational margin is\n\n    (9850032/2563925)(207/112)-10353/2048-2\n       =72320939/1598105600>0.\n\nTherefore P'(1)<0, and hence P decreases throughout[0,1].\nEquation(9) is minimized at sigma=1:\n\n    qR_0^2/R^2 >= 1+C0 A^2/(q t).                        (11)\n\nDefine the explicit positive constant\n\n    Dflow=C0 A^2/q=A(A+B)^2/(B b q).\n\nThen EVERY legal six-edge flow satisfies\n\n    R^2/R_0^2 <= q t/(t+Dflow).                            (12)\n\n## 5. Attainment, every normal, and exact optimum\n\nEquality in (12) is attained by a specific tree with only three edges:\n\n    a_03=R, a_01=x, a_12=Y,\n    a_02=a_13=a_23=0,\n    Y^2=R^2 A^2/(q t),\n    x^2=Y^2(1+B/A)/b,\n    R_0^2=R^2[1+Dflow/t]/q.                              (13)\n\nThe root comparison with vertex1 is equality by (11).\nIts comparison with terminal vertex3 follows because\ns x^2 >= A R^2/t; after substitution this is\nA(A+B)>=B b q, true since A>1, A+B>B and bq<1.\nIts comparison with vertex2 is zero, and comparison with zero follows\nfrom the vertex1 comparison.\n\nIn phase1 the comparison with its child vertex2 is equality:\nb x^2-Y^2=(B/A)Y^2.\nIts comparison with waiting terminal vertex3 follows from\nY^2/(AB)>=R^2/t, equivalently A>=Bq, true since Bq<1<A.\nIts own normal is positive and all past comparisons are nonpositive.\nIn phase2 the terminal comparison is equality:\nqY^2/A^2=R^2/t; all other comparisons are nonpositive.\nIn phase3 t>=1 gives the normal against zero and all past vertices.\n\nEvery distinct-gradient inner product in this replacement is nonpositive.\nFor an ordinary partial mass u the own-minus-other normal derivative is\n-||p_i||^2+<p_i,p_j><0. Thus each displayed endpoint check implies ALL\nactual queries within that block. The real vectors, fixed function,\nphysical chronology and optimum are now fully checked.\n\nFor any legal original flow with given R,R_0,t, (12) allows the same\nreplacement (13) using its original R_0 and the displayed x,Y. If (11)\nis slack, the root normal comparisons remain valid. This replacement\npreserves R,R_0,t, hence preserves exactly the objective(2), primitive\ndefect(3), and true initial distance. It also preserves the entire last\nordinary block's queried jets. Earlier jets can change; no claim of\npreserving them is made.\n\nLet D0=2-1/M>=1. Equations(2),(12),(13) give the EXACT optimum, for fixed t,\nover all legal nonnegative six-edge flows:\n\n    E_full(t)=q(2t-D0)/[M t(t+Dflow)],  t>=1.              (14)\n\nIts derivative has numerator -2t^2+2D0 t+D0 Dflow.\nConsequently the global optimum over t is attained at\n\n    t_opt=(D0+sqrt(D0^2+2D0 Dflow))/2 >=D0>=1,\n\nand equals\n\n    E_full_max =\n      2q/[M(D0+Dflow+sqrt(D0^2+2D0 Dflow))].              (15)\n\nThis is an attained quantified family optimum, not merely a necessary cap.\n\n## 6. Near-critical objective comparison, INCLUDING free terminal completion\n\nNow use the actual four copies of H_(m-2) inside H_m, m>=128.\nEvery phase contains queried points. For ANY convex 1-smooth completion f\nof ALL the original queried jets and the retained optimum(0,0,0), each\nminimizer z satisfies <p_i,z><=0 by smooth-convex interpolation:\nthe queried value is <x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2.\nAll four p_i are queried, so their triangular inequalities force every\ncoordinate of z nonpositive. Thus the true initial distance remains R_0.\nThis uses the full-prefix setting of accepted1252b8823bb5a290, and imposes\nNO terminal gradient or terminal value on f.\n\nThe original last physical step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at its retained\nlast query gives\n\n    2f(x_n)/R_0^2\n      <= [2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2] R^2/(M^2 t^2 R_0^2).     (16)\n\nThe same upper expression holds for the replacement because it preserves\nthe last block's positions, gradients and values. Since M>=M_1=2+a,\n\n    2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\n\nUse (12) in (16), retaining this sign, to get\n\n    2f(x_n)/R_0^2 < 2q/[M(t+Dflow)].                     (17)\n\nThe true ruler mass is M_m=M[4+q(c+2)]<=M c^2.\nWriting K=1/(2M_m-1), (17) gives\n\n    [2f(x_n)/R_0^2]/K <4c^2 q/(t+Dflow).                 (18)\n\nA short uniform rational estimate suffices. Since s+1/s>=2 and\nb<273/256,\n\n    C0>1024/273>15/4,\n    Dflow=C0 A^2/q > (15/4)(49/25)(16/7)=84/5.\n\nAlso 4c^2q<10647/1024 and t>=1. Therefore\n\n    [2f(x_n)/dist(x_init,argmin f)^2]/K\n        <53235/91136 <3/5.                               (19)\n\nThis bound covers EVERY smooth-convex free terminal completion of EVERY\nlegal original six-edge flow in the stated actual four-block family.\nNo terminal interpolation inequalities are silently retained as queries;\n(16) is a necessary smoothness bound from the last ACTUAL queried jet.\nAll old queried constraints remain part of the completion class.\n\n## 7. What remains outside the result\n\nThis is the complete nonnegative triangular coordinate-flow class on the\nfour chosen phases, including all shared coordinates and all six edges.\nIts optimum is attained by the tree (13), and even freeing the endpoint\nleaves a uniform loss below K. The result excludes more than one specified\ndiamond, but it is not a theorem about all real Gram matrices or all\nnonlinear functions.\n\nLarger graphs can preload several different future coordinates before a\nlocal four-phase window begins. Such inherited coordinate mass changes the\nroot normal used in (6); (8)-(11) do not automatically survive. Signed\ncoordinate masses, revisiting an exhausted coordinate, and arbitrary\npolytope geometry are also outside the argument. Those are the exact\nremaining correlation interfaces; the original full-profile theorem is open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["1f86874ef45e59b4", "1d9af6b7bf48b5e3", "0e15279742ae0415", "2f1477b9cbf7cce2"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "research/c39_complete_four_phase_flow.md"}, "glossary": {"Dflow": "Explicit scalar A(A+B)^2/(B b q), the exact four-phase coordinate-flow transfer cost.", "E_full": "Exact attained objective optimum over the complete six-edge flow family at fixed terminal parameter t.", "C0": "Positive coefficient (A+B)^2/(A B b) in the root-normal elimination.", "C1": "Positive coefficient2+1/s-(1+s)/b; it penalizes early loading of coordinate2.", "K0": "Coefficient(A+B)B/(A b) in the remaining scalar merge polynomial.", "sigma_flow": "Fraction(z+v)/R of terminal coordinate loaded before the final transfer.", "zeta_flow": "Fraction z/R of terminal coordinate loaded directly by the root."}}
{"id": "cd74f7b37ded4d52", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:17:44.882144+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "SHARP all-anchor transverse-cone obstruction in ANY dimension: if every queried gradient has nonnegative projection onto the normalized initial direction and their perpendicular components have pairwise nonnegative inner products, then2f_last<=1/(2S_pre+1), with equality attained by Huber; EVERY free completion and b in[0,2] obeys2F<=1/[2S_pre+1+b(2-b)]. Complete elliptic applications now close EVERY nonnegative chronological-coefficient orbit, the ENTIRE disk |z|S_pre<=4/9, and the ENTIRE sector0<Re(z)/|z|<=7/40 on the assigned near-critical ruler, all metrics/conditioning/values allowed. Their benchmark ratio is uniformly<=2/e asymptotically. Success needs a negative longitudinal projection or a negative transverse correlation; the whole single-mode class remains open.", "evidence": "# Every queried anchor: a sharp transverse-cone obstruction\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No new fact_submit, solver or numerical\nevaluation. All new claims used in the applications are proved in this file.\nAccepted facts1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d supply the interpolation\nconvention and the benchmark recursions. Earlier unverified frequency results\nare not used as lemmas.\n\n## 1. General theorem, any dimension and any nonnegative schedule\n\nLet h_0,...,h_(q-1)>=0, S=sum h_i, and let the queried real jets\n(x_i,g_i,f_i),0<=i<=q, together with the optimum(0,0,0), be interpolable\nby a convex differentiable1-smooth function. Assume\n\n    ||x_0||=1, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\n\nPut e=x_0 and decompose each queried gradient as\n\n    g_i=G_i e+u_i, G_i=<e,g_i>, <e,u_i>=0.\n\nSuppose\n\n    G_i>=0 for every queried i,\n    <u_i,u_j>>=0 for every queried i,j.                 (1)\n\nThen\n\n    2f_q<=1/(2S+1).                                   (2)\n\nThis queried bound is SHARP within the class for every supplied schedule.\nMoreover, for EVERY interpolant F of the same jets and every b in[0,2],\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)].                    (3)\n\nThe endpoint gradient and value are free; they are not required to satisfy\n(1). The dimension and all vectors may vary with the horizon. Zero steps\nand S=0 are allowed.\n\nThus this is a whole-class obstruction for genuinely correlated transverse\nvectors, not merely orthogonal gradients. A witness exceeding (2), or\nexceeding the corresponding free-completion bound (3), must have a negative\ninitial-direction gradient projection or a negative transverse pair\ncorrelation among its actual queried data.\n\n## 2. Proof using TWO ordered inequalities at each actual anchor\n\nDefine\n\n    X_i=1-sum_(t<i)h_t G_t,\n    W_i=sum_(t<i)h_t u_t,\n    x_i=X_i e-W_i.\n\nBy (1), X_i>=X_q for i<=q. Add Q_(q,i)<=0 and Q_(i,star)<=0 in\nthe convention of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290. For i=q use just the\noptimum inequality, which gives the same expression. The result is\n\n    f_q<=<g_q,x_q>+<g_i-g_q,x_i>\n                        -||g_i||^2/2-||g_i-g_q||^2/2. (4)\n\nThe transverse part of the first two terms equals\n\n    -<u_q,W_q-W_i>-<u_i,W_i><=0,                      (5)\n\nbecause both W_q-W_i and W_i are nonnegative weighted sums of queried\ntransverse vectors. Dropping the nonpositive transverse terms of both\nsquared norms as well, (4) implies\n\n    f_q<=G_q X_q+(G_i-G_q)X_i\n                           -G_i^2/2-(G_i-G_q)^2/2.    (6)\n\nChoose an actual queried index i minimizing G_i, and write p=G_i>=0.\nThen G_q>=p and X_i>=X_q, so\n\n    f_q<=p X_q-p^2/2-(G_q-p)^2/2.\n\nEvery G_t>=p, hence X_q=1-sum h_t G_t<=1-Sp. We obtain\n\n    2f_q<=2p-(2S+1)p^2-(G_q-p)^2<=1/(2S+1),           (7)\n\nby completing the square. This also covers p=0, when f_q=0.\n\nFor a free final step b in[0,2], let lambda=b(2-b)>=0. Smoothness gives\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)<=2f_q-lambda||g_q||^2\n       <=2p-(2S+1)p^2-(G_q-p)^2-lambda G_q^2.\n\nSince G_q>=p>=0, the last two subtracted terms are at least lambda p^2.\nAnother completion of the square proves (3). No terminal interpolation\ncondition has been deleted inside the queried prefix.\n\nFor sharpness of (2), let p=1/(2S+1) and use the one-dimensional Huber\nfunction\n\n    F_p(x)=x^2/2                         if |x|<=p,\n    F_p(x)=p|x|-p^2/2                    if |x|>=p.\n\nStart at x_0=1. Every queried point is 1-p sum_(t<i)h_t>=1-pS>=p,\nso all queried gradients equal p and the endpoint value is\n2f_q=2p-(2S+1)p^2=1/(2S+1). The minimizer is0 and the initial distance\nto it is exactly1. This is the accepted global-Huber construction in\nb98bb807d514e80d, given explicitly here. Sharpness of the relaxed\nfree-completion estimate (3) is NOT asserted.\n\nThe two-edge combination (4) is essential. An old-gradient support plane\nby itself gives a LOWER bound on a later value, not the upper bound used\nhere. The proof does not replace (4) by that invalid inequality.\n\n## 3. Single elliptic modes: the whole positive-coefficient class\n\nConsider queried polynomial data\n\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),\n    x_i=T(Re P_i,Im P_i),\n    g_i=T(Re[zP_i],Im[zP_i]), ||T e_1||=1,\n\nwith arbitrary invertible real T and z=alpha+i beta. Let sigma=|z|^2 and\nA=T [[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]] T^(-1). The actual physical vectors obey\n\n    g_i=A x_i, A^2-2alpha A+sigma I=0.\n\nDefine the REAL coefficients C_i,V_i by\n\n    C_0=1, V_0=0,\n    V_(i+1)=V_i+h_i C_i,\n    C_(i+1)=C_i-2alpha h_i C_i-sigma h_i V_i.           (8)\n\nDirect substitution gives\n\n    x_i=(C_i+2alpha V_i)x_0-V_i g_0,\n    g_i=C_i g_0+sigma V_i x_0.                        (9)\n\nFor ANY fully legal prefix of this form with\n\n    C_i>=0 at every queried index,                     (10)\n\nthe general bounds (2)--(3) hold. Indeed V_i>=0 by (8). Write\ng_0=t_0 e+w with e=x_0 and w perpendicular to e. Initial interpolation\ngives t_0>=||g_0||^2>=0. Then\n\n    G_i=C_i t_0+sigma V_i>=0, u_i=C_i w,\n    <u_i,u_j>=C_i C_j||w||^2>=0.\n\nThe same argument includes singular horizon-dependent metric limits;\nno bound on T enters it. Condition (10) is an actual finite chronological\ncoefficient test, not a universal spectral lens.\n\nHere is a useful sufficient condition for (10). If alpha>=0 and\n\n    2alpha S+sigma S^2/2<1,                            (11)\n\nthen, with s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, induction in (8) gives\n\n    0<C_i<=1, 0<=V_i<=s_i,\n    C_i>=1-2alpha s_i-sigma s_i^2/2>0.                 (12)\n\nWhile the preceding C's are positive, V is nondecreasing with increments\nat most h_i, and C is nonincreasing. Summing the decrements gives\nC_i>=1-2alpha s_i-sigma sum_(t<i)h_t s_t, and\nsum_(t<i)h_t s_t<=s_i^2/2. This closes the induction.\n\nIn particular (11) holds whenever |z|S<=4/9 and alpha>=0, because\n\n    2alpha S+sigma S^2/2<=8/9+8/81=80/81<1.            (13)\n\nThis improves the earlier tiny-disk screen to the ENTIRE mass-scaled\ndisk |z|S<=4/9, with the exact queried Huber bound (2). On a nonzero\nlegal elliptic prefix containing a positive step, alpha>0 follows from\nthe first positive-step/optimum calculation in the next section. For z=0\nall values are zero. If S=0, all coefficients remain C_i=1,V_i=0 and\nthe cone theorem applies directly. Hence no angle or conditioning\nexception is hidden in this disk statement.\n\n## 4. Assigned near-critical family and its entire wide-angle sector\n\nFix m>=128, s=1+sqrt(2), c=s(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and\n\n    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1.\n\nKeep these parameters fixed within the horizon. Retain H_m with its final\nd_0 deleted, and all queries0,...,n-1; let S be the retained sum. Then\nthe entire single elliptic common-metric class with\n\n    0<alpha/|z|<=7/40                                 (14)\n\nsatisfies (10), and hence (2)--(3), at ALL spectral magnitudes.\nWe re-prove the chronological restriction needed for this conclusion.\n\nAssume z!=0 and set r=|z|, omega=alpha/r. Invertibility of A gives\nR_0=||g_0||>0. Let t_0=<x_0,g_0>. Initial optimum inequalities give\nt_0>=R_0^2 and f_0<=t_0-R_0^2/2. The first step d_0 is positive.\nAdding the two inequalities between queries0 and1 and dividing by d_0^2\ngives <A g_0,g_0>>=||A g_0||^2>=0. Therefore\n\n    2alpha R_0^2-sigma t_0>=0,\n    alpha>0, hcrit=2alpha/sigma>=1,\n    f_0<=(hcrit-1/2)R_0^2.                            (15)\n\nOn any beginning of the retained prefix with mass T satisfying\n2alpha T+sigma T^2/2<1, the coefficient induction (12) applies.\nThe ordered interpolation inequalities give\n\n    f_i-f_(i+1)>=h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n                              +||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2.\n\nBy (9), t_0>=0 and positivity of all C,V,\n<g_(i+1),g_i>>=C_(i+1)C_i R_0^2. Put\ne_i=2alpha s_i+sigma s_i^2/2. Since\nC_i C_(i+1)>=(1-e_i)(1-e_(i+1))>=1-e_i-e_(i+1), summing yields\n\n    f_0-f_end>=[T-2alpha T^2-sigma T^3/2]R_0^2.       (16)\n\nThe exact summation identities are\nsum h_i(s_i+s_(i+1))=T^2 and\nsum h_i(s_i^2+s_(i+1)^2)=(2/3)T^3+(1/3)sum h_i^3<=T^3.\n\nNow 2<c<49/20 and 0<a<c-2. The latter follows from\nc(c-2)>s(s-2)=1>ac; the former follows from\nc<2193/896<49/20. The partial sum S_j=sum H_j has\n\n    1+S_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).\n\nEach occurrence of a level-j step has at least mass S_j before it.\nConsequently an actual retained step with preceding mass u satisfies\n\n    h_t=1+a c^j<=(c-2)u+c-1.                          (17)\n\nLet k=143/100. If S>=k(hcrit-1/2), choose the FIRST actual query whose\npreceding mass T reaches this level. There is no subdivision or added\nquery. By (17), with tau=1/hcrit in(0,1] and v=T/hcrit,\n\n    L(tau)<=v<U(tau),\n    L(tau)=k(1-tau/2),\n    U(tau)=(29/20)[k+(1-k/2)tau].                      (18)\n\nSince U(tau)<=9947/4000<5/2 and omega^2<=49/1600<1/32,\n\n    2alpha T+sigma T^2/2\n      =omega^2(4v+2v^2)<45/64<1.\n\nThus (16) holds at this actual query. Its value is nonnegative, and (15)\nforces\n\n    1-tau/2 >=E(v),\n    E(v)=v-(49/1600)(4v^2+2v^3).                     (19)\n\nBut E is concave on nonnegative arguments. The functions\nE(L(tau))-1+tau/2 and E(U(tau))-1+tau/2 are concave in tau and are\npositive at both endpoints0,1. At0 the exact values are\n\n    E(143/100)-1=313657/800000000>0,\n    E(4147/2000)-1=5062894373/6400000000000>0.\n\nAt1 use49/1600<1/32 and L(1)<3/4,12/5<U(1)<5/2 to get\n\n    E(L(1))>143/200-99/1024>1/2,\n    E(U(1))>12/5-225/128>1/2.\n\nConcavity first in tau, then in v, contradicts (19) throughout (18).\nTherefore\n\n    S<k(hcrit-1/2), rS<1001/2000.                     (20)\n\nFor the WHOLE retained prefix S/hcrit<k<5/2. The same bound45/64 proves\n2alpha S+sigma S^2/2<1. The full induction (12) gives C_i>0 at every\nqueried index. This proves (10), hence the sharp cone bound (2) and the\nfree-completion bound (3), for the entire sector (14).\n\n## 5. Uniform constant gap against the exact benchmark\n\nFor the assigned family the complete ruler sum is\n\n    S_full=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2), S=S_full-d_0.\n\nSince a/(c-2) tends to1, c^m/s^m tends to e, and 2/s<1, one has\n\n    S/s^m ->e.                                        (21)\n\nWe include the needed sharper benchmark estimate rather than using an\nunverified shared lemma. Put rho=log_2 s and C_n=S_circle(n)+1. The\naccepted recursions have increasing primitive and dominant joins\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gjoin(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nFor V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, differentiation with t=exp(u) gives\n\n    d^2/du^2 log V(exp(u))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive because rho>1. Convexity at u and -u gives\nV(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=(s-1)^2=2. With\nt=(A/B)^(1/rho), D=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho this is\n(D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, so Omega(A,B)<=D. Zero arguments follow by continuity.\nInduction on the primitive split k+l=n-1 proves C_n<=(n+1)^rho.\nSince sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, one has Gjoin(A,D)<=4A+D. Induction on\nthe dominant split gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho.               (22)\n\nCombine (3),(21),(22), with n=2^m-1. UNIFORMLY over all legal prefixes\nsatisfying (1), and in particular over the two elliptic regions proved\nabove, and every final b in[0,2],\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2F(x_(n-1)-b g_(n-1))\n                       <=2/e<1.                      (23)\n\nThe original final step d_0 is less than2. The two newly closed elliptic\nregions are the entire disk |z|S<=4/9 and the entire angular sector\n0<Re(z)/|z|<=7/40, with every common metric and every legal value choice.\nThe sufficient region2Re(z)S+|z|^2 S^2/2<1 can be used directly as well.\nThe coefficient criterion C_i>=0 for all queried indices is broader than\nthese explicit spectral regions and is itself a complete scoped result.\n\n## 6. What the next mechanism must change\n\nA successful near-critical common-metric witness outside these bounds\nmust have at least one negative coefficient C_i, unless it already leaves\nthe homogeneous single-mode class. More geometrically, the general theorem\nrequires either a negative transverse pair correlation or a negative\ninitial-direction gradient projection. Keeping every transverse queried\ngradient on the same nonnegative ray while all initial-direction\nprojections remain nonnegative cannot give the missing constant, even\nthough the function values and terminal gradient are fully free.\n\nThe general theorem allows many transverse directions with pairwise\nnonnegative correlations. It does not cover a signed transverse pattern,\nnegative longitudinal projections, all mixtures, or unrestricted W.\nNothing here proves the original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile target.\n\nFact search found no accepted transverse-cone result. Matlas returned\narXiv2111.07720,2310.17610,2003.04201, none of which supplies the statement\nor is used. The wide-angle search returned unrelated results from\nmath/0509250,2104.12931,1308.2952. {"id": "78b6a094a6346b4b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:26:06.002503+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "In-flight single-mode derivation completed before the joint pivot: ANY legal elliptic common-metric prefix with0<omega=Re(z)/|z|<=1/2 obeys |P_i(z)|²>=1-2omega at EVERY actual query, for arbitrary steps and conditioning. An exact physical Lyapunov energy and a2x2 Gram discriminant prove the barrier. On the actual near-critical ruler this excludes every sequence with7/40<=omega<=49/100, |z|->0 and |z|S_pre->infinity. It is a SINGLE-mode legality theorem, not a mixed bound or a payoff result; now prioritizing TASK_max2_joint_moment_nearcritical.", "evidence": "# Exact elliptic energy barrier and an intermediate-scale exclusion\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. This finishes the in-flight single-mode\nderivation allowed by guidanceea594a814f06f984; the next task is the JOINT\nnear-critical mixture audit. No new fact_submit or numerical evaluation.\nOnly accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the interpolation convention below.\nNo previous unverified frequency result is used as a premise.\n\n## 1. A necessary amplitude barrier for every actual query\n\nLet h_0,...,h_(N-1)>=0 be arbitrary, P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), and\nlet queried data0,...,N be\n\n    x_i=T(Re P_i,Im P_i),\n    g_i=T(Re[zP_i],Im[zP_i]), ||T e_1||=1,\n\nfor an arbitrary invertible real2x2 map T. Include the optimum jet(0,0,0)\nand assume full convex1-smooth interpolation by some values f_i.\nPut z=alpha+i beta, sigma=|z|^2>0, and\n\n    omega=alpha/sqrt(sigma), 0<omega<=1/2.              (1)\n\nThen EVERY actual queried polynomial obeys\n\n    |P_i(z)|^2>=1-2omega.                              (2)\n\nAll metrics, spectra and values may vary with the horizon. This is a\nsingle common-metric theorem. Its proof does not impose individual-mode\nbudgets on a jointly legal mixture and cannot be applied that way.\n\n## 2. Exact energy identity and interpolation drop\n\nSet A=T [[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]] T^(-1). Thus g_i=A x_i and\nA^2-2alpha A+sigma I=0. The positive definite matrix\n\n    Nmat=sigma I+A^T A\n\nsatisfies the exact identity\n\n    A^T Nmat+Nmat A=4alpha A^T A.                      (3)\n\nIndeed (A^T)^2 A+A^T A^2=4alpha A^T A-sigma(A+A^T), which cancels the\nother two terms. Define the quadratic energy\n\n    J(x)=[sigma||x||^2+||A x||^2]/(4alpha).\n\nFor a step x_+=x-hg with g=A x and g_+=g-hA g, (3) gives\n\n    J(x)-J(x_+)\n       =h||g||^2-h^2[sigma||g||^2+||A g||^2]/(4alpha).\n\nThe reverse ordered interpolation inequality is\n\n    f(x)-f(x_+)\n       >=h||g||^2-h^2<g,A g>+h^2||A g||^2/2.\n\nThe difference between its right side and J(x)-J(x_+) is h^2 times\n\n    [(1+2alpha)/(4alpha)]\n          ||A g-[2alpha/(1+2alpha)]g||^2\n       +[sigma/(4alpha)-alpha/(1+2alpha)]||g||^2.       (4)\n\nBy (1), sigma>=4alpha^2, so the final coefficient is nonnegative.\nThus every actual step, of arbitrary size, obeys\n\n    f_i-f_(i+1)>=J(x_i)-J(x_(i+1)).\n\nAfter summing, f_i>=0 and f_0<=<x_0,g_0>-||g_0||^2/2 imply\n\n    sigma||x_i||^2+||g_i||^2\n       >=sigma+(1+2alpha)||g_0||^2-4alpha<x_0,g_0>.    (5)\n\nNo operator-norm estimate or metric averaging has entered (3)--(5).\n\n## 3. Eliminate the physical Gram parameters\n\nFix an actual query and write P_i=u+i v. Since beta is nonzero under(1),\nput\n\n    V=-v/beta, C=u+alpha v/beta, U=u-alpha v/beta,\n    d=sigma V^2, R=|P_i|^2=UC+d,\n    t=<x_0,g_0>, Q=||g_0||^2.\n\nThen U-C=2alpha V and the exact physical representations are\n\n    x_i=U x_0-V g_0, g_i=C g_0+sigma V x_0.\n\nExpanding the left side of(5) yields\n\n    sigma(U^2+d)+(C^2+d)Q-4alpha d t.\n\nTherefore, with\n\n    A0=1-C^2-d, B0=1-U^2-d, A1=A0+2alpha,\n\ninequality(5) is equivalent to\n\n    A1 Q-4alpha(1-d)t+sigma B0<=0.                    (6)\n\nSuppose, contrary to(2), that R<1-2omega. The quadratic forms C^2+d\nand U^2+d in(u,v) each have eigenvalues1/(1+omega),1/(1-omega).\nFor example C=u+(alpha/beta)v and d=sigma v^2/beta^2, so the form's\ntrace is2/(1-omega^2) and its determinant1/(1-omega^2). It follows that\n\n    C^2+d, U^2+d <=R/(1-omega)<1.\n\nThus A0,B0,A1 are positive. Since ||x_0||=1, Q>=t^2, so(6) requires\n\n    A1 t^2-4alpha(1-d)t+sigma B0<=0.                  (7)\n\nBut direct expansion using U-C=2alpha V and UC+d=R gives\n\n    A0 B0=(1-R)^2-4omega^2 d(1-d).\n\nThe discriminant of(7), divided by4sigma, is consequently\n\n    4omega^2(1-d)^2-A1 B0\n      =4omega^2(1-d)-(1-R)^2-2alpha B0\n      <=4omega^2-(1-R)^2<0.\n\nA quadratic with positive leading coefficient and negative discriminant\nis positive for every real t, contradicting(7). This proves(2).\n\n## 4. Consequence on the actual near-critical ruler\n\nFor each m use\n\n    s=1+sqrt(2), c=s(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    b_j=a c^j, d_j=1+b_j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1.\n\nRetain H_m with its last d_0 deleted, all its actual queried data, and\nlet S_pre be its sum. There is NO sequence of fully legal single-mode\ncommon-metric prefixes with m tending to infinity for which\n\n    7/40<=Re(z_m)/|z_m|<=49/100,\n    |z_m| ->0, |z_m|S_pre ->infinity.                 (8)\n\nThis is a precise intermediate-scale legality obstruction. It neither\ndecides order-one scaled frequencies nor gives a mixed-mode theorem.\nWe prove it directly from(2) and the actual first subword at a spectral scale.\n\nSuppose(8) holds. Write r=|z_m|, omega=Re(z_m)/r and L=11/2.\nChoose the first level j with b_j r>=L omega. For all sufficiently large m\nthis level exists with j<=m-1, because\n\n    b_(m-1)/S_pre ->(s-2)/s>0.\n\nAlso j tends to infinity since r tends to0 and a stays bounded away from0.\nMinimality gives, with v=b_j r and w=v/omega,\n\n    L<=w<cL.                                          (9)\n\nThe complete prefix H_j and its endpoint occur inside the retained word:\nH_j is inside the first H_(m-1). Its polynomial is\n\n    p_j(z)=product_(ell<j)(1-d_ell z)^(2^(j-ell-1)).\n\nIf any factor vanishes, (2) is violated immediately. Otherwise applying\nlog(1+x)<=x to each squared factor gives\n\n    log|p_j(z)|^2 <=-2Re(z) S_j+|z|^2 T_j,             (10)\n\nwhere the exact first and second step sums of H_j are\n\n    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),\n    T_j=2^j-1+2a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2)\n                         +a^2(c^(2j)-2^j)/(c^2-2).\n\nBecause b_j r=v stays in a positive compact interval, r2^j tends to0.\nExplicitly, c^j=v/(ar) and\nr2^j=r(v/(ar))^(log_c 2), with log_c 2 bounded strictly below1.\nThe other omitted terms vanish as well, so(10) gives uniformly\n\n    log|p_j(z)|^2\n       <=-omega^2[2w/(c-2)-w^2/(c^2-2)]+o(1).        (11)\n\nAt the limiting c=s, the bracket is the concave quadratic\n\n    F(w)=2s w-w^2/(2s-1), L<=w<=sL.\n\nBoth endpoint values exceed18. The exact differences are\n\n    F(L)-18=(39-12s)/[4(2s-1)]>0,\n    F(sL)-18=(83-34s)/[4(2s-1)]>0,\n\nusing s<17/7. Concavity makes the bracket exceed18 throughout that compact\ninterval. Continuity in c and(9), followed by(11), therefore yields, for\nall sufficiently large m,\n\n    |p_j(z)|^2 <=exp(-17omega^2).                      (12)\n\nThe harmless o(1) is absorbed uniformly because omega>=7/40.\n\nOn the whole interval[7/40,49/100],\n\n    exp(-17omega^2)<1-2omega.                          (13)\n\nFor a fully explicit check, D(omega)=1-2omega-exp(-17omega^2) has\nD''=34exp(-17omega^2)(1-34omega^2)<0 on that interval. At7/40,\nthe exponent833/1600 exceeds1/2, so its exponential exceeds\n1+1/2+1/8=13/8>20/13. At49/100 the exponent40817/10000 exceeds4,\nand exp(4)>(8/3)^4>50, since exp(1)>8/3. Thus D is positive at both\nendpoints, and concavity proves(13). Equations(12),(13) contradict(2)\nat an ACTUAL queried endpoint. This proves the asserted impossibility(8).\n\n## 5. Scope and handoff\n\nThe amplitude barrier is metric-uniform and uses a real physical energy,\nnot a normal decomposition. The near-critical application rules out a\ndiverging mass-scaled frequency in the stated angle interval when |z|\ntends to0. It does not rule out bounded |z|S_pre, angles above49/100,\nfrequencies away from0, or joint repair between different modes.\n\nIn particular, one cannot sum(2) with mixture weights: its derivation uses\nan initial/terminal value budget for ONE operator with ONE quadratic\nminimal polynomial. A jointly legal mixture need not admit those separate\nbudgets. The new assignment requires a genuinely common cycle on the\ndirect sum; that audit is the next work item.\n\nFact search found no accepted version of this energy barrier. Matlas\nreturned arXiv2006.09946,2104.12931,1509.09102, none used here. All new\nidentities and inequalities needed for the conclusion are displayed above.\nThe complete original exact-profile theorem remains open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["eccbb0561081ef4d", "cd74f7b37ded4d52"], "master_guidance": "ea594a814f06f984", "artifact": "research/elliptic_energy_barrier_and_scale_localization.md", "status": "complete_unverified_candidate", "new_fact_submit_calls": 0, "new_numerical_evaluations": 0}, "glossary": {"Nmat": "Positive definite physical matrix |z|²I+A^T A, satisfying A^T Nmat+Nmat A=4Re(z)A^T A.", "J": "Quadratic energy (|z|²||x||²+||Ax||²)/(4Re(z)) in this candidate.", "omega": "The angular ratio Re(z)/|z| for a nonzero elliptic frequency.", "S_pre": "Mass of the actual near-critical queried prefix with the final d0 removed."}}

{"id": "e1d71a04bf4d0921", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:31:08.245199+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C40 scalable shared-coordinate obstruction: every legal fixed-split TWO-PREDECESSOR relay (a merge at every phase) on near-critical H_m, m>=65536 and N=2^k>=4096 dyadic blocks, has even FREE-terminal normalized score/K<5(201/200)exp(-N/1024)<=201/640. Hence limsup B_n score<=201/[320e]<1, and zero when N grows. Explicit real vectors, all endpoint normals, retained inherited mass, true distance and primitive defect are included. Smaller phase counts and variable splits remain open.", "evidence": "# C40. A scalable two-predecessor shared-coordinate family has a uniform loss\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No solver, sweep or fact_submit.\nThis screens an actual growing family with a merge at every phase.\nThe proved range is explicit: m>=65536 and at least4096 phases.\nSmaller phase counts, varying split fractions and longer memory are not closed.\n\n## 1. One real simplex and its entire chronological transcript\n\nPut lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), n=2^m-1.\nFix 0<=r<=m-12. Set k=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M,\nA=1+q, and\n\n    q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n    T_(N-1)=Mt, t>=1.\n\nThese are the ACTUAL N copies of H_r and all intervening checkpoints.\nAll their individual steps are retained in their actual order.\n\nChoose ONE split fraction theta in[0,1], fixed over the whole horizon.\nChoose positive R_i, let E_i=R_i^2, and define nonnegative transfers\n\n    a_01=R_1, a_02=theta R_2,\n    a_(i,i+1)=(1-theta)R_(i+1), i>=1,\n    a_(i,i+2)=theta R_(i+2), i>=1,\n\nomitting indices beyond N-1. Every vertex j>=2 receives total mass R_j\nfrom its two predecessors. Define actual vectors in R^N by\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n    C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n    F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe triangular vector matrix has positive diagonal; this is a real simplex.\nProjection variational inequalities and maximization differences give\ngrad F_C=P_C, convexity and1-smoothness.\n\nStart at R_0e_0. Each checkpoint exhausts its current coordinate and\nadds the displayed transfers to the next two coordinates. For i>=1\nthe state before phase i has exactly\n\n    R_i e_i+theta R_(i+1)e_(i+1),\n\nwith the second term absent at the terminal phase. During the ordinary\nblock of mass M-1, subtract its actual partial mass times p_i.\nThis supplies one fixed complete GD trajectory whenever its normals hold.\n\nThe overlaps are genuine. For i>=1,\n\n    <p_i,p_(i+1)>\n       =(1-theta)[-E_(i+1)+theta E_(i+2)]/(T_i T_(i+1)),\n    <p_i,p_(i+2)>=-theta E_(i+2)/(T_i T_(i+2)).\n\nThe inherited first term can be opposed by shared-child correlation;\nno orthogonality or sign assumption is imposed on the legal family.\n\n## 2. Exact ALL-normal test, including inherited mass\n\nAt ordinary partial mass u put z=(u+1)/M, so z ranges from1/M to1.\nFor i>=1, multiplying own and future normal values by M gives\n\n own:\n [(tau_i-z)E_i\n  -(1-theta)(theta tau_i+z(1-theta))E_(i+1)\n  -z theta^2 E_(i+2)]/tau_i^2;                            (1)\n\n vertex i+1:\n [(theta+z(1-theta)/tau_i)E_(i+1)\n        -z theta(1-theta)E_(i+2)/tau_i]/tau_(i+1);          (2)\n\n vertex i+2:\n z theta E_(i+2)/(tau_i tau_(i+2)).                        (3)\n\nMissing terms at the end are zero and tau_(N-1)=t. All later normal\nvalues are zero. Every past normal is nonpositive because its positive\ncoordinate has been exhausted and the proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nThe zero vertex still requires the own normal to be nonnegative.\n\nThus (1)>=(2),(3),0 at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is a necessary and sufficient\ntest against every vertex at every actual query: the differences are affine.\nAt the root, compute the same two endpoints with\np_0=(R_0e_0-R_1e_1-theta R_2e_2)/(MA).\nAt the terminal phase, t>=1 suffices. No old coordinate term is discarded.\n\nThe class is nonempty for each theta and finite schedule: set E_(N-1)=1\nand work backward, choosing E_i sufficiently large to meet the finitely\nmany endpoint inequalities. Their own E_i coefficient is positive\n(tau_i-z>=q_i>0), and all other terms depend only on already fixed future\nenergies. Then choose E_0 by its two endpoint tests. This is a finite\nsymbolic construction of positive parameters; no optimization assumption\nis needed for the upper bounds below.\n\nThe minimizer set is C^polar. Backward induction in <p_i,z><=0 forces\nall coordinates nonpositive; hence0 is the actual closest minimizer to\nR_0e_0. The original objective and primitive defect, with htot the total\nschedule mass, are exactly\n\n    score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0),\n    V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.         (4)\n\nFor the second identity use\nF_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and telescope\n2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2.\n\n## 3. Two different normal constraints bound each low/high pair\n\nThe direct ruler identities give\n\n    2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16, M_m:=1+sum H_m<=M c^k,\n    cq>=ac=1-1/m>=99/100.                                (5)\n\nFor example M_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j with0<a<c-2,\nso a<=q<=c-2; the rational bounds follow from lambda<169/70 and\na>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5.\n\nConsider an internal even index i, 2<=i<=N-4. It is a low checkpoint q_i=q.\nLet Q=q_(i+1)>=99/100, so tau_i=tau_(i+2)=A and tau_(i+1)=1+Q.\nThe endpoint comparison (1)>=(2) gives\n\n q E_i >= (1+theta q)(1-theta+A/(1+Q))E_(i+1)\n             +theta[theta-(1-theta)A/(1+Q)]E_(i+2).       (6)\n\nThe HIGH phase's normal against zero gives\nQ E_(i+1)>=(1-theta)(1+theta Q)E_(i+2), after dropping its other\nnonnegative cost. Inserting this lower bound into (6) yields\n\n    E_(i+2)/E_i <= qQ/F(q,Q,theta),\n\nwhere\n F=(1-theta)^2(1+theta q)(1+theta Q)+Q theta^2\n      +(1-theta)(1+q)/(1+Q)[1+theta q+theta^2 qQ].         (7)\n\nIndependently, the LOW comparison (1)>=(3), retaining just E_(i+2),\ngives E_(i+2)/E_i<=q/[theta(1+theta)], with the latter cap infinite\nat theta=0. Together,\n\n    E_(i+2)/E_i <= qQ/L(q,Q,theta),\n    L=max(F(q,Q,theta), Q theta(1+theta)).                 (8)\n\nBoth comparisons are to actual future vertices retained in the SAME\npolytope. Their distinct costs are essential.\n\nAt the initial pair, the root endpoint against vertex2 gives\nq E_0>=E_1+theta(1+theta)E_2.\nThe phase1 zero comparison gives\nQ E_1>=(1-theta)(1+theta Q)E_2, Q=cq.\nCombining and using Q>=1/2 proves the simpler bound\n\n    E_2/E_0 <=qQ/[1-theta+2Q theta] <=qQ.                 (9)\n\n## 4. An explicit uniform loss at every level-one high checkpoint\n\nWe prove\n\n    L(q,Q,theta)>=1                       for Q>=99/100,\n    L(q,Q,theta)>=kappa:=201/200           for\n                                  99/100<=Q<=273/256.   (10)\n\nIf theta>=2/3 the second entry of the maximum is at least\n(99/100)(10/9)=11/10, proving both statements.\nSuppose 0<=theta<=2/3. Formula(7) increases in q, so replace q by2/5.\nWrite\n F(2/5,Q,theta)=l Q+d+e/(1+Q), where\n\n l=theta-(3/5)theta^2+(1/5)theta^3+(2/5)theta^4,\n d=1-(8/5)theta+(19/25)theta^2-(4/25)theta^3,\n e=7/5-(21/25)theta-(28/25)theta^2+(14/25)theta^3.\n\nLet P(Q)=(1+Q)(F-1)=l Q^2+(l+d-1)Q+d-1+e.\nFor Q>=99/100,\n P'(Q)>=(3l+d-1)-l/50>= (41/125)theta>=0.\nIndeed 3l+d-1=(7/5)theta-(26/25)theta^2\n +(11/25)theta^3+(6/5)theta^4 >=(9/25)theta,\nand l<=(8/5)theta.\n\nNext\n P(1)=7/5-(51/25)theta-(4/5)theta^2\n                      +(16/25)theta^3+(4/5)theta^4.\nIt decreases on[0,2/3]: its derivative is at most\n-51/25+(48/25)(4/9)+(16/5)(8/27)<0.\nIts value at2/3 is13/405. Also\n3l+d-1<=878/675 on this interval. Therefore\n\n P(99/100)>=P(1)-(3l+d-1)/100\n            >=13/405-878/67500=1933/101250>0.\n\nThis proves F>1 for all Q>=99/100. If Q<=273/256, it also gives\n\n    F-1>= (1933/101250)(256/529)>1/200.\n\nThis proves (10) for every theta, with no numerical search.\n\n## 5. Entire-energy product and the terminal sample\n\nAmong the N/2-1 consecutive low/high pairs from phase0 through N-3,\nthere are N/4 level-one highs. The initial pair uses (9); all other\npairs use(8). Thus N/4-1 pairs pay the factor kappa, and all others\npay at least1. Counting the ruler levels gives\n\n    E_(N-2)/E_0\n       <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/\n                          [q kappa^(N/4-1)].             (11)\n\nNow FREE the terminal gradient and value, retaining every actual queried\njet and the optimum(0,0,0), as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every original p_i is actually queried. The interpolation\ninequality with any new minimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0, because its queried\nvalue is <x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. Thus the actual new closest distance\nremains R_0. The last step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at its retained\nlast query implies\n\n    2f(x_n)/E_0 <=\n     [2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2t^2 E_0)\n                    <2E_(N-1)/(Mt E_0),                 (12)\n\nsince M>=2+a gives 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\n\nThe final low phase i=N-2 compared with the terminal vertex gives exactly\n\n    q E_i >=(1+theta q)[(1-theta)+A/t]E_(N-1).\n\nHence E_(N-1)/t<=q E_i/A. Multiplying(11),(12) and\nK^(-1)=2M_m-1<2Mc^k yields\n\n    [2f(x_n)/dist(x_init,argmin f)^2]/K\n        <(4/A)(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).                  (13)\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal p_(N-1) is unqueried, so that normal may NOT\nconstrain the new minimizer. The retained initial gradient instead gives\ndist(x_init,argmin f)^2>=E_0/A^2. At the last query the position is\nR_i e_i+theta R_(N-1)e_(N-1), and\np_i=[R_i e_i-(1-theta)R_(N-1)e_(N-1)]/A.\nOne smoothness step gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))E_i\n   +[-2theta(1-theta)/A\n      +(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-theta)^2]E_(N-1)\n          <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive because A<23/16. Equations(11) and the\ndistance lower bound now give\n\n    [2f(x_n)/dist(x_init,argmin f)^2]/K\n       <2(2+q)(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).                 (14)\n\nThus a uniform constant5 bounds the prefactors in BOTH cases. Every old\nquery remains in the completion class; (12)-(14) use only necessary\nsmoothness bounds, not an unproved attainment assertion.\n\n## 6. Quantitative horizon estimate and actual benchmark comparison\n\nFor m>=65536,\n\n c(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2\n          <1+8/m<=1+1/8192.\n\nConsequently qc<=1+1/8192. A binomial expansion gives\n(1+1/8192)^4<1+1/1024; dividing by kappa=201/200 gives\n\n    (qc)^4/kappa <255/256.\n\nUsing (13)-(14) and N>=4096,\n\n    score_free/K\n       <5 kappa (255/256)^(N/4)\n       <=5 kappa exp(-N/1024)\n       <=201/640<1.                                     (15)\n\nHere score_free denotes the actual normalized score of ANY free terminal\ncompletion of ANY legal original family member. The inequalities\nlog(1-v)<=-v and exp(4)>16 justify the last two steps.\nIf the number N of phases tends to infinity, (15) tends to zero uniformly.\n\nFor an explicit comparison to the TRUE benchmark, put rho=log_2(lambda).\nAccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d supplies the exact primitive and dominant\nrecursions, with joins\nOmega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2 and\nGamma(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\nThey imply\n\n    C_n:=1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho,  B_n<=1+4n^rho.         (16)\n\nHere is a direct derivation, included rather than borrowing an unverified\npower estimate. Let F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. Differentiating\nlog F(exp z) twice gives\nrho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\nConvexity at z,-z yields F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=2. Substituting\nA=t^rho B shows\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\nInduction in the exact primitive recursion gives the first bound in(16).\nSince Gamma(A,D)<=4A+D, induction at every dominant split gives the\nsecond, using (x+y)^rho>=x^rho+y^rho for nonnegative x,y.\n\nFinally\n M_m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m[\n   a/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))(2/c)^m] ->exp(1).\nThus K B_(2^m-1) has limit superior at most2/exp(1).\nCombining with(15) yields, for ANY choices of r,theta,energies and free\ncompletion in the proved range as m grows,\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) score_free <=201/[320 exp(1)]<1.      (17)\n\nIf N tends to infinity, this limit superior is zero.\nThis is a quantitative whole-class obstruction on the actual near-critical\nword, including the exact benchmark and actual initial-distance cost.\n\n## 7. Precise scope of the next unknown\n\nThe same theta is used at every merge within each horizon, but it may depend\narbitrarily on m and r. The theorem includes all energy choices and every\nlegal terminal completion. No negative primitive sign is used in its place.\n\nThe proof does NOT cover fewer than4096 phases (except the separate complete\nfour-phase result), phase-dependent splitting fractions, transfers to more\ndistant old/future coordinates, signed coordinate masses or repeated use of\nan exhausted coordinate. Those changes alter (6)-(8), rather than merely\nadding more fresh children.
