{"id": "64333922c8f1a563", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:39:28.510572+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C41 enlarges C40 to TWO independent alternating merge fractions and closes the full scalable class: m>=512,N>=256, every legal real two-predecessor flow and EVERY free terminal completion have score/K<(45/8)exp(-N/80)<45/64; limsup B_n score<=45/(32e)<1. Both high-phase future normals are essential. Fully varying splits, shorter phase counts and longer memory remain open.", "evidence": "# C41. Two independent alternating merge fractions: complete scalable obstruction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier, numerical solver or sweep.\nThis strictly enlarges the fixed-split construction and improves the explicit\nrange to m>=512 and N>=256 phases. Every required argument is included here;\nearlier unverified findings are motivation only.\n\n## 1. The fixed real family and all normal comparisons\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Choose 0<=r<=m-8, let k=m-r,\nN=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, and\n\n    A=1+q,\n    q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n    tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nThe N ordinary blocks are exactly the actual copies of H_r; their\ncheckpoints have excesses Mq_i. All original physical steps are retained.\n\nChoose independently u,v in[0,1]. Define theta_j=u for even j and theta_j=v\nfor odd j. Choose positive R_i, E_i=R_i^2, and transfers\n\n    a_01=R_1, a_02=u R_2,\n    a_(i,i+1)=(1-theta_(i+1))R_(i+1), i>=1,\n    a_(i,i+2)=theta_(i+2)R_(i+2), i>=1,\n\nomitting out-of-range indices. Every j>=2 receives total mass R_j\nfrom its two actual predecessors. Put\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n    C=conv(0,p_i), F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe p_i are actual independent vectors in R^N with triangular positive\ndiagonal, not a formal covariance matrix. The projection variational\ninequality and maximizing-difference bounds prove\ngrad F_C=P_C, convexity and1-smoothness.\n\nStart at R_0e_0. Before phase i>=1 its only nonzero coordinates are\nR_i e_i+theta_(i+1)R_(i+1)e_(i+1); subtract each actual step times p_i.\nThe checkpoint exhausts coordinate i and creates the next prescribed state.\n\nHere is the entire legality test. During phase i>=1 put\nz=(physical partial ordinary mass+1)/M in[1/M,1],\nd=theta_(i+1), e=theta_(i+2).\nMultiply all normal values by M. The own value is\n\n [(tau_i-z)E_i\n  -(1-d)(d tau_i+z(1-d))E_(i+1)\n  -z e^2 E_(i+2)]/tau_i^2.                               (1)\n\nThe only possibly nonzero FUTURE comparisons are\n\n [(d+z(1-d)/tau_i)E_(i+1)\n           -z e(1-e)E_(i+2)/tau_i]/tau_(i+1),            (2)\n z e E_(i+2)/(tau_i tau_(i+2)).                           (3)\n\nEvery past normal is nonpositive because its positive coordinate has\nbeen exhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nAll other future normals are zero; the zero vertex must also be tested.\nTherefore (1)>=(2),(3),0 at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is necessary and sufficient\nfor every actual query. At the root use its displayed p_0 to test the two\nendpoints directly. The terminal block requires t>=1.\nAll omitted final terms are zero.\n\nThe class is nonempty for every u,v and finite word: fix terminal energy1,\nthen choose E_i successively backward sufficiently large to satisfy these\nfinite inequalities. Its own coefficient tau_i-z>=q_i>0 and every\nother term uses already fixed future energies. Finally choose E_0.\nThis is a constructive finite specification; the upper bound covers EVERY\nlegal energy choice, not just this conservative policy.\n\nEvery original query has F_C(x_i)=<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2.\nThe minimizer set C^polar has all coordinates nonpositive, by backward\ninduction in <p_i,z><=0. Thus0 is the actual closest minimizer and its\ninitial squared distance is E_0. The original objective and primitive\ndefect, with htot the entire physical mass, are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (4)\n\nThe defect follows by telescoping the identity\n2h_iF_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2.\n\n## 2. Combine BOTH high-phase future comparisons\n\nConsider an internal low/high pair i,i+1, where i is even and2<=i<=N-4.\nPut Q=q_(i+1), B=1+Q and rho=A/B.\nThe low phase has d=v,e=u. Its comparisons with i+1 and i+2 give\n\n q E_i >= L1 E_(i+1)+L2 E_(i+2),\n    L1=(1+vq)(1-v+rho), L2=u[u-(1-u)rho],                 (5)\n E_(i+2)/E_i <= q/[u(1+u)],                              (6)\n\nwith (6) read as no restriction at u=0.\n\nThe HIGH phase has d=u,e=v. Its two future comparisons are\n\n Q E_(i+1) >= H1 E_(i+2)+H2 E_(i+3),\n    H1=(1+uQ)(1-u+B/A),\n    H2=v[v-(1-v)B/A],                                   (7)\n Q E_(i+1) >= H3 E_(i+2)+H4 E_(i+3),\n    H3=(1-u)(1+uQ),\n    H4=v[v+B/Bnext],                                    (8)\n\nwhere Bnext=tau_(i+3)>0 is the actual next high capacity, or terminal t.\nNo future residual has been reset.\n\nIf H2>=0, dropping its nonnegative term in(7) gives coefficient H1.\nIf H2<0, then0<v<1 and H4>0. Use(8) to upper-bound E_(i+3), multiply\nthat upper bound by H2<0, and insert it into(7). This gives\n\n Q E_(i+1) >= [(H1 H4-H2 H3)/(H4-H2)]E_(i+2).\n\nThe coefficient is exactly\n\n    (1+uQ)[1-u+(v Bnext+B)/(A+(1-v)Bnext)].\n\nThe ratio here lies between B/A and v/(1-v), because it is a ratio of\npositive affine functions of Bnext. At v=0, (7) has H2=0 and gives H1\ndirectly; v=1 belongs to the nonnegative case. Thus in ALL cases,\n\n Q E_(i+1) >=\n    (1+uQ)[1-u+min(B/A,v/(1-v))]E_(i+2),                 (9)\n\nwith v/(1-v)=+infinity at v=1. This is the inherited-coordinate cost\nmissed by using only the high zero comparison.\n\nCombine(5),(9),(6). The exact necessary pair bound is\n\n    E_(i+2)/E_i <= qQ / max(G, Q u(1+u)),                 (10)\n\nwhere\n\n G=(1+vq)(1-v+rho)(1+uQ)\n                       [1-u+min(1/rho,v/(1-v))]\n       +Q u[u-(1-u)rho].                                (11)\n\nThe denominator in(10) is shown positive next, so no sign assumption\nis hidden in taking its reciprocal.\n\n## 3. The scalar comparison: two splits still force a loss\n\nDefine G0=(1-u)(1+rho)+Qu. We prove\n\n    G>=min(G0,A).                                        (12)\n\nWhen v<=1/(1+rho), the minimum in(11) is v/(1-v).\nSet w=1-v. Then\n\n G=(A-qw)(1+rho/w)(1-uw)(1+uQ)\n                         +Qu^2-Qrho u(1-u).\n\nBoth nonnegative factors (A-qw)(1+rho/w) and1-uw increase as v increases:\nthe first has w derivative -q-A rho/w^2<0, and the second has w\nderivative -u<=0. Therefore its minimum is at v=0, where direct\ncancellation gives G=G0.\n\nFor v>=1/(1+rho), the minimum is1/rho. The only v-dependent factor\nis (1+qv)(1-v+rho), a concave quadratic. Its minimum on this interval\nis at an endpoint. At the lower endpoint the preceding result gives G>=G0.\nAt v=1, expansion gives\n\n G=A(1+uQ)+Qu^2+rho(1-u)[A+qQu] >=A.\n\nThis proves(12), including all endpoints u,v=0,1.\n\nThe ruler parameters obey, for m>=512,\n\n    2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16, Q>=cq>=1-1/m>=99/100.      (13)\n\nIndeed M_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j, with0<a<c-2, gives\na<=q<=c-2 and M_m<=M c^k. The rational bounds follow from\nlambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5.\n\nIf Q>=1, G0 is a convex combination of1+rho and Q, so G0>=1;\nalso A>1. If99/100<=Q<1, then rho=A/(1+Q)>7/10.\nFor u<=3/4, G0>=99/100+(1/4)(1/100+7/10)>1.\nFor u>=3/4, Q u(1+u)>=2079/1600>1.\nThus\n\n    max(G,Q u(1+u))>=1 at EVERY high checkpoint.           (14)\n\nAt a LEVEL-ONE high checkpoint Q=cq<=273/256,\nrho>=1792/2645>2/3. If u<=3/4,\n\n    G0>=99/100+(1/4)(1/100+2/3)\n         =1391/1200>9/8,\n\nand A>7/5>9/8. If u>=3/4 the second term is again at least2079/1600.\nHence, uniformly in BOTH independent splits,\n\n    max(G,Q u(1+u))>=kappa:=9/8 at every level-one high.  (15)\n\n## 4. Root and terminal costs, with no forgotten minimizer change\n\nFor the first pair, the root endpoint against vertex2 gives\nqE_0>=E_1+u(u+1)E_2.\nThe high phase's zero comparison gives\nQ E_1>=(1-u)(1+uQ)E_2, Q=cq.\nConsequently\n\n    E_2/E_0<=qQ/[1-u+2Qu]<=qQ.                           (16)\n\nThere are N/4 level-one highs in the whole word. The initial one uses\n(16); all N/4-1 remaining ones pay kappa. Other pairs pay at least1.\nCounting the exact ruler levels therefore yields\n\n    E_(N-2)/E_0 <= (qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/\n                                  [q kappa^(N/4-1)].    (17)\n\nNow retain ALL original queried jets and the optimum(0,0,0), and let f\nbe ANY convex1-smooth terminal completion, as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nWhen r>=1, every original p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to a\nnew minimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0 from its queried value\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. Their triangular inequalities force all coordinates\nof z nonpositive;0 remains the true closest minimizer, with distance R_0.\n\nThe last step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at that actual retained query gives\n\n    2f(x_n)/E_0 <=\n     [2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n                     <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a gives 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nThe last low phase has incoming fraction v and obeys\n\n    qE_(N-2)>=(1+vq)[1-v+A/t]E_(N-1).\n\nThus E_(N-1)/t<=qE_(N-2)/A. Put K=1/(2M_m-1) and use M_m<=Mc^k\nwith(17). This proves\n\n    score_free/K <(4/A)(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).          (18)\n\nWhen r=0, the old terminal normal is unqueried and must not constrain the\nnew minimum. The retained initial gradient instead gives\ndist(x_init,argmin f)^2>=E_0/A^2.\nAt the last query the position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(N-1)e_(N-1) and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(N-1)e_(N-1)]/A.\nOne smoothness step bounds2f(x_n) by\n\n (1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(N-1)\n       <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive because A<23/16.\nCombining the TRUE distance lower bound and(17) gives\n\n    score_free/K <2(2+q)(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).         (19)\n\nA uniform constant5 bounds both prefactors. These are necessary bounds\non every full completion, not assertions that the smoothness cap is attained.\n\n## 5. Explicit horizon bounds and the actual B_n comparison\n\nFor m>=512,\n\n    qc<=c(c-2)\n      =1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2\n      <1+8/m<=65/64.\n\nLet z0=65/64>1. The exact\nrational inequality\n\n    z0^4/(9/8)=142805000/150994944<19/20\n\nand(18)-(19) yield\n\n    score_free/K <5(9/8)(19/20)^(N/4)\n                 <= (45/8)exp(-N/80).                    (20)\n\nFor N>=256, N/80>3 and exp(3)>8, so\n\n    score_free/K <45/64<1.                               (21)\n\nIf N grows, this ratio tends to0 uniformly in the choices of r,u,v,\nall positive energies, terminal parameter t, and full completion.\n\nThe comparison with the TRUE benchmark can be proved from its accepted\nrecursions, rather than assuming an asymptotic constant. Put rho_s=log_2 lambda.\nFor F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1, the second derivative of log F(exp w) is\nrho_s z(1+z)^(rho_s-2)[(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s z]/F(z)^2>0.\nConvexity at w and-w gives F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2.\nConsequently the accepted primitive join obeys\nOmega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\nInduction in b98bb807d514e80d gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s.\nIts dominant join is at most4X+D, so its exact recursion gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\n\nThe explicit mass formula above gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), hence\nlimsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1). Therefore every family in the stated range has\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) score_free <=45/[32 exp(1)]<1,        (22)\n\nand the limit superior is zero when N tends to infinity.\nThe original objective, primitive defect and normalization were all tracked;\nnone was inferred from a sign-only witness.\n\n## 6. Scope\n\nThe two split fractions may depend arbitrarily on m and r, but remain fixed\non their respective parities within a horizon. Every later coordinate\nmerges two real branches, and the full old-query test(1)-(3) includes all\ninherited residuals. The endpoint is completely free after retaining the\nqueried prefix and the optimum.\n\nThis closes the entire specified alternating-split family for m>=512,\nN>=256. It does not cover fewer phases, splits changing independently\nat each coordinate, longer shared-coordinate memory, signed masses, or\nrevisiting an exhausted coordinate. It is neither an unrestricted objective\nupper certificate nor a proof of the original exact-profile target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["168b2b5bc5c80024", "e1d71a04bf4d0921", "209666ace998cb98"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "research/c41_alternating_split_merge_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"u_split": "Incoming two-back transfer fraction on even coordinate indices.", "v_split": "Incoming two-back transfer fraction on odd coordinate indices.", "G_pair": "Combined necessary low/high pair coefficient in equation(11).", "kappa_pair": "Uniform factor9/8 paid at each internal level-one high checkpoint.", "Bnext": "Actual capacity of the next high phase, or the terminal parameter t; retained during elimination.", "score_free": "Actual closest-distance normalized objective of any smooth-convex completion retaining all queried jets and the optimum."}}

Definite 2m-mode logarithmic ansatz z_(j,l)=[t_l/(1000d_j)](t_l+i sqrt(1-t_l^2)), t_1=1/32,t_2=1/64, has a whole-ansatz benchmark ratio limsup<=lambda^2/(16e)<3/20, despite arbitrary joint values/weights and free terminal completion. This is a complete scoped obstruction and construction, not all-normal closure or a sharp benchmark witness.", "evidence": "# A genuinely joint logarithmic-spectrum obstruction on the actual word\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate, without a new fact_submit allocation.\nAll new estimates are proved below. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the\ninterpolation convention and free terminal completion; accepteddfde2fa326c908bf\nsupplies the algebraic complex-cycle identity; acceptedb98bb807d514e80d\nsupplies only the exact benchmark recurrences. No unverified spectral\nexclusion is used as a premise.\n\n## 1. Actual schedule, finite ansatz, and uniform result\n\nLet m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1, and D=d_(m-1).\nThe parameters c,a are fixed at this horizon. Use the retained prefix\nH_m^- obtained by removing the ORIGINAL last d_0 step. Its last query\nindex is q=n-1. Let P_i(z) be the chronological prefix polynomial, 0<=i<=q.\n\nHere is one definite finite probability ansatz. Set angular parameters\nt_1=1/32 and t_2=1/64, and, for 0<=j<m and ell in{1,2}, put\n\n    r_(j,ell)=t_ell/(1000d_j),\n    z_(j,ell)=r_(j,ell)[t_ell+i sqrt(1-t_ell^2)].       (1)\n\nChoose ANY nonnegative weights w_(j,ell) summing to1 and form ACTUAL\nreal vectors in R^(4m): the two coordinates in block(j,ell) are\n\n    x_i^(j,ell)=sqrt(w_(j,ell))(Re P_i(z_(j,ell)),Im P_i(z_(j,ell))),\n    g_i^(j,ell)=sqrt(w_(j,ell))(Re[z_(j,ell)P_i(z_(j,ell))],\n                              Im[z_(j,ell)P_i(z_(j,ell))]).    (2)\n\nThus ||x_0||=1 and the actual queried recursion is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\nInclude the optimum jet(0,0,0). Values f_i are ARBITRARY JOINTLY legal\nvalues: all ordered interpolation inequalities must hold for these same\nvectors. Individual component legality and separability are NOT assumed.\n\nFor EVERY legal choice of weights and values, every interpolant F of\nthe whole queried prefix, and EVERY b in[0,2], the result is\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q) <= 1/(64D).                           (3)\n\nIn particular this includes the original b=d_0, since 1<d_0<3/2, and\nthe maximal free completion of accepted1252b8823bb5a290. No constraint\nis placed on the new terminal gradient. The bound holds uniformly in\nthe weights, dimension, and values, not merely mode by mode.\n\nMore generally (3) follows from the following whole-spectrum statement.\nFix any 0<t_max<=1/2. Allow ANY finite list of complex numbers z with\n\n    r=|z|>0, t=Re(z)/r in(0,t_max], r<=t/(1000d_0),     (4)\n\nand optionally z=0, with arbitrary probability weights and the vectors(2).\nAll jointly legal such prefixes obey\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)<=16t_max^2/D, b in[0,2].             (5)\n\nBoth signs of the imaginary part are allowed in (4). Formula(1) is a\nspecific finite family contained in it, with two different angular types\nand all logarithmic physical scales d_j. The theorem does not include\nall normal spectra, all common metrics, or unrestricted W_n.\n\n## 2. Elementary uniform schedule estimates\n\nFor m>=128,\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, a>1/3, a/(c-2)<1.\n\nIndeed lambda>12/5 follows from sqrt(2)>7/5; lambda<5/2 follows from\nsqrt(2)<3/2, and its actual value also gives lambda(129/128)<5/2\n(squaring sqrt(2)<191/129 proves the last assertion). The a bound follows\nfrom (127/128)/(5/2)>1/3. Since lambda(lambda-2)=1, c(c-2)>1 and\na/(c-2)=(1-1/m)/[c(c-2)]<1.\n\nLet S_k and T_k be the sum and squared-step sum of full H_k. Counting\nmultiplicities gives\n\n    S_k=2^k-1+a(c^k-2^k)/(c-2),\n    T_k=(2^k-1)+2a(c^k-2^k)/(c-2)\n                             +a^2(c^(2k)-2^k)/(c^2-2).\n\nThe positivity of 1-a/(c-2) implies\n\n    S_k >= [d_k-(c-1)]/(c-2).\n\nAlso 2^k<=c^k, 1/a<3 and 1/(c-2)<5/2 imply S_k<6d_k.\nConsequently, whenever d_k>=2000,\n\n    S_k>=(19/10)d_k,\n    T_k<=d_k^2/(c^2-2)+2S_k\n         <=[25/94+12/2000]d_k^2 <(7/25)d_k^2.          (6)\n\nThese estimates have constants independent of m. The largest physical\nstep D=d_(m-1)>2000 follows already from a>1/3,c>2,m>=128.\n\n## 3. One common indexed cycle and exact algebra\n\nFor a single parameter z=alpha+i beta define sigma=|z|^2,\nkappa=alpha-sigma, and eta=sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2. Under(4),\n\n    alpha>0, kappa>0, eta>=sigma(1-2t^2)>0.             (7)\n\nHere sigma/alpha=r/t<=1/(1000d_0). Define the full-block polynomial p_k,\nand define R_k=p_k(1-d_k z), so p_(k+1)=R_k p_k. Define\n\n    L_k=sum_(i in H_k) h_i |P_i(z)|^2,\n    E_k=sum_(i in H_k) h_i^2 sigma |P_i(z)|^2.\n\nAll P_i in these two sums start locally at1. Let Dcal_k be the weight\nof the directed interpolation cycle from the optimum to the end of H_k,\nbackwards along its queries to the start, then to the optimum. The\ncomplex cycle identity in accepteddfde2fa326c908bf gives EXACTLY\n\n    Dcal_k=beta^2 L_k-(kappa/2)(1+|p_k|^2+E_k).         (8)\n\nThis is an algebraic expression for a cycle weight, not an assumption\nthat this mode is individually legal. Telescoping squared norms gives\n1-|p_k|^2=2alpha L_k-E_k. Hence also\n\n    Dcal_k=[eta/(2alpha)](1+E_k)\n               -[sigma(1-alpha)/(2alpha)]|p_k|^2.       (9)\n\nThe actual word recursion gives\nE_(k+1)=(1+|R_k|^2)E_k+d_k^2 sigma |p_k|^2. Substitute this and\np_(k+1)=R_k p_k into(9); use\nd_k^2 sigma |p_k|^2-|R_k|^2=(2d_k alpha-1)|p_k|^2 and\n[sigma(1-alpha)-eta]/(2alpha)=kappa. The result is\n\n    Dcal_(k+1)=(1+|R_k|^2)Dcal_k\n                        +|p_k|^2(kappa+d_k eta).       (10)\n\nWrite Dcal_k^- for the SAME optimum/reverse-path cycle after removing\nthe last d_0 step from H_k. Directly subtracting that last reverse edge\nand changing the optimum-to-end edge gives\n\n    Dcal_k-Dcal_k^-\n      =d_0 sigma[1-alpha-d_0 kappa]|p_k^-|^2.          (11)\n\nFor clarity, the removed reverse edge has weight\nd_0 sigma(1-d_0 alpha+d_0 sigma/2)|p_k^-|^2, and the change in the\noptimum edge is (sigma/2)(|p_k|^2-|p_k^-|^2). Their sum is (11).\n\nUse the last H_(m-1)^- copy in the ACTUAL retained prefix H_m^-.\nIts starting polynomial is R_(m-1), and its ending polynomial is\nP_q=R_(m-1)p_(m-1)^-. Therefore the SAME indexed cycle on the JOINT\nreal data has weight\n\n    sum_z w_z |R_(m-1)(z)|^2 Dcal_(m-1)^-(z) <=0.      (12)\n\nThe inequality follows by summing the actual interpolation constraints\non that one cycle. No index or cycle in(12) depends on the mode.\n\n## 4. Modes whose contraction threshold is reached\n\nThe contraction threshold for a mode is h_c=2alpha/sigma=2t/r.\nIt is at least2000d_0, by(4). Suppose D>=h_c and let k<=m-1 be the\nfirst level with d_k>=h_c. Then k>=1, all steps of H_k strictly contract\nthe modulus, d_k>=2000, and, since d_k<c d_(k-1),\n\n    v=d_k r lies in[2t,5t].\n\nFor a nonzero factor, log|1-hz|^2<=-2alpha h+sigma h^2. No factor is\nzero because t<=1/2 forces beta!=0. Using(6),\n\n    log|p_k|^2 <=-(19/5)t v+(7/25)v^2 <=-6t^2.         (13)\n\nFor the second inequality put v=t u. The concave function\n19u/5-7u^2/25 has endpoint values162/25 and12 on[2,5], both at least6.\n\nThe reverse ordered edge at a step h, starting at local position P_i, is\n\n    h sigma(1-h alpha+h sigma/2)|P_i|^2\n      =[sigma/(2alpha)](|P_i|^2-|P_(i+1)|^2)\n                       +[h^2 sigma eta/(2alpha)]|P_i|^2.\n\nThe two optimum edges add (sigma/2)|p_k|^2-alpha+sigma/2.\nBecause eta>0, summing and discarding nonnegative terms gives\n\n    Dcal_k >= [sigma/(2alpha)](1-|p_k|^2)-alpha\n      >=alpha[(1-exp(-6t^2))/(2t^2)-1] >alpha/2.       (14)\n\nIndeed (1-exp(-6s))/s decreases for s>0, since exp(6s)>=1+6s.\nHere t^2<=1/4, and exp(3/2)>1+3/2+9/8+27/48>4, so the bracket at\nt^2=1/4 is 2(1-exp(-3/2))-1>1/2.\n\nBy(7),(10), Dcal_(u+1)>=|R_u|^2 Dcal_u once Dcal_u is positive.\nAlso |p_k|<=1. Thus propagation to u=m-1 gives\n\n    Dcal_(m-1)>=(alpha/2)|p_(m-1)|^2.                 (15)\n\nThis conclusion was obtained for every parameter with a reached\nthreshold, without applying interpolation separately to that parameter.\n\nTo remove the final step, note that d_0 alpha<=t^2/1000<=1/4000 and\nd_0 sigma<=alpha/1000. Therefore |1-d_0 z|^2>=1999/2000, and (11),(15)\nimply\n\n    Dcal_(m-1)^-\n      >=alpha[1999/4000-1/1000]|p_(m-1)^-|^2\n      >=(2alpha/5)|p_(m-1)^-|^2.                      (16)\n\nMultiplying by |R_(m-1)|^2 proves that the contribution of such a mode\nto the common cycle(12) is at least (2alpha/5)|P_q|^2.\n\n## 5. All possible negative credit on that same cycle\n\nThe remaining nonzero modes have D<h_c. All factors throughout H_m\nthen contract, so |P_i|<=1, |R_(m-1)|<=1, and\n\n    alpha<2t^2/D<=2t_max^2/D.\n\nFor the last truncated H_(m-1) block, E_(m-1)^-<=sigma T_(m-1).\nUsing(6) at d_(m-1)=D and rD<2t gives\n\n    E_(m-1)^- <=(28/25)t^2<=7/25.\n\nApply(8) to the truncated word. Its negative part is at most\n\n    -Dcal_(m-1)^- <=kappa[1+E_(m-1)^-/2]\n                    <=(6/5)alpha.\n\nConsequently each such mode supplies at most(12/5)t_max^2/D negative\ncredit to(12). Their total probability is at most1, so the same bound\nholds for their TOTAL credit. The zero mode supplies zero credit.\n\nSince all reached-threshold modes have the positive bound(16), joint\nlegality(12) implies\n\n    sum_(reached) w_z alpha |P_q(z)|^2\n                         <=6t_max^2/D.                (17)\n\nFor the remaining modes, sum w_z alpha |P_q|^2<=2t_max^2/D. Add this\nto(17) to obtain the single common moment bound\n\n    2sum_z w_z alpha |P_q(z)|^2<=16t_max^2/D.           (18)\n\nThis is where joint repair is explicitly paid for. Reached-threshold\nmodes may be individually illegal and need not have zero weight.\n\n## 6. Actual free terminal step and the exact benchmark\n\nFor ANY legal values on the prefix, the optimum inequality at its last\nquery gives 2f_q<=2<x_q,g_q>-||g_q||^2. Smoothness at a final b in[0,2]\ngives 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=2f_q-b(2-b)||g_q||^2. Therefore\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)\n      <=sum_z w_z [2alpha-(1+b(2-b))sigma]|P_q(z)|^2\n      <=2sum_z w_z alpha|P_q(z)|^2.\n\nThis proves(5), then(3) with t_max=1/32. All queried anchors, including\nthe optimum, remain present. The only normalization is sum w_z=1,\nwhich gives ||x_0||=1 exactly. There is no conditioning or dimension loss.\n\nFor completeness derive the needed exact-profile comparison from the\naccepted recursions. Put rho=log_2 lambda and C_k=S_circle(k)+1. Their\nprimitive and dominant join maps are\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nLet V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For t=exp(u), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/du^2 log V(exp(u))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.\n\nConvexity at u,-u implies V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. For A,B>0, put\nt=(A/B)^(1/rho) and M=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho. The last inequality\nis (M-A)(M-B)>=2AB; hence Omega(A,B)<=M. Continuity covers zero\narguments. Induction in the primitive recurrence gives C_k<=(k+1)^rho.\nSince G(A,B)<=4A+B, the dominant recurrence and superadditivity of\nt^rho on nonnegative arguments give B_k<=1+4k^rho.\n\nFinally D=1+(1-1/m)c^(m-2), so\n\n    D/lambda^m -> e/lambda^2.\n\nAt n=2^m-1, (3) gives the UNIFORM comparison\n\n    limsup_m B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q) <=lambda^2/(16e)<3/20.   (19)\n\nFor the last strict inequality use lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)<6 and\ne>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3: the right side is less than9/64<3/20.\nThe supremum over all jointly legal mixtures, values, and b in[0,2]\ncan be included inside this limsup because every preceding estimate\nwas uniform. This is a constant gap for the WHOLE finite ansatz(1)-(2),\nnot a statement about the unrestricted worst-case error.\n\nThe general bound(5) gives limsup<=64lambda^2 t_max^2/e. Its displayed\nconstant only excludes a benchmark witness when that number is below1;\nno exclusion for all t_max<=1/2 is inferred.\n\n## 7. A fully explicit legal mixture containing individually illegal modes\n\nThe obstruction is not vacuous. Here are definite strictly positive weights,\nJOINT values, a finite-dimensional global realization, and a quantitative\npayoff for every horizon m>=128. No pairwise test or numerical calibration\nis left to perform.\n\nUse exactly the 2m modes(1), and select the base mode z_base=z_(m-1,1).\nWrite r_base=t_1/(1000D), alpha_base=t_1 r_base,\nsigma_base=r_base^2 and kappa_base=alpha_base-sigma_base. Let S=sum H_m^-.\nBy Section2, S<S_m<6d_m<15D. Consequently\n\n    sigma_base S/alpha_base <15/1000,\n    r_base S <15t_1/1000<1/1000,\n    kappa_base>(99/100)alpha_base.                     (20)\n\nEvery base-mode factor contracts, and all its prefix moduli are between\n1-r_base S and1. The lower bound follows from\nproduct(1-r_base h_i)>=1-r_base sum h_i, by induction. All these factors\nare nonzero.\n\nDefine scalar base values by the actual trapezoidal gradient work:\n\n    f_0^base=alpha_base/2,\n    f_(i+1)^base=f_i^base\n         -h_i sigma_base(1-alpha_base h_i/2)|P_i(z_base)|^2.\n\nSince alpha_base h_i<1, these values decrease and (20) gives\n\n    (47/100)alpha_base <= f_i^base <= alpha_base/2.      (21)\n\nWe now check EVERY ordered pair for the base mode. Fix i<j and\nT=sum_(i<=s<j)h_s. The product telescoping identity bounds\n|P_s/P_i-1|<=exp(r_base T)-1<=2r_base T. Hence\n\n    |P_j-P_i|>=r_base T |P_i|(1-2r_base T),\n    |P_j-P_i|^2>=(9/10)sigma_base T^2 |P_i|^2.         (22)\n\nThe closed polygon comprising the chronological path i,...,j followed\nby the chord back to i has signed doubled area A_ij. Translation by\n-P_i and the GD recursion show, by expanding the two sums, that\n\n    A_ij=sigma_base sum_(i<=s<t<j)\n                         h_s h_t Im(conj(P_s)P_t).\n\nFor any subproduct, telescope its difference from its complex conjugate.\nEach changed factor contributes at most2|Im z_base|h_s, and the other\nfactor moduli are bounded by product(1+r_base h_s). Therefore\n\n    |Im(conj(P_s)P_t)|\n      <=|P_s|^2 |Im z_base| T exp(r_base T)\n      <=2|P_i|^2 |Im z_base| T.\n\nSince sum_(s<t)h_s h_t<=T^2/2, this yields\n\n    |(Im z_base)A_ij|\n                   <=sigma_base^2 T^3 |P_i|^2.         (23)\n\nExpanding the trapezoidal work gives\nf_j^base-f_i^base=(alpha_base/2)(|P_j|^2-|P_i|^2)\n  +(Im z_base)sum_(s=i)^(j-1) Im(conj(P_s)P_(s+1)).\nTogether with the exact edge identity in accepteddfde2fa326c908bf,\nthis says the two ordered interpolation slacks for the pair are\n\n    (kappa_base/2)|P_j-P_i|^2 +/- (Im z_base)A_ij.\n\nUsing(20),(22),(23), both slacks exceed\n\n    [(9/20)(99/100)-15/1000]\n            alpha_base sigma_base T^2 |P_i|^2\n      >(2/5)alpha_base sigma_base T^2 |P_i|^2.\n\nAll h_i>=d_0>1, so T>1, and |P_i|>=999/1000. In particular every\nordered finite-pair residual Q_ij is at most -delta_base, where\n\n    delta_base=alpha_base sigma_base/4>0.              (24)\n\nThe two optimum constraints are also strict with this same margin.\nIndeed (21) and sigma_base/alpha_base<1/1000 show\nf_i^base-sigma_base|P_i|^2/2>(2/5)alpha_base, while\n(alpha_base-sigma_base/2)|P_i|^2-f_i^base\n  >(2/5)alpha_base. Both exceed delta_base. This checks the actual\noptimum, not just cycles among finite queries.\n\nFor all OTHER modes assign temporary scalar values zero at every query.\nThese are NOT claimed legal. Set r_max=t_1/(1000d_0),\nM=exp(r_max S), K=4M^2, and\n\n    epsilon=delta_base/[2(K+delta_base)].\n\nGive the base weight1-epsilon and each of the other2m-1 modes weight\nepsilon/(2m-1). These are explicit positive weights summing to1. Every\nprefix polynomial in every mode has modulus at most M, because\n|1-hz|<=1+h r_max and product(1+h r_max)<=exp(r_max S).\nAlso all spectral moduli are less than1. For their zero temporary values,\neach ordered finite or optimum residual satisfies\n\n    Q_ij<=2r_max M^2+2r_max^2 M^2<=K.\n\nNow use vectors(2) and the single JOINT value choice\n\n    f_i=(1-epsilon)f_i^base, f_star=0.                 (25)\n\nOrthogonality of the real blocks makes each joint residual exactly the\nweighted sum of the component residuals. Equations(24)-(25) imply\n\n    Q_ij<=-(1-epsilon)delta_base+epsilon K\n           =-delta_base/2<0\n\nfor EVERY distinct ordered pair, including every optimum pair. Thus all\ncycles hold on this SAME probability mixture. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290\nconstructs one everywhere finite convex1-smooth interpolant on R^(4m):\nwith cjet_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i, take\n\n    H(v)=max_(i in{star,0,...,q})\n       {cjet_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.\n\nIt interpolates all the actual jets, F(0)=min F=0, and ||x_0||=1.\nIt is a joint function; the temporary zero-value components are not\nseparately interpolated.\n\nThere really are individually illegal components with positive weight.\nFor example z_(0,1) has h_c=2000d_0. Our D exceeds this threshold for\nm>=128 (already D>2^127/3 and 2000d_0<3000). Section4 gives a positive\ncycle Dcal_k for an initial H_k inside the retained prefix, so that\ncomponent alone cannot admit ANY legal values with the specified optimum.\nThe same holds for z_(0,2). Both nevertheless occur in the legal joint\nconstruction above.\n\nThere is also a uniform quantitative objective lower bound. At the last\nquery, (21) and epsilon<1/2 give 2f_q>=47alpha_base/100. Moreover\n\n    ||g_q||^2<=sigma_base+epsilon M^2\n             <=sigma_base+delta_base/8<=2sigma_base.\n\nConvexity of this same F gives F(x_q-b g_q)>=f_q-b||g_q||^2. Hence for\nevery b in[0,2], using sigma_base/alpha_base<1/1000,\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)>=47alpha_base/100-8sigma_base\n                   >=alpha_base/4=1/(4096000D).       (26)\n\nThis has order lambda^(-m), with a very large explicit loss in its\nconstant. It does NOT reach the exact benchmark. To spell out that loss,\nthe accepted primitive recurrence gives C_(2^(m-1)-1)>=lambda^(m-1)\nby balanced splits, and C_k is increasing by the split Omega(C_(k-1),1).\nThe dominant split with right length0 gives B_n>=2C_(n-1), so\nB_n>=2lambda^(m-1). Consequently the constructed family satisfies\n\n    liminf_m B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                >=lambda/(2048000e)>0,\n\nuniformly in b in[0,2], while the whole ansatz satisfies(19). These\nnormalization constants are explicit; the tiny weights of illegal modes\nare not being represented as a sharp benchmark witness.\n\n## 8. Status and the remaining problem\n\nThis argument repairs the small-step/scale hypotheses explicitly, uses\nthe actual truncated last copy, and pays for all modes on ONE cycle.\nIt does not use the invalid old sector-product estimate. In particular\nthe growing near-unit parameters of finding35d2f3dd8f474b19 are outside\n(4). Those parameters, mixtures involving a broader range of angles,\nand nonnormal cross-mode correlations remain open.\n\nThe obstruction needs only a necessary cycle from the complete legal\nprefix. It therefore applies to every interpolation-feasible assignment\nof values; it does not assert that satisfying that one cycle is sufficient\nfor legality. Any positive construction still has to check all pairs.\n\nMatlas returned unrelated positive-operator and sub-Laplacian statements;\nnone is used. The cycle identities and estimates are included above in\nfull. This document is shared evidence only, not a verifier-accepted fact.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "preceding_audit": "35d2f3dd8f474b19"}, "glossary": {"t_max": "Maximum positive ratio Re(z)/|z| in the scoped spectrum, at most1/2.", "t_1": "1/32, first angular type of the definite finite ansatz.", "t_2": "1/64, second angular type.", "D": "Largest physical step d_(m-1) of the near-critical word.", "H_m^-": "H_m with its original last d_0 step deleted.", "Dcal_k": "Reverse chronological interpolation cycle weight through full H_k and the optimum.", "Dcal_k^-": "The corresponding cycle with the actual last d_0 step removed.", "R_k": "p_k(z)(1-d_k z), the start multiplier of the right H_k copy.", "eta": "sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2, used only as the scalar recurrence coefficient in this finding.", "delta_base": "alpha_base sigma_base/4, a uniform strict interpolation margin for the small last-scale base mode.", "epsilon": "delta_base/[2(4 exp(2r_max S)+delta_base)], the total weight on all non-base modes.", "r_max": "t_1/(1000d_0), maximum modulus in the finite ansatz.", "S": "Actual retained sum sum H_m^-."}}
{"id": "8a4c55a6de4727ae", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:44:07.669817+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C41 strengthened scope: EVERY even-coordinate split may vary independently; only the odd-coordinate split must be common. The full proof and same free-terminal/true-distance bound hold: score/K<(45/8)exp(-N/80)<45/64 for m>=512,N>=256, limsup B_n score<=45/(32e)<1. Thus arbitrary ruler-LEVEL-dependent splits theta_j=b_(nu_2(j)) are also excluded. Only variation of odd splits, shorter phase counts or longer/repeated memory remains outside.", "evidence": "# C41, expanded scope. Arbitrary even-coordinate splits with one odd split\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier, numerical solver or sweep.\nThis allows every even-coordinate split to vary INDEPENDENTLY and keeps only\none common odd-coordinate split. The explicit range is m>=512 and N>=256\nphases. Every required argument is included here;\nearlier unverified findings are motivation only.\n\n## 1. The fixed real family and all normal comparisons\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Choose 0<=r<=m-8, let k=m-r,\nN=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, and\n\n    A=1+q,\n    q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n    tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nThe N ordinary blocks are exactly the actual copies of H_r; their\ncheckpoints have excesses Mq_i. All original physical steps are retained.\n\nChoose v in[0,1] and, independently, theta_j in[0,1] for EVERY positive\neven j. Set theta_j=v for odd j. Choose positive R_i, E_i=R_i^2, and transfers\n\n    a_01=R_1, a_02=theta_2 R_2,\n    a_(i,i+1)=(1-theta_(i+1))R_(i+1), i>=1,\n    a_(i,i+2)=theta_(i+2)R_(i+2), i>=1,\n\nomitting out-of-range indices. Every j>=2 receives total mass R_j\nfrom its two actual predecessors. Put\n\n    p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n    C=conv(0,p_i), F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nThe p_i are actual independent vectors in R^N with triangular positive\ndiagonal, not a formal covariance matrix. The projection variational\ninequality and maximizing-difference bounds prove\ngrad F_C=P_C, convexity and1-smoothness.\n\nStart at R_0e_0. Before phase i>=1 its only nonzero coordinates are\nR_i e_i+theta_(i+1)R_(i+1)e_(i+1); subtract each actual step times p_i.\nThe checkpoint exhausts coordinate i and creates the next prescribed state.\n\nHere is the entire legality test. During phase i>=1 put\nz=(physical partial ordinary mass+1)/M in[1/M,1],\nd=theta_(i+1), e=theta_(i+2).\nMultiply all normal values by M. The own value is\n\n [(tau_i-z)E_i\n  -(1-d)(d tau_i+z(1-d))E_(i+1)\n  -z e^2 E_(i+2)]/tau_i^2.                               (1)\n\nThe only possibly nonzero FUTURE comparisons are\n\n [(d+z(1-d)/tau_i)E_(i+1)\n           -z e(1-e)E_(i+2)/tau_i]/tau_(i+1),            (2)\n z e E_(i+2)/(tau_i tau_(i+2)).                           (3)\n\nEvery past normal is nonpositive because its positive coordinate has\nbeen exhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nAll other future normals are zero; the zero vertex must also be tested.\nTherefore (1)>=(2),(3),0 at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is necessary and sufficient\nfor every actual query. At the root use its displayed p_0 to test the two\nendpoints directly. The terminal block requires t>=1.\nAll omitted final terms are zero.\n\nThe class is nonempty for every chosen sequence theta_j and finite word: fix terminal energy1,\nthen choose E_i successively backward sufficiently large to satisfy these\nfinite inequalities. Its own coefficient tau_i-z>=q_i>0 and every\nother term uses already fixed future energies. Finally choose E_0.\nThis is a constructive finite specification; the upper bound covers EVERY\nlegal energy choice, not just this conservative policy.\n\nEvery original query has F_C(x_i)=<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2.\nThe minimizer set C^polar has all coordinates nonpositive, by backward\ninduction in <p_i,z><=0. Thus0 is the actual closest minimizer and its\ninitial squared distance is E_0. The original objective and primitive\ndefect, with htot the entire physical mass, are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (4)\n\nThe defect follows by telescoping the identity\n2h_iF_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2.\n\n## 2. Combine BOTH high-phase future comparisons\n\nConsider an internal low/high pair i,i+1, where i is even and2<=i<=N-4.\nPut Q=q_(i+1), B=1+Q, rho=A/B and LOCAL u=theta_(i+2).\nThis local u can differ arbitrarily from pair to pair. The odd fractions\nat i+1 and i+3 are both v; this is the ONLY constancy used below.\nThe low phase has d=v,e=u. Its comparisons with i+1 and i+2 give\n\n q E_i >= L1 E_(i+1)+L2 E_(i+2),\n    L1=(1+vq)(1-v+rho), L2=u[u-(1-u)rho],                 (5)\n E_(i+2)/E_i <= q/[u(1+u)],                              (6)\n\nwith (6) read as no restriction at u=0.\n\nThe HIGH phase has d=u,e=v. Its two future comparisons are\n\n Q E_(i+1) >= H1 E_(i+2)+H2 E_(i+3),\n    H1=(1+uQ)(1-u+B/A),\n    H2=v[v-(1-v)B/A],                                   (7)\n Q E_(i+1) >= H3 E_(i+2)+H4 E_(i+3),\n    H3=(1-u)(1+uQ),\n    H4=v[v+B/Bnext],                                    (8)\n\nwhere Bnext=tau_(i+3)>0 is the actual next high capacity, or terminal t.\nNo future residual has been reset.\n\nIf H2>=0, dropping its nonnegative term in(7) gives coefficient H1.\nIf H2<0, then0<v<1 and H4>0. Use(8) to upper-bound E_(i+3), multiply\nthat upper bound by H2<0, and insert it into(7). This gives\n\n Q E_(i+1) >= [(H1 H4-H2 H3)/(H4-H2)]E_(i+2).\n\nThe coefficient is exactly\n\n    (1+uQ)[1-u+(v Bnext+B)/(A+(1-v)Bnext)].\n\nThe ratio here lies between B/A and v/(1-v), because it is a ratio of\npositive affine functions of Bnext. At v=0, (7) has H2=0 and gives H1\ndirectly; v=1 belongs to the nonnegative case. Thus in ALL cases,\n\n Q E_(i+1) >=\n    (1+uQ)[1-u+min(B/A,v/(1-v))]E_(i+2),                 (9)\n\nwith v/(1-v)=+infinity at v=1. This is the inherited-coordinate cost\nmissed by using only the high zero comparison.\n\nCombine(5),(9),(6). The exact necessary pair bound is\n\n    E_(i+2)/E_i <= qQ / max(G, Q u(1+u)),                 (10)\n\nwhere\n\n G=(1+vq)(1-v+rho)(1+uQ)\n                       [1-u+min(1/rho,v/(1-v))]\n       +Q u[u-(1-u)rho].                                (11)\n\nThe denominator in(10) is shown positive next, so no sign assumption\nis hidden in taking its reciprocal.\n\n## 3. The scalar comparison: two splits still force a loss\n\nDefine G0=(1-u)(1+rho)+Qu. We prove\n\n    G>=min(G0,A).                                        (12)\n\nWhen v<=1/(1+rho), the minimum in(11) is v/(1-v).\nSet w=1-v. Then\n\n G=(A-qw)(1+rho/w)(1-uw)(1+uQ)\n                         +Qu^2-Qrho u(1-u).\n\nBoth nonnegative factors (A-qw)(1+rho/w) and1-uw increase as v increases:\nthe first has w derivative -q-A rho/w^2<0, and the second has w\nderivative -u<=0. Therefore its minimum is at v=0, where direct\ncancellation gives G=G0.\n\nFor v>=1/(1+rho), the minimum is1/rho. The only v-dependent factor\nis (1+qv)(1-v+rho), a concave quadratic. Its minimum on this interval\nis at an endpoint. At the lower endpoint the preceding result gives G>=G0.\nAt v=1, expansion gives\n\n G=A(1+uQ)+Qu^2+rho(1-u)[A+qQu] >=A.\n\nThis proves(12), including all endpoints u,v=0,1.\n\nThe ruler parameters obey, for m>=512,\n\n    2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16, Q>=cq>=1-1/m>=99/100.      (13)\n\nIndeed M_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j, with0<a<c-2, gives\na<=q<=c-2 and M_m<=M c^k. The rational bounds follow from\nlambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5.\n\nIf Q>=1, G0 is a convex combination of1+rho and Q, so G0>=1;\nalso A>1. If99/100<=Q<1, then rho=A/(1+Q)>7/10.\nFor u<=3/4, G0>=99/100+(1/4)(1/100+7/10)>1.\nFor u>=3/4, Q u(1+u)>=2079/1600>1.\nThus\n\n    max(G,Q u(1+u))>=1 at EVERY high checkpoint.           (14)\n\nAt a LEVEL-ONE high checkpoint Q=cq<=273/256,\nrho>=1792/2645>2/3. If u<=3/4,\n\n    G0>=99/100+(1/4)(1/100+2/3)\n         =1391/1200>9/8,\n\nand A>7/5>9/8. If u>=3/4 the second term is again at least2079/1600.\nHence, uniformly in BOTH independent splits,\n\n    max(G,Q u(1+u))>=kappa:=9/8 at every level-one high.  (15)\n\n## 4. Root and terminal costs, with no forgotten minimizer change\n\nFor the first pair, set LOCAL u=theta_2. The root endpoint against vertex2\ngives qE_0>=E_1+u(u+1)E_2.\nThe high phase's zero comparison gives\nQ E_1>=(1-u)(1+uQ)E_2, Q=cq.\nConsequently\n\n    E_2/E_0<=qQ/[1-u+2Qu]<=qQ.                           (16)\n\nThere are N/4 level-one highs in the whole word. The initial one uses\n(16); all N/4-1 remaining ones pay kappa. Other pairs pay at least1.\nCounting the exact ruler levels therefore yields\n\n    E_(N-2)/E_0 <= (qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/\n                                  [q kappa^(N/4-1)].    (17)\n\nNow retain ALL original queried jets and the optimum(0,0,0), and let f\nbe ANY convex1-smooth terminal completion, as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nWhen r>=1, every original p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to a\nnew minimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0 from its queried value\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. Their triangular inequalities force all coordinates\nof z nonpositive;0 remains the true closest minimizer, with distance R_0.\n\nThe last step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at that actual retained query gives\n\n    2f(x_n)/E_0 <=\n     [2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n                     <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a gives 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nThe last low phase has incoming fraction v and obeys\n\n    qE_(N-2)>=(1+vq)[1-v+A/t]E_(N-1).\n\nThus E_(N-1)/t<=qE_(N-2)/A. Put K=1/(2M_m-1) and use M_m<=Mc^k\nwith(17). This proves\n\n    score_free/K <(4/A)(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).          (18)\n\nWhen r=0, the old terminal normal is unqueried and must not constrain the\nnew minimum. The retained initial gradient instead gives\ndist(x_init,argmin f)^2>=E_0/A^2.\nAt the last query the position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(N-1)e_(N-1) and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(N-1)e_(N-1)]/A.\nOne smoothness step bounds2f(x_n) by\n\n (1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(N-1)\n       <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive because A<23/16.\nCombining the TRUE distance lower bound and(17) gives\n\n    score_free/K <2(2+q)(qc)^(N-1)/kappa^(N/4-1).         (19)\n\nA uniform constant5 bounds both prefactors. These are necessary bounds\non every full completion, not assertions that the smoothness cap is attained.\n\n## 5. Explicit horizon bounds and the actual B_n comparison\n\nFor m>=512,\n\n    qc<=c(c-2)\n      =1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2\n      <1+8/m<=65/64.\n\nLet z0=65/64>1. To include qc<1, use (qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N. The exact\nrational inequality\n\n    z0^4/(9/8)=142805000/150994944<19/20\n\nand(18)-(19) yield\n\n    score_free/K <5(9/8)(19/20)^(N/4)\n                 <= (45/8)exp(-N/80).                    (20)\n\nFor N>=256, N/80>3 and exp(3)>8, so\n\n    score_free/K <45/64<1.                               (21)\n\nIf N grows, this ratio tends to0 uniformly in the choices of r,\nall positive energies, all even-coordinate splits, the common odd split,\nterminal parameter t, and full completion.\n\nThe comparison with the TRUE benchmark can be proved from its accepted\nrecursions, rather than assuming an asymptotic constant. Put rho_s=log_2 lambda.\nFor F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1, the second derivative of log F(exp w) is\nrho_s z(1+z)^(rho_s-2)[(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s z]/F(z)^2>0.\nConvexity at w and-w gives F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2.\nConsequently the accepted primitive join obeys\nOmega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\nInduction in b98bb807d514e80d gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s.\nIts dominant join is at most4X+D, so its exact recursion gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\n\nThe explicit mass formula above gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), hence\nlimsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1). Therefore every family in the stated range has\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1) score_free <=45/[32 exp(1)]<1,        (22)\n\nand the limit superior is zero when N tends to infinity.\nThe original objective, primitive defect and normalization were all tracked;\nnone was inferred from a sign-only witness.\n\n## 6. Scope\n\nEvery even-coordinate split is independent, including arbitrary dependence\non the horizon, ruler level, or earlier deterministic schedule data. Only\nthe odd-coordinate fractions are required to equal one common v; that v\nmay also depend on m,r. In particular ALL policies theta_j=b_(nu_2(j))\nwith an arbitrary scalar parameter for each binary valuation level are\ncovered, because the odd j all have valuation0. Every later coordinate\nmerges two real branches, and the full old-query test(1)-(3) includes all\ninherited residuals. The endpoint is completely free after retaining the\nqueried prefix and the optimum.\n\nThis closes the entire stated family for m>=512,N>=256. The proof is local\nin u=theta_(i+2), so no bound, product or construction above assumes equality\nof different even fractions. It does not cover varying the ODD fractions,\nfewer phases, longer shared-coordinate memory, signed masses, or revisiting\nan exhausted coordinate. It is neither an unrestricted objective upper\ncertificate nor a proof of the original exact-profile target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["64333922c8f1a563", "e1d71a04bf4d0921", "209666ace998cb98"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "research/c41_arbitrary_even_split_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"theta_even_j": "An independently chosen incoming two-back fraction at each positive even coordinate index j.", "v_split": "The common incoming two-back fraction at every odd index; it may depend on the horizon.", "u_local": "Local even split theta_(i+2) for one low/high pair; no equality between different pairs is assumed.", "b_level": "Arbitrary valuation-level split parameters in the covered specialization theta_j=b_(nu_2(j))."}}
{"id": "5bd01e2b19002a59", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:47:27.037186+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New candidate variable-matrix re-selection theorem at unbounded lengths, without any saturated cut: any FULL signed E_theta certificate whose weighted forward/backward cut sums obey (h_k-1)F_k<=B_k at every h_k>1 has an explicit ordinary adjacent-edge certificate. A useful support corollary allows arbitrary backward edges, adjacent forward prefix edges, and terminal chords with nonpositive cumulative signed sums. A constant4/3 signed family at every length n>=2 meets the hypotheses; both full matrices are PSD although their difference is indefinite. This is a scoped repair, not general credit-cone equality or an improving objective family.", "evidence": "# Variable-matrix repair of a terminal-chord certificate class\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No new solver or\nfact_submit call. All identities below are proved directly. The theorem has\narbitrary positive length and does not require a saturated composition cut.\nIt is a support-and-cumulative-sign theorem, not general credit re-selection.\n\n## 1. Definitions and exact statement\n\nLet n>=1 and let h_0,...,h_(n-1)>0. Put\n\n P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, p_i=1+P_i, s=P_n, C=1+s,\n R_i=sum_(t>=i) h_t=C-p_i, with R_n=0.\n\nUse the actual GD substitutions x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                            -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nSuppose a real signed array lambda cancels every value coefficient in\nV_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij and its FULL remainder M is PSD on independent\ng_0,...,g_n. Suppose its only possibly nonzero entries are\n\n lambda_(i,i+1)=a_i, lambda_(i+1,i)=b_i       (0<=i<n),\n lambda_(i,n)=c_i                           (0<=i<n-1).\n\nThe latter are the nonadjacent terminal chords; the adjacent last incoming\nedge is a_(n-1), not another c entry. Set c_(n-1)=0 for summation and\n\n zeta_k=sum_(i<=k)c_i,     0<=k<n.\n\nAssume the following cumulative condition at each long nonfinal step:\n\n           zeta_k<=0 whenever k<n-1 and h_k>1.             (1)\n\nThen the following array is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_h:\n\n a_i^0=2P_(i+1)p_i/h_i,\n b_i^0=2P_(i+1)p_(i+1)/h_i,                              (2)\n\non the two adjacent directions, with all other entries zero. Its complete\nformal remainder is\n\n          M^0=sum_(i<n) w_i ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2,            (3)\n w_i=(P_(i+1)/h_i)[p_i-(h_i-1)p_(i+1)]>=0.\n\nIn particular, if the input is an actual E_theta certificate represented\nby lambda, with lambda_(i,n)>=-theta h_i, 0<=theta<1, and all other entries\nnonnegative, it can be repaired to the ordinary certificate (2). The proof\ndoes not need the numerical bound theta once the displayed support, full\nPSD, flow and cumulative condition are assumed. The incoming chords c_i\ncan have both signs; only their indicated cumulative sums are restricted.\n\nNo property is asserted for a certificate with extra remote prefix edges,\nextra outgoing terminal edges, or positive zeta_k at a step h_k>1. All these\nare genuine remaining cases for the general cone question.\n\n## 2. Full affine-kernel identity and weighted circulation\n\nThis section holds for any full-support real multiplier array. Let e be\nthe all-one column, L=diag(lambda e)-lambda the row Laplacian, and let A\nhave A_ii=1, A_it=h_t for t<i and zero entries for t>i. Put\n\n omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n D_quad=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nValue cancellation is exactly L^T e=omega, and expansion gives\n\n                M=D_quad+sym(L A),   sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.         (4)\n\nFor completeness, the position terms in sum lambda Q are sym(L(A-I)).\nThe squared-gradient differences contribute sym(L)-diag(omega)/2.\nAdding the diagonal gradient terms of V gives (4).\n\nEvaluating this value-canceling quadratic on a constant-gradient legal\naffine segment gives e^T M e=0: all Q vanish, and V=0. Such a segment can\nbe realized by a scalar Huber function started sufficiently far along an\naffine ray. Since M is PSD, M e=0. Equivalently this follows by considering\nthe quadratic on e+t v for arbitrary v and both signs of t.\n\nAs A e=p, equation M e=0 yields\n\n                 Lp=-2D_quad e-A^T omega=diag(p)omega.         (5)\n\nHere, for i<n,\n\n (A^T omega)_i=-2h_i+2h_i P_(i+1),\n -2h_i(2-h_i)-(A^T omega)_i=-2h_i p_i;\n\nand the terminal component is 2s(s+1). These explicitly verify (5).\nCombining (5) with L^T e=omega shows that\n\n                 mu_ij=lambda_ij p_j                    (6)\n\nhas equal incoming and outgoing sums at every node. Indeed (5) says\nsum_j lambda_ij p_j=p_i sum_j lambda_ji. Thus mu is a real circulation,\nthough its terminal edges can still be negative.\n\nThe separate value-flow condition must be retained: with u_i=1/p_i,\n\n          sum_j mu_ij(u_j-u_i)=omega_i.                  (7)\n\nCirculation alone does not imply (7), positivity, or PSD.\n\n## 3. The input adjacent coefficients are forced by the chords\n\nSum the exact value flows over vertices 0,...,k. The only crossing edges\nare the adjacent pair k,k+1 and the terminal chords from this prefix, so\n\n               a_k-b_k+zeta_k=-2P_(k+1).                 (8)\n\nSum the circulation balances (6) over the same prefix:\n\n               a_k p_(k+1)-b_k p_k+C zeta_k=0.           (9)\n\nFor k=n-1 the formula counts the last adjacent incoming edge only in a_k;\nc_(n-1)=0 prevents double counting. Solving (8)-(9) gives\n\n a_k=a_k^0-(R_k/h_k)zeta_k,\n b_k=b_k^0-(R_(k+1)/h_k)zeta_k.                         (10)\n\nThis retains all actual coefficients rather than selecting an assumed\nmatrix. It follows from the full input PSD through its affine kernel.\n\n## 4. A diagonal test in the full gradient-difference coordinates\n\nFor each k<n let z^(k) be the formal gradient column equal to1 on indices\n0,...,k and0 thereafter. This is a legitimate direction in the FULL\noriginal gradient space, regardless of whether it is itself a legal run.\nFor ANY real array satisfying value cancellation and (5), a direct\ncalculation yields\n\n (z^(k))^T M z^(k)\n     =-P_(k+1)^2\n          +sum_(i<=k<j)lambda_ij[1+p_j-p_(k+1)].         (11)\n\nHere are all terms of that calculation. Set every value to zero. In a Q\nwith both indices in the prefix, the gradient coefficient is p_i-p_j.\nFor a prefix-to-suffix pair it is p_i-p_(k+1)+1/2. For a suffix-to-prefix\npair it is1/2, and it is0 inside the suffix. Sum (5) over prefix rows to\nreplace the first group and the p_i-p_j part of the forward crossing group\nby -2sum_(i<=k)h_i p_i. The remaining forward coefficient is\np_j-p_(k+1)+1/2. By the value cut flow, the total reverse crossing mass is\nthe forward mass plus2P_(k+1); this adds another1/2 to each forward weight\nand contributes P_(k+1). Finally\n\n sum_(i<=k)h_i(1-h_i)-2sum_(i<=k)h_i p_i+P_(k+1)\n       =-sum_(i<=k)h_i^2-2sum_(i<=k)h_i P_i\n       =-P_(k+1)^2.\n\nThis proves (11) without suppressing any matrix direction.\n\nFor the stated adjacent-plus-terminal support, (11) becomes\n\n (z^(k))^T M z^(k)\n       =-P_(k+1)^2+a_k+(1+R_(k+1))zeta_k\n       =w_k+[(h_k-1)R_(k+1)/h_k]zeta_k.               (12)\n\nThe second equality follows from (10), R_k=h_k+R_(k+1), and\na_k^0-P_(k+1)^2=w_k. Because M is PSD, the left side is nonnegative.\n\nIf h_k>1 and k<n-1, (1) makes the last term in (12) nonpositive, whence\nw_k>=0. If k=n-1 it vanishes because R_n=0, with the same conclusion.\nIf 0<h_k<=1, the formula\n\n w_k=(P_(k+1)/h_k)[(2-h_k)p_k+h_k(1-h_k)]\n\nis strictly positive directly. Thus every w_k in (3) is nonnegative.\n\n## 5. Complete repaired certificate\n\nThe coefficients (2) are nonnegative. Across edge k their net forward\nflow is a_k^0-b_k^0=-2P_(k+1), so the difference between adjacent cuts\nis -2h_k, with the terminal flow2s. Hence every original value coefficient\ncancels. Direct expansion of the adjacent Q terms gives a tridiagonal\ngradient matrix. Its affine kernel vanishes, as can be checked from (2)\nor (9) with zeta=0. Its off-diagonal entry on edge k is\n\n [ -a_k^0+(h_k-1)b_k^0 ]/2=-w_k.\n\nTherefore the full matrix is precisely the weighted path Laplacian (3):\nthe diagonal entries are w_(k-1)+w_k, with endpoint conventions. Since\nall w_k>=0, (3) proves PSD on ALL independent original gradients. No equality\nbetween two formal gradients, no rank-one test, and no primal-witness-only\nPSD inference is used.\n\nThe construction actually changes the matrix. It does not assert that\nM^0-M is PSD, and the next family shows that difference can be indefinite.\n\n## 6. A nonsaturated signed input family at every positive length n>=2\n\nTake h_i=t=4/3 for all i<n, let d_i=g_i-g_(i+1), and put\n\n w_k=(k+1)[2-t^2+k t(2-t)],\n epsilon=(2-t^2)/(4t n^2)=1/(24n^2),\n theta=epsilon/t=1/(32n^2).\n\nStart with (2) and add these four signed changes:\n\n Delta lambda_01=n epsilon,\n Delta lambda_10=(n-1)epsilon,\n Delta lambda_0n=-epsilon,\n Delta lambda_1n=epsilon.                              (13)\n\nFor n=2 the last change is added to the existing adjacent coefficient.\nEvery actual E_theta multiplier is nonnegative: add theta h_i=epsilon\nto every incoming terminal edge. In particular the only negative signed\nentry, lambda_0n=-epsilon, becomes0. Value flow is unchanged at every node.\nFor n>=3 the chord cumulative sums are zeta_0=-epsilon and zeta_k=0 for\nk>=1. For n=2 the sole relevant cumulative sum is zeta_0=-epsilon.\nThus (1) holds.\n\nFor a complete independent check of input PSD, expand (13):\n\n Delta M=epsilon[nQ_01+(n-1)Q_10-Q_0n+Q_1n]\n   =-epsilon(n-1)(t-1)||d_0||^2\n      -epsilon sum_(k=1)^(n-1)[1+t(n-1-k)]<d_0,d_k>.    (14)\n\nThis follows by collecting g_0^2, g_1^2 and g_0g_1 and then using\ng_j-g_1=-sum_(k=1)^(j-1)d_k. All value coefficients in (14) are zero.\nWrite beta_k=1+t(n-1-k). Completing squares gives\n\n M=sum_(k=1)^(n-1) w_k ||d_k-epsilon beta_k d_0/(2w_k)||^2\n       +[w_0-epsilon(n-1)(t-1)\n                   -epsilon^2/4 sum_(k=1)^(n-1)beta_k^2/w_k]||d_0||^2.\n                                                                  (15)\n\nHere w_0=2-t^2=2/9, w_k>=(k+1)w_0>0, beta_k<tn, and\nsum_(k=1)^(n-1)beta_k^2/w_k<t^2 n^3/w_0. Consequently the two deductions\nfrom w_0 in the last coefficient of (15) are respectively less than\nw_0/4 and w_0/(64n). Their sum is strictly less than w_0, so the last\ncoefficient is positive. This proves full input PSD (strict positivity\nin the difference coordinates) for every n>=2.\n\nThe repair (2) removes (13), producing sum w_k||d_k||^2. By (14), M^0-M\nhas a nonzero d_0 d_1 coefficient but zero d_1^2 coefficient. Restricting\nto (d_0,d_1) gives determinant -epsilon^2 beta_1^2/4<0. Thus the matrix\nchange is indefinite, even though both complete endpoint matrices are PSD.\n\nThere is no saturated cut in any nonempty constant-t block. For a cut at\nstep i, let A_cut=1+it, D_tail=1+(n-i-1)t and r=t-1=1/3. Since A_cut,D_tail>=1 and\nA_cut+D_tail=2+(n-1)t, their product is minimized at an endpoint and obeys\n\n A_cut D_tail>=1+(n-1)t=(4n-1)/3,\n rC=(4n+3)/9,\n A_cut D_tail-rC>=(8n-6)/9>0.\n\nThus lengths n>=3 in this family are outside the recursive class requiring\nan exactly saturated first cut and a <=2-step eventual continuation leaf.\nThe new support-based repair is not merely that saturated-cut argument.\n\n## 7. Scope and remaining interface\n\nCondition w_k>=0 is equivalent to\n\n h_k<=[1-p_k+sqrt(p_k^2+6p_k+1)]/2<2.\n\nThus every schedule admitted by this repair theorem has C<1+2n. This is\nan unbounded-length re-selection class, not an improving primitive seed\nfamily or an asymptotic separation from the exact objective profile.\n\nThe circulation equations (6)-(7) isolate why deleting negative chords\narbitrarily is invalid: both conservation and reciprocal-p drift must hold.\nThe argument succeeds here because the support forces (10) and the full\nPSD then forces each coefficient of the independent repaired path squares.\nSection 8 below allows arbitrary backward edges and also gives an explicit\nweighted-cut criterion for full-support arrays. No result here is claimed\nas a new fact until verifier acceptance; no verifier allocation was used.\n\n## 8. General full-support cut identity and a stronger repair theorem\n\nReturn to a real array with FULL support, exact value cancellation, full\nPSD, and nonnegative entries except possibly into the terminal. Define,\nfor each k<n, the following weighted actual forward and backward cut sums:\n\n F_k=sum_(i<=k<j)lambda_ij[p_j-p_(k+1)],\n B_k=sum_(j<=k<i)lambda_ij[p_k-p_j].                       (16)\n\nThe adjacent edge contributes zero to either sum. Every term in B_k is\nnonnegative; F_k can be negative only through signed incoming-terminal\nedges. There is no omitted interpolation coefficient in these definitions.\n\nThe exact FULL matrix identity on the cut direction is\n\n (z^(k))^T M z^(k)=w_k+[(h_k-1)F_k-B_k]/h_k.             (17)\n\nTo derive it, let O_k and I_k be the total forward and backward crossing\nmasses without weights. The value-flow cut says O_k-I_k=-2P_(k+1).\nThe weighted circulation cut says\n\n sum_(i<=k<j)lambda_ij p_j\n                   =sum_(j<=k<i)lambda_ij p_j.\n\nThe left side is p_(k+1)O_k+F_k and the right side is p_k I_k-B_k.\nUsing p_(k+1)-p_k=h_k therefore gives\n\n O_k=[2p_k P_(k+1)-F_k-B_k]/h_k.\n\nFormula (11) equals -P_(k+1)^2+O_k+F_k, which becomes (17) because\n2p_k P_(k+1)/h_k-P_(k+1)^2=w_k. This proves (17) for the complete\nmultiplier array and full original matrix.\n\n**Full-support repair criterion.** If at every k with h_k>1 the input\narray satisfies the explicit inequalities\n\n                       (h_k-1)F_k<=B_k,                 (18)\n\nthen the ordinary array (2) and full PSD remainder (3) repair it.\nIndeed (17) and full input PSD force w_k>=0 at such k; at h_k<=1 the\ndirect positive formula in Section 4 does so. Section 5 checks every\nactual coefficient, value flow and formal PSD direction of the output.\nCondition (18) is a checkable sufficient condition on a supplied full\ncertificate, not a proposed characterization of all credit certificates.\n\n**Arbitrary backward edges.** In particular, the original repair remains\nvalid if ANY additional nonnegative backward edges i->j with i>j are\nallowed. Assume every forward edge with j<n is adjacent, while arbitrary\nsigned incoming-terminal chords obey (1). Then\n\n F_k=R_(k+1) zeta_k, B_k>=0.\n\nAt a long nonfinal step (1) gives F_k<=0; at the final step F_(n-1)=0.\nThus (18) holds. Extra terminal-to-prefix edges and long backward prefix\nedges are all covered, with no assumption that their coefficients vanish.\nThe signed constant-4/3 family in Section 6 supplies nonordinary inputs at\narbitrarily large lengths in this class, outside the saturated-cut class.\n\n**Quantitative constraint on genuinely long steps.** Conversely, if\nw_k<0, then (17) forces\n\n                    (h_k-1)F_k-B_k>=-h_k w_k>0.          (19)\n\nSuch a step necessarily has h_k>1. Hence some positive forward multiplier\ncrosses the cut and lands strictly beyond k+1; adjacent-only forward mass\ncannot supply (19). If the input is an actual E_theta certificate with\nnonnegative multipliers Lambda, so that\n\n lambda_(i,n)=Lambda_(i,n)-theta h_i,\n\nand other lambda entries equal their actual Lambda entries, define\nF_k^actual by (16) with Lambda. Then the exact conversion is\n\n             F_k=F_k^actual-theta P_(k+1)R_(k+1),\n\nand (19) becomes\n\n (h_k-1)F_k^actual-B_k\n      >=theta(h_k-1)P_(k+1)R_(k+1)-h_k w_k.              (20)\n\nThus terminal credit does not eliminate the positive forward-cut weight\nobligation for these long steps. It increases its required actual weight\nby the displayed amount. Equation (20) is a necessary coefficient bound,\nnot a bound on what those weights can achieve and not a universal repair.\nNo conclusion about asymptotic objective minimax follows from it alone.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "be38e23574a71815", "closed_probe_id": "83671fcd8ba27b46", "related_findings": ["666b5b9b14038a0f", "2d75b4696641736c", "4aaee6131a92da0e"], "local_evidence": "local_memory/terminal_chord_path_repair.md"}, "glossary": {"P_i": "Sum of physical steps strictly before query i.", "p_i": "1+P_i.", "R_i": "Sum of physical steps from i onward; R_n=0.", "C": "1+sum h_i, the physical capacity in this finding.", "L": "Row Laplacian diag(lambda e)-lambda of a real signed multiplier array.", "D_quad": "Diagonal matrix in equation(4), defining the complete value-canceling gradient remainder.", "mu_ij": "Destination-weighted signed interpolation multiplier lambda_ij p_j.", "zeta_k": "Cumulative signed nonadjacent incoming-terminal chord sum through index k.", "a_i": "Signed forward adjacent multiplier lambda_(i,i+1).", "b_i": "Signed backward adjacent multiplier lambda_(i+1,i).", "w_i": "Coefficient of the i-th independent gradient-difference square in the repaired certificate, equation(3).", "F_k": "Weighted signed forward cut sum in equation(16), not an objective function.", "B_k": "Weighted backward cut sum in equation(16), not the benchmark sequence B_n; the evidence defines both scope and index.", "Lambda": "Actual nonnegative E_theta interpolation multipliers; lambda subtracts theta h_i from incoming-terminal entries.", "beta_k": "1+t(n-1-k), the cross-term coefficient in the explicit constant-step input family."}}
{"id": "910ba7bac773e2a3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:54:28.761823+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "NEW complete JOINT fast/slow normal-mixture theorem on the actual retained near-critical prefix. All fast parameters z=1-u/m±i sqrt(u-theta)/sqrt(m), u in[5,6], theta in[1/2,1], have exponentially growing queried polynomials. Yet arbitrary joint mixtures of these with ANY slow spectra |z|<=R/S, R<=1/8, satisfy 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=exp(2R)[2R/S+(5/4)m R^3/S^3] for EVERY b in[0,2], giving limsup exact-benchmark ratio <=exp(-3/4)<1/2 atR=1/8. One SAME last-three-query cycle forces fast endpoint mass O(m/S^3); no individual legality assumed. ALSO an explicit finite R^(4m+4) mixture has strictly positive weights on two individually illegal growing fast modes plus two slow angular types at logarithmic scales, fully checked ordered/optimum constraints, and one fixed global F. One terminal vector perpendicular to g_q meets EVERY anchor and proves 2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(64S) for ALL real b. Its exact-benchmark ratio liminf is >=3/(32lambda e), with the constant loss explicit. Unverified, no new allocation; intermediate-frequency donors and all-normal/unrestricted targets remain open.", "evidence": "# Growing near-unit modes cannot be repaired to benchmark strength by slow modes\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No new verifier, solver, numerical\nprobe or round allocation. Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290 (full prefix\ninterpolation and free completion), dfde2fa326c908bf (algebraic cycle\nidentity), and b98bb807d514e80d (exact benchmark recurrences). All other\nsteps needed here are proved in this document.\n\n## 1. A whole joint spectral class on the actual schedule\n\nFix m>=4096, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), and n=2^m-1.\nKeep c,a fixed within this horizon. Remove the original final d_0 step\nto form the queried prefix H_m^-, whose length is q=n-1. Let S=sum H_m^-\nand let P_i be its chronological polynomials, 0<=i<=q. In particular\nthe last two executed steps of this retained prefix are d_0,d_1, where\nd_1=2-1/m. Its original terminal displacement is x_q-d_0 g_q.\n\nLet 0<R<=1/8 be fixed. Allow ANY finite probability mixture supported on\nthe union of these two sets:\n\n    Slow_m(R)={z in C: |z|<=R/S},\n\n    Fast_m={1-u/m +/- i sqrt(u-theta)/sqrt(m):\n                           5<=u<=6, 1/2<=theta<=1}.       (1)\n\nThe words slow and fast refer only to these displayed sets. No component\nis presumed legal. For weights w_z>=0 summing to1, use actual real vectors\n\n    x_i=(sqrt(w_z) Re P_i(z), sqrt(w_z) Im P_i(z))_z,\n    g_i=(sqrt(w_z) Re[zP_i(z)],sqrt(w_z) Im[zP_i(z)])_z.   (2)\n\nInclude the optimum(0,0,0), and allow ANY JOINT values f_i satisfying\nALL ordered interpolation constraints for these same vectors. Let F be\nANY legal convex1-smooth interpolant, including the maximal completion.\nThen uniformly for every b in[0,2],\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)\n       <=exp(2R)[2R/S+(5/4)m R^3/S^3].                 (3)\n\nFor R=1/8 this gives a whole-class constant gap from the exact benchmark:\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                    <=exp(-3/4)<1/2.                  (4)\n\nThe supremum over mixtures, dimension, values and b in[0,2] may be taken\ninside this limsup. This includes the original final step d_0. It does\nnot bound W_n for arbitrary gradients or arbitrary normal spectra.\n\nSection5 gives a definite finite mixture from this class, with POSITIVE\nweights on individually illegal exponentially growing fast modes, checks\nevery ordered pair including the optimum, and constructs one fixed F with\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(64S) for EVERY real b.             (5)\n\nThus this is also an explicit fixed-prefix, all-final-step witness of\nthe benchmark's order, with its non-sharp constant loss quantified.\n\n## 2. One triangle made of the last three actual queries\n\nFor a scalar parameter z=alpha+i beta set sigma=|z|^2 and\nkappa=alpha-sigma. Starting locally at1, the last two steps have positions\n\n    v_0=1, v_1=1-d_0 z, v_2=(1-d_0 z)(1-d_1 z).\n\nThe forward triangle0->1->2->0 has signed doubled area\n\n    Im(conj(v_0)v_1+conj(v_1)v_2+conj(v_2)v_0)\n                          =-d_0^2 d_1 sigma beta.      (6)\n\nTo check it, the first two path-edge areas are -d_0 beta and\n-d_1 beta|1-d_0 z|^2, while the return edge contributes\nbeta(d_0+d_1-2d_0d_1 alpha). Their sum is(6).\nThe triangle's squared-chord energy is sigma E(z), where\n\n    E(z)=d_0^2+d_1^2|1-d_0 z|^2\n                       +|d_0+d_1-d_0d_1 z|^2.         (7)\n\nHence the REVERSE triangle, by the exact cycle identity in accepted\ndfde2fa326c908bf, has weight\n\n    K(z)=sigma[d_0^2 d_1 beta^2-(kappa/2)E(z)].        (8)\n\nPut A(z)=P_(q-2)(z). The same three ACTUAL indices q-2,q-1,q, with\nthe same reverse orientation, give the JOINT necessary condition\n\n    sum_z w_z |A(z)|^2 K(z)<=0.                        (9)\n\nThere is no optimum vertex in this cycle. Nevertheless the optimum\nremains imposed elsewhere on the joint prefix. All function values\ncancel in(9); no separate component values are chosen in using it.\n\n## 3. Positive fast defects and cubic slow credit\n\nFor the parameters in(1), elementary bounds valid for m>=4096 are\n\n    11/8<=d_0<=3/2, 31/16<=d_1<=2,\n    1-6/m<=alpha<=1, |beta|<=1/20,\n    sigma>=49/50, 0<kappa<=1/m, beta^2>=4/m.           (10)\n\nFor kappa, expand it exactly as theta/m-u^2/m^2. For sigma use\nsigma>=alpha^2>49/50. The step bounds follow directly from a,c, and\nbeta^2<=(11/2)/4096<1/400.\n\nSince d_0 alpha>1 and d_1 alpha>1, (10) gives\n\n    |1-d_0z|<=1/2+(3/2)(1/20)<3/5,\n    |1-d_1z|<=1+2/20=11/10.\n\nAlso 0<d_0+d_1-d_0d_1<=3/4 on the step rectangle in(10), and\n\n    |d_0+d_1-d_0d_1z|\n       <=3/4+3(6/m+|beta|)<1.\n\nConsequently E(z)<9/4+4(3/5)^2+1<5. Since\nd_0^2d_1>7/2, equations(8),(10) imply\n\n    K(z)>=(49/50)(14-5/2)/m>10/m,\n    |(1-d_0 z)(1-d_1 z)|^2<1/2.                       (11)\n\nThus each fast mode contributes at least(20/m)|P_q(z)|^2 to the\ncommon cycle(9), regardless of its own illegal prefix.\n\nFor a slow mode, all prefix polynomials have modulus at most exp(R):\nuse |1-hz|<=1+h|z| and product(1+h|z|)<=exp(|z|S). In particular\n|A(z)|^2<=exp(2R). Since |z|<=1/8, (7) gives\n\n    E(z)<=9/4+4(19/16)^2+(31/8)^2<25.\n\nThe negative part of K(z) is at most\n\n    (sigma/2) max(kappa,0) E(z)<= (25/2)|z|^3.\n\nHere max(kappa,0)<=max(alpha,0)<=|z|; no sign assumption on a slow\nmode's real part is needed. Its total possible negative contribution\nto(9), after summing all slow probabilities, is therefore at most\n\n    (25/2)exp(2R) R^3/S^3.                             (12)\n\nCombining(9),(11),(12) yields the genuinely JOINT endpoint bound\n\n    sum_(fast z) w_z |P_q(z)|^2\n                         <=(5/8)m exp(2R)R^3/S^3.    (13)\n\nThis is the cubic credit constraint missed by an optimum cycle alone.\nIt allows positive weights on illegal fast components, but those weights\ntimes their terminal amplification are uniformly controlled.\n\nFor any legal interpolant, optimum interpolation at q and final-step\nsmoothness give, for b in[0,2],\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)\n       <=sum_z w_z[2alpha-(1+b(2-b))sigma]|P_q(z)|^2.\n\nOn fast modes alpha<=1, so(13) bounds their contribution by twice its\nright side. On slow modes alpha<=|z|<=R/S and |P_q|<=exp(R), so their\ncontribution is at most2R exp(2R)/S. This proves(3), with every queried\nanchor and the actual last displacement retained.\n\n## 4. Benchmark estimate, and why the fast modes are relevant\n\nThe exact recursions from acceptedb98bb807d514e80d have mass joins\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nWrite rho=log_2 lambda and V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For t=exp(s),\n\n    d^2/ds^2 log V(exp(s))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.\n\nThus V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. Substituting t=(A/B)^(1/rho) shows\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho. Continuity covers zeros.\nInduction gives C_k=S_circle(k)+1<=(k+1)^rho. Since G(A,B)<=4A+B,\ninduction and superadditivity of t^rho give B_k<=1+4k^rho.\n\nThe exact retained sum is\n\n    S=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)-d_0.\n\nHere a/(c-2)->1, c^m/lambda^m->e and (2/lambda)^m->0; hence\nS/lambda^m->e. In particular m/S^2->0. Multiply(3) by B_n to obtain\nlimsup<=8R exp(2R-1), which gives(4) at R=1/8.\nThe strict inequality exp(-3/4)<1/2 follows from\nexp(3/4)>1+3/4+9/32+27/384>2.\n\nEvery fast parameter in(1) has exponentially growing ACTUAL queried\npolynomial for sufficiently large m, uniformly in its displayed compact\nparameter set. Here is the calculation, to avoid appealing to an\nunverified earlier counterexample. Put\n\n    L_m(z)=sum_(j>=0)2^(-j-1) log|1-d_j z|,\n    A_j=1+1/(a c^j),\n    D_m=1+1/[a(2-c^(-1))],\n    J_m=1+2/[a(2-c^(-1))]+1/[a^2(2-c^(-2))].\n\nAll A_j<=4 for sufficiently large m. For z=1+v in(1), expand\nlog(1+A_jv)=A_jv-A_j^2v^2/2+O(|v|^3), with remainder uniformly\nbounded by2|A_jv|^3 when |A_jv|<=1/2. The weights sum to1, so\n\n    mL_m(z)=-1-uD_m+(u-theta)J_m/2+O(m^(-1/2)).\n\nThe limits are D=1+D1, J=1+2D1+D2 with\nD1=lambda^2/(2lambda-1), D2=lambda^4/(2lambda^2-1). One has\nD1<8/5 and D2>31/10, by 12/5<lambda<5/2 and the increasing functions\nt^2/(2t-1), t^4/(2t^2-1) on t>1 (at12/5 the latter is20736/6575>31/10).\nThe limiting coefficient increases with u because J/2-D=(D2-1)/2>0,\nand decreases with theta. Its minimum is therefore\n\n    -1-5D+2J=-4-D1+2D2>3/5.\n\nThus L_m(z)>=1/(2m) uniformly for large m. The tail j>=m omitted from\nthe actual finite product, multiplied by2^m, is O(m), since its terms\nare bounded above by C+j log c. The removed last factor |1-d_0z| stays\nbounded above and away from0. Hence uniformly on Fast_m,\n\n    log|P_q(z)|>=2^m/(3m)\n\nfor all sufficiently large m. This is a polynomial-growth statement;\nlegality is supplied only for joint constructions such as the next one.\n\n## 5. Definite positive weights, every ordered constraint, and one global F\n\nUse R=1/8. Choose exactly two fast modes\n\n    z_fast,1=1-5/m+2i/sqrt(m),\n    z_fast,2=1-6/m+i sqrt(5)/sqrt(m).\n\nFor every j=0,...,m-1 choose two slow modes\n\n    r_j=d_0/(8S d_j),\n    z_(j,ell)=r_j[t_ell+i sqrt(1-t_ell^2)],\n    t_1=3/4, t_2=1/2.                                  (14)\n\nThese are 2m+2 complex blocks, realized by(2) in R^(4m+4). All slow\nmoduli are at most1/(8S), with explicit logarithmic physical scales.\nSelect z_base=z_(0,1), whose parameters are\n\n    r=1/(8S), alpha=3/(32S), sigma=1/(64S^2).\n\nThe actual schedule has S>=100. In the next base-mode calculation,\nwrite p_i=P_i(z_base). Every factor contracts because h_i r<=1/8<2t_1.\nHence 7/8<=|p_i|<=1. Define\n\n    f_0^base=3alpha/4,\n    f_(i+1)^base=f_i^base\n                         -h_i sigma(1-alpha h_i/2)|p_i|^2.\n\nThe decrements are nonnegative and total at most sigma S=alpha/6, so\n\n    7alpha/12<=f_i^base<=3alpha/4.                     (15)\n\nFor EVERY finite pair i<j, let T=sum_(i<=s<j)h_s. Telescoping the\ncontracting products gives |p_s-p_i|<=r(s_s-s_i)|p_i|, with s_s-s_i\nthe intervening step sum. Therefore\n\n    |p_j-p_i|>=rT|p_i|(1-rT/2)>=(15/16)rT|p_i|.       (16)\n\nFor the closed path i,...,j,i, its signed area is\nA_ij=sigma sum_(i<=s<t<j)h_s h_t Im(conj(p_s)p_t).\nTelescoping the difference between a subproduct and its conjugate,\nwith all remaining factors of modulus at most1, gives\n|Im(conj(p_s)p_t)|<=|p_i|^2 |Im z_base| T. Consequently\n\n    |(Im z_base)A_ij|<=sigma^2 T^3|p_i|^2/2.          (17)\n\nThe trapezoidal work identity and the scalar edge identity show that\nthe two ordered pair slacks equal\n(kappa/2)|p_j-p_i|^2 +/- (Im z_base)A_ij. Since\nkappa>=5alpha/6 and sigma T<=alpha/6, (16)-(17) bound both slacks below by\n\n    [1125/3072-1/12]alpha sigma T^2|p_i|^2\n        >(alpha sigma/4)T^2|p_i|^2.\n\nEach h_i>1, so T>1, and |p_i|>=7/8. Thus all ordered finite residuals\nare at most -delta, where delta=alpha sigma/8.\n\nThe optimum constraints have this margin too. The lower slack is at least\n7alpha/12-sigma/2>alpha/2, and the upper slack is at least\n\n    (alpha-sigma/2)(49/64)-3alpha/4\n      =alpha[1/64-49/(768S)]>alpha/100>delta.           (18)\n\nThis verifies ALL base pairs including the true optimum.\n\nAssign zero temporary values to every non-base mode; these values are\nnot individually legal. Every mode has |z|<=1. Put M=exp(S), K=4M^2,\nepsilon=delta/[2(K+delta)], and give the base weight1-epsilon and\neach of the other2m+1 modes weight epsilon/(2m+1). Use actual vectors(2)\nand JOINT values f_i=(1-epsilon)f_i^base, f_star=0. All weights are\npositive and sum to1, so ||x_0||=1 exactly.\n\nFor any non-base mode, |P_i(z)|<=M and |zP_i(z)|<=M, so every ordered\nresidual with its temporary zero values is at most4M^2=K. Orthogonality\nof the blocks makes each joint residual the weighted sum of these\ncomponent residuals. Consequently\n\n    Q_ij<=-(1-epsilon)delta+epsilon K=-delta/2<0        (19)\n\nfor EVERY distinct ordered finite or optimum pair. All cycles therefore\nhold on this same joint data. In particular the positive K(z_fast,l)\ntriangle weights in(11), which forbid individual legality, are repaired\njointly by the slow data; no modewise legality assumption has slipped in.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 now gives a concrete everywhere finite convex\n1-smooth function on R^(4m+4):\n\n    H(v)=max_(i in{star,0,...,q})\n      {<g_i,x_i>-f_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                            (20)\n\nIt interpolates these same vectors and values, with F(0)=min F=0.\nThis is the full finite-dimensional realization, not a covariance kernel\nor a different function selected for each constraint.\n\n## 6. One terminal vector checks EVERY anchor for ALL final b\n\nLet Z be the orthogonal projection of the BASE BLOCK of x_q onto the\nline perpendicular to the base block of g_q, with zero coordinates in\nall other blocks. Then <Z,g_q>=0 and\n\n    ||Z||^2=(1-epsilon)|p_q|^2(1-t_1^2).\n\nAs K>=4 and delta=alpha sigma/8<1, epsilon<1/8. Hence\n\n    ||Z||^2>=(7/8)(49/64)(7/16)=2401/8192>2/7.        (21)\n\nSet w=alpha Z. This is one fixed vector, independent of the final b,\nwith <w,g_q>=0 and ||w||<=alpha.
