For the optimum anchor in(20),\n\n    2<x_q,w>-||w||^2=(2alpha-alpha^2)||Z||^2>=alpha/2,\n\nsince alpha<=1/4 and(21) holds.\n\nFor each finite anchor put y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nWe have ||g_i||^2<=sigma+epsilon M^2<=sigma+delta/8<=2sigma.\nAlso the base block of x_q-y_i has norm at most rS+r<=101/800,\nusing S>=100 and contraction. Other blocks do not contribute to its\ninner product with w. From(15) and1-epsilon>=7/8,\n\n    2delta_i>=49alpha/48-2sigma.\n\nThus every finite anchor satisfies\n\n    2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,w>-||w||^2\n       >=alpha[49/48-1/300-101/400-3/3200]>alpha/2.     (22)\n\nHere 2sigma/alpha=1/(3S)<=1/300 and alpha<=3/3200 were used.\nEquations(21)-(22) check every actual anchor in(20). For every real b,\n\n    2F(x_q-b g_q)\n      >=2<x_q-b g_q,w>-2H(w)\n       =min_i{2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,w>-||w||^2}\n       >=alpha/2=3/(64S),\n\nwith y_star=delta_star=0. This proves(5) for the SAME fixed function\nand prefix, including all nonnegative final steps. The new terminal\ngradient is free and need not be a spectral polynomial.\n\nAll losses are now explicit. Balanced primitive splits give\nC_(2^(m-1)-1)>=lambda^(m-1), C_k increases by the split Omega(C_(k-1),1),\nand the dominant split with right length0 gives B_n>=2C_(n-1).\nThus B_n>=2lambda^(m-1), and the constructed family satisfies\n\n    liminf_m B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(32lambda e)>0        (23)\n\nuniformly for ALL real b. The whole spectral class nevertheless obeys\nthe upper bound(4) at every b in[0,2]. The construction has the benchmark\norder with the loss(23), not the sharp benchmark constant.\n\n## 7. Exact scope left open\n\nThe donor condition |z|<=R/S is essential to the cubic credit estimate.\nMixtures containing intermediate frequencies, including illegal modes\nat many larger physical scales, are outside this theorem. Such modes\nmay supply more triangle credit and must be checked against other common\ncycles; a union of individual-mode exclusions would not settle them.\nArbitrary normal mixtures, nonnormal cross-mode models, and the original\nall-horizon/all-schedule exact target remain unresolved.\n\nMatlas returned unrelated momentum and Ritz-value statements; none is\nused. The exact cycle and all construction estimates are proved above.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "prior_audit": "35d2f3dd8f474b19", "other_joint_ansatz": "b11a7ebe86847ffc"}, "glossary": {"lambda": "1+sqrt(2), the silver ratio.", "S": "Sum of the ACTUAL retained word H_m with its original final d_0 step removed.", "q": "Last queried index n-1, where n=2^m-1, in this finding.", "Slow_m(R)": "All complex spectral parameters z with |z|<=R/S.", "Fast_m": "All z=1-u/m±i sqrt(u-theta)/sqrt(m), 5<=u<=6 and1/2<=theta<=1.", "K": "In Section2 K(z) is the scalar weight of the last-three-query reverse cycle; in Section5 the explicitly reintroduced scalar K=4 exp(2S) bounds non-base pair residuals.", "E": "Squared-chord energy divided by |z|^2 for the local last-two-step triangle.", "A": "Polynomial P_(q-2), scaling the last-three-query triangle.", "Z": "Projection of the base block of x_q onto the line perpendicular to the base block of g_q, extended by zero on other blocks.", "w": "The one fixed terminal test vector alpha Z, perpendicular to the full joint g_q.", "delta": "alpha sigma/8, strict pair margin for the base mode.", "epsilon": "delta/[2(4 exp(2S)+delta)], the total non-base probability."}}
{"id": "abc85cd67257bcf4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:55:09.731401+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "General full-matrix credit-removal candidate with an EXACT physical cost: any actual E_theta certificate,0<=theta<2, for nonnegative h yields an ordinary certificate for h'=((2-theta)/(2+theta s))h of the SAME length. Every actual multiplier is scaled by2/(2+theta s), and the full new remainder is a positive multiple of the old remainder plus two explicit PSD forms. Capacity becomes C'=2C/[2+theta(C-1)]. This works with all remote edges and zero steps, but changes h and therefore does not prove same-schedule cone equality.", "evidence": "# General removal of terminal credit with an explicit physical contraction\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. This result changes\nthe physical steps and does not prove same-schedule credit re-selection.\nIt uses all actual interpolation coefficients and a full PSD identity.\nNo solver or verifier call, and no external theorem used as a premise.\n\n## 1. Statement, including zero steps\n\nFix n>=0 and a nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Write\ns=sum h_i, C=1+s. On independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_n and values\nf_0,...,f_n, substitute x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and put\n\n Q_ij^h=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                            -s(s+1)||g_n||^2,\n J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n)^h,\n E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.\n\nSuppose 0<=theta<2 and actual nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij for all\ndistinct finite pairs give an exact certificate\n\n              E_theta+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij^h=M,       (1)\n\nwhere all value coefficients vanish and M is PSD on the FULL independent\ngradient space. This includes the project range 0<=theta<1.\n\nDefine positive parameters and the contracted schedule by\n\n a=1-theta/2,\n kappa=theta C/(2-theta),\n t=1/(1+kappa)=(2-theta)/(2+theta s),\n c=2/(2+theta s),\n h'_i=t h_i,     Lambda'_ij=c Lambda_ij.                  (2)\n\nThen Lambda' is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_(h') with the\ncomplete PSD remainder\n\n V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')\n       =(t^2/a)[M+kappa(S_Lambda+a Var_h)],               (3)\n\nwhere\n\n S_Lambda=(1/2)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2,\n Var_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2+||g_n||^2\n                     -(1/C)||sum_(i<n)h_i g_i+g_n||^2.   (4)\n\nBoth added forms are PSD, with no missing gradient directions. Every\nactual output multiplier is nonnegative because c>0. Zero steps remain\nzero, and the proof works on the original formal space without identifying\nduplicate gradients or invoking compression. Empty and all-zero schedules\nare included.\n\nThe actual physical capacity after this same-length conversion is exactly\n\n             C'=1+sum h'_i=2C/[2+theta(C-1)].             (5)\n\nNo prefix or bridge is appended. The loss in (5) must be charged before\nclaiming an improving ordinary seed.\n\n## 2. Two identities before changing the schedule\n\nPut H_g=sum_(i<n)h_i g_i and define the rank-at-most-two quadratic\n\n                    D_h=||H_g+g_n||^2-C^2||g_n||^2.\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n                    V_h-2J_h=D_h,                       (6)\n                    E_theta=a V_h+(1-a)D_h.\n\nHere is an explicit verification of (6). The value terms cancel. Write\nx_n-x_i=-sum_(j=i)^(n-1)h_j g_j in J_h. Expanding 2J_h and canceling the\ndiagonal norm terms in V_h leaves\n\n 2sum_(i<=j<n)h_i h_j<g_i,g_j>-sum_(i<n)h_i^2||g_i||^2\n     +2<H_g,g_n>-[s(s+1)+s]||g_n||^2.\n\nThe first two terms equal ||H_g||^2, and s(s+1)+s=C^2-1. The displayed\nexpression is therefore D_h, proving (6).\n\nAlso define the formal scalar value combination\n\n                    F_h=sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n).\n\nThe value part of E_theta is 2a F_h. Exact cancellation in (1) forces\n\n              sum Lambda_ij(f_i-f_j)=-2a F_h.            (7)\n\nThis checks the complete finite-node flow, not only its total sum.\n\n## 3. Complete coefficient identity under contraction\n\nAll query differences scale by t, so, as an identity in independent\nvalues and gradients,\n\n Q_ij^(h')=t Q_ij^h\n                +(1-t)[f_i-f_j+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].         (8)\n\nLet K_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2-s||g_n||^2. The value and gradient terms of\nV_(h') give exactly\n\n                 V_(h')=t^2 V_h+t(1-t)(2F_h+K_h).       (9)\n\nUsing Lambda'=c Lambda, equations (1), (6), (7), and (8) now yield\n\n V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')\n  =V_(h')+ct[M-aV_h-(1-a)D_h]\n                         +c(1-t)[-2aF_h+S_Lambda].      (10)\n\nThe parameter identities t=ac, ct=t^2/a, 1-t=kappa t and\n1-a=a kappa/C are exact consequences of (2). Substitute (9) into (10):\nthe V_h coefficients cancel since cta=t^2, and the F_h coefficients cancel\nsince ca=t. The remaining expression is\n\n ct M+t(1-t)K_h-ct(1-a)D_h+c(1-t)S_Lambda\n   =ct[M+kappa S_Lambda+a kappa(K_h-D_h/C)].              (11)\n\nFinally K_h-D_h/C is precisely Var_h, because C-s=1. Equations (11) and\nct=t^2/a prove the entire formal identity (3).\n\n## 4. Positivity on the full formal space\n\nS_Lambda is a nonnegative sum of squared gradient differences. For Var_h,\nassign weights w_i=h_i for i<n and w_n=1, whose sum is C>0. Expanding\nthe squared differences gives\n\n Var_h=(1/C)sum_(0<=i<j<=n)w_i w_j||g_i-g_j||^2>=0.     (12)\n\nThis remains valid when any h_i vanishes. Since a,t>0 and kappa>=0, (3)\nand the assumed full PSD of M prove full PSD of the repaired matrix.\nAll output interpolation coefficients are positive scalar multiples of\nactual nonnegative coefficients, so none of their signs is omitted.\nThe cancellation of the independent F_h terms in Section 3 explicitly\nchecks the new value flow. This establishes the statement in Section 1.\n\nFor an all-zero schedule, t=a, c=1, Var_h=0, and the input value flow is\nzero. Its remainder is M=S_Lambda, so (3) reduces to the same matrix M.\nFor n=0 every sum of distinct finite pairs is empty and M=0. Thus these\nboundary cases satisfy the formula directly.\n\n## 5. Explicit target-relevance cost\n\nSumming the contracted physical steps gives (5). Let K>1 be any desired\ncapacity threshold for an ordinary seed of this same length. Algebra shows\n\n C'>K\n   iff (2-theta K)C>K(2-theta).                         (13)\n\nConsequently a necessary condition is theta<2/K. When theta<2/K,\ncondition (13) is equivalent to the explicit input requirement\n\n                    C>K(2-theta)/(2-theta K).            (14)\n\nIf theta>=2/K and theta<2, no finite C>=1 can satisfy C'>K: the left side\nof (13) is nonpositive and its right side is strictly positive. In\nparticular, for the previously proposed useful primitive-seed threshold\nK=(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2), this same-length conversion requires\ntheta<2/(n+1)^rho and then (14). This is only an exact cost comparison;\nthe conditional asymptotic amplification argument is not invoked here as\na verified premise, and no seed meeting the threshold has been found.\n\nWhen theta>0 is fixed and C tends to infinity, C' tends to2/theta. Thus\nfixed positive credit cannot yield unbounded ordinary capacity by THIS\nconversion. An improving sequence by this mechanism must control theta\nat the corresponding inverse-capacity scale. Other conversions with\nprefixes or bridges may behave differently and are not excluded.\n\n## 6. A useful corollary about uniform contraction\n\nGiven an ordinary nonnegative certificate for h, adding theta h_i to\nevery incoming-terminal multiplier makes (1) hold with the SAME M for\nany 0<=theta<2: those additions cancel -theta J_h. For any prescribed\n0<t<=1, choose\n\n                    theta=2(1-t)/(1+t s).\n\nIt lies in [0,2), and (2) gives exactly that t. Therefore every uniform\ncontraction t h of a certified ordinary schedule has the explicit ordinary\ncertificate (3). This statement concerns certificates on the full formal\nspace, including zero steps, and does not assert that enlarging steps is\nsafe or that arbitrary independent contractions preserve the property.\n\n## Remaining issue\n\nThis general transformation removes credit by paying an exact physical\nsum loss. It neither fixes h nor proves that E_theta and V_h have the same\ncertificate cone. The same-schedule problem remains open outside the\nsaturated-cut and weighted-cut repair classes. Any target-level use must\nproduce a concrete E_theta family surviving (5), an exact ordinary\ncertificate as above, and the full infinite-horizon benchmark comparison.\n\n\nExact audit performed this round: all5 values and15 independent Gram monomials match for the complete h=(1,2,1,1),theta=1/2 certificate, giving h'=h/3 and C'=8/3. Also checked all original independent coefficients with an interior zero-step h=(1,0),theta=3/5 and an all-zero h=(0,0),theta=3/2. All three fixed rational audits passed; no SDP/PEP or verifier call. The tiny exact audit is local_memory/check_credit_contraction.py, results credit_contraction_exact_audit.json.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"related_findings": ["5bd01e2b19002a59", "36fc441692438487", "83671fcd8ba27b46", "2973c2322cdee545"], "local_evidence": "local_memory/credit_uniform_contraction.md"}, "glossary": {"C": "1+sum h_i, the input physical capacity.", "C_prime": "1+sum h'_i=2C/[2+theta(C-1)], the output physical capacity.", "a": "1-theta/2, positive for theta<2.", "kappa": "theta C/(2-theta), the contraction parameter.", "t": "(2-theta)/(2+theta s), the common physical step scaling.", "c": "2/(2+theta s), the positive actual-multiplier scaling.", "Lambda": "Actual nonnegative E_theta multipliers on all distinct finite interpolation pairs.", "S_Lambda": "Half the Lambda-weighted sum of squared gradient differences, equation(4).", "Var_h": "Weighted variance of all original independent gradients with weights(h_0,...,h_(n-1),1).", "D_h": "||sum h_i g_i+g_n||²-C²||g_n||², equal identically to V_h-2J_h.", "F_h": "The formal value combination sum h_i(f_i-f_n).", "K_h": "The quadratic sum h_i||g_i||²-s||g_n||²."}}
{"id": "aad4e2d16318961c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:56:51.762901+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Sharpness upgrade to the general credit contraction: for s>0, the ONLY ordinary schedule whose full certificate uses a positive GLOBAL SCALAR MULTIPLE of an actual E_theta multiplier array is h'=((2-theta)/(2+theta s))h, with multiplier factor2/(2+theta s). Thus the exact capacity cost2C/[2+theta(C-1)] is unavoidable within this entire proportional-array conversion class; fixed-h repair at theta>0 must change relative multipliers.", "evidence": "# General removal of terminal credit with an explicit physical contraction\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. This includes the project range 0<=theta<1.\n\nDefine positive parameters and the contracted schedule by\n\n a=1-theta/2,\n kappa=theta C/(2-theta),\n t=1/(1+kappa)=(2-theta)/(2+theta s),\n c=2/(2+theta s),\n h'_i=t h_i,     Lambda'_ij=c Lambda_ij.                  (2)\n\nThen Lambda' is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_(h') with the\ncomplete PSD remainder\n\n V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')\n       =(t^2/a)[M+kappa(S_Lambda+a Var_h)],               (3)\n\nwhere\n\n S_Lambda=(1/2)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2,\n Var_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2+||g_n||^2\n                     -(1/C)||sum_(i<n)h_i g_i+g_n||^2.   (4)\n\nBoth added forms are PSD, with no missing gradient directions. Every\nactual output multiplier is nonnegative because c>0. Zero steps remain\nzero, and the proof works on the original formal space without identifying\nduplicate gradients or invoking compression. Empty and all-zero schedules\nare included.\n\nThe actual physical capacity after this same-length conversion is exactly\n\n             C'=1+sum h'_i=2C/[2+theta(C-1)].             (5)\n\nNo prefix or bridge is appended. The loss in (5) must be charged before\nclaiming an improving ordinary seed.\n\n## 2. Two identities before changing the schedule\n\nPut H_g=sum_(i<n)h_i g_i and define the rank-at-most-two quadratic\n\n                    D_h=||H_g+g_n||^2-C^2||g_n||^2.\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n                    V_h-2J_h=D_h,                       (6)\n                    E_theta=a V_h+(1-a)D_h.\n\nHere is an explicit verification of (6). The value terms cancel. Write\nx_n-x_i=-sum_(j=i)^(n-1)h_j g_j in J_h. Expanding 2J_h and canceling the\ndiagonal norm terms in V_h leaves\n\n 2sum_(i<=j<n)h_i h_j<g_i,g_j>-sum_(i<n)h_i^2||g_i||^2\n     +2<H_g,g_n>-[s(s+1)+s]||g_n||^2.\n\nThe first two terms equal ||H_g||^2, and s(s+1)+s=C^2-1. The displayed\nexpression is therefore D_h, proving (6).\n\nAlso define the formal scalar value combination\n\n                    F_h=sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n).\n\nThe value part of E_theta is 2a F_h. Exact cancellation in (1) forces\n\n              sum Lambda_ij(f_i-f_j)=-2a F_h.            (7)\n\nThis checks the complete finite-node flow, not only its total sum.\n\n## 3. Complete coefficient identity under contraction\n\nAll query differences scale by t, so, as an identity in independent\nvalues and gradients,\n\n Q_ij^(h')=t Q_ij^h\n                +(1-t)[f_i-f_j+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].         (8)\n\nLet K_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2-s||g_n||^2. The value and gradient terms of\nV_(h') give exactly\n\n                 V_(h')=t^2 V_h+t(1-t)(2F_h+K_h).       (9)\n\nUsing Lambda'=c Lambda, equations (1), (6), (7), and (8) now yield\n\n V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')\n  =V_(h')+ct[M-aV_h-(1-a)D_h]\n                         +c(1-t)[-2aF_h+S_Lambda].      (10)\n\nThe parameter identities t=ac, ct=t^2/a, 1-t=kappa t and\n1-a=a kappa/C are exact consequences of (2). Substitute (9) into (10):\nthe V_h coefficients cancel since cta=t^2, and the F_h coefficients cancel\nsince ca=t. The remaining expression is\n\n ct M+t(1-t)K_h-ct(1-a)D_h+c(1-t)S_Lambda\n   =ct[M+kappa S_Lambda+a kappa(K_h-D_h/C)].              (11)\n\nFinally K_h-D_h/C is precisely Var_h, because C-s=1. Equations (11) and\nct=t^2/a prove the entire formal identity (3).\n\n## 4. Positivity on the full formal space\n\nS_Lambda is a nonnegative sum of squared gradient differences. For Var_h,\nassign weights w_i=h_i for i<n and w_n=1, whose sum is C>0. Expanding\nthe squared differences gives\n\n Var_h=(1/C)sum_(0<=i<j<=n)w_i w_j||g_i-g_j||^2>=0.     (12)\n\nThis remains valid when any h_i vanishes. Since a,t>0 and kappa>=0, (3)\nand the assumed full PSD of M prove full PSD of the repaired matrix.\nAll output interpolation coefficients are positive scalar multiples of\nactual nonnegative coefficients, so none of their signs is omitted.\nThe cancellation of the independent F_h terms in Section 3 explicitly\nchecks the new value flow. This establishes the statement in Section 1.\n\nFor an all-zero schedule, t=a, c=1, Var_h=0, and the input value flow is\nzero. Its remainder is M=S_Lambda, so (3) reduces to the same matrix M.\nFor n=0 every sum of distinct finite pairs is empty and M=0. Thus these\nboundary cases satisfy the formula directly.\n\n## 5. Explicit target-relevance cost\n\nSumming the contracted physical steps gives (5). Let K>1 be any desired\ncapacity threshold for an ordinary seed of this same length. Algebra shows\n\n C'>K\n   iff (2-theta K)C>K(2-theta).                         (13)\n\nConsequently a necessary condition is theta<2/K. When theta<2/K,\ncondition (13) is equivalent to the explicit input requirement\n\n                    C>K(2-theta)/(2-theta K).            (14)\n\nIf theta>=2/K and theta<2, no finite C>=1 can satisfy C'>K: the left side\nof (13) is nonpositive and its right side is strictly positive. In\nparticular, for the previously proposed useful primitive-seed threshold\nK=(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2), this same-length conversion requires\ntheta<2/(n+1)^rho and then (14). This is only an exact cost comparison;\nthe conditional asymptotic amplification argument is not invoked here as\na verified premise, and no seed meeting the threshold has been found.\n\nWhen theta>0 is fixed and C tends to infinity, C' tends to2/theta. Thus\nfixed positive credit cannot yield unbounded ordinary capacity by THIS\nconversion. An improving sequence by this mechanism must control theta\nat the corresponding inverse-capacity scale. Other conversions with\nprefixes or bridges may behave differently and are not excluded.\n\n## 6. A useful corollary about uniform contraction\n\nGiven an ordinary nonnegative certificate for h, adding theta h_i to\nevery incoming-terminal multiplier makes (1) hold with the SAME M for\nany 0<=theta<2: those additions cancel -theta J_h. For any prescribed\n0<t<=1, choose\n\n                    theta=2(1-t)/(1+t s).\n\nIt lies in [0,2), and (2) gives exactly that t. Therefore every uniform\ncontraction t h of a certified ordinary schedule has the explicit ordinary\ncertificate (3). This statement concerns certificates on the full formal\nspace, including zero steps, and does not assert that enlarging steps is\nsafe or that arbitrary independent contractions preserve the property.\n\n## 7. The cost is forced for every proportional-multiplier conversion\n\nAssume s>0. Suppose an ordinary certificate for SOME nonnegative schedule\nq=(q_0,...,q_(n-1)) uses multipliers c_0 Lambda with a single c_0>0 and\nhas a PSD remainder on the full independent-gradient space. Then q and\nc_0 must be EXACTLY the h' and c in (2). Thus (5) is not merely one\nconservative choice within this proportional-multiplier class.\n\nIndeed (7) and ordinary value cancellation require, separately at every\nnonterminal index, q_i=c_0 a h_i. Hence q=t_0 h with t_0=c_0 a>0.\nThe expansion (10)-(11), before imposing the special value of t, gives\nthe complete output matrix\n\n M_out=c_0 t_0 M+t_0(1-t_0)K_h\n       -c_0 t_0(1-a)D_h+c_0(1-t_0)S_Lambda.              (15)\n\nLet e be the all-one formal gradient column and v=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),-s)^T.\nThe input M has M e=0: evaluate its value-canceling identity on a legal\nconstant-gradient affine Huber segment to get e^T M e=0, then use full\nPSD. Also S_Lambda e=0, K_h e=v and D_h e=Cv, where quadratic forms\nare identified with their symmetric matrices. Consequently\n\n M_out e=t_0[(1-t_0)-c_0(1-a)C]v.                       (16)\n\nAll four terms of (15) have zero quadratic value on e, so e^T M_out e=0.\nThe assumed full output PSD forces M_out e=0. Since s>0, v is nonzero;\nsince t_0>0, equation (16), together with c_0=t_0/a, uniquely forces\n\n          1-t_0=t_0(1-a)C/a,\n          t_0=a/[a+(1-a)C]=(2-theta)/(2+theta s).\n\nIt then forces c_0=t_0/a=c. This proves the uniqueness claim, including\nschedules with some zero steps. In particular, when theta>0 and s>0,\nsimply normalizing or globally rescaling the ACTUAL E_theta multipliers\ncannot repair the original unchanged physical schedule. Any such repair\nmust also change the relative multiplier array or use a different method.\nThe all-zero case is excluded from this uniqueness assertion because\nv=0, and its physical schedule remains zero under every scaling.\n\n## Remaining issue\n\nThis general transformation removes credit by paying an exact physical\nsum loss. It neither fixes h nor proves that E_theta and V_h have the same\ncertificate cone. The same-schedule problem remains open outside the\nsaturated-cut and weighted-cut repair classes. Any target-level use must\nproduce a concrete E_theta family surviving (5), an exact ordinary\ncertificate as above, and the full infinite-horizon benchmark comparison.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"extends_finding_id": "abc85cd67257bcf4", "local_evidence": "local_memory/credit_uniform_contraction.md", "status": "unverified; no new solver or verifier call"}, "glossary": {"a": "1-theta/2.", "t_0": "Candidate common physical step factor, forced by independent value flow.", "c_0": "Candidate positive global factor applied to the actual E_theta multiplier array.", "v": "Nonzero affine-kernel direction (h_0,...,h_(n-1),-s)^T when s>0.", "M_out": "Full independent-gradient remainder of a candidate ordinary proportional-array certificate."}}
{"id": "c6a9d1e4910e3681", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:07:21.222586+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "General coefficient obstruction added to the credit-contraction package: for s>0 every actual E_theta full-PSD certificate obeys sum_(j<n)Lambda_(n,j)(s-P_j)=2s(s+1), hence total incoming-terminal mass is at least2+theta s. For theta>0 NO ordinary certificate for the unchanged h can dominate the actual Lambda entrywise, even if its full PSD matrix is completely changed. The proof includes all zero-step cases. This leaves arbitrary subtract-and-add re-selection open.", "evidence": "# General removal of terminal credit with an explicit physical contraction\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. This result changes\nthe physical steps and does not prove same-schedule credit re-selection.\nIt uses all actual interpolation coefficients and a full PSD identity.\nNo solver or verifier call, and no external theorem used as a premise.\n\n## 1. Statement, including zero steps\n\nFix n>=0 and a nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Write\ns=sum h_i, C=1+s. On independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_n and values\nf_0,...,f_n, substitute x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and put\n\n Q_ij^h=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                            -s(s+1)||g_n||^2,\n J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n)^h,\n E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.\n\nSuppose 0<=theta<2 and actual nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij for all\ndistinct finite pairs give an exact certificate\n\n              E_theta+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij^h=M,       (1)\n\nwhere all value coefficients vanish and M is PSD on the FULL independent\ngradient space. This includes the project range 0<=theta<1.\n\nDefine positive parameters and the contracted schedule by\n\n a=1-theta/2,\n kappa=theta C/(2-theta),\n t=1/(1+kappa)=(2-theta)/(2+theta s),\n c=2/(2+theta s),\n h'_i=t h_i,     Lambda'_ij=c Lambda_ij.                  (2)\n\nThen Lambda' is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_(h') with the\ncomplete PSD remainder\n\n V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')\n       =(t^2/a)[M+kappa(S_Lambda+a Var_h)],               (3)\n\nwhere\n\n S_Lambda=(1/2)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2,\n Var_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2+||g_n||^2\n                     -(1/C)||sum_(i<n)h_i g_i+g_n||^2.   (4)\n\nBoth added forms are PSD, with no missing gradient directions. Every\nactual output multiplier is nonnegative because c>0. Zero steps remain\nzero, and the proof works on the original formal space without identifying\nduplicate gradients or invoking compression. Empty and all-zero schedules\nare included.\n\nThe actual physical capacity after this same-length conversion is exactly\n\n             C'=1+sum h'_i=2C/[2+theta(C-1)].             (5)\n\nNo prefix or bridge is appended. The loss in (5) must be charged before\nclaiming an improving ordinary seed.\n\n## 2. Two identities before changing the schedule\n\nPut H_g=sum_(i<n)h_i g_i and define the rank-at-most-two quadratic\n\n                    D_h=||H_g+g_n||^2-C^2||g_n||^2.\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n                    V_h-2J_h=D_h,                       (6)\n                    E_theta=a V_h+(1-a)D_h.\n\nHere is an explicit verification of (6). The value terms cancel. Write\nx_n-x_i=-sum_(j=i)^(n-1)h_j g_j in J_h. Expanding 2J_h and canceling the\ndiagonal norm terms in V_h leaves\n\n 2sum_(i<=j<n)h_i h_j<g_i,g_j>-sum_(i<n)h_i^2||g_i||^2\n     +2<H_g,g_n>-[s(s+1)+s]||g_n||^2.\n\nThe first two terms equal ||H_g||^2, and s(s+1)+s=C^2-1. The displayed\nexpression is therefore D_h, proving (6).\n\nAlso define the formal scalar value combination\n\n                    F_h=sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n).\n\nThe value part of E_theta is 2a F_h. Exact cancellation in (1) forces\n\n              sum Lambda_ij(f_i-f_j)=-2a F_h.            (7)\n\nThis checks the complete finite-node flow, not only its total sum.\n\n## 3. Complete coefficient identity under contraction\n\nAll query differences scale by t, so, as an identity in independent\nvalues and gradients,\n\n Q_ij^(h')=t Q_ij^h\n                +(1-t)[f_i-f_j+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].         (8)\n\nLet K_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2-s||g_n||^2. The value and gradient terms of\nV_(h') give exactly\n\n                 V_(h')=t^2 V_h+t(1-t)(2F_h+K_h).       (9)\n\nUsing Lambda'=c Lambda, equations (1), (6), (7), and (8) now yield\n\n V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')\n  =V_(h')+ct[M-aV_h-(1-a)D_h]\n                         +c(1-t)[-2aF_h+S_Lambda].      (10)\n\nThe parameter identities t=ac, ct=t^2/a, 1-t=kappa t and\n1-a=a kappa/C are exact consequences of (2). Substitute (9) into (10):\nthe V_h coefficients cancel since cta=t^2, and the F_h coefficients cancel\nsince ca=t. The remaining expression is\n\n ct M+t(1-t)K_h-ct(1-a)D_h+c(1-t)S_Lambda\n   =ct[M+kappa S_Lambda+a kappa(K_h-D_h/C)].              (11)\n\nFinally K_h-D_h/C is precisely Var_h, because C-s=1. Equations (11) and\nct=t^2/a prove the entire formal identity (3).\n\n## 4. Positivity on the full formal space\n\nS_Lambda is a nonnegative sum of squared gradient differences. For Var_h,\nassign weights w_i=h_i for i<n and w_n=1, whose sum is C>0. Expanding\nthe squared differences gives\n\n Var_h=(1/C)sum_(0<=i<j<=n)w_i w_j||g_i-g_j||^2>=0.     (12)\n\nThis remains valid when any h_i vanishes. Since a,t>0 and kappa>=0, (3)\nand the assumed full PSD of M prove full PSD of the repaired matrix.\nAll output interpolation coefficients are positive scalar multiples of\nactual nonnegative coefficients, so none of their signs is omitted.\nThe cancellation of the independent F_h terms in Section 3 explicitly\nchecks the new value flow. This establishes the statement in Section 1.\n\nFor an all-zero schedule, t=a, c=1, Var_h=0, and the input value flow is\nzero. Its remainder is M=S_Lambda, so (3) reduces to the same matrix M.\nFor n=0 every sum of distinct finite pairs is empty and M=0. Thus these\nboundary cases satisfy the formula directly.\n\n## 5. Explicit target-relevance cost\n\nSumming the contracted physical steps gives (5). Let K>1 be any desired\ncapacity threshold for an ordinary seed of this same length. Algebra shows\n\n C'>K\n   iff (2-theta K)C>K(2-theta).                         (13)\n\nConsequently a necessary condition is theta<2/K. When theta<2/K,\ncondition (13) is equivalent to the explicit input requirement\n\n                    C>K(2-theta)/(2-theta K).            (14)\n\nIf theta>=2/K and theta<2, no finite C>=1 can satisfy C'>K: the left side\nof (13) is nonpositive and its right side is strictly positive. In\nparticular, for the previously proposed useful primitive-seed threshold\nK=(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2), this same-length conversion requires\ntheta<2/(n+1)^rho and then (14). This is only an exact cost comparison;\nthe conditional asymptotic amplification argument is not invoked here as\na verified premise, and no seed meeting the threshold has been found.\n\nWhen theta>0 is fixed and C tends to infinity, C' tends to2/theta. Thus\nfixed positive credit cannot yield unbounded ordinary capacity by THIS\nconversion. An improving sequence by this mechanism must control theta\nat the corresponding inverse-capacity scale. Other conversions with\nprefixes or bridges may behave differently and are not excluded.\n\n## 6. A useful corollary about uniform contraction\n\nGiven an ordinary nonnegative certificate for h, adding theta h_i to\nevery incoming-terminal multiplier makes (1) hold with the SAME M for\nany 0<=theta<2: those additions cancel -theta J_h. For any prescribed\n0<t<=1, choose\n\n                    theta=2(1-t)/(1+t s).\n\nIt lies in [0,2), and (2) gives exactly that t. Therefore every uniform\ncontraction t h of a certified ordinary schedule has the explicit ordinary\ncertificate (3). This statement concerns certificates on the full formal\nspace, including zero steps, and does not assert that enlarging steps is\nsafe or that arbitrary independent contractions preserve the property.\n\n## 7. The cost is forced for every proportional-multiplier conversion\n\nAssume s>0. Suppose an ordinary certificate for SOME nonnegative schedule\nq=(q_0,...,q_(n-1)) uses multipliers c_0 Lambda with a single c_0>0 and\nhas a PSD remainder on the full independent-gradient space. Then q and\nc_0 must be EXACTLY the h' and c in (2). Thus (5) is not merely one\nconservative choice within this proportional-multiplier class.\n\nIndeed (7) and ordinary value cancellation require, separately at every\nnonterminal index, q_i=c_0 a h_i. Hence q=t_0 h with t_0=c_0 a>0.\nThe expansion (10)-(11), before imposing the special value of t, gives\nthe complete output matrix\n\n M_out=c_0 t_0 M+t_0(1-t_0)K_h\n       -c_0 t_0(1-a)D_h+c_0(1-t_0)S_Lambda.              (15)\n\nLet e be the all-one formal gradient column and v=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),-s)^T.\nThe input M has M e=0: evaluate its value-canceling identity on a legal\nconstant-gradient affine Huber segment to get e^T M e=0, then use full\nPSD. Also S_Lambda e=0, K_h e=v and D_h e=Cv, where quadratic forms\nare identified with their symmetric matrices. Consequently\n\n M_out e=t_0[(1-t_0)-c_0(1-a)C]v.                       (16)\n\nAll four terms of (15) have zero quadratic value on e, so e^T M_out e=0.\nThe assumed full output PSD forces M_out e=0. Since s>0, v is nonzero;\nsince t_0>0, equation (16), together with c_0=t_0/a, uniquely forces\n\n          1-t_0=t_0(1-a)C/a,\n          t_0=a/[a+(1-a)C]=(2-theta)/(2+theta s).\n\nIt then forces c_0=t_0/a=c. This proves the uniqueness claim, including\nschedules with some zero steps. In particular, when theta>0 and s>0,\nsimply normalizing or globally rescaling the ACTUAL E_theta multipliers\ncannot repair the original unchanged physical schedule. Any such repair\nmust also change the relative multiplier array or use a different method.\nThe all-zero case is excluded from this uniqueness assertion because\nv=0, and its physical schedule remains zero under every scaling.\n\n## 8. Incoming mass floor and failure of monotone coefficient repair\n\nAssume s>0 and retain the ACTUAL nonnegative array Lambda in (1). Let\nP_i=sum_(j<i)h_j, R_i=s-P_i. The full affine kernel forces\n\n               sum_(j<n)Lambda_(n,j) R_j=2sC.            (17)\n\nTo check this directly, identify each gradient quadratic with its symmetric\nmatrix and multiply by the all-one vector e. Each squared-difference form\nin a Q kills e. Positions have no g_n coefficient. Thus only a Q with source\nn contributes to row n of the position part, and its contribution to the\nrow sum is R_j/2. The terminal row of the V_h gradient diagonal contributes\n-sC, while the terminal row sum of J_h is0. Since M e=0, this gives (17).\n\nWrite Out_n=sum_(j<n)Lambda_(n,j) and In_n=sum_(i<n)Lambda_(i,n).\nBecause 0<=R_j<=s and every actual coefficient is nonnegative, (17) yields\nOut_n>=2C. The terminal value-flow equation is\n\n                Out_n-In_n=(2-theta)s.\n\nIt follows that EVERY full E_theta certificate for s>0 satisfies\n\n                    In_n>=2+theta s.                    (18)\n\nIn particular every ordinary intrinsic certificate requires incoming-terminal\nmass at least2. A theorem eliminating all incoming terminal inequalities\nfor a LAST-OBJECTIVE certificate cannot be applied unchanged to this intrinsic\nenergy certificate. Equation (18) is about actual formal certificates; it\ndoes not say which incoming coefficients of a signed V array are negative.\n\nThere is also an obstruction to a simple variable-matrix repair: when\ntheta>0, NO ordinary certificate mu for the SAME physical h can satisfy\nmu_ij>=Lambda_ij for every ordered pair. This allows the entire PSD matrix\nto change; it rules out repair by only adding nonnegative coefficients.\n\nFor strictly positive h, (17) applies equally to mu and Lambda, with R_j>0\nfor every j<n. Entrywise domination therefore forces mu_(n,j)=Lambda_(n,j)\nfor every j<n. But ordinary terminal flow requires its total incoming mass\nto be smaller by exactly theta s, contradicting entrywise domination.\n\nHere is the full zero-step argument. Let T={i:P_i=s}, the final group of\nindices after the last positive step, including n, and I its complement.\nFor any i in T other than n, h_i=0. Its row in the V_h diagonal vanishes;\nits row sum in J_h is zero; and every position coefficient of g_i vanishes\nbecause h_i=0. The affine-kernel equation therefore gives\n\n                sum_j Lambda_ij(s-P_j)=0.\n\nAll summands are nonnegative, so Lambda_ij=0 whenever i is in T other than\nn and j is in I. The same holds for any ordinary mu. Equation (17) and\nentrywise domination force mu_(n,j)=Lambda_(n,j) for j in I, because their\nweights R_j are positive. Thus the total flow OUT OF the set T is unchanged,\nwhile its incoming flow under mu cannot be smaller. Its net outgoing flow\nunder mu is therefore at most its net outgoing flow under Lambda. Summing\nthe value-flow equations over T instead makes these two net flows respectively\n2s and (2-theta)s. Since theta s>0, this is a contradiction. This handles\nevery possible leading, interior and trailing zero step on the full original\nformal space.\n\nThis obstruction does not refute same-schedule repair: a successful repair\nmay subtract some old coefficients while adding others. The proportional\ncontraction (2), for example, has c<1 when theta s>0 and also changes the\nphysical steps. Neither construction violates the obstruction just proved.\n\n## Remaining issue\n\nThis general transformation removes credit by paying an exact physical\nsum loss. It neither fixes h nor proves that E_theta and V_h have the same\ncertificate cone. The same-schedule problem remains open outside the\nsaturated-cut and weighted-cut repair classes. Any target-level use must\nproduce a concrete E_theta family surviving (5), an exact ordinary\ncertificate as above, and the full infinite-horizon benchmark comparison.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"extends_finding_id": "aad4e2d16318961c", "related_findings": ["abc85cd67257bcf4", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "local_evidence": "local_memory/credit_uniform_contraction.md"}, "glossary": {"P_i": "Cumulative physical step sum strictly before index i.", "R_i": "Remaining physical sum s-P_i.", "Out_n": "Total actual interpolation coefficient mass leaving terminal index n.", "In_n": "Total actual interpolation coefficient mass entering terminal index n.", "T": "Final index group {i:P_i=s}, including trailing zero-step indices and n.", "I": "Complement of T in {0,...,n}."}}
{"id": "0b2dfaee171c78eb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:27:13.806672+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Candidate full-matrix subcritical joins and gradient-stable prepending: an actual E_theta tail of capacity D admits a gradient-norm certificate after any physical bridge 1+r with theta D/(2-theta)<=r<=D. Ordinary prefix capacity A and credited tail can also be joined at ANY r>=0 with r(A+D+r)<=AD, using an explicit credit budget and a nonnegative cross-block square. This gives reversal closure of an unbounded recursive subcritical class, not general reversal or an improving objective family.", "evidence": "# Full certificates for gradient-stable prepending and subcritical credit joins\n\nStatus: complete candidate identities, NOT verifier-accepted. No new SDP or\nverifier call. These are actual coefficients on all independent gradients;\nthe result neither asserts general reversal nor an improving objective family.\n\n## 1. Notation\n\nNormalize smoothness to1. Let a and b be nonnegative physical step lists,\nof capacities A=1+sum a_i and D=1+sum b_j. The actual joined schedule is\n\n                       h=(a,1+r,b),   T=A+D+r.\n\nLet L be the query just before its bridge, p=g_L, and z its final query,\nq=g_z. Tail indices B run from the query just after the bridge through z.\nWrite H_b=sum_(j<z,j in B)b_j g_j and W_b=H_b+q. Thus the physical bridge\nand tail relations are x_(L+1)=x_L-(1+r)p and x_z=x_(L+1)-H_b.\n\nEvery Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 uses these actual joined\npositions. V_a and V_b are the respective intrinsic energies\n\n V_c=2sum c_i(f_i-f_end)+sum c_i(1-c_i)||g_i||^2\n                                   -(sum c_i)(1+sum c_i)||g_end||^2,\n\nand J_b=sum_(j<z,j in B)b_j Q_(j,z), E_theta,b=V_b-theta J_b.\nThe prefix endpoint L belongs to V_a; all tail gradients are independent\nof the prefix gradients. Empty lists are allowed, with intrinsic energy0.\n\nFor concise interpolation sums put\n\n G_b=sum_(j<z,j in B)b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L),\n C_r=G_b+r Q_(L,z)+J_b.                                (1)\n\nAssume the input certificates, when invoked, have exact value cancellation\nand full PSD remainders:\n\n V_a+sum mu_ij Q_ij=M_a,      mu_ij>=0,\n E_theta,b+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=M_b,      Lambda_ij>=0.     (2)\n\nTheir finite pairs are embedded in their original prefix and tail indices.\n\n## 2. Direct identities valid without any certificate hypothesis\n\nExpand (1) using the displayed GD relations. The value and gradient parts\nof C_r are exactly\n\n C_r,f=2sum b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z,\n C_r,g=sum b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n               +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.     (3)\n\nFor the gradient calculation, sum the position terms and use\n2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>\n    =||H_b||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2.\nThe terms involving p,H_b,q cancel in (3); the remaining coefficients\nare the displayed ones. Also direct expansion of V_b and J_b gives\n\n                V_b-2J_b=||W_b||^2-D^2||q||^2.          (4)\n\nThe interpolation sum with coefficient D on Q_(L,z), regardless of the\nphysical bridge r, is C_D=G_b+D Q_(L,z)+J_b. Since\n\n Q_(L,z)=f_L-f_z-(r+1/2)||p||^2+||q||^2/2-<p,W_b>,\n\nsubtracting (D-r)Q_(L,z) from V_b-C_r in (3) gives\n\n        V_b-C_D=Dr||p||^2-D^2||q||^2+(D-r)<p,W_b>.     (5)\n\nThese are formal identities in EVERY value and gradient, not just identities\non equality examples.\n\n## 3. Gradient-norm stability from a credited tail\n\nSuppose 0<=theta<1 and the tail certificate in (2) is supplied. Choose\n\n              theta D/(2-theta)<=r<=D,\n eta=(D-r)/(2D), K=D(D+r)/2,\n gamma=r/D-(1-eta)theta>=0.                             (6)\n\nThen the physical schedule (1+r,b) has the following complete nonnegative\ncertificate for ||p||^2-||q||^2>=0:\n\n K(||p||^2-||q||^2)\n    +G_b+D Q_(L,z)+gamma J_b\n    +(1-eta)sum Lambda_ij Q_ij\n        =(1-eta)M_b+eta||W_b-Dp||^2.                    (7)\n\nEvery coefficient on the left is nonnegative, and the right is PSD on the\nfull independent p and tail gradient space. Thus one actual smooth convex\ntrajectory obeys ||g_z||<=||g_L||. No reset or change of its function occurs.\n\nTo prove (7), subtract eta times (4) from (5). Since\nD-r=2D eta and K=(1-eta)D^2, completing the one square gives\n\n K(||p||^2-||q||^2)\n   =(1-eta)V_b-[G_b+D Q_(L,z)+(r/D)J_b]\n                                          +eta||W_b-Dp||^2.\n\nSubstitute V_b=E_theta,b+theta J_b and then (2). This is exactly (7).\nThe lower bound on r in (6) is equivalent to gamma>=0. This supplies all\nsigns, not only the PSD congruence. For theta=0, the entire interval0<=r<=D\nis allowed. In particular a primitive tail of capacity D can be preceded\nby any bridge in [1,D+1] without increasing the gradient norm from the\npre-bridge query to the final query.\n\n## 4. Subcritical primitive join with an exact credit budget\n\nLet r>=0 satisfy the physical subcritical condition\n\n                         rT<=AD.                        (8)\n\nIt implies r<D. Define\n\n kappa=T^2/D^2,\n delta=(A+r)(AD-rT)/[D^2(D+r)]>=0,\n u=2T(A+r)/[D(D+r)],\n v=2A(A+r)/(D+r),\n j=kappa-1-delta\n   =2(A+r)[(D+r)^2+Ar]/[D^2(D+r)]>0.                  (9)\n\nThe FULL identity is\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-u G_b-v Q_(L,z)-j J_b\n                                      +delta||W_b-Dp||^2. (10)\n\nHere is an independent derivation from the preceding formal expansions.\nLet kappa_0=1+2(A+r)/(D-r) and\nalpha=A^2-kappa_0 r^2=(A+r)(AD-rT)/(D-r). Value matching and (3) give\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa_0 V_b-(kappa_0-1)C_r\n                                 +alpha(||p||^2-||q||^2). (11)\n\nTo check the remaining diagonal coefficients, kappa_0(D-r)=2A+D+r,\nso the p-square discrepancy is A^2-kappa_0 r^2=alpha and the q-square\ndiscrepancy is kappa_0 D^2-T^2=-alpha. All other coefficients already\nmatch (3). Substitute the identity preceding (7) with theta=0 into (11).\nThe V_b coefficient becomes kappa_0+alpha/D^2=T^2/D^2. The square\ncoefficient becomes alpha(D-r)/[D^2(D+r)]=delta. The G_b coefficient is\nkappa_0-1+2alpha/[D(D+r)]=u; the Q_(L,z) coefficient is\nr(kappa_0-1)+2alpha/(D+r)=v. The J_b coefficient is\nkappa_0-1+2r alpha/[D^2(D+r)]=j. These exact equalities yield (10).\n\nConsequently, if the two input certificates (2) are supplied and\n\n                         theta<=j/kappa,                (12)\n\nthen the ordinary certificate for the actual h is\n\n V_h+sum mu_ij Q_ij+kappa sum Lambda_ij Q_ij\n         +u G_b+v Q_(L,z)+(j-kappa theta)J_b\n                 =M_a+kappa M_b+delta||W_b-Dp||^2.       (13)\n\nEvery ACTUAL coefficient in (13) is nonnegative, and its entire formal\nremainder is PSD. If several terms use the same ordered pair, add their\nnonnegative coefficients. This also covers empty inputs, singleton inputs\nand zero steps within the input blocks on the original independent-gradient\nspace. The boundary gradient p is not identified with any tail gradient.\n\nAt rT=AD, delta=0 and j/kappa=1-D^2/T^2, recovering the saturated credit\njoin. At r=0 (a physical unit bridge), the budget is\n\n                    j/kappa=2AD/(A+D)^2.                (14)\n\nThus (13) gives a concrete nonsaturated full-matrix construction with a\nnonzero PSD cross-block square. It does not infer nonsaturated necessity\nor extend the complementarity-based C_spine extraction theorem.\n\n## 5. An explicit reversal class at arbitrary lengths\n\nDefine a recursive class from the empty schedule: if a,b belong to it,\nadjoin h=(a,1+r,b) for ANY r>=0 satisfying r(A+D+r)<=AD, using the ACTUAL\ncapacities of the chosen children. Formula (13) with theta=0, starting\nfrom the empty zero certificate, constructs an ordinary full certificate\nat every node. Thus every schedule in this class is certified primitive.\n\nReversing h gives (reverse(b),1+r,reverse(a)). Inductively its children\nbelong to the same class, and the condition r(A+D+r)<=AD is symmetric\nin A,D. Applying (13) to the reversed children supplies all actual\nnonnegative coefficients and a full PSD matrix for the reversed schedule.\nThis proves reversal closure for the ENTIRE stated subcritical recursive\nclass, without importing a fixed-point vertex assumption or supposing\nan input multiplier array is diagonally symmetrizable.\n\nThe class contains every usual saturated basic tree and all choices of\nsubcritical bridge values within the stated interval. It has unbounded\nlength and need not have any saturated cut. It is not asserted to contain\nall primitive schedules or every schedule in the signed-credit cone.\n\nNo capacity advantage is produced by shortening a bridge: (8) implies\nT<=Omega(A,D):=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2. Define P_0=1 and\nP_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(P_k,P_(n-1-k)). This is exactly\nP_n=1+S_circle(n) in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d, as is seen by\nsubstituting A=1+x,D=1+y in its displayed phi recurrence. Both partial\nderivatives of Omega are positive for A,D>=1. Induction on the tree,\nusing its two child lengths, therefore bounds every capacity here by P_n.\nHence this reversal\nclass is a structural partial result, not an improving objective family\nor a proof of the unrestricted profile conjecture.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["2d75b4696641736c", "e7b2c6a164a4c24e"], "artifact": "local_memory/subcritical_credit_join.md", "exact_audit": "local_memory/subcritical_credit_join_exact_audit.json", "status": "candidate_only_no_verifier_allocation"}, "glossary": {"A": "Capacity 1+sum of nonnegative prefix steps.", "D": "Capacity 1+sum of nonnegative tail steps.", "T": "Joined capacity A+D+r.", "r": "Physical bridge is 1+r.", "W_b": "Sum of step-weighted tail gradients plus the tail terminal gradient.", "G_b": "Sum b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L), where L is the pre-bridge query and z is terminal.", "J_b": "Sum b_j Q_(j,z).", "E_theta": "Intrinsic tail energy V_b minus theta J_b.", "kappa": "T^2/D^2.", "delta": "(A+r)(AD-rT)/(D^2(D+r)), the square weight.", "u": "2T(A+r)/(D(D+r)), coefficient of G_b.", "v": "2A(A+r)/(D+r), coefficient of Q_(L,z).", "j": "kappa-1-delta, coefficient budget for J_b.", "P_n": "Primitive benchmark capacity 1+S_circle(n), defined in accepted b98bb807d514e80d."}}
{"id": "4a064f93111ac5a2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:35:30.918556+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Explicit intrinsic reversal involution with ALL value flows and full PSD retained: K_rev=4D^2 Delta K^(-1)Delta-(w_rev+2D e_n)e_n^T, lambda_rev,ij=-4D^2(Delta K^(-1)Delta)_ij, and M_rev=4D^2 Delta K^(-1) M K^(-T)Delta. The map is involutive. Every positive-step E_theta input, theta<1, has invertible K, so applicability is automatic; the exact remaining ordinary-sign condition is nonpositive OFF-diagonal entries of Delta K^(-1)Delta. This gives a precise source/performance interface, NOT a proof that some certificate always passes the sign test.", "evidence": "# Exact intrinsic reversal involution: full PSD congruence and precise signs\n\nStatus: candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or verifier call.\nThis proves an explicit transformation of UNSIGNED intrinsic certificates,\nwith a necessary and sufficient sign test for this particular output. It\ndoes not assert that all schedules admit a certificate passing that test.\nEvery identity below is derived here, with no unverified finding as a lemma.\n\n## 1. The complete intrinsic affine matrix system\n\nLet h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be nonnegative, s=sum h_i, D=1+s, and use\nx_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t with independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_n.\nThe interpolation quantities and energy are\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                         -sD||g_n||^2.\n\nAn unsigned certificate is a real off-diagonal array lambda such that\n\n             V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij=g^T M g,\n\nwith ALL value coefficients zero and M PSD on the FULL n+1 gradient space.\nWrite e=(1,...,1)^T, e_n=(0,...,0,1)^T, hbar=(h,0)^T, w=hbar+e_n,\np_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t. Let A be lower triangular with A_ii=1 and\nA_it=h_t for t<i, so A e=p and e_n^T A=w^T. Define the row Laplacian\nL_ij=-lambda_ij for i!=j and L_ii=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij. Value cancellation\nis exactly\n\n L^T e=omega,             omega=-2hbar+2s e_n.           (1)\n\nDefine the nonsymmetric grounded matrix\n\n K=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w).                     (2)\n\nHere and below sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. Direct expansion gives\n\n                 M=sym(K A)-D^2 e_n e_n^T.             (3)\n\nTo verify it fully, the gradient part of Q_ij is\n<g_i,(A g)_i-(A g)_j>+(||g_j||^2-||g_i||^2)/2.\nThus its weighted sum has matrix sym(L A)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V_h\ngives sym(L A)+diag(h_i(2-h_i),1-D^2). On the other hand,\nsym([diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w)]A)\n=diag(h_i(2-h_i),1): all off-diagonal terms cancel, proving (3).\n\nEquations (1)--(2) imply\n\n K e=w,             K^T e=D e_n.                       (4)\n\nIt follows from (3) that e^T M e=D p_n-D^2=0. Since M is PSD,\nM e=0 (apply nonnegativity to e+t z for every real t). Therefore\n\n K p=-D w+2D^2 e_n.                                   (5)\n\nIf K is invertible, (4)--(5) give the useful fixed inverse boundaries\n\n K^(-1)w=e,\n K^(-1)e_n=(p+D e)/(2D^2),\n e_n^T K^(-1)=e^T/D.                                  (6)\n\nAll these equations hold for signed as well as ordinary multipliers.\n\n## 2. Reverse the actual schedule, with an explicit array\n\nLet hrev=(h_(n-1),...,h_0), and put wrev=(hrev,1)^T,\np_rev,i=1+sum_(t<i)hrev_t. Define the symmetric invertible matrix\n\n Delta_ij=1_(j=n-i)-1_(j=n-i-1),                       (7)\n\nwhere out-of-range indicators are0, and write P=Delta^(-1).\nThe following identities follow directly from these entries:\n\n Delta e=e_n, Delta p=wrev, Delta p_rev=w,\n P e_n=e,             Delta A_rev=A^T Delta.            (8)\n\nThe last identity can be checked one row at a time: row i<n of\nDelta A_rev is row n-i minus row n-i-1 of A_rev, and row n is\nrow0. The resulting two adjacent column entries agree with A^T Delta.\n\nAssume K is invertible. Put vrev=wrev+2D e_n and define\n\n K_rev=4D^2 Delta K^(-1) Delta-vrev e_n^T.              (9)\n\nDefine the ACTUAL off-diagonal array for the reversed schedule by\n\n lambda_rev,ij=-4D^2(Delta K^(-1) Delta)_ij,  i!=j.     (10)\n\nThis is a signed array until its signs are checked. From (6)--(8),\n\n K_rev e=wrev,        K_rev^T e=D e_n,                  (11)\n K_rev p_rev=-D wrev+2D^2 e_n.\n\nFor example, the row-sum identity is\n4D^2 Delta K^(-1)e_n-vrev=2(wrev+D e_n)-vrev=wrev;\nthe column sum uses e^T vrev=3D. The p_rev identity follows from\nDelta p_rev=w and K^(-1)w=e.\n\nThe matrix in (9) is exactly the grounded matrix (2) of (10): off\nthe last column this follows entrywise; in that column the subtracted\nwrev_i equals hrev_i for i<n, precisely the required grounding term.\nThe diagonals then follow from the row sums. The column equation in\n(11) now proves ALL reversed value flows\n\n out_i^rev-in_i^rev=-2hrev_i for i<n, and 2s for i=n.    (12)\n\nNo formal value or zero-step gradient has been dropped.\n\n## 3. Full PSD is preserved by a displayed congruence\n\nLet M_rev be the entire remainder associated with (10), so by the direct\nexpansion (3), M_rev=sym(K_rev A_rev)-D^2 e_n e_n^T.\nThen\n\n M_rev=4D^2 Delta K^(-1) M K^(-T) Delta.               (13)\n\nIndeed, substitute (9) into M_rev. Its non-rank-one term is\n4D^2 sym(Delta K^(-1) A^T Delta), by (8). Its remaining terms equal\n\n -sym((wrev+2D e_n)wrev^T)-D^2 e_n e_n^T\n                       =-(wrev+D e_n)(wrev+D e_n)^T.\n\nSubstituting (3) into the right side of (13) gives exactly those terms:\nthe first by symmetrization, and the second by\nDelta K^(-1)e_n=(wrev+D e_n)/(2D^2). This proves (13).\nThus the remainder is PSD on ALL independent reversed gradients.\nThis proof needs no external H-duality theorem or strong-convexity limit.\n\n## 4. This matrix map is an involution, not a one-way relaxation\n\nLet B=4D^2 Delta K^(-1) Delta. Then\nB^(-1)=P K P/(4D^2). Equations (4)--(8) show\n\n B^(-1)vrev=(p_rev+2D e)/(4D),\n e_n^T B^(-1)=e^T/(4D),\n e_n^T B^(-1)vrev=3/4.\n\nConsequently K_rev=B-vrev e_n^T is invertible, and direct rank-one\ninversion yields\n\n K_rev^(-1)=[P K P+(p_rev+2D e)e^T]/(4D^2).           (14)\n\nThis formula may alternatively be verified by multiplying by (9);\nthe denominator in rank-one inversion is1-3/4=1/4, so it cannot vanish.\nApply (9) to K_rev with hrev in place of h. Equations (8),(14) give\n\n 4D^2 Delta K_rev^(-1)Delta-(w+2D e_n)e_n^T=K.          (15)\n\nThus the map is an involution on the stated affine/full-PSD domain.\n\n## 5. Actual coefficient signs: the entire remaining condition\n\nThe output of this particular map is an ORDINARY reversed certificate\nif and only if\n\n          (Delta K^(-1)Delta)_ij<=0 for every i!=j.      (16)\n\nIf one wants an E_eta certificate instead, (10) shows the exact test:\nall entries targeting j!=n must still be nonnegative, while\nlambda_rev,(i,n)>=-eta hrev_i for i<n. This does not license negative\ninternal entries. Equation (13) supplies the full PSD condition separately.\n\nFor an actual E_theta input with 0<=theta<1, let Lambda be its nonnegative\narray and set lambda_(i,n)=Lambda_(i,n)-theta h_i, leaving other entries\nunchanged. Then it has exactly the intrinsic flow and full M used above.\nIf every physical h_i is positive, K has nonpositive off-diagonal entries:\nK_(i,n)=-Lambda_(i,n)-(1-theta)h_i and all other off-diagonals are\n-Lambda_ij. Its row-sum surplus is w_i>0. Strict diagonal dominance\ntherefore proves invertibility and makes (9)--(16) applicable without\nan additional invertibility assumption. With zero steps, the same full\nformulas hold whenever K is invertible; universal invertibility in that\ncase is NOT asserted and no duplicated formal gradients are identified.\n\nThe ordinary input family in local candidate37622e6cdcb606e6 supplies an\nexplicit failure of (16) at every n>=4; that separate proof gives all\ninput coefficients and the positive-square remainder independently.\nHence (16) cannot be discarded, even for a full ordinary input. Conversely,\nthe signed E_(1/2) calibration h=(1,2,1,1) passes this sign test; its exact\n5x5 audit also checks all flows, congruence and involution. Neither example\nproves existential certificate re-selection for arbitrary h.\n\n## 6. Relation to the actual G,G/D,D performance interface\n\nThe matrix identity can also be understood without guessing a performance\nfunctional. For a tail of capacity D, prepend physical step D+1. The exact\ngradient-norm identity is\n\n D^2(||g_pre||^2-||g_n||^2)\n +sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,pre)+Q_(n,pre)+D Q_(pre,n)\n +sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n)+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=M.\n\nIts grounded, normalized G,G matrix is -K/(2D^2). Reversing the physical\nschedule produces (hrev,D+1). For ANY intrinsic signed array lambda_rev,\nthe corresponding distance identity for that appended schedule is\n\n ||x0-xstar||^2-||x_out-xstar||^2\n +2sum_(i<n)hrev_i Q_(i,star)+2(D+1)Q_(n,star)+4D Q_(star,n)\n +sum lambda_rev,ij Q_ij=M_rev,                        (17)\n\nwhere x_out=x_n-(D+1)g_n and the optimum jet is zero after translation.\nTo check (17), set H=sum hrev_i g_i. Expand the distance difference\n2<x0,H+(D+1)g_n>-||H+(D+1)g_n||^2 and the displayed optimum Q terms.\nThe x0 and H cross terms cancel; the remaining value and square terms\nare precisely V_hrev. This proves the identity on the full original space.\nAfter division by2, its reduced D,D matrix has finite off-diagonals\nlambda_rev/2, optimum outgoing weights (hrev,D+1), and optimum incoming\nweight2D at n. These are exactly the inverse-difference matrix's row\nand column deficits. This explicitly explains both performance endpoints.\n\nSources motivating this derivation, not used as black-box premises:\nShu--Wang, arXiv:2609.03281v1, Sections4.4--4.5; source applicability\nand the q>0 check are separately written in37622e6cdcb606e6. Matlas and\nthe fact graph were queried; no accepted intrinsic reversal theorem was\nfound. All new identities here remain candidate evidence.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["37622e6cdcb606e6", "0b2dfaee171c78eb", "666b5b9b14038a0f"], "guidance_id": "66512436ef464e46", "artifact": "local_memory/intrinsic_reversal_involution.md", "exact_audit": "local_memory/intrinsic_reversal_exact_audit.json", "source_urls": ["https://arxiv.org/html/2609.03281v1"], "status": "candidate_only_no_routine_verifier"}, "glossary": {"hrev": "Chronological reversal of the physical schedule h.", "D": "Capacity 1+sum h_i.", "w": "(h_0,...,h_(n-1),1)^T.", "p_i": "Prefix capacity 1+sum_(t<i)h_t.", "A": "Lower triangular matrix with diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i.", "L": "Row Laplacian of the intrinsic signed multiplier array.", "K": "L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w), where hbar=(h,0).", "Delta": "Symmetric inverse cumulative-reversal matrix with entry 1_(j=n-i)-1_(j=n-i-1).", "K_rev": "Displayed transformed grounded matrix for hrev.", "lambda_rev": "Displayed actual signed multiplier array for hrev.", "M_rev": "Complete independent-gradient remainder for hrev.", "P": "Matrix Delta^(-1), not a scalar benchmark or prefix sum."}}
{"id": "076f54b4ac0215d4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:50:52.884792+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New bounded discovery pre-solve model derived: exact finite-spectrum SOCP with full joint prefix inequalities, exact initial normalization, and genuinely free terminal gradient via one perspective cone per complex block. Calibration predictions derived before solving: spectrum{1/2}, empty retained prefix, b1 has exact payoff1/4; spectrum{(1+i)/2}, retained prefix{1}, has positive cycle1/4 and is infeasible. Local Python has no checked solver packages; existing Wolfram connector is being used without installation. Solver count remains0 until calibration submission.", "evidence": "# Exact finite-spectrum SOCP and pre-solve calibrations\n\nStatus: mathematical derivation candidate, not verifier-accepted.\nNumerical discovery is separately authorized by TASK.md and shared\nguidance cff73936ae9079ca: at most12 evaluations and120 measured\nbuild-plus-solve seconds, no installation, n<=63 and at most64 spectra.\n\nFor a fixed retained prefix h_0,...,h_(q-1) and final step b, let\np_i,z=P_i(z), a_i,z=zP_i(z), t_z=(1-bz)P_q(z). The optimum has all\nthese prefix columns and value zero. For any finite spectra and weights,\nthe actual real prefix vectors are sqrt(w_z)(Re p_i,z,Im p_i,z) and\nsqrt(w_z)(Re a_i,z,Im a_i,z), with w_z>=0, sum w_z=1.\n\nPrefix interpolation is exactly the linear system, for ALL ordered pairs,\n\n f_i-f_j+sum_z w_z[\n   Re(conj(a_i,z)(p_j,z-p_i,z))+|a_i,z-a_j,z|^2/2]<=0.\n\nThe values are joint unknowns. This equation never assumes that individual\nmodes or modewise function values are legal.\n\nFor the freely chosen real terminal gradient v_z in each spectral plane\nintroduce u_z=sqrt(w_z)v_z and s_z>=||u_z||^2/w_z, with the closed convention\nw_z=0 implies u_z=0,s_z>=0. This is precisely the rotated cone\n\n  ||(2u_z, s_z-w_z)|| <=s_z+w_z, w_z,s_z>=0.\n\nALL outgoing terminal inequalities, including the optimum anchor, are\n\n f_T-f_j+sum_z [\n    <u_z, p_j,z-t_z-a_j,z>+w_z|a_j,z|^2/2]\n                                      +(1/2)sum_z s_z<=0.\n\nThe inner product is the real one. Maximize2f_T subject to this system.\n\nRealizability and equivalence: if w_z>0 set v_z=u_z/sqrt(w_z); for w_z=0\nset v_z=0. Its actual squared norm is <=sum s_z, so every displayed\noutgoing inequality implies the true one. All prefix constraints hold.\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 constructs the maximal convex1-smooth interpolant\nand gives F(x_T)>=f_T. Conversely any terminal gradient can be projected\nonto the supplied spectral-coordinate space; removing a perpendicular\ncomponent decreases every outgoing residual. Set s_z=||v_z||^2 to recover\nthe conic data. At an optimum all perspective slack can be removed.\nThus this is the exact finite-spectrum witness supremum with unrestricted\nterminal completion, not a separable or normalized-denominator relaxation.\nRepeated columns and zero weights cause no exception.\n\nFor column scaling choose c_z>=1 bounding |p_i,z|,|a_i,z|,|t_z|.\nReplace columns by columns/c_z and variables by beta_z=c_z^2 w_z.\nThen the EXACT initial normalization is sum beta_z/c_z^2=1.\nUse perspective u_z=sqrt(beta_z)v_z. All coefficients of the prefix and\nterminal forms use the normalized columns. Reconstruct weights as\nbeta_z/c_z^2. The normalization coefficients can become tiny for large\npolynomial columns; their range must be inspected and any unresolved\nconditioning closes the batch. Scaling does not permit relaxing sum w=1.\n\nCalibration A, before solving: empty retained prefix, spectrum{1/2},\nb=1. The sole query is x_0=1,g_0=1/2 and x_T=1/2.\nPrefix values obey1/8<=f_0<=3/8. For a terminal gradient v the two\noutgoing anchors give\n f_T<=v/2-v^2/2 and f_T<=1/4-v^2/2.\nIf v<=1/2 the first is at most1/8; if v>=1/2 the second is at most1/8.\nPerpendicular gradient components only decrease these bounds.\nThe Huber function with threshold1/2 realizes equality. Therefore the\nexact maximum normalized objective is1/4.\n\nCalibration B, before solving: retained prefix{1}, spectrum{(1+i)/2},\nany final b. Initial and next optimum pairs, with kappa=0, force\nf_0=1/4 and f_1=1/8. Here p_1=(1-i)/2 and a_1=1/2.\nThe reverse edge Q_(1,0) then equals\n 1/8-1/4+1/4+1/8=1/4>0.\nEquivalently the optimum/reverse cycle has weight1/4. Thus the ENTIRE\nprefix system is infeasible, independently of all terminal variables.\n\nKnown baselines for discovery must be evaluated on the same actual word:\ncanonical Huber1/(1+2sum full_h), scalar quadratic values\nr product(1-h_i r)^2, and exact B_n from the accepted recursions.\nNumerical primal values are never a verified mathematical conclusion.\n\nImplementation uses only existing Wolfram Language SecondOrderConeOptimization;\nsyntax checked at https://reference.wolfram.com/language/ref/SecondOrderConeOptimization.html.\nNo strong-duality claim from that documentation is used as a math premise.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max2/research/finite_spectrum_socp_model.md", "implementation": "workers/max2/research/finite_spectrum_model.wl", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "allocation": "joint-finite-spectrum-witness-discovery"}, "glossary": {}}
{"id": "1f8863f8894876ab", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:56:12.413012+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Concrete SAME-SCHEDULE variable-full-matrix repair when h0=1: move every remote outgoing0->j edge through query1 by an explicit four-edge identity. This sets lambda01=2, lambda0j=0, lambda10=4-sum_(i>=2)lambda_i0, and lambda1j=lambda1j+lambda0j; the full remainder is exactly the PSD pinching diag(1,C) of its gradient-difference matrix. Ordinary repair is guaranteed under the stated exact incoming-mass and terminal-sign tests, including an unbounded E_(1/n) unit family. A legal h=(1,1/2) example proves the incoming-mass test cannot be silently omitted.", "evidence": "# An exact full-PSD pinching move with actual coefficient tests\n\nStatus: candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. This is a concrete variable-matrix\nrepair on the SAME physical schedule, not just an SDP restatement. It is\nconditional on two explicit sign tests, and no capacity improvement is claimed.\n\n## Statement and complete array\n\nLet n>=2, h0=1, and h1,...,h_(n-1)>=0. Put P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t,\nso P_1=1. Use the original Q_ij and intrinsic V_h. Suppose a signed array\nlambda cancels every value coefficient in V_h+sum lambda Q and leaves\na FULL PSD remainder M; only incoming-terminal lambda_(i,n) may be negative.\nThis includes the signed version lambda=Lambda-theta h on terminal edges\nof an actual E_theta certificate. No duplicated zero-step gradient is removed.\n\nDefine the new array by\n\n lambda'_01=2,\n lambda'_(0,j)=0                         (2<=j<=n),\n lambda'_10=lambda10+sum_(j=2)^n(P_j-1)lambda_(0,j)\n           =4-sum_(i=2)^n lambda_(i,0),\n lambda'_(1,j)=lambda_(1,j)+lambda_(0,j)  (2<=j<=n),     (1)\n\nand leave all remaining ordered pairs unchanged. Every value coefficient\nstill cancels, and the FULL new remainder M' is PSD. In gradient-difference\ncoordinates d_k=g_k-g_(k+1), write the original remainder as\n\n            M=||d0||^2+2<d0,b^T d_tail>+d_tail^T C d_tail,\n\nwhere d_tail=(d1,...,d_(n-1)). Then exactly\n\n                         M'=||d0||^2+d_tail^T C d_tail. (2)\n\nThus it removes all cross terms involving d0 while retaining the entire\ntail block. Since C is a principal block of a PSD matrix, (2) is PSD.\nThe difference M'-M is generally indefinite, consistent with the obstruction\ncc59ae14a26f3e78; no monotonicity in the PSD order is required.\n\nEvery actual nonterminal-directed coefficient in (1) is nonnegative if\nand only if\n\n                       sum_(i=2)^n lambda_(i,0)<=4.     (3)\n\nAll other potentially problematic signs are displayed explicitly:\nlambda'_(1,n)=lambda_(1,n)+lambda_(0,n), while incoming-terminal\ncoefficients for i>=2 are unchanged. In particular (1) is an ORDINARY\nrepair whenever (3) holds, lambda_(i,n)>=0 for every i>=2, and\n\n                       lambda_(1,n)+lambda_(0,n)>=0.    (4)\n\nThere is no physical prefix, bridge, contraction, or change of horizon.\nThe tests involve the actual original multiplier array, not only a witness.\n\n## Derivation on every formal gradient and value\n\nLet A have diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i, and let L be the row Laplacian\nof lambda. Expansion of Q and cancellation of the values give\n\n M=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2))+sym(L A), s=sum h_i.\n\nThe constant-gradient form is zero. PSD therefore makes the constant\nvector a kernel vector. Because A has first column e when h0=1,\n(L A)_(i,0)=0 for every i. Hence M00=1 and, for j>=1,\n\n             2M_(0,j)=-lambda_(0,j)-h_j sum_(t>j)lambda_(0,t),\n\nwhere h_n=0. Summing this row, using M e=0, gives the exact identity\n\n                      sum_(j=1)^n P_j lambda_(0,j)=2.   (5)\n\nUse a cut-vector basis for the differences: g_j=g_n+sum_(k>=j)d_k.\nThe first mixed coefficient is b_k=sum_(j=0)^k M_(0,j). Equation (5)\nand the displayed row formula yield, for 1<=k<n,\n\n b_k=(1/2)sum_(j=k+1)^n a_(j,k) lambda_(0,j),\n a_(j,k)=1+sum_(t=k+1)^(j-1)h_t=1+P_j-P_(k+1).        (6)\n\nFor each j>=2 the following FOUR-EDGE identity is exact:\n\n P_j Q01+(P_j-1)Q10-Q_(0,j)+Q_(1,j)\n       =-<d0,sum_(k=1)^(j-1) a_(j,k)d_k>.              (7)\n\nTo check it, the values cancel coefficient by coefficient. Substitute\nx1=x0-g0 and x_j=x1-sum_(t=1)^(j-1)h_t g_t in the gradient terms.\nThe result is <g0-g1,sum_(t=1)^(j-1)h_t g_t+g_j-P_j g1>.\nWriting g_t-g1=-sum_(k=1)^(t-1)d_k gives the right side of (7).\n\nAdd lambda_(0,j) times (7) for every j>=2. Its actual changes are\ndelta lambda01=P_j lambda_(0,j),\ndelta lambda10=(P_j-1)lambda_(0,j),\ndelta lambda_(0,j)=-lambda_(0,j),\ndelta lambda_(1,j)=lambda_(0,j).\nEquation (5) makes the new01 entry2, and (6) proves that the resulting\nquadratic change is exactly -2<d0,b^T d_tail>, establishing (1)--(2).\nEach four-edge operation has zero value flow, even when its scalar is\nnegative. The new incoming flow at query0 equals its outgoing flow plus2,\nhence is4; all incoming entries except10 were unchanged. This proves\nthe second expression for lambda'_10 in (1).\n\nFor targets other than n, the only new entries are2,0, lambda'_10,\nand sums of two nonnegative entries lambda1j+lambda0j. Thus (3) is\nexactly their required sign test; the terminal signs are precisely (4)\nand the unchanged incoming entries. This checks the entire actual array.\n\n## The sign test cannot be omitted\n\nTake h=(1,1/2), theta=1/2, and the signed array\n\n lambda01=11/4, lambda10=3/20, lambda02=-1/2,\n lambda20=41/10, lambda12=43/10, lambda21=27/10.\n\nIts full exact remainder is\n\n ||d0||^2-(1/2)<d0,d1>+(31/20)||d1||^2\n             =||d0-d1/4||^2+(119/80)||d1||^2.\n\nThe value flows are (-2,-1,3). Its actual E_(1/2) array replaces\nlambda02 by0 and lambda12 by91/20, leaving all other entries positive;\ntherefore the full credited certificate is valid. But (1) produces\nlambda'_10=4-41/10=-1/10. The PSD identity remains true, yet this\nparticular pinching is not an ordinary certificate. This demonstrates\nwhy (3) is part of the theorem rather than an inferred sign consequence.\n\n## Nonvacuity at arbitrary lengths\n\nFor every n>=2 start with the ordinary adjacent unit-step certificate\nlambda^0_(k,k+1)=2(k+1)^2, lambda^0_(k+1,k)=2(k+1)(k+2).\nLet epsilon=theta=1/n and modify its01,10,0n,1n entries by\nn epsilon,(n-1)epsilon,-epsilon,+epsilon respectively. The full remainder is\n\n sum_(k>=1)(k+1)^2||d_k-epsilon(n-k)d0/[2(k+1)^2]||^2\n +[1-epsilon^2/4 sum_(k>=1)(n-k)^2/(k+1)^2]||d0||^2,\n\nwhose last coefficient is greater than3/4 by the telescoping bound\nsum_(j=2)^n1/j^2<sum_(j=2)^n1/[j(j-1)]<1. Adding theta to all incoming\nterminal entries makes the actual E_theta array nonnegative. All remote\nincoming coefficients lambda_(i,0),i>=2 vanish, and the negative lambda0n\nis offset by lambda1n. Hence (3)--(4) hold. The explicit move (1) repairs\nthis entire family, exactly returning its original ordinary adjacent array.\n\nThe theorem permits arbitrary nonnegative tail steps and arbitrary other\ncertificate support satisfying the listed tests. The unit family establishes\nnonvacuity, not a claimed completeness theorem. Arbitrary incoming-terminal\ncredit at other indices, a first step different from1, and failure of (3)\nremain outside this move's ordinary-repair guarantee.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["cc59ae14a26f3e78", "4aaee6131a92da0e", "666b5b9b14038a0f"], "artifact": "local_memory/unit_first_step_pinching_repair.md", "status": "candidate_only"}, "glossary": {"P_j": "Physical prefix sum sum_(t<j)h_t; here P_1=1.", "lambda": "Signed intrinsic array with only incoming-terminal negative entries allowed.", "lambda_prime": "Explicit new multiplier array given by the four-edge redistribution.", "d_k": "g_k-g_(k+1), retaining all original formal gradients.", "b": "Cross block between d0 and the other differences in the full remainder.", "C": "Principal block on all differences d1,...,d_(n-1).", "a_jk": "1+sum_(t=k+1)^(j-1)h_t, coefficient in the exact four-edge identity.", "M_prime": "Pinched full remainder ||d0||^2+d_tail^T C d_tail."}}
{"id": "e20fa4c2bad75795", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:11:00.899027+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New directed full-matrix mechanism: sparse rank-one grounded-matrix changes preserve all value and affine-kernel conditions, with exact coefficient intervals, a two-eigenvalue PSD interval, and an explicit inverse update. A local positive-square move always deletes a two-step backward edge across physical steps (1,b), b>0. It repairs the earlier four-step canonical inverse-sign counterexample by actual coefficient re-selection, and combined with unit-first pinching it weakens the incoming-mass condition and repairs the explicit (1,1/2) failure. No universal sign-selection or improving capacity claim.", "evidence": "# Directed full-matrix moves: exact intervals, a square move, and sign repair\n\nStatus: candidate proofs, NOT verifier-accepted. No SDP or fact_submit call.\nThese screen plans8a9fe85173280c05 and e8ab4e3f583a448e by actual algebra.\nThey do not prove that a legal improving pivot always exists.\n\n## 1. A concrete family preserving all affine conditions\n\nUse nonnegative h, s=sum h, D=1+s, independent gradients g0,...,gn,\noriginal Q_ij, intrinsic V_h, and a value-cancelling signed array lambda.\nLet A have diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i, e=(1,...,1), p=A e, and\nlet L be the row Laplacian of lambda. Expanding every Q gives\n\n M=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2))+sym(L A).\n\nThus for a coefficient change with zero value flow, the full matrix\nchange is sym(delta L A). Put\nK=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w), hbar=(h,0), w=(h,1).\n\nChoose real vectors u,v satisfying e^T u=0, v^T e=0, v^T p=0. The\nactual change\n\n delta lambda_ij=-t u_i v_j (i!=j),  delta L=t u v^T,\n delta K=t u v^T,  delta M=t sym(u z^T), z=A^T v       (1)\n\npreserves row sums, every value flow, and the constant-gradient kernel.\nIndeed delta L e=0, delta L^T e=0 and delta L p=0, while z^T e=0.\nThe off-diagonal rule and v^T e=0 also give the stated Laplacian diagonal.\nEvery actual E_theta multiplier is Lambda_ij-t u_i v_j. Its signs give\nexplicit scalar half-intervals, one for EACH ordered pair; no sign is dropped.\n\nAssume u,z lie in the range of M. Let M^+ mean the inverse on that range\nand zero on its kernel. Put alpha=u^T M^+u, beta=z^T M^+z,\nsigma=u^T M^+z, d=sqrt(alpha beta). The exact PSD interval for (1) is\n\n             -2/(sigma+d)<=t<=2/(d-sigma),             (2)\n\nwhere a zero lower denominator means no lower bound, and a zero upper\ndenominator means no upper bound. If either vector vanishes, every t is allowed.\nTo prove (2), congruence by the inverse square root of M on its range\nreduces the condition to I+t sym(U Z^T)>=0. The only possibly nonzero\neigenvalues of sym(U Z^T) are (sigma+d)/2 and (sigma-d)/2, as follows\nby restricting to span{U,Z}, or from its trace sigma and determinant\n(sigma^2-alpha beta)/4. Intersection with every coefficient interval is\ntherefore an EXACT legal one-parameter move. For a remainder positive\ndefinite on e-perp, the range assumptions follow from the displayed\northogonality conditions. Singular faces need their actual range checked.\n\nFor invertible K write Phi=-Delta K^(-1)Delta, with\nDelta_ij=1_(j=n-i)-1_(j=n-i-1). If 1+t c is nonzero, where\nc=v^T K^(-1)u, then direct rank-one inversion gives\n\n Phi(t)=Phi+[t/(1+t c)] x y^T,\n x=Delta K^(-1)u,   y^T=v^T K^(-1)Delta.               (3)\n\nMultiplication of K+t u v^T by the displayed inverse verifies (3).\nOn a positive-step E_theta sign-feasible interval, theta<1, K(t) has\nnonpositive off-diagonals and strictly positive row-sum surplus, so is\ninvertible; the denominator cannot cross zero there. This supplies an\nexact targeted inverse-entry test, but not a universal existence theorem.\n\n## 2. Square-producing moves require no range hypothesis\n\nFor ANY v perpendicular to e,p, set u=A^T v. Then e^T u=0 and\n\n                    delta M=t u u^T>=0 for t>=0.       (4)\n\nThis gives a legal full-PSD update whenever all actual coefficient signs\nsurvive. Its maximal nonnegative parameter is the minimum of\nLambda_ij/(u_i v_j) over all u_i v_j>0; omit zero products. The move\nis useful only when the desired coefficient increases and every required\ndonor is present. It does not contradict the impossibility of a universal\none-direction matrix-order repair.\n\nThere is a particularly explicit local version. For THREE consecutive\nqueries j,j+1,j+2, put a=h_j>0,b=h_(j+1)>0 and take, on those coordinates,\n\n v=(b,-a-b,a),\n u=(b(1-a),-a-b(1-a),a),                              (5)\n\nand zero elsewhere. Direct summation shows v^T e=v^T p=0 and u=A^T v,\nincluding all earlier and later original coordinates. The six changes,\nwith row/column labels0,1,2 local to these three queries, are\n\n delta lambda01= t b(1-a)(a+b),\n delta lambda02=-t ab(1-a),\n delta lambda10= t b[a+b(1-a)],\n delta lambda12= t a[a+b(1-a)],\n delta lambda20=-t ab,\n delta lambda21= t a(a+b).                            (6)\n\nEvery value flow cancels and the FULL remainder increases by\nt||b(1-a)g_j-[a+b(1-a)]g_(j+1)+a g_(j+2)||^2.\n\nIf a=1, the backward edge j+2->j can ALWAYS be deleted. Set\nt=lambda_(j+2,j)/b>=0. Formula (6) only decreases that one actual\nnonterminal-directed coefficient; all other changes are nonnegative.\nThe exact underlying interpolation identity is\n\n b Q_(j+1,j)+(b+1)Q_(j+2,j+1)+Q_(j+1,j+2)-b Q_(j+2,j)\n                              =||g_(j+2)-g_(j+1)||^2. (7)\n\nIt holds in every formal dimension using the physical consecutive steps\n1,b. Thus this is a genuine directed coefficient elimination on arbitrary\nlonger schedules, independent of any symmetric graph assumption.\n\nFor 0<a<=1 the same deletion is allowed if the forward donor obeys\nlambda_(j,j+2)>=(1-a)lambda_(j+2,j), using ordinary input coefficients.\nFor a>1 and b<=a/(a-1), (6) instead increases the forward remote edge.\nIf j+2=n and q=-lambda_(j,n)>0, it removes that negative terminal\ncoefficient with t=q/[ab(a-1)] provided\n\n lambda_(n,j)>=q/(a-1),\n lambda_(j,j+1)>=q(a+b)/a.                            (8)\n\nAll other changed entries then increase. If every unchanged coefficient\nis already ordinary, (8) gives an ordinary SAME-SCHEDULE repair with a\ndisplayed positive square. These donor conditions are assumptions, not\nconsequences of PSD claimed without proof.\n\n## 3. Compound repair strengthens the first-unit-step pinching theorem\n\nAssume h0=1,h1=b>0. Start with a full signed certificate.
