The four-edge\nredistribution eliminating all outgoing0->j, j>=2, gives\n\n lambda'_01=2, lambda'_0j=0,\n lambda'_10=4-sum_(i>=2)lambda_(i,0),\n lambda'_1j=lambda_(1,j)+lambda_(0,j),                  (9)\n\nand changes the full difference matrix [[1,bvec^T],[bvec,C]] to diag(1,C).\nHere is a direct check: the identity\nP_j Q01+(P_j-1)Q10-Q0j+Q1j\n=-<g0-g1,sum_(k=1)^(j-1)(1+P_j-P_(k+1))(g_k-g_(k+1))>\nwith P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, multiplied by lambda0j and summed, removes\nexactly that cross block; the constant-gradient kernel gives\nsum_j P_j lambda0j=2. Thus (9) is a full formal PSD pinching even if\nits10 coefficient is temporarily negative.\n\nNow let d=lambda20>=0 and add (d/b) times (7) with j=0. This removes\nthe backward20 edge, increases10 by d,12 by d/b,21 by(1+b)d/b,\nand adds (d/b)||g2-g1||^2 to the full remainder. The final array has\n\n lambda''_10=4-sum_(i>=3)lambda_(i,0), lambda''_20=0,   (10)\n\nwith all other changes as explicitly stated. Hence the original mass\ncondition is relaxed to sum_(i>=3)lambda_(i,0)<=4. All nonterminal signs\nare then nonnegative. The only terminal test is\n\n lambda1n+lambda0n+1_(n=2)d/b>=0,\n\nalong with unchanged lambda_(i,n)>=0 for 2<=i<n. This is a concrete\ncompound repair with an indefinite pinching followed by a positive square;\nthe final full matrix and every actual coefficient are checked.\n\nFor the earlier failing credited input h=(1,1/2), whose signed array is\nlambda01=11/4,10=3/20,02=-1/2,20=41/10,12=43/10,21=27/10,\npinching gives10=-1/10. Adding only1/5 times (7) already makes10=0,\nleaves20=4, and yields the ordinary array\n\n lambda01=2,10=0,02=0,20=4,12=4,21=3,\n\nwhose full remainder is ||g0-g1||^2+(7/4)||g1-g2||^2. Thus the isolated\npinching failure is repaired by a complete legal certificate, not by\ndropping the negative entry or by changing the physical schedule.\n\n## 4. Exact screening against the inverse-sign counterexample\n\nUse the n=4 unit schedule. To make this example self-contained, its complete\nnonzero input array is\nlambda01=29/15,10=58/15,02=1/30,20=1/10,12=469/60,21=469/40,\n13=1/40,31=1/20,23=717/40,32=239/10,34=32,43=40.\nAll remaining entries are0. Its flows are(-2,-2,-2,-2,8), and direct\nsubstitution into Q gives the full remainder\n(59/60)||g0-g1||^2+(1/60)||g0-g2||^2\n+(929/240)||g1-g2||^2+(1/80)||g1-g3||^2\n+(143/16)||g2-g3||^2+16||g3-g4||^2.\nThis is the input also discussed in37622e6cdcb606e6; its full data and\npositive-square proof are supplied here independently.\n\nThe first pivot u=e0-e1, v=e0-2e1+e2 only rescales its bad inverse entry:\n\n          Phi13(t)=-3/[4(168373+42990t)].               (11)\n\nThus it has no finite zero crossing in its sign-feasible interval. An exact\ncalculation initially attempted to divide by the vanishing zero-crossing\ndenominator and raised `ZeroDivisionError: Fraction(1, 0)`. Rechecking the\nrank-one numerator gave (11); that failed check was not counted as success.\n\nInstead use the backward-skip deletion (7) on indices1,2,3, with a=b=1.\nThe actual backward coefficient lambda31=1/20 is deleted using t=1/20.\nThe only other changes are +1/20 on21, +1/20 on23, and +1/10 on32.\nEvery input coefficient remains nonnegative, and the full remainder gains\n(1/20)||g3-g2||^2. Set Knew=K+(1/20)(e3-e2)(e1-2e2+e3)^T.\nIts EXACT Phi=-Delta Knew^(-1)Delta is the following rational matrix:\n\n [-1/40, 0, 0, 0, 1/200],\n [1/160, -1/24, 0, 0, 37/2400],\n [1/160,18919/1350744,-4760/56281,120/56281,5698397/135074400],\n [1/160,18679/1350744,2440/56281,-14250/56281,22930397/135074400],\n [1/160,18683/1350744,2320/56281,14130/56281,-58401443/135074400].\n\nTogether with the displayed original K and update, this is a finite exact\ninverse certificate: Knew P Phi P=-I, P=Delta^(-1). Every off-diagonal\nis now nonnegative, including Phi13=0; the fixed row/column deficits are\nunchanged. Thus the canonical intrinsic inverse of this RE-SELECTED array\nhas all legal ordinary coefficients. This repairs the exhibited failure\nof one supplied certificate, in accord with its deliberately limited scope.\nIt does not prove a legal pivot exists for every arbitrary certificate.\n\nThe positive edges0->2 and1->3 are both retained. Their endpoints cross\nin chronological order, yet every transformed off-diagonal is now legal.\nThus noncrossing of the undirected support is not NECESSARY for this\nnonsymmetric intrinsic inverse-sign test. This is not a contradiction to\na theorem assuming a symmetric optimal-vertex fixed-point certificate.\n\nOnly tiny5x5 Fraction arithmetic was used for the explicit matrices. The\nfull identities (1)--(10) are symbolic and dimension-independent. No solver\nor verifier allocation was used or reopened.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["4a064f93111ac5a2", "37622e6cdcb606e6", "1f8863f8894876ab", "cc59ae14a26f3e78"], "plan_ids": ["8a9fe85173280c05", "e8ab4e3f583a448e"], "artifact": "local_memory/directed_rank_one_certificate_moves.md", "exact_audits": ["local_memory/rank_one_inverse_pivot_exact_audit.json", "local_memory/backward_skip_inverse_repair_audit.json"], "status": "candidate_only"}, "glossary": {"A": "Trajectory matrix with diagonal1 and A_it=h_t below the diagonal.", "p": "A e, the vector of prefix capacities.", "K": "Grounded matrix L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w).", "u": "Left vector of a rank-one coefficient change; e^T u=0.", "v": "Right vector, orthogonal to e and p.", "z": "A^T v.", "t": "Real scalar pivot parameter.", "M_plus": "Inverse of M on its range and zero on its kernel, denoted M^+ in the proof.", "Phi": "-Delta K^(-1)Delta, whose nonnegative off-diagonals give ordinary reversed coefficients up to a positive scale.", "a": "First of two consecutive physical step sizes in the local square move.", "b": "Second consecutive physical step size in that move.", "d": "Incoming coefficient lambda20 in the compound first-unit repair, separately distinguished from the square-root scalar in the generic PSD-interval section."}}
{"id": "2ae69597611b35a6", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:16:48.958968+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete candidate theorem at arbitrary length, including ZERO steps: every actual E_theta certificate with0<=theta<1 and ZERO full independent-gradient remainder is ALREADY an ordinary intrinsic certificate in the same signed array. Positive short steps force M_ii>=h_i>0, so every positive step on this face is>1; terminal-column identities then force all credited incoming coefficients nonnegative. Zero-step vertices are isolated. Thus any strict terminal-credit enlargement must use a NONZERO full PSD remainder. Also, with all h_i>=1, M_nn=0 alone makes every supplied signed coefficient ordinary.", "evidence": "# Zero full remainder leaves no terminal-credit enlargement\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit call. This solves the scoped mathematical target of plan\nmax4_zero_remainder_directed_reselection; it does not solve general repair.\n\n## Statement\n\nLet h=(h0,...,h_(n-1)) be ANY finite nonnegative schedule, including zero\nsteps and the empty list. Normalize smoothness to1 and put\n\n x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n s=sum h_i,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                          -s(s+1)||g_n||^2,\n J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n), E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.\n\nSuppose 0<=theta<1 and an ACTUAL nonnegative array Lambda cancels every\nformal value coefficient in E_theta+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij and leaves the\nZERO quadratic form on ALL n+1 independent formal gradients. Define\n\n lambda_(i,n)=Lambda_(i,n)-theta h_i for i<n,\n lambda_ij=Lambda_ij on all other distinct pairs.\n\nThen EVERY lambda_ij is already nonnegative. Thus the SAME signed array\nis an ordinary certificate V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0. No re-selection,\nchange of physical steps, discarded gradient, or new PSD matrix is needed.\nIn addition every positive physical step is strictly larger than1.\nAny zero-step query i<n is isolated in the entire array: all outgoing\nand incoming lambda entries at i vanish.\n\n## Full matrix expansion\n\nLet A have diagonal1, A_it=h_t for t<i, and zero above the diagonal.\nLet L be the row Laplacian of lambda, so L_ii=out_i=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij\nand L_ij=-lambda_ij for i!=j. Value cancellation gives\n\n                    out_i-in_i=-2h_i  (i<n),\n                    out_n-in_n=2s.\n\nThe gradient part of Q_ij is\n<g_i,(A g)_i-(A g)_j>+(||g_j||^2-||g_i||^2)/2.\nSumming and inserting the value flows yields the entire symmetric matrix\n\n             M=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2))+sym(L A).       (1)\n\nThis derivation uses all original formal indices, even at zero steps.\n\n## Diagonal constraints exclude positive short steps\n\nFor i<n put Back_i=sum_(j<i)lambda_ij>=0. Since column i of A is zero\nabove i and equal to h_i below i,\n\n M_ii=h_i(2-h_i)+(1-h_i)out_i+h_i Back_i.              (2)\n\nAll outgoing coefficients are nonnegative except possibly lambda_(i,n),\nwhich is bounded below by -theta h_i. Thus out_i>=-theta h_i.\nIf 0<h_i<=1, then (2) gives\n\n M_ii>=h_i[(2-h_i)-theta(1-h_i)]+h_i Back_i\n       =h_i[(2-theta)-(1-theta)h_i]+h_i Back_i\n       >=h_i>0.                                      (3)\n\nThis contradicts M=0. Consequently every positive h_i is greater than1.\nIf h_i=0, every outgoing lambda_ij is nonnegative, including the\nterminal entry because its floor is0. Equation (2) reduces to\nM_ii=out_i=0, forcing every outgoing entry to vanish. Its value flow is0,\nso all incoming entries, also nonnegative because i is nonterminal, vanish.\nThis proves the stated zero-step isolation without identifying gradients.\n\n## Terminal-column constraints force the credited signs\n\nFor i<n put R_i=sum_(k=0)^i lambda_(n,k). Every summand is nonnegative\nbecause its target is nonterminal. The two off-diagonal entries of L A\nneeded for (1) are\n\n (L A)_(i,n)=-lambda_(i,n),\n (L A)_(n,i)=-lambda_(n,i)+h_i R_i.\n\nFor the second formula, use sum_(k>i)L_(n,k)=R_i, a consequence of\nthe zero row sum of L. Thus\n\n              2M_(i,n)=h_i R_i-lambda_(n,i)-lambda_(i,n). (4)\n\nSince M=0, if h_i>1 this gives\n\n lambda_(i,n)=(h_i-1)lambda_(n,i)\n                         +h_i sum_(k<i)lambda_(n,k)>=0. (5)\n\nIf h_i=0 its outgoing entries already vanish. These are all possibilities\nby (3), and all other signed coefficients were nonnegative from the start.\nThis proves the claim, including the empty and all-zero schedules.\n\n## Two useful quantified extensions\n\nFor ANY full PSD E_theta remainder, not necessarily zero, (3) still holds:\na positive step at most1 forces M_ii>=h_i. Such a step cannot occur on\na zero-diagonal face at that query.\n\nIf M is PSD and M_nn=0, its entire terminal column vanishes: for every\ncoordinate i, nonnegativity on e_n+t e_i rules out a nonzero linear term.\nConsequently, if all physical steps are at least1, (4)--(5) already show\nthat the supplied signed array is ordinary even when M is NONZERO.\n\nMore quantitatively, for any index with h_i>=1 and an actually negative\nlambda_(i,n)=-q, q>0, equation (4) gives M_(i,n)>=q/2. The2x2 PSD\nprincipal minor then forces\n\n                         M_ii M_nn>=q^2/4.            (6)\n\nSo such credit requires a nonzero terminal diagonal and nonzero matrix\ncoupling; it cannot live on the terminal-kernel face. For h_i=0 the credit\nfloor already forbids negativity. The full zero-remainder theorem needs\nno extra step-range assumption because (3) supplies it automatically.\n\n## Scope and retrieval\n\nA strictly enlarged E_theta cone, if it exists, must exploit a NONZERO\nfull PSD remainder. The theorem neither moves a general certificate to\nzero remainder nor asserts that an optimal schedule has zero remainder.\nIt does not classify the ordinary zero-remainder schedules or settle the\nobjective profile. Generic inverse-map signs also remain a separate issue.\n\nFact search found no accepted theorem for this intrinsic zero-remainder\nclaim. Matlas returned unrelated1306.6419 Theorem3.1 and2102.07922 Lemma6;\nneither is used. Equations (1)--(6) give the proof without external premises.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_zero_remainder_directed_reselection", "plan_finding_id": "04fc458b83ddb0e7", "plan_screening_status": "candidate proof complete; not verifier-accepted", "finding_ids": ["cc59ae14a26f3e78", "4a064f93111ac5a2"], "artifact": "local_memory/zero_remainder_credit_is_ordinary.md", "status": "candidate_only_no_verifier_allocation"}, "glossary": {"Lambda": "Actual nonnegative interpolation multipliers of the E_theta certificate.", "lambda": "Equivalent intrinsic signed array, subtracting theta h_i from i->n.", "M": "Entire symmetric remainder on the original n+1 formal gradients.", "L": "Row Laplacian of lambda.", "A": "Lower triangular trajectory matrix with diagonal1 and A_it=h_t for t<i.", "out_i": "Sum of all signed multipliers leaving query i.", "in_i": "Sum of all signed multipliers entering query i.", "Back_i": "Sum_(j<i)lambda_ij, necessarily nonnegative.", "R_i": "Sum_(k<=i)lambda_(n,k), necessarily nonnegative.", "q": "Magnitude -lambda_(i,n)>0 of a negative terminal coefficient in the quantitative extension."}}
{"id": "51463365645476f0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:37:32.783508+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete candidate closure ofa0f6a1f128f59b70, extended to EVERY PSD2x2 coupling of mode differences: at z1=(1+i)/(2S),z2=(4+i)/(2S), arbitrary joint legal values, probability weights and unbounded PSD coupling obey2F(xq-bgq)<=21/(50S) for all sufficiently large actual near-critical horizons and everyb in[0,2]. Original exact-profile limsup<=42/(25e)<.63. No verifier call, no unrestrictedW claim.", "evidence": "# Two bridge-adjacent anchors suppress arbitrary PSD difference coupling\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No routine fact_submit\nis allocated. The only accepted inputs are the interpolation convention and\nsmoothness proof in fact1252b8823bb5a290 and the benchmark recursions in\nfactb98bb807d514e80d. No earlier unverified covariance or spectral obstruction\nis used. All new estimates and the exact asymptotic comparison are proved here.\n\n## 1. The actual family and the scope of the claim\n\nFor integers m>=128 put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c_m=lambda(1+1/m),\na_m=(1-1/m)/c_m, d_j=1+a_m c_m^j, H_0=(), and\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). Parameters stay fixed within the horizon.\nLet n=2^m-1, q=n-1, and retain H_m^- by deleting ONLY the original\nlast step d_0. Write S_m=sum H_m^->0 and\nP_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) for the retained chronology, 0<=i<=q.\n\nFix zeta_1=(1+i)/2 and zeta_2=(4+i)/2, and z_l=zeta_l/S_m.\nChoose ANY w_m in [0,1] and ANY real matrix B_m with two columns,\nwithout an upper bound on its entries or number of rows. Set\nR(v)=(Re v,-Im v)^T and define actual Euclidean vectors\n\n x_i=(sqrt(w_m) Re P_i(z_1),sqrt(w_m) Im P_i(z_1),\n      sqrt(1-w_m) Re P_i(z_2),sqrt(1-w_m) Im P_i(z_2),\n      B_m R(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))),\n g_i=(sqrt(w_m) Re[z_1 P_i(z_1)],sqrt(w_m) Im[z_1 P_i(z_1)],\n      sqrt(1-w_m) Re[z_2 P_i(z_2)],sqrt(1-w_m) Im[z_2 P_i(z_2)],\n      B_m R(z_1 P_i(z_1)-z_2 P_i(z_2))).                    (1)\n\nThen ||x_0||=1 and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i exactly. The added coordinates\nstart at zero. The one-row choice B_m=sqrt(rho)(Re omega,Im omega)\nrecovers the whole proposed modela0f6a1f128f59b70, with every rho>=0 and\n|omega|=1. More generally C_m=B_m^T B_m is an arbitrary PSD2x2 matrix.\nSince only C_m affects the data, two added coordinates suffice.\n\nAssume arbitrary JOINT queried values, including the optimum(0,0,0),\nare interpolated by a convex differentiable1-smooth function F with\nF(0)=0=min F. No modewise legality or separable values are imposed.\nFor all sufficiently large m, uniformly over w_m,B_m,F, and b in[0,2],\n\n       2F(x_q-b g_q) <= 21/(50 S_m).                     (2)\n\nThus at the exact project benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(w_m,B_m,F,b) B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n       <=42/(25e)<63/100<1.                             (3)\n\nThe original last step b=d_0 belongs to[0,2]. This is a scoped\nobstruction to the stated two-mode PSD-difference class, including\nunbounded coupling strength and arbitrary joint values. It is not an\nupper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m), or on every zero-initial\ncross-mode metric, or on arbitrary finite normal spectra.\n\n## 2. A single shared two-anchor interpolation inequality\n\nFor any queried index r, the sum of the actual inequalities\nQ_(q,r)<=0 and Q_(r,star)<=0 gives\n\n F(x_q) <= <g_q,x_q>+<g_r-g_q,x_r>\n                 -||g_r||^2/2-||g_q-g_r||^2/2.           (4)\n\nThis follows by expanding Q as in fact1252b8823bb5a290; the joint\nfunction value at r cancels. Drop the two nonnegative squares.\nTake r_-=2^(m-1)-1 and r_+=r_-+1, the actual queries immediately\nbefore and after the unique largest bridge d_(m-1). Average (4)\nwith coefficient1/2 on each. These are shared indices for every mode.\n\nFor l=1,2 define\n\n p_l,m=P_(r_-)(z_l), v_l,m=P_(r_+)(z_l), t_l,m=P_q(z_l),\n D_l,m=Re(zeta_l t_l,m conj(t_l,m))\n       +(1/2)Re[zeta_l(p_l,m-t_l,m)conj(p_l,m)\n                       +zeta_l(v_l,m-t_l,m)conj(v_l,m)].  (5)\n\nFor k in{-,+,q} let Y_k,m be respectively\np_1,m-p_2,m, v_1,m-v_2,m, t_1,m-t_2,m; let U_k,m be the same\ndifferences with the l-th term multiplied by zeta_l. For real column\nvectors a,b let sym(a,b)=(ab^T+ba^T)/2. Define the real symmetric matrix\n\n K_m=sym(R(U_q,m),R(Y_q,m))\n       +(1/2)sym(R(U_-,m-U_q,m),R(Y_-,m))\n       +(1/2)sym(R(U_+,m-U_q,m),R(Y_+,m)).                (6)\n\nThe averaged inequality is the EXACT finite-m bound\n\n S_m F(x_q) <= w_m D_1,m+(1-w_m)D_2,m+trace(C_m K_m).     (7)\n\nNo approximation, deletion of a prefix constraint, or scaling of\nthe initial norm occurs in (7). It suffices to show D_l,m<21/100\nand K_m negative semidefinite for every sufficiently large m.\n\n## 3. Exact scaled product limits for the actual truncated word\n\nLet delta=1-2/lambda=3-2sqrt(2). Direct summation gives\n\n S_m=2^m-2-a_m+a_m(c_m^m-2^m)/(c_m-2),\n S_m/lambda^m -> e/[lambda(lambda-2)]=e.                 (8)\n\nConsequently, for each fixed integer j>=0,\n\n d_(m-1-j)/S_m -> delta lambda^(-j).                     (9)\n\nIn the left H_(m-1) copy, d_(m-1-j) occurs2^(j-1) times for j>=1.\nHence, for either zeta=zeta_l,\n\n L(zeta)=product_(j>=1)(1-delta lambda^(-j)zeta)^(2^(j-1)),\n p_l,m -> p_l=L(zeta_l),\n v_l,m -> v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l,\n t_l,m -> t_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l^2=p_l v_l.              (10)\n\nHere are convergence details, including the many small steps. Write\ne_j,m=d_(m-1-j)/S_m=1/S_m+A_m c_m^(-j), where\nA_m=a_m c_m^(m-1)/S_m ->delta and is bounded. The summed contribution\nof the1/S_m terms in the left copy is at most2^m/S_m ->0. The tail\nsum of the remaining terms over j>=J is bounded by a constant times\nsum_(j>=J)(2/lambda)^j, tending to zero uniformly as J->infinity.\nFor all large m, max_(j>=1)|zeta_l e_j,m|<1/2. On that disk the\nabsolutely convergent series for log(1-u) obeys\n|log(1-u)|<=2|u|. Fixed-j convergence from(9), followed by the tail\nbound, proves convergence of the log products and their exponentials.\nThe identities for v and t use the finite identities\n\n v_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m,\n t_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m^2/(1-d_0 z_l).\n\nThe latter denominator tends to1, since d_0 is bounded and S_m->infinity.\nThis explicitly retains the removed original d_0 and never replaces it\nby an appended unit step. Therefore D_l,m and K_m converge to D_l and K\nobtained by inserting p_l,v_l,t_l into(5)--(6).\n\n## 4. Rational enclosures for the limiting two-by-two matrix\n\nThe needed numerical-looking bounds below are rational inequalities,\nwith a finite power-series derivation. They do not rely on a solver.\nSince the left factors have power sums\n\n mu_r=sum_(j>=1)2^(j-1)(delta lambda^(-j))^r\n       =delta^r/(lambda^r-2),\n\nthe absolutely convergent logarithm is\n\n       log L(zeta)=-sum_(r>=1)mu_r zeta^r/r.              (11)\n\nPut A_4(zeta)=-sum_(r=1)^4 mu_r zeta^r/r. The maximum left coefficient\nis delta/lambda and eta=|zeta|delta/lambda<3/20 for both zeta values.\nAlso mu_2<1/125 and |zeta|^2<=17/4. Bounding mu_r by\nmu_2(delta/lambda)^(r-2), the tail obeys\n\n |log L-A_4| <= mu_2 |zeta|^2 eta^3/[5(1-eta)]\n             <27/10^6.                                 (12)\n\nThe inequality eta<3/20 follows from |zeta|<=sqrt(17)/2<21/10\nand delta/lambda<1/14. The inequality mu_2<1/125 follows by substituting\nlambda=1+sqrt(2), or the square-root enclosure immediately below.\n\nFor complete finite arithmetic details, enclose sqrt(2) by\n\n 14142135623/10^10 < sqrt(2) < 14142135624/10^10,\n\nwhose squared endpoints straddle2. Form lambda,delta,mu_1,...,mu_4\nusing interval addition, multiplication, and reciprocal of positive\nintervals; form A_4. Its real part is negative and its modulus is<1.\nEvaluate E_12(A)=sum_(j=0)^12 A^j/j! by the recurrence t_0=1,\nt_j=t_(j-1) A/j and E_12=sum t_j. Ordinary rational interval arithmetic\ngives the following outward enclosures (every finite decimal is exact):\n\n| zeta | Re E_12(A_4) | Im E_12(A_4) |\n|---|---|---|\n| (1+i)/2 | [.79526080,.79526082] | [-.16872576,-.16872574] |\n| (4+i)/2 | [.42009743,.42009746] | [-.09205447,-.09205445] |\n\nThe exponential-series tail is bounded by e/13!<3/13!. Because\nRe A_4<0, changing A_4 by the log tail changes its exponential by\nat most (27/10^6)/(1-27/10^6). The sum of these two errors is<31/10^6.\nThus the following coarser intervals safely enclose the exact p_l:\n\n| l | Re p_l | Im p_l |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | [.7952,.7953] | [-.1688,-.1686] |\n| 2 | [.4200,.4202] | [-.0921,-.0920] |\n\nFor clarity, the arithmetic in the last substitution can be reproduced\nwithout complex transcendental operations. For intervals a,b,c,d use\n(a+ib)(c+id)=(ac-bd)+i(ad+bc), with conjugation negating the imaginary\ninterval. Use delta in[.17157,.17158], set v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l\nand t_l=p_l v_l, then evaluate(5)--(6) by these operations. The resulting\noutward rational bounds are\n\n 0<D_1<209/1000,        0<D_2<209/1000,\n -101/1000<K_11<-97/1000,\n -57/1000<K_22<-54/1000,\n -64/1000<K_12=K_21<-61/1000.                            (13)\n\nThese deliberately loose bounds avoid relying on printed floating digits.\nThey imply K_11<0 and\n\n det K >[(97)(54)-64^2]/10^6=1142/10^6>0.\n\nCompleting the square in a real two-variable quadratic now proves K is\nnegative definite. Since all its entries and D_l,m converge, for all\nsufficiently large m, K_m is negative definite and D_l,m<21/100.\nThis is uniform in w_m,C_m because these parameters do not enter the\ncoefficient limits. In particular C_m is allowed to diverge arbitrarily\nfast: trace(C_m K_m)<=0 holds at the EXACT finite m, before any limit.\nEquation(7) gives F(x_q)<=21/(100S_m).\n\nFor audit convenience only, two_anchor_interval_check.py repeats the\ndisplayed finite rational operations in a few milliseconds. Its output\nis not a replacement for(11)--(13) and their explicit remainder bounds.\nThe first script attempt had a Python Interval/ComplexInterval dispatch\nTypeError; reversing a scalar-complex multiplication repaired it. The\ncorrected script exits0 and checks all the displayed enclosures. No\noptimization call was made in this arithmetic check.\n\n## 5. Free terminal step and the exact original benchmark\n\nFor any actual interpolating F, integrating its1-Lipschitz gradient on\nthe segment from x_q to x_q-b g_q gives\n\n F(x_q-b g_q)<=F(x_q)-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=F(x_q),\n\nfor every b in[0,2]. The terminal gradient is unrestricted. This proves(2).\n\nWe include the benchmark estimate rather than importing an unverified\ncomparison. Put r=log_2 lambda, C_k=S_circle(k)+1, and B_k=2S_bullet(k)+1.\nThe accepted recursions in factb98bb807d514e80d give\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nFor V(t)=(1+t)^r-1, direct differentiation gives\n\n d^2/du^2 log V(exp u)\n =r t(1+t)^(r-2)[(1+t)^r-1-r t]/V(t)^2>0,  t=exp u.\n\nThe final strict positivity follows by differentiating\n(1+t)^r-1-r t, which vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for t>0.\nMidpoint convexity at u,-u implies\nV(t)V(1/t)>=(2^r-1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.\nFor A,B>0 set t=(A/B)^(1/r) and M=(A^(1/r)+B^(1/r))^r.\nThen (M-A)(M-B)>=2AB, so M>=Omega(A,B), by the positive root of\nthe quadratic and M>max(A,B). Induction yields C_k<=(k+1)^r.\nAlso sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A gives G(A,B)<=4A+B. Using\nu^r+v^r<=(u+v)^r for nonnegative u,v, induction yields B_n<=1+4n^r.\nThat elementary inequality follows by differentiating (1+t)^r-t^r.\n\nAt n=2^m-1, n^r/lambda^m->1, while(8) gives S_m/lambda^m->e.\nCombining this with(2) proves the first bound in(3). Finally\ne>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3 gives42/(25e)<63/100.\n\n## 6. What remains open\n\nThis closes the concrete order-one two-block proposal, more broadly for\nevery PSD coupling of the real and imaginary mode differences. It does\nnot close all PSD additions annihilating the initial vector. That larger\nzero-initial subspace has dimension3: one may also independently combine\nIm P_i(z_1) and Im P_i(z_2), instead of requiring opposite coefficients.\nThe same two-anchor matrix on that three-dimensional space is not negative\nsemidefinite; a specific positive direction is screened separately.\nNeither such a positive certificate coefficient nor the finite solver\ndiagnostics supplies a legal trajectory. {"id": "c7f10ed5dc99275b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:39:58.378841+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Constructive full-objective repair of the failed critical-array join: exact nonnegative multipliers now certify H_3 (including the ORIGINAL m3 diagnostic) using a retained cross-block square. Explicit q_3 is given in (26a), with all value/Gram coefficients and optimum edges. The new sufficient recursion fixes H=ell=0, permits supercritical joins, and retains every child remainder, but its exact storage law Y_(j+1)=Y_j²/c² forces multiplier failure by j=ceil(log2 m)+4 on the growing critical words. Thus this repaired rule cannot certify the target horizon; nonzero mixed boundary terms in the full recurrence (18) remain open.", "evidence": "# Direct objective gluing on the actual critical words\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint calculation and certificate-space obstruction.\nNo solver and no fact_submit. The negative formal vectors below are NOT legal\nsmooth-convex trajectories. They refute a specified full multiplier array and\nits stated repairs, not the schedule or the original profile conjecture.\n\nThe only mathematical predecessor used is accepted fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` for interpolation, and `b98bb807d514e80d` for the\nHuber/cut witnesses and the exact benchmark recursions. The recursive pattern\nwas suggested by Altschuler--Parrilo, arXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4; all\nidentities needed here are derived below rather than imported from it.\n\n## 1. Normalization and a fully specified multiplier array\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every sample let\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n    C_ij=-2 Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.\n\nEvery position is substituted using the actual GD steps. In the full formal\nquadratic, x_0,g_0,...,g_n are independent; they are not restricted to any\nchosen primal family.\n\nFix positive numbers a,c, with c>2. Write\n\n    d_j=a c^j,  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThroughout this calculation require d_j<M_j at the levels being used.\nDefine the following exact scalar recurrence:\n\n    Z_0=1, t_0=1,\n    z_j=(M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    r_j=1+2z_j=(3M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    Z_(j+1)=r_j Z_j,\n    t_(j+1)=(1+sqrt(4 Z_(j+1)-3))/2,\n    delta_j=Z_j-r_j d_j^2.                              (1)\n\nThus Z_j=t_j^2-t_j+1. All r_j>1, so the square roots are real and\nt_j>=1. This fixes every parameter; there is no numerical optimization.\n\nHere is the actual nonnegative array lambda^(j) on C_ij. Its base has only\nlambda^0_(star,0)=1. Suppose its block has length n=n_j, sum S=S_j,\nand put p=n, q=2n+1, b=1+d_j, z=z_j, r=r_j, t=t_j, t'=t_(j+1).\n\n1. Embed the old array on the left sample indices {0,...,p,star}.\n2. Add r times the old array on {p+1,...,q,star}.\n3. For each right queried index i=p+1,...,q-1, whose physical step is h_i,\n   add z h_i to entries (p,i) and (q,i), and subtract 2z h_i from (star,i).\n4. Add the following six sparse corrections:\n\n    (p,q): z,                 (q,p): z d_j,\n    (p,star): -S,             (q,star): 2S+b-rS,\n    (star,p): b-t,            (star,q): t'-rt.           (2)\n\nThe corrections individually may be signed, but the FINAL array is\nnonnegative. In fact its optimum entries are exactly\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i for i<q,\n    lambda_(star,q)=t',\n    lambda_(i,star)=0 for i<q,\n    lambda_(q,star)=2S+b.                                (3)\n\nTo verify this inductively, left interior entries are unchanged, the old\nleft terminal entry t becomes b, right interior entries become\n(r-2z)h_i=h_i, and the right terminal entry becomes t'. Incoming optimum\nentries at the left terminal cancel, and the right terminal entry becomes\n2S+b. Every other update either embeds a nonnegative old coefficient or\nadds z h_i, z, or z d_j. This proves actual coefficient signs, including\nall optimum edges, rather than just signs of an auxiliary matrix.\n\n## 2. Complete identity and calibration\n\nThere is an exact gradient-only remainder E_j, defined on all the formal\ngradients of the block, such that\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-t_j g_end||^2-2t_j f_end\n       =sum lambda^(j)_ik C_ik + E_j.                  (4)\n\nIts recursion is\n\n    E_0=0,\n    E_(j+1)=E_j(left)+r_j E_j(right)\n                         +delta_j ||g_p||^2.            (5)\n\nHere each child is evaluated at its actual chronological sample indices.\nIn particular E_j contains no terminal-gradient term of its own: its\nvariables are only the midpoint gradients of all internal recursion nodes.\n\nWe verify (4)--(5) without omitting any function coefficient. Denote the\nleft endpoint and gradient by x,g and the right endpoint and gradient by\ny,v. Their values are f,F. The weighted right displacement is\n\n    w=sum_(p<i<q) h_i g_i=x-bg-y.\n\nThe rank-one correction in step 3 sums to\n\n    2zS(f+F)-zS(||g||^2+||v||^2)\n                         -2z<w,x+y-g-v>.               (6)\n\nCombine the two child identities, the correction (6), and (2). The\ncoefficient of f is\n\n    2[(z-1)S-b+z-zd_j]=0,\n\nbecause z(S+2-b)=S+b. The coefficient of F is -2t', and every\ninterior value has already canceled within its child and (6). The\noptimum value is zero under normalization; before normalization the\ntotal value flow cancels it against the terminal coefficient.\n\nThe x^2, <x,g>, <x,v>, <y,g>, and <g,v> coefficients all cancel.\nThe remaining expression is\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||y-t'v||^2-2t'F\n       +(r d_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2\n       +(Z_(j+1)-r Z_j)||v||^2\n       -E_j(left)-r E_j(right).\n\nThe last gradient coefficient is zero by (1). This is exactly (4)--(5).\nThis calculation also follows by direct expansion of (6); the cancellations\nuse only z(S+2-b)=S+b and r=1+2z.\n\nThe calibration is exact. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2), a=1/lambda, c=lambda.\nThen M_j=lambda^j, d_j=lambda^(j-1), z_j=lambda, r_j=lambda^2,\nZ_j=M_j^2, and delta_j=0 at every level. Thus E_j=0 and (4) is a\nvalid full initial-distance certificate at the undeformed silver word.\nIt is the canonical endpoint-square guarantee, not a claim that this\nparticular certificate attains the sharper Huber constant.\n\nFor an arbitrary real q_cert, the SAME nonnegative array supplies the\nfollowing fully value-canceled candidate objective matrix:\n\n    q_cert ||x_0||^2-2 f_n\n       +sum_(i!=k) [2 lambda^(m)_ki/t_m] Q_ik\n     =(q_cert-1/t_m)||x_0||^2\n       +[||x_n-t_m g_n||^2+E_m]/t_m.                 (7)\n\nThis explicitly includes the true initial vector and the optimum index.\nIts validity as an upper bound depends on FULL PSD of the right side.\n\n## 3. An exact negative midpoint for the assigned parameters\n\nNow set m>=128, epsilon=1/m, and use the assigned family\n\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c.\n\nFor these parameters 2<c<3 and 0<a<c-2<1. The exact mass formula is\n\n    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2),                 (8)\n\nso 0<A<1 and M_j-d_j=A(3-c)c^j+(1-A)2^j>0.\nThus every coefficient in the proposed array is defined and nonnegative.\n\nNevertheless delta_2<0. Here is a finite exact proof with an explicit\nparameter interval, not a limiting differential equation. Put s=sqrt(2).\nFor j=0,1,2, write r_j=N_j/D_j, where\n\n    N_0=(2+s)+(4-s)epsilon,\n    D_0=(2-s)+s epsilon,\n    N_1=(4+3s)+(9-3s)epsilon-epsilon^2,\n    D_1=s+(3-s)epsilon+epsilon^2,\n    N_2=(10+7s)+(19-5s)epsilon-(4+s)epsilon^2\n                                                 -(1+s)epsilon^3,\n    D_2=(2+s)+(5-3s)epsilon+s epsilon^2\n                                                 +(1+s)epsilon^3.\n\nAll denominator coefficients are positive. Since d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n\n    delta_2=P(epsilon)/(D_0 D_1 D_2),\n    P=N_0 N_1 D_2-lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)^2 N_2 D_0 D_1.\n\nThe coefficients of P, in increasing degree 0 through 10, are\n\n    0,\n    -112-96s,\n    -96-84s,\n    640+12s,\n    528+368s,\n    88+92s,\n    -320-167s,\n    -202-158s,\n    -2-4s,\n    42+30s,\n    10+7s.                                           (9)\n\nThese coefficients follow by multiplying the displayed degree-at-most-three\npolynomials. A tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) polynomial multiplication independently\nchecked the list; the displayed identity is the mathematical evidence.\nUsing s<3/2, the sum of absolute upper bounds for coefficients of degree\nat least two is 3311. Consequently, for 0<epsilon<=1/128,\n\n    P(epsilon)/epsilon\n       <=-112-96s+3311 epsilon\n       <-112+3311/128<0.                             (10)\n\nFor comparison, the first derivative is\n\n    delta_2=-(48+28sqrt(2))epsilon+O(epsilon^2).\n\nThe same negative coefficient occurs at the finite diagnostic m=3:\n\n    P(1/3)=(-1059632-2141552sqrt(2))/59049<0.\n\nThe three relevant denominators are positive there as well. This agrees\nwith failure of the ARRAY even though max3's unrestricted numerical H_3\ndiagnostic reported a Huber match. That diagnostic is not used as a premise.\n\n## 4. Negative FULL formal directions, and limits of repairs\n\nEvery occurrence of a level-two midpoint contributes\n\n    omega_i delta_2 ||g_i||^2\n\nto E_m, with omega_i>0, a product of right-child weights r_j from its\nancestors. There are K=2^(m-3) such indices. Their actual step is the same\nnumber b_2=1+d_2. No other term of E_m uses any of those gradient\ncoordinates, by the diagonal recursion (5).\n\nFirst choose one such index i, set x_0=0, g_i=v!=0, all other queried\ngradients zero, and g_n=-(b_2/t_m)v. Then\n\n    x_n=-b_2 v, x_n-t_m g_n=0, E_m=omega_i delta_2||v||^2<0.\n\nThus (7) is not PSD for ANY q_cert. Merely increasing the initial-distance\ncoefficient does not repair the proposed array.\n\nThere is a larger obstruction when m>=4. Work first with scalar formal\ngradients, put x_0=g_n=0, allow arbitrary g_i at the K selected indices\nsubject to sum_i g_i=0, and set every other gradient to zero. This is a\nsubspace D of dimension K-1 on which\n\n    x_n=0, g_(n-1)=0,\n    E_m=delta_2 sum_i omega_i g_i^2<0\n\nfor every nonzero vector. The weights need not coincide. It follows that:\n\n* Any added quadratic depending only on x_0,x_n,g_n,g_(n-1) vanishes on D.\n* Replacing only the final physical step leaves every position unchanged\n  on D, because g_(n-1)=0. With the same array, the negative evaluation\n  persists for every prescribed final-step replacement and every q_cert.\n* Any PSD matrix addition of rank less than K-1 has a nonzero kernel vector\n  in D, by dimension counting, and therefore cannot repair the full matrix.\n\nThe last statement is about additions to this fixed matrix. Changing\nrelative interpolation multipliers may have an indefinite difference and\nis NOT excluded. Likewise a correction of growing rank is not excluded.\nThe selected vectors deliberately do not satisfy the interpolation\nconstraints; no legal lower witness, no W upper bound, and no impossibility\ntheorem for H_m is inferred.\n\n## 5. Fixed-constant deformations: the same ansatz always fails for tau>0\n\nConsider the allowed neighboring family\n\n    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=(1-sigma/m)/c,                (11)\n\nwith fixed real tau,sigma, for all sufficiently large m. Positivity and\nd_j<M_j hold uniformly over the word once 0<a<1 and 2<c<3: indeed\n\n    M_j/d_j=1/(c-2)\n               +[1/a-1/(c-2)](2/c)^j\n\nlies between 1/a and 1/(c-2), both greater than one.\n\nIf tau>0, the specified multiplier recursion always develops a negative\ncoefficient at a FIXED level as m tends to infinity. If sigma+tau<0,\nthen d_0>1/lambda and delta_0=1-r_0 d_0^2<0, since\nd maps to d^2(3+d)/(1-d) is strictly increasing for 0<d<1 and equals\none at d=1/lambda.\n\nFor sigma+tau>=0 the fixed level j=4 suffices. To show this, temporarily\nregard epsilon as a real variable tending to zero and set\n\n    u=2/lambda, D=sigma+(1+lambda^2)tau,\n    T_j=lambda^2 tau-D u^j.\n\nDifferentiation at epsilon=0 of the explicit finite formulas gives\n\n    M_j(0)=lambda^j,\n    (d/depsilon)log M_j |0=j tau-D(1-u^j),\n    (d/depsilon)(d_j/M_j)|0=(1/lambda)T_j.\n\nFor r(x)=(3+x)/(1-x), (d/dx)log r at x=1/lambda equals 2.\nUsing 1-u=1/lambda^2 and\n2(1/lambda-1/lambda^2)/(1-u)=2sqrt(2), equation (1) therefore gives\n\n    (d/depsilon)[delta_j/M_j^2]|0\n       =2sqrt(2)[tau(j-lambda^2)-D(1-2u^j)].          (12)\n\nOne can also verify (12) by summing the finite geometric series for T_j.\nAt j=4, 4-lambda^2<0 and 1-2u^4>0. The latter follows from\nu^4=272-192sqrt(2)<1/2; squaring 543<384sqrt(2) verifies it with\npositive sides. Moreover D>=lambda^2 tau>0. Thus (12) is strictly\nnegative. The expression is an analytic finite rational function near\nzero, so delta_4<0 for every sufficiently large m. This uses a derivative\nat one fixed finite depth, not an unproved continuum recurrence.\n\nThe same full-vector argument as in Section 4 then excludes the array and\nits stated endpoint or fixed-rank patches. This generalization does not\nexclude a different full certificate for any of the deformed schedules.\n\n## 6. Rechecking the exact comparison and an actual deformation filter\n\nSet rho=log_2(lambda). From the accepted exact recursions, primitive shifted\nmass C_n=S_circle(n)+1 has join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand the benchmark B_n has join\n\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nHere is a proof of C_n<=(n+1)^rho and B_n<=1+4n^rho, retained rather\nthan assuming the earlier draft comparison. Define F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1.\nFor t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp(z))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n        [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe numerator bracket is positive since rho>1 and its derivative is\nrho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0. Convexity at z,-z gives\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. With A=t^rho B and\nE=(1+t)^rho B, this implies (E-A)(E-B)>=2AB, so\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nInduction in the primitive recursion gives its claimed bound. Also\nG(A,D)<=4A+D. If k+l+1=n, the dominant induction now gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho.\n\nThe last inequality follows from superadditivity of t^rho on nonnegative\narguments, which follows by integrating its increasing derivative.\n\nFor (11), the exact mass formula (8) gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+tau/m)^m [A+(1-A)(2/c)^m] -> exp(tau).       (13)\n\nThus the actual benchmark satisfies\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)/(2M_m-1)<=2 exp(-tau).             (14)\n\nIn particular tau=1 recovers 2/e. This comparison alone is not an\nobjective upper bound; the failed matrix in Section 4 cannot supply one.\n\nThere is also a useful LOWER bound for the exact benchmark. Balanced\nprimitive joins give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j. Put b_0=1 and\n\n    b_(j+1)=G(1,b_j)/lambda.\n\nBalanced dominant joins imply B_(2^j-1)>=b_j lambda^j. The map in this\nrecursion is strictly increasing. Its fixed point above one is\n\n    b_infty=sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2))>2.                (15)\n\nTo verify the fixed point, squaring the equation G(1,b)=lambda b\ngives lambda(lambda-1)b^2-4lambda b+4=0; the positive root (15)\nsatisfies the original equation, whereas the smaller algebraic root does\nnot. Alternatively uniqueness above one follows because\nG(1,b)/b=2/b+1/2+sqrt(1+8/b)/2 is strictly decreasing. Since\n1<b_1=4/lambda<b_infty, monotonicity shows b_j increases to b_infty.\n\nThis yields an ACTUAL legal-witness filter even when the final step is\nreplaced arbitrarily. Let P_m=S_m-(1+d_0) be the sum of the queried prefix\nbefore that final step, and prescribe any b_m>=0. For P_m>=1, the two\naccepted Huber/cut witnesses give\n\n    W_(n_m)(H_m with final step b_m)\n       >=max{1/(2P_m+2b_m+1),\n                    (b_m-1)_+^2/(P_m+1)^2}\n       >=1/(2P_m+3+2sqrt(P_m)).                       (16)\n\nFor b_m<=1+sqrt(P_m), the first term proves (16). For larger b_m, the\nsecond is at least P_m/(P_m+1)^2, which is at least its right side\nbecause P_m^2+P_m+2P_m sqrt(P_m)-1>=0 for P_m>=1.\nEquations (13), (15), and (16) prove the uniform statement\n\n    liminf_m B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(modified word)\n                     >=b_infty/(2 exp(tau)).           (17)\n\nConsequently no fixed asymptotic separation below the benchmark is possible\nwithin these fixed-constant deformations, even with an arbitrary final step,\nwhen tau<=log(b_infty/2). This is a legal lower-bound conclusion for that\nparameter region. It is distinct from the formal-matrix obstruction for\ntau>0, and does not settle the assigned tau=1 family.\n\n## 7. A richer exact join, with the unresolved full-matrix issue exposed\n\nThe following calculation records the next concrete repair interface. It\ndoes not assert that a scalar local condition closes the target.\n\nReplace the canonical child storage by\n\n    I=||x_start||^2-||x_end-u0 g_end||^2-2v0 f_end\n       =sum lambda C+E,\n    Z=(u0-1)^2+v0,\n    k=S+u0-v0>=0.\n\nAssume its optimum entries are h_i on (star,i), u0 on the terminal\n(star,end), zero on other (i,star), and k on (end,star); retain the\nENTIRE gradient remainder E. These properties hold for the calibrated\ncanonical family. They are assumptions of this interface, not all full\ncertificates.\n\nUse two nonnegative correction weights A0,B0. Scale the right child by\nr=1+A0+B0. For each right queried i add A0 h_i on (p,i), B0 h_i on\n(q,i), and -(A0+B0)h_i on (star,i). Choose cross coefficients p0,q0>=0\nwith\n\n    p0-q0=b+(1-A0)S.\n\nAdjust optimum entries to physical h_i, terminal u1, and\nk1=2S+b+u1-v1>=0 exactly as in (2), with child k in place of S.\nThus the actual final array is nonnegative provided the named scalar\ncoefficients and u1,k1 are nonnegative. Put\n\n    d=b-1, M=S+1, t=A0-B0,\n    H=M+d-B0(M-d)-t M,\n    ell=p0-B0-t,\n    Z1=(u1-1)^2+v1,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q.\n\nFull coefficient expansion, including all function values, gives\n\n    E_new=E_left+r E_right\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g_p+ell g_q>\n       +(Z-rd^2)||g_p||^2+(rZ-Z1)||g_q||^2.           (18)\n\nFor example the canonical construction sets A0=B0=z, p0=z,\nq0=zd, H=ell=t=0 and Z1=rZ, recovering (5).\nTo check (18), first use D=x_p-x_q. The local coefficients of\nD^2, D g_p, D g_q are t,-2(ell+t-H),2(ell+t).\nSubstituting D=V+g_p-g_q makes the g_p g_q coefficient zero and gives\nexactly the displayed diagonal and cross terms. The value coefficient\nat p is 2[(A0-1)S-b+p0-q0]=0 and the terminal coefficient is -2v1.\n\nDemanding the isolated three-vector matrix in (18) be PSD is only a\nSUFFICIENT restriction. It forces Z>=rd^2 and Z1<=rZ, and risks\nrepeating the already recorded scalar-deficit failure. The useful open\nquestion is whether the FULL child remainders can pay for an indefinite\nlocal term.\n\nThere is a finite exact way to retain that information for this array\nclass. Define the gradient moment z_block=sum h_i g_i+g_end. If a child\nremainder is decomposed into nonnegative internal squares plus the sharp\ntwo-variable form\n\n    a0||z_block||^2+2b0<z_block,g_end>+c0||g_end||^2,\n\nthen the parent remainder before elimination is the explicit four-variable\nform, in L=z_left, g=g_p, R=z_right, e=g_q,\n\n    a0||L||^2+2b0<L,g>+c0||g||^2\n      +r[a0||R||^2+2b0<R,e>+c0||e||^2]\n      +t||d g+R||^2+2<d g+R,(H-ell)g+ell e>\n      +(Z-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ-Z1)||e||^2.                (19)\n\nThe parent moment is exactly z_parent=L+d g+R. Substituting\nL=z_parent-dg-R and completing squares in g,R gives its next\ntwo-variable form when the corresponding eliminated block is PSD and\nthe cross columns lie in its range. Negative eliminated directions are\nfull-matrix obstructions and must not be dropped. This is an explicit\nrecursively generated multiplier family and exact remainder interface;\nno choice of its free parameters or uniform constant establishing the\nrequested asymptotic separation has yet been proved.\n\nThe original target remains open. The finished result of this attempt is\nthe fully exhibited obstruction to (1)--(7), the exact parameter filter\n(17), and the identified need to change the full recursive array rather\nthan inflate its endpoint bound.\n\n## 8. A constructive cross-block repair, and its exact finite-depth limit\n\nThere is an actual positive repair of the first bad join in Section 3.\nIt is useful to record it before diagnosing its later failure. Everything\nin this section remains part of the same unverified joint calculation.\n\nFirst an elementary seed, including its full initial-distance certificate.\nFor one physical step h with 0<h<=sqrt(2), put M=1+h. Direct expansion\nof the interpolation expressions gives\n\n    V_h=2h(f_0-f_1)+h(1-h)||g_0||^2-hM||g_1||^2\n       =M C_(0,1)+C_(1,0)+(2-h^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nAdding h C_(star,0)+M C_(star,1) gives\n\n    ||x_0||^2-||x_1-Mg_1||^2-2(2M-1)f_1\n       =sum lambda C+(2-h^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.             (20)\n\nAll four displayed multipliers are nonnegative. Its parameters in Section\n7 are u0=M, v0=2M-1, Z=M^2, k=0. Its exact remainder is\na0||z_block-Mg_1||^2 with a0=(2-h^2)/h^2.\n\nHere is an explicit subcritical join that builds the H_2 child needed next.\nSuppose a block of mass M has u0=M,v0=2M-1,Z=M^2,k=0 and a full PSD\nremainder E. Put x=d/M, assume 0<x<sqrt(2)-1, and set\n\n    A0=1/x, B0=2+x, r=3+x+1/x,\n    t=1/x-2-x>0,\n    eta=x^2+2x-1<0,\n    ell=M eta/x, p0=(1+M eta)/x, q0=M,\n    u1=2M+d, v1=2(2M+d)-1, Z1=(2M+d)^2.             (21)\n\nRequire p0>=0. These parameters satisfy the value-flow and optimum-edge\nconditions of Section 7. If the child remainder is at least\na0||z_block-Mg_end||^2, then the FULL new remainder is at least\n\n    a0||U||^2+(r a0+t)||V||^2\n                    +M^2(-eta)||g_p-g_q||^2,          (22)\n\nwhere U=z_left-Mg_p and V=z_right-Mg_q. All omitted terms here are\nthe actual nonnegative child remainders after their stated lower forms\nare removed. Formula (22) follows by substituting (21) into (18);\nthe mixed coefficient between V and g_p-g_q is A0 d-M=0.\nThe parent moment obeys\n\n    z_parent-(2M+d)g_q=U+V+(M+d)(g_p-g_q).\n\nWeighted completion of squares therefore also gives the sharp condensed\nlower form a1||z_parent-(2M+d)g_q||^2, where\n\n    1/a1=1/a0+1/(r a0+t)+(1+x)^2/(-eta).             (23)\n\nThere is no discarded negative direction in this completion: the\ndifference between the three squares in (22) and (23) is a sum of\nnonnegative squares, or follows by expanding weighted Cauchy--Schwarz.\n\nApply (20) with h=1+d_0 for the assigned epsilon=1/m<=1/128.\nHere 1<h<sqrt(2). At the next bridge d_1=1-epsilon,\n\n    d_1-(sqrt(2)-1)M_1\n      =-epsilon[1-2(sqrt(2)-1)^2/(1+epsilon)]<0.\n\nThus (21) is subcritical. Also 3/8<x<sqrt(2)-1 and M_1<5/2, so\neta>=-7/64 and 1+M_1 eta>=1-35/128>0. All actual multipliers in\n(21) are therefore nonnegative. This proves a full H_2 certificate with\nZ_2=M_2^2 and a positive condensed coefficient a1, directly from (20)--(23).\n\nThe next bridge is genuinely supercritical. With r0=sqrt(2)-1,\n\n    d_2-r0 M_2\n      =epsilon[(4-7r0)-2epsilon-lambda epsilon^2]\n                                                   /(1+epsilon)>0. (24)\n\nThe numerator bracket is positive since 4-7r0>1 and epsilon<=1/128.\nAlso x=d_2/M_2<1/2: the ratios d_j/M_j increase to c-2<1/2.\n\nFor ANY primitive child with mass M and any bridge ratio\n\n    sqrt(2)-1 < x=d/M <= (sqrt(5)-1)/2,\n\nchoose instead\n\n    r=1/x^2,\n    A0=1/x, B0=1/x^2-1-1/x>=0,\n    t=A0-B0=(x^2+2x-1)/x^2>0,\n    ell=H=0, p0=A0, q0=B0 d,\n    Z1=M^2/x^2.                                     (25)\n\nChoose u1>=0 solving u1^2-u1+(2M+d)=Z1, and put\nv1=2M+d-1+u1, whenever that root is real and v1>0. At the present\nH_2-to-H_3 join this holds: x<1/2 and M=M_2>4 give\nZ1>4M^2>2M+d. Thus u1=(1+sqrt(1+4(Z1-2M-d)))/2>1,\nv1>0, and k1=0.\n\nThe relation p0-q0=b+(1-A0)S is exact. All actual multipliers are\nnonnegative, and (18) becomes\n\n    E_new=E_left+r E_right\n                   +t||x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q||^2 >=0.       (26)\n\nBoth endpoint diagonal residuals in (18) vanish because Z=M^2,\nr=1/x^2, and Z1=rZ. This is a FULL valid initial-distance objective\ncertificate, with q_cert=1/v1 and multipliers 2lambda_ki/v1 on Q_ik.\nIts matrix is\n\n    [||x_n-u1 g_n||^2+E_new]/v1,\n\non the full original formal vectors. It repairs the first failed midpoint\nof the canonical array. The claim is for the finite H_3 subblock with the\nparameters of the assigned m, not an asymptotic bound for H_m.\n\nThe same explicit construction ALSO applies to the original finite m=3\ndiagnostic, without the small-epsilon estimates. In that case\n\n    d_0=(sqrt(2)-1)/2, d_1=2/3, d_2=8lambda/9,\n    M_1=2+(sqrt(2)-1)/2,\n    M_2=11/3+sqrt(2), M_3=(74+26sqrt(2))/9,\n    Z_3=(10707-1022sqrt(2))/64.\n\nFor the subcritical join, 8/27<d_1/M_1<sqrt(2)-1 and M_1<9/4.\nThus 1+M_1 eta>1-(9/4)(233/729)>0. At the next join\nd_2-(sqrt(2)-1)M_2=[7-16(sqrt(2)-1)]/9>0 and d_2/M_2<1/2.\nEvery sign condition in (20)--(26) therefore holds. An exact upper level is\n\n    q_3=1/[M_3-1+(1+sqrt(1+4(Z_3-M_3)))/2].          (26a)\n\nThis level exceeds the Huber value because Z_3<M_3^2. It is an exact\nfinite full certificate, not a reconstruction or validation of max3's\nfloating optimal dual, and not a benchmark improvement.\n\nFor completeness, (26) retains more information than an endpoint scalar.\nIf both children have sharp remainder a1||z-Mg||^2, its condensed\nremainder is\n\n    a2||z_parent+(M/x)g_q||^2,\n    1/a2=2/a1+(1+x)^2/(x^2+2x-1).                   (27)\n\nTo verify this, set U=z_left-Mg_p, V=z_right-Mg_q,\nT=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q=d g_p+z_right. Then\nz_parent+(M/x)g_q=U-V/x+(1+1/x)T, while (26) is bounded below by\na1||U||^2+(a1/x^2)||V||^2+[(x^2+2x-1)/x^2]||T||^2.\nWeighted completion gives (27). The positive sign of the terminal term\nin (27) matters; replacing it by z_parent-M_new g_q loses the state.\n\nOne natural continuation of this repair has a precise limit. Keep H=ell=0\nin (18), and certify the new local term separately by its three\nnonnegative square coefficients. For arbitrary child storage Z, the\nconditions are\n\n    t>=0, Z>=r d^2, Z1<=rZ,\n    A0=(M+rd)/(M+d),\n    B0=[rM-2M-d]/(M+d)>=0.                           (28)\n\nConsequently r>=2+d/M and Z1<=Z^2/d^2. Choosing the largest\nadmissible r=Z/d^2 and Z1=rZ gives an actual recursive full certificate\nas long as B0,t>=0 and the endpoint root defining u1 remains admissible.\nIt preserves the full child matrices in (26); the following failure is\nnot caused by deleting them.\n\nAt the first supercritical input j=2, Z_2=M_2^2. Under the maximal rule\nput Y_j=Z_j/d_j^2. Because d_(j+1)=c d_j, its exact recurrence is\n\n    Y_(j+1)=Y_j^2/c^2,\n    Y_j=c^2 T^(2^(j-2)),\n    T=M_2^2/(c^2 d_2^2).                             (29)\n\nFor epsilon<=1/128 one has T<=1-epsilon/2. An explicit check is as\nfollows. The ratio M_2/(c d_2) has numerator\n\n    lambda^2+(8-2lambda)epsilon-epsilon^2\n\nand denominator lambda^2(1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^3. The denominator\ntimes (1-epsilon/2), minus the numerator, is\n\n    epsilon[(5lambda-13/2)+(1-lambda^2)epsilon\n                    -2lambda^2 epsilon^2+(lambda^2/2)epsilon^4]>0.\n\nIndeed 5lambda-13/2>5, lambda^2<6, and the negative terms have\nmagnitude less than 5/128+12/128^2. Squaring the resulting positive\nratio bound gives T<=(1-epsilon/2)^2<=1-epsilon/2.\n\nUsing c<5/2, equation (29) implies\n\n    Y_j <= (25/4) exp[-2^(j-2)/(2m)].                 (30)\n\nFor j=ceil(log_2 m)+4, the right side is at most (25/4)exp(-2)<2,\nbecause exp(2)>1+2+2^2/2=5>25/8. But (28) requires\nY_j>=r>=2+d_j/M_j>2. Hence this continuation must fail no later\nthan that level; for m>=128 the level is strictly below m.\n\nThe same upper recurrence bounds ANY continuation within the isolated\nPSD-local-square restriction (28) that starts at Z_2<=M_2^2: smaller\nZ or smaller r cannot improve the later bound. Such a continuation can\nfail earlier, including from an endpoint root or a different coefficient\nsign, but cannot survive to the required horizon. This is a limitation\nof (28), not of the general full recurrence (18). Allowing H or ell to\nbe nonzero and using the entire indefinite local/positive child matrix\ninteraction remains outside this obstruction.\n\nAn independent tiny rational coefficient audit in\n`research/direct_gluing_exact_audit.py` checked the one-step seed, a\nsubcritical three-step join, and a supercritical seven-step join. It matched\nEVERY function and FULL Gram coefficient exactly and checked every final\nmultiplier sign using Fraction arithmetic. {"id": "dee433e21acc29dc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:49:10.749704+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Nonzero-remainder directed selection: complete candidate finite two-move-per-leaf selector on an explicit saturated tree class, with exact actual-coefficient intervals and a full inverse-sign composition table. Also an exact long-step obstruction to range-restricted pivot termination: ordinary rank-one remainders can freeze every such pivot while Phi_(n-2,n)<-1/36. General alternate-certificate selection and the exact profile remain open.", "evidence": "# Directed selection with a nonzero singular remainder\n\nStatus: complete candidate derivations, NOT verifier-accepted. No optimization\nor verifier call. These are a scoped multi-move selection theorem and an exact\nobstruction to a specified pivot rule, not the unrestricted profile theorem.\nAll identities needed below are derived here; earlier findings are motivation,\nnot proof premises. The interpolation convention is the one in accepted fact\n1252b8823bb5a290. No external theorem is used as a black box.\n\n## 1. Full affine system and the exact reversal sign condition\n\nFor positive steps h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put s=sum h_i, D=1+s and\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. All values f_i and all n+1 gradients g_i\nare independent formal variables. Write\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                                -sD||g_n||^2.\n\nA signed intrinsic array lambda has every value coefficient zero in\nV_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij, leaving the FULL matrix M. An actual E_theta array\nis Lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(i,n)+theta h_i, with other entries unchanged;\nall Lambda must be nonnegative, where 0<=theta<1. Ordinary means theta=0.\n\nLet e be the all-ones vector, e_n the last coordinate, hbar=(h,0),\nw=(h,1), and let the trajectory matrix A_mat have diagonal1 and entry h_t\nin position(i,t) when t<i. Set pi=A_mat e, so pi_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t.\nThe row Laplacian has L_ij=-lambda_ij off the diagonal and diagonal row\nsum of lambda. Define\n\n K=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w).\n\nValue cancellation says L^T e=-2hbar+2s e_n. Direct expansion of Q gives\n\n M=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2))+sym(L A_mat)\n  =sym(K A_mat)-D^2 e_n e_n^T.                         (1)\n\nIndeed the gradient part of Q_ij is\n<g_i,(A_mat g)_i-(A_mat g)_j>+(||g_j||^2-||g_i||^2)/2;\nthe grounding terms have symmetric product with A_mat equal to\ndiag(h_i(2-h_i),1). This checks every original gradient coefficient.\nThe affine equations and the constant-gradient kernel are\n\n K e=w,  K^T e=D e_n,  K pi=-D w+2D^2 e_n.             (2)\n\nFor the last equation, (1) gives e^T M e=0; PSD implies M e=0 by\napplying its quadratic form to e+t z for all real t, and A_mat^T e_n=w.\nFor actual positive-step E_theta arrays, off-diagonal K entries are\nnonpositive, including K_(i,n)=-Lambda_(i,n)-(1-theta)h_i. Its row\nsurpluses w_i are strictly positive. If K v=0, a coordinate of largest\nabsolute value and this strict surplus force v=0. Thus K is invertible.\n\nDefine the symmetric difference-reversal matrix\nDelta_ij=1_(j=n-i)-1_(j=n-i-1), with out-of-range indicators zero,\nand Phi=-Delta K^(-1)Delta. The precise canonical output is\n\n lambda_rev,ij=4D^2 Phi_ij, i!=j,\n K_rev=4D^2 Delta K^(-1)Delta-(w_rev+2D e_n)e_n^T.      (3)\n\nHere h_rev is the reversed physical list and w_rev=(h_rev,1).\nThe ACTUAL ordinary sign condition is exactly Phi_ij>=0 for ALL i!=j.\nPSD or value flow alone does not replace this condition.\n\nFor completeness the full verification of (3) uses\n\n K^(-1)w=e, K^(-1)e_n=(pi+D e)/(2D^2),\n e_n^T K^(-1)=e^T/D,\n Delta e=e_n, Delta pi=w_rev, Delta pi_rev=w,\n Delta A_rev=A_mat^T Delta.\n\nThe first line follows by applying K to pi+D e, and the remaining\nDelta identities follow directly from its two nonzero entries per row.\nThey give K_rev e=w_rev and K_rev^T e=D e_n. Off the last column, its\noff-diagonal entries are minus (3); in that column the additional\n-h_rev grounding term is exactly the displayed rank-one subtraction.\nThus all reversed value coefficients cancel. Substitution in (1) yields\n\n M_rev=4D^2 Delta K^(-1) M K^(-T)Delta.                (4)\n\nBoth sides have non-rank-one part\n4D^2 sym(Delta K^(-1) A_mat^T Delta) and remaining part\n-(w_rev+D e_n)(w_rev+D e_n)^T. This proves full PSD on all reversed\ngradients. Formula (4) is a congruence, not a monotone matrix-order path.\n\n## 2. Complete two-step leaf cone and two directed moves\n\nFor the physical leaf (1,1), put d0=g0-g1 and d1=g1-g2. Choose real\nell,c satisfying\n\n c>=ell^2/4, c<=4, ell<=min(1,c).                     (5)\n\nThe six signed entries are\n\n lambda01=2-2ell, lambda02=ell, lambda10=c-ell,\n lambda12=4+c-ell, lambda20=4-c, lambda21=4+2c.         (6)\n\nTheir flows are (-2,-2,4). Expanding them in Q gives exactly\n\n V_(1,1)+sum lambda Q=||d0||^2+ell<d0,d1>+c||d1||^2. (7)\n\nPSD is precisely c>=ell^2/4. Every nonterminal-targeted entry is\nnonnegative precisely under the remaining tests in (5). An ordinary\nleaf also requires ell>=0; an E_theta leaf requires ell>=-theta.\nConversely EVERY such two-step intrinsic array is of form (6): if ell\nis lambda02, the constant-gradient first row gives lambda01=2-2ell;\nflow0 gives lambda10=4-ell-lambda20. Defining c=4-lambda20, terminal\nflow and the terminal diagonal give the two other entries in (6).\n\nFirst use the four-edge pinching, with parameter eta:\n\n ell -> (1-eta)ell, c fixed;\n delta(lambda01,lambda10,lambda02,lambda12)\n               =eta(2ell,ell,-ell,ell).              (8)\n\nThe changes cancel every value flow. The FULL matrix change is\n-eta ell<d0,d1>, generally indefinite. At eta=1 it leaves\n||d0||^2+c||d1||^2, PSD even when the original matrix is singular.\nThe incoming-mass hypothesis has not been discarded:\nlambda20=4-c<=4 is exactly the condition making the new lambda10=c\nnonnegative. Also lambda12+lambda02=4+c>=0.\n\nFor an isolated E_theta leaf, the exact permissible eta interval is\nthe intersection of\n\n c>=(1-eta)^2 ell^2/4,\n -theta<=(1-eta)ell<=min(1,c),                        (9)\n\nwith c<=4. The remaining terminal test\n4+c-(1-eta)ell+theta>=0 follows from these displayed bounds.\nWhen ell=0 the move is identically zero. When ell!=0, the PSD interval\nis [1-2sqrt(c)/|ell|,1+2sqrt(c)/|ell|]. In particular the entire\nsegment 0<=eta<=1 is legal for every input satisfying (5),(9).\n\nSecond, after eta=1, use v=(1,-2,1) and u=A_mat^T v=(0,-1,1):\n\n delta(lambda10,lambda12,lambda20,lambda21)=(t,t,-t,2t),\n delta M=t||d1||^2.                                 (10)\n\nThe underlying formal identity is\nQ10+Q12-Q20+2Q21=||g2-g1||^2. Its values cancel individually.\nThe EXACT full PSD and sign interval for t is [-c,4-c]: PSD and\nlambda10 require c+t>=0, while lambda20 requires c+t<=4; all other\nentries remain nonnegative. Select\n\n                 t=max(0,2-c), c_new=max(c,2).       (11)\n\nThis is always inside the exact interval. Thus the selector consists\nof a full pinching (8), then the targeted directed square move (10).\nIt does not infer a sign from a Loewner-order argument.\n\nHere is the entire inverse sign calculation. Put R_leaf=4+c-2ell>0.\nFor (6), the six off-diagonal entries of Phi are\n\n Phi01=(4-c)/(6R_leaf),\n Phi02=(2c-ell-4)/(18R_leaf),\n Phi10=(2+ell)/(6R_leaf),\n Phi12=(14+2c-ell)/(18R_leaf),\n Phi20=(1-ell)/(3R_leaf),\n Phi21=(2+c)/(3R_leaf).                              (12)\n\nTo check without a symbolic black box, solve Kx=y. Since K^T e=3e2,\nx2=(y0+y1+y2)/3. The two differences a=x0-x1,b=x1-x2 solve\n\n (4-ell)a+(2+ell)b=y0-x2,\n (ell-c)a+(6+c-ell)b=y1-x2,\n\nwhose determinant is 6R_leaf. Substituting the three columns\n(0,-1,1),(-1,1,0),(1,0,0) of Delta yields (12).\nAfter (8)--(11), ell=0 and 2<=c_new<=4, so ALL six output signs\nare nonnegative. Equations (3)--(4) now give an actual ordinary\ncertificate on the reversed (here unchanged) leaf, with its full PSD.\n\n## 3. A full composition identity, retaining the actual arrays\n\nTake lists a,b of respective lengths k,m and capacities A=1+sum a,\nB=1+sum b. Let r>0 satisfy r(A+B+r)=AB, put T=A+B+r and\nkappa=(A/r)^2=(T/B)^2. Form h=(a,1+r,b). Let L be its bridge\nquery (the terminal query of a), and z its last query. Prefix and tail\nquery sets are disjoint. Define J_b=sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,z), and\n\n C_cross=sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).\n\nThe exact identity is\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)(C_cross+J_b).             (13)\n\nOne direct coefficient audit is as follows. With p_grad=g_L and q_grad=g_z,\nthe value and gradient parts of C_cross+J_b are, respectively,\n\n 2sum b_j f_j+(r-B)f_L+(2-B-r)f_z,\n sum b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(B-r)/2-r^2]||p_grad||^2\n                                      +(B+r)||q_grad||^2/2.\n\nThese follow by substituting x_tail,0=x_L-(1+r)p_grad and expanding\nsum b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>; its double is the squared sum minus\nsum b_j^2||g_j||^2. The mixed terms cancel. The identities\n(kappa-1)(B-r)=2(A+r), kappa r^2=A^2, kappa B^2=T^2\nthen verify every value and Gram coefficient of (13).\n\nGiven local arrays lambda^a,lambda^b, define the joined array by:\nretain lambda^a in the prefix; use kappa lambda^b in the tail; add\n(kappa-1)b_j to tail j->z; and set the only cross entries to\n\n lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),\n lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1, lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1).         (14)\n\nAll unlisted cross entries vanish. Then (13) proves, including every\nfunction value and full gradient,\n\n                       M_h=M_a+kappa M_b.            (15)\n\nOrdinary nonnegative local arrays therefore compose to ordinary arrays.\nThe same identity is valid for signed arrays; their actual root signs\nmust separately be checked. It is not a theorem that every schedule or\nevery supplied array has this decomposition.\n\n## 4. Exact inverse-sign transport through the same join\n\nThis section proves that sign-good canonical inverses of BOTH blocks\ngive a sign-good canonical inverse of the joined array (14). Write\ntau=r/A in (0,1), so kappa=tau^(-2) and\nB=A tau(1+tau)/(1-tau). Let pi_a be the local prefix-capacity vector,\ne_a,e_b the two all-ones vectors and e_L,e_z their terminal units.\nThe full grounded matrix of (14), obtained directly from its entries, is\n\n K_h=[ K_a+(w_a+r(kappa+1)e_L)e_L^T,\n                         -(w_a+r kappa e_L)e_z^T;\n       -(kappa-1)w_b e_L^T,              kappa K_b ]. (16)\n\nThe semicolon separates block rows. The Schur complement of kappa K_b\nis K_a+(w_a/kappa+2r e_L)e_L^T. Its inverse is obtained using\nK_a^(-1)w_a=e_a and K_a^(-1)e_L=(pi_a+A e_a)/(2A^2).\nThe scalar denominator is (1+tau)^2>0. Thus, writing K_h^(-1)\nin blocks X,Y,Z,W, multiplication or rank-one inversion gives\n\n X=K_a^(-1)-[alpha e_a+beta pi_a]e_a^T,\n Y=[tau e_a/(2B)+tau^2 pi_a/(2B^2)]e_b^T,\n Z=(tau/B)e_b e_a^T,\n W=tau^2 K_b^(-1)+tau(1-tau)e_b e_b^T/B,              (17)\n\n alpha=tau/[A(1+tau)], beta=tau/[A^2(1+tau)^2]>0.\nFor example e_L^T X=e_a^T/[A(1+tau)^2], which checks the lower\nleft block; the other blocks follow by multiplication with (16).\nThese formulas prove invertibility even for signed local blocks as long\nas their displayed inverses exist. They do not assume positive definite M.\n\nIndex the rows/columns after reversal as: m internal tail differences\nB_int; one crossing difference cros=e_tail,0-e_L; k internal prefix\ndifferences A_int; final unit last=e_prefix,0. Using (17), the entire\nOFF-diagonal list for Phi_h is\n\n (B_i,B_j): tau^2 Phi_b,ij (i!=j),\n (B_i,cros): tau^2 Phi_b,i,m,\n (cros,B_j): tau^2 Phi_b,m,j,\n (A_i,A_j): Phi_a,ij (i!=j),\n (A_i,last): Phi_a,i,k+beta a_rev,i,\n (last,A_j): Phi_a,k,j,\n (A_i,cros): beta a_rev,i,\n (last,cros): beta,\n (cros,last): tau^2/[A(1+tau)^2].                     (18)\n\nEVERY other off-diagonal entry is zero. Here a_rev,i is the physical\nprefix step in reversed order. To check (18), any internal difference\nannihilates the relevant all-ones vector; a prefix difference paired\nwith pi_a gives exactly a_rev,i. For the crossing row/last column use\n-tau/B+1/[A(1+tau)^2]=tau^2/[A(1+tau)^2]. For a prefix internal\nrow/crossing column the scalar multiplying its pi_a difference is\n1/(2A^2)-beta-tau^2/(2B^2)=beta. For last/crossing, the remaining\nconstant scalar cancels since tau/B=(1-tau)/[A(1+tau)]. These checks\nalso cover empty blocks, when the relevant internal group is empty.\n\nEvery added term in (18) is nonnegative. Consequently ordinary signs\nof both canonical block inverses imply ordinary signs of the canonical\njoined inverse. Formula (4), separately, gives its full PSD and flows.\nThis is an actual coefficient theorem, not an appeal to PSD preservation.\n\n## 5. Finite multi-move theorem with explicit decomposition hypotheses\n\nFix a finite binary tree whose leaves are either empty lists, or the\ntwo-step list (1,1) supplied with (5)--(7). Construct every internal\nnode by (13)--(14), with its own positive saturated r. Assume the\nresulting root array is an actual E_theta array for some 0<=theta<1;\ncheck all root signs, including every original incoming-terminal entry.\nThe construction itself specifies the array, not merely the schedule.\n\nApply (8) with eta=1 at each nonempty leaf, then (10)--(11) there.\nIf the product of ancestor right-child factors kappa is rho_leaf>0,\nscale that leaf's four-edge and square updates by rho_leaf. Identities\n(13)--(15) show that these are actual zero-flow root-array changes,\nand the full root remainder at every stage is the sum of the disjoint\nleaf remainders with their positive rho_leaf factors. Hence the stated\nPSD intervals remain exact, including singular leaves.\n\nNo coefficient sign is hidden by the tree. Within a nonempty leaf,\nancestor additions can affect only the two edges into its own terminal,\nand add the same quantity J_leaf>=0 to each (both physical steps are1).\nNo ancestor addition affects leaf01,10,20 or21. For the pinching,\nput q_leaf=(J_leaf+theta*1_(leaf terminal is root terminal))/rho_leaf.\nThe exact root coefficient interval for the leaf move is\n\n -q_leaf<=(1-eta)ell<=min(1,c),                       (19)\n\ntogether with the PSD interval in (9). Its other terminal test is\n4+c-(1-eta)ell+q_leaf>=0 and is redundant under (19). Root feasibility\ngives (19) at eta=0, and eta=1 satisfies it; thus the whole chosen\nsegment is legal. After pinching, every leaf is ordinary. For the\nsquare update the exact interval remains [-c,4-c], because unchanged\nancestor additions cannot alter the bounding leaf10 or20 coefficients.\nEvery coefficient outside the leaf is unchanged. Updates on distinct\nleaves have disjoint support and commute.\n\nThere are at most two moves per nonempty leaf. At their end every leaf\nis ordinary and satisfies all canonical inverse signs by (12). Empty\nleaves have K=1, M=0 and no off-diagonal signs. Induction with (18)\nnow makes the ENTIRE canonical root inverse ordinary. Equations\n(3)--(4) produce the complete reversed-schedule certificate. The root\narray itself has also become ordinary after the pinching stage.\n\nThis is a genuine finite directed selection result on the stated tree\nclass. The first move can be indefinite; no positive-definite remainder\nor monotone Loewner repair is assumed. The class includes arbitrarily\nlarge bridge steps by repeated balanced joins of capacity3 leaves.\nIt remains a decomposition-dependent class. Its physical leaf (1,1)\nhas capacity3, which is below the standard two-step primitive capacity4;\nthere is NO improved seed or asymptotic profile gain claimed here.\n\n## 6. Exact obstruction to range-restricted pivot termination\n\nConsider the definite rule allowing only pivots\n\n delta L=t u v^T, e^T u=0, v^T e=v^T pi=0,\n z_vec=A_mat^T v, with BOTH u,z_vec in Range(M),      (20)\n\nand intersecting the exact range-restricted PSD interval with every\nactual coefficient interval. This is the range-restricted mechanism\nwhose interval was developed in e20fa4c2bad75795. It is a proper\nsubclass of all valid rank-one changes and is not universally terminating.\n\nAt the two-step leaf choose ell=c=0. The input is entirely ordinary:\nlambda01=2, lambda12=4, lambda20=4, lambda21=4, others0.\nIts full nonzero remainder is ||g0-g1||^2, while (12) gives\nPhi02=-1/18<0. Thus canonical reversal of the supplied array fails.\n\nSince h0=1, column0 of A_mat is e. Consequently z_vec,0=e^T v=0.\nBut Range(M)=span(e0-e1), whose only vector with coordinate0 equal\nto zero is zero. Therefore z_vec=0, hence v=0 since A_mat is invertible.\nEVERY pivot (20) is identically zero. No sequence of such pivots can\nreach an ordinary canonical inverse from this supplied array.\n\nThis is not a zero-remainder example and not a failure of the schedule:\nthe kernel-changing move (10), t=2, is legal. It gives c=2, whose\nPhi is exactly\n\n [-1/9, 1/18, 0;\n   1/18,-5/18,1/6;\n   1/18, 2/9,-1/2].                                 (21)\n\nAll six off-diagonals are now nonnegative. The full rank has increased.\nThe obstruction excludes a universal termination claim for (20), not\nall directed moves or all alternate-certificate selection.\n\nThe same obstruction has ACTUAL LONG-STEP relevance. Set sigma=1+sqrt(2).\nStarting with b_0 empty, construct b_(m+1) from two copies of b_m by the\nsaturated balanced join. Its capacity B_m=sigma^m and length2^m-1;\nthe bridge at that join is 1+(sqrt(2)-1)B_m. Empty leaves have M=0,\nso (13)--(15) give explicit ordinary arrays with FULL M_b_m=0.\nEquation (18) inductively gives their ordinary canonical inverse signs.\n\nJoin a=(1,1), with ell=c=0 as above, to b_m:\n\n H_m=(1,1,1+r_m,b_m),\n r_m(3+B_m+r_m)=3B_m, 0<r_m<3.\n\nThe resulting ordinary array is exactly (14), with FULL remainder\n||g0-g1||^2 and horizon n=2^m+2. For every m>=1, B_m>2, hence\nr_m>1 (evaluate the increasing defining polynomial at1), so the\nactual bridge is >2. As m grows these are unbounded lengths.\n\nThe argument using coordinate0 again makes every pivot (20) zero.\nMoreover (18), with A=3, gives the explicit offending inverse entry\n\n Phi_H_m,(n-2,n)=-1/18+r_m/[3(3+r_m)^2]<-1/36<0.     (22)\n\nThe strict bound follows from (3+r_m)^2>12r_m for r_m<3.\nThus the blocked algorithm is not cured by adding arbitrary-length\nSilver tails or long physical steps. There is still an explicit escape:\nthe leaf move t=2 changes its kernel, and (18) plus the already supplied\ntail construction checks every inverse sign. No global selection or\nquantitative profile claim follows from this scoped repair.\n\n## 7. Exact singular PSD intervals beyond the restricted rule\n\nFor completeness, the missing range boundary can be characterized\nwithout assuming M positive definite. Let M>=0 and split vectors into\ntheir orthogonal Range(M) and Ker(M) parts, denoted u_R,u_N and z_R,z_N.\nConsider M+t sym(u z^T). A zero u or z gives the zero update.\n\nIf both kernel projections vanish, put\na=u^T M^+u, b=z^T M^+z, c0=u^T M^+z, d=sqrt(ab).\nThe exact PSD interval is [-2/(c0+d),2/(d-c0)], with a vanishing\nendpoint denominator removing that bound. To see it, whiten on\nRange(M); the two possible nonzero update eigenvalues are (c0+d)/2\nand (c0-d)/2, determined by trace and determinant on span(u,z).\n\nIf exactly one kernel projection vanishes, there is no nonzero t\nunless the whole corresponding vector vanishes: the kernel block is\nzero and PSD forces its entire mixed row block to vanish.\nIf both kernel projections are nonzero but not collinear, the kernel\nblock t sym(u_N z_N^T) is indefinite for every t!=0, by its negative\ntwo-dimensional determinant.\n\nIn the remaining case write z_N=k u_N with k!=0. A nonzero t is\nfeasible if and only if\n\n tk>0 and (t/(4k)) (k u_R-z_R)^T M^+ (k u_R-z_R)<=1. (23)\n\nIndeed the kernel block is tk u_Nu_N^T, the mixed block is\n(t/2)(k u_R+z_R)u_N^T, and completing its one square leaves exactly\nM_R-(t/(4k))(k u_R-z_R)(k u_R-z_R)^T. Whiten M_R to obtain (23).\nThis derives both necessity and sufficiency including all kernel rows.\nTogether with every actual multiplier inequality, it is the exact\none-parameter feasibility test. Formula (10) has k=1 and u=z, so\nthe final quadratic in (23) vanishes: kernel expansion really is legal.\n\nLiterature grounding for this last linear-algebra interface: Matlas returned\nLu--Pong, arXiv:1009.1909, Lemma3.1, the PSD generalized Schur complement\nwith its range condition. The PDF was downloaded and its extracted page4\nand notation were read through the browser. That paper states the lemma\nwithout its proof; the square completion above proves the needed special\ncase here, so no external lemma is a proof dependency. A local pdftotext\nattempt failed because that executable is absent; no package was installed.\n\n## Scope\n\nThe unrestricted all-schedule target remains open. No fact has been\nsubmitted or accepted in this round. The new obstruction is specifically\nto range-restricted pivot termination. The new finite selector requires\nthe displayed saturated tree and leaf-array hypotheses, retains actual\nlong steps, and supplies all full affine/PSD/sign checks. It is not a\nclaim that all feasible certificates have that structure.\n\n\nExact audit of the displayed identities: a SINGLE fixed five-step composition (1,1,3sqrt(2)-2,1,1), with left leaf (ell,c)=(1/2,1/2) and right leaf (-1/2,1/16), was expanded in Q(sqrt(2)) through both pinches and both square moves. All original function-value and Gram coefficients, every root coefficient sign, all off-diagonal inverse-transport formulas, and the complete reverse congruence passed. Negative reversed-entry counts were2,2,2,1,0. The audit took0.161963466 seconds of trivial exact arithmetic; it is not verification or an optimization.
